# Site audit findings, 2026-10-20

Raw output of a multi-agent audit of this repository at commit `4d1bbbf`. Thirty "finder" agents, six per area, each read the code through one lens and drove the pages in headless Chromium against a local copy. Every finding below is the finder's claim with its evidence; where three independent verifiers (reproduce, impact, refute) had voted before the run was stopped, the votes are shown. **Unvoted findings are unverified** and some will be wrong; treat them as leads.

How to resume is in `README.md` in this folder.

## Security (82 findings: 4 critical, 20 high, 38 medium, 20 low)

_Scope: security, Firestore rules, booking integrity, referral abuse, auth flows, data model and cost._

### [CRITICAL] Welcome credit can be farmed by confirm, cancel, rebook
`admin.js:128` · security · lens: referral-credits

The friend's welcome credit is written to the ledger the moment the owner clicks Confirm, and nothing takes it back when the visit is cancelled or declined. isRepeat() only counts earlier requests whose status is done or confirmed, so a cancelled request is invisible and the same person can be confirmed again (with the same or another code) and collect another welcome credit.

**Evidence.** admin.js:128 `if (o.id === r.id || (o.status !== "done" && o.status !== "confirmed")) return false;` and admin.js:170-173 grant `welcome-<requestId>` on act === "confirmed". Mock-backend run (signedin.js): fixtures had again@x.com with an earlier CANCELLED request R2prev (refWelcomeGranted true, ledger welcome-R2prev +$25) and a new request R2 with code ABCDEF. The R2 card showed no returning-customer warning (R2_has_repeat_warning=false) and already listed "Client credit: $25"; clicking Confirm wrote welcome-R2cccccc +$25, leaving again_balance = [welcome-R2prevvv:25, welcome-R2cccccc:25] and the Credits tab showing again@x.com $50. Each cycle needs only the client cancelling from account.html (backend.js:361 cancelRequest touches status and holds only) and the owner's routine Confirm click.

**Impact.** With maxPercentOfVisit at 100, a visitor can stack $25 welcome credits ($50 commercial) across confirm/cancel cycles until a visit is free; the owner pays real money for a customer who has never had a visit, and the only hint is a growing balance on the card.

**Suggestion.** Treat anyone who already holds a welcome credit (or any earlier request with refWelcomeGranted) as a repeat in isRepeat(), and on cancel/decline of a confirmed-but-unvisited request either delete `welcome-<id>` or grant the welcome credit only at Done (and say so in the copy).

### [CRITICAL] Anyone can create requests and holds; App Check not enforced
`firestore.rules:117` · security · lens: data-model-cost

Unauthenticated writes to requests (and a hold in the same batch) are allowed by the rules, and App Check is still in monitor-only mode, so any script with the public config at /__/firebase/init.json can flood the inbox or fill every open window on the public calendar. Nothing rate-limits creates.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:117 `allow create: if isAdmin() || quoteFields(request.resource.data);` and 150-154 let a visitor create `holds/{hid}` when `hid == request.resource.data.requestId` in the same write. backend.js:54 only activates App Check client-side (`useAppCheck = SITE && !useEmu && !!C.appCheckKey`); docs/SETUP-firebase.md:201 says enforcement 'starts in monitor-only mode'. Ran the repo's rules in the Firestore emulator (scratchpad/work/security/data-model-cost/rules-test/test.js, run.log): an unauthenticated client created a well-formed request (A1), one under another person's email that then appeared in that customer's own query (A3, C1), and two request+hold batches for 2026-11-03 0800 (A6.0/A6.1), which with plans.js:74 `capacity: 2` marks that window full. Also confirmed the public hold set is listable without filter (B1).

**Impact.** A bot or a bored competitor can mark the whole 60-day calendar full (about 2 holds x 4 windows x 6 days x 9 weeks = ~430 cheap writes), push junk into the admin 'New' list and tab-title count, and plant fake requests inside real customers' account pages. Each junk request can also trigger nothing visible to the owner because Formspree is only called from the real form.

**Suggestion.** Flip App Check to Enforce for Cloud Firestore in the console (the documented step), then add a server-side guard for creates (a Cloud Function on requests onCreate that auto-declines bursts, or a Function-only create path) since rules cannot count holds or rate-limit. Keep the PR-preview/emulator exemption in mind when enforcing (see the preview-channel finding).

### [CRITICAL] Visitors can fill every calendar window; holds validate nothing but a digit pattern
`firestore.rules:150` · security · lens: rules-adversarial

The visitor path for holds (line 150-156) only requires holdShape() plus a request created in the same batch. holdShape (lines 126-130) checks that date matches ^[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}$ and slot is a non-empty string of 10 chars or fewer: nothing about capacity, closed days, blocked windows, leadDays, horizonDays, weekdays or real slot ids, and there is no per-visitor limit. App Check is still monitor-only (docs/SETUP-firebase.md:201), so a script needs no browser.

**Evidence.** Loaded the repo's firestore.rules into the Firestore emulator (@firebase/rules-unit-testing, script rules-attack.test.js in my scratch dir) and replayed backend.js submitRequest() batches as an unauthenticated client: V07 five requests+holds on the same day and window (plans.js capacity: 2) all accepted; V02 date '9999-99-99' slot 'zzz' accepted; V03 hold on a day seeded as days/2026-12-25 {closed:true} accepted; V04 hold in a window listed in days/{date}.blockedSlots accepted; V05 past date 2000-01-01 accepted; V06 a date three years past horizonDays accepted. calendar.js:52-55 counts holds per day+slot against capacity, so after V07 the public calendar shows that window as Full.

**Impact.** Anyone with a short script (or a patient person using the form) can make every bookable window show 'Full' for the next 60 days, and bury the admin inbox under fake requests. Cleanup is manual: the owner must type DELETE per request (admin.js:271-275) or remove each hold in the day panel (admin.js:405-407). Real clients cannot book meanwhile.

**Suggestion.** Short term: turn App Check to Enforce (the SETUP doc already names the step) and add cheap checks to the hold rule: slot in ['0800','1130','1500','1830'], date between request.time + leadDays and + horizonDays (timestamp.date(int(parts[0]), int(parts[1]), int(parts[2])) from date.split('-')), and !get(/days/$(date)).data.closed. Proper fix: create request+hold through a callable Cloud Function (Blaze is on) that counts holds for the date+slot in a transaction and refuses when capacity is reached, since rules alone cannot enforce a count race-free.

### [CRITICAL] Calendar capacity is enforced only in page code, never by rules
`firestore.rules:150` · security · lens: booking-integrity

Nothing server-side counts holds against plans.js `capacity`. The rules accept any number of request+hold pairs for any day and window from unsigned visitors, so two honest visitors can both take the last spot, and a script can mark every window for the next 60 days as Full.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:150-156 `allow create: if holdShape(request.resource.data) && (isAdmin() || (hid == request.resource.data.requestId && ((getAfter(reqPath(hid)).data.createdAt == request.time && mirrors(hid)) || customerHolds(hid))))`; holdShape (126-129) only checks `isDate(h.date) && str(h.slot, 10)`, and requests create (117) is `isAdmin() || quoteFields(...)` with no sign-in. backend.js:148-166 writes request+hold in one browser-side batch and calendar.js:52-55 is the only place `capacity` is read (`left = Math.max(0, cap - taken)`). Stubbing three holds in a capacity-2 window on the local copy renders "Morning Full" on availability.html and "Midday 11:30–2:30 · 3 of 2 taken" on admin.html. App Check is monitor-only (docs/SETUP-firebase.md:201), so a plain script is not blocked; one 500-write commit creates 250 request+hold pairs.

**Impact.** Near-full windows get over-requested and the owner has to turn a confirmed-looking client away; worse, anyone can fill the whole calendar so every visitor sees "Full" and bookings are lost. A bot that uses the form also burns the 50 Formspree emails a month.

**Suggestion.** Give holds deterministic ids `{date}_{slot}_{n}` with n ≤ capacity and have the rules require that id shape (a create on an existing id fails, so Firestore itself enforces capacity; the client tries n = 1..capacity), then switch App Check to Enforce; or move writes behind a Cloud Function that counts holds in a transaction.

### [HIGH] Legacy staff-only notes on requests are readable by the customer
`admin.js:44` · security · lens: data-model-cost

Older requests stored adminNotes and assignedTo on the customer-readable request document; they are only moved to internal/{id} when staff save that request's edit panel, so until then the client's own query returns the staff's private notes about them.

**Evidence.** admin.js:44-45 comment: 'Older requests kept the staff-only fields on the request itself; the internal/ document wins once it exists, and saving moves them over.' admin.js:292 only deletes them on a manual Save. backend.js:103-105 keeps `"adminNotes", "assignedTo"` in the STR whitelist of cleanRequest(), and firestore.rules:116 `allow read: if isAdmin() || isCustomerOf(resource.data)` returns whole documents. Emulator C1 (run.log) with a seeded legacy request: the customer's own emailLower query returned `adminNotes = "STAFF ONLY: dog bites, enter by side door, owes $40"`. Whether such documents still exist in production can only be checked in the console.

**Impact.** Any client who opens DevTools on account.html (or uses the SDK with their own sign-in) can read whatever staff wrote about them before the internal/ split, which may include unflattering notes, amounts owed or access details.

**Suggestion.** Run a one-time migration (admin page button or a script with the Admin SDK) that moves adminNotes/assignedTo from every request into internal/{id} and deletes them from the request, then remove the two keys from STR in backend.js and from the merge fallback in admin.js.

### [HIGH] Applied credit ignores the confirmed final price; You pay is wrong
`admin.js:123` · bug · lens: referral-credits

visitPrice() only reads the client's estimate (estimatePerVisit/estimateOneVisit) and never the quotedPrice the owner sets, so both the per-visit cap and the stored youPay are computed from the estimate. The client then sees a confirmed final price and a different You pay figure on the same card.

**Evidence.** admin.js:123 `function visitPrice(r) { return typeof r.estimatePerVisit === "number" ? r.estimatePerVisit : ... : null; }`; admin.js:309 `youPay: ... Math.max(0, rounded(price - n))`. Mock run on request R3 (estimatePerVisit 200, quotedPrice 150, $25 balance): the card read "Estimate $150 per visit · confirmed price (estimate was $200 per visit)" and after Apply the write was {creditApplied: 25, youPay: 175} and the card said "Credit applied: $25. Client pays $175." account.html (account-390.png, account.js:112-114) then shows "$150 per visit · confirmed price", "Credit applied −$25", "You pay $175" in both EN and ES.

**Impact.** Every client whose final price differs from the placeholder estimate is told a wrong amount to pay: too high when the final price is lower (disputes, lost trust), too low when it is higher (revenue loss or arguments at the door). The README says the estimates are placeholders, so this is the normal case today.

**Suggestion.** Make visitPrice() prefer r.quotedPrice when it is a number, and recompute youPay whenever quotedPrice is saved after credit was applied (or compute youPay at render time instead of storing it).

### [HIGH] Repeat-customer phone match fails when a country code differs
`admin.js:130` · security · lens: referral-credits

isRepeat() compares phone numbers by stripping non-digits and testing strict equality, so "+1 980 555 0100" (11 digits, as iPhone autofill writes it) never matches "(980) 555-0100" (10 digits). The new-customer check that gates both the friend's and the referrer's credit is defeated by the most common formatting difference.

**Evidence.** admin.js:130 `return o.emailLower === r.emailLower || (digits(o.phone).length >= 10 && digits(o.phone) === digits(r.phone));`. quote.js:296 accepts any phone with 10+ digits. Mock run: an earlier DONE request with phone "+1 980 555 0100" and a new referred request with "(980) 555-0100" (R1_isRepeat_eval: r0="19805550100", r1="9805550100", equal=false); the R1 card showed no "Looks like a returning customer" warning and Confirm wrote welcome-R1bbbbbb +$25, then Mark done wrote referral-R1bbbbbb +$25 to referrer@x.com.

**Impact.** An existing customer who books under a second email with a slightly differently formatted phone collects a welcome credit and earns their friend a reward, contrary to refer.html rule 1 ("your friend must not have had a visit with us before"); the owner pays $50-$100 per missed match.

**Suggestion.** Normalise to the last 10 digits (strip a leading 1 when 11 digits) before comparing, and apply the same normalisation to the stored phone at submit time.

### [HIGH] Status, hold and credit changes are separate writes, not one batch
`admin.js:315` · bug · lens: data-model-cost

Confirm/decline/done writes the request first and only then releases or creates the hold in a second request; applying credit writes the ledger line first and the request second. A dropped connection between the two leaves a declined job still blocking the public calendar, or a 'used' credit line the request never shows.

**Evidence.** admin.js:315-317 `B.updateRequest(id, { status: what }).then(function () { if (CLOSED[what]) return B.releaseHolds(id); return B.ensureHold(...); })`; admin.js:308-309 `B.addCredit({ id: "use-" + id, ... amount: -n ...}).then(function () { return B.updateRequest(id, { creditApplied: n, youPay: ... }); })`; timeclock.js:131 repeats the two-step pattern. Nothing in the rules keeps staff writes in step (customer writes are: rules:96-113). Emulator check D2/D3 (run.log): owner set status 'declined' without touching the hold, and the anonymous calendar query still returned that hold as taken. By contrast backend.js:362-367 cancelRequest() and :423-430 deleteRequest() correctly use one batch.

**Impact.** On a phone with flaky signal (the admin page's main use), a declined or finished visit keeps its window 'full' so a new customer cannot book it; a credit can be deducted from a client's balance with no 'applied' line on the request and no Undo button, and a retry then sets `creditApplied` from a fresh input while the ledger keeps the first amount.

**Suggestion.** Add backend.setStatus(id, status) and applyCredit(id, amount) that put the request patch, the hold set/delete and the ledger line in a single db.batch() (as cancelRequest already does), and have admin.js and timeclock.js call those.

### [HIGH] Credit stays spent when the visit is cancelled or declined
`admin.js:316` · bug · lens: referral-credits

Applying credit writes a `use-<id>` ledger line of -n. When that request is later cancelled by the client, declined by the owner, or deleted, nothing reverses the line: the client's balance stays reduced and the cancelled card still says the credit was applied.

**Evidence.** admin.js:316 `if (CLOSED[what]) return B.releaseHolds(id);` (decline/done only free the hold); backend.js:361-367 cancelRequest() only sets status/cancelledBy and deletes holds; backend.js:420-427 deleteRequest() never touches credits. Mock run, request R4 (status cancelled by client, creditApplied 25, ledger welcome +25 and use-R4 -25): the Finished list showed "CANCELLED ... Credit applied: $25. Client pays $155. Undo" and the Credits tab listed cancelme@x.com $0. account.js:112-114 shows the same "Credit applied −$25 / You pay" rows on the client's cancelled card.

**Impact.** A client who cancels a confirmed visit (allowed free with 24 hours' notice per terms) silently loses the credit they earned unless the owner notices the Undo link on a cancelled card; the terms promise credit "doesn't expire" and "stays yours".

**Suggestion.** When a request with creditApplied > 0 is cancelled, declined or deleted, delete `use-<id>` and clear creditApplied/youPay (admin paths in act(), client cancel in backend.cancelRequest via the rules' customerCancels allowing that field), and show a warning on cancelled cards that still carry applied credit.

### [HIGH] Admin "Remove" drops the hold but leaves the request confirmed
`admin.js:407` · bug · lens: booking-integrity

The day panel's Remove button deletes only the holds document. The request keeps its status, date and window, so the owner's lists and the client's account still say Confirmed while the public calendar reopens the window.

**Evidence.** admin.js:405-407 `} else if (what === "drop") { if (!confirm(t("adm.removeConfirm"))) return; p = B.deleteHold(b.getAttribute("data-hold")); }` and backend.js:429 `function deleteHold(id) { return holdRef(id).delete(); }`; the prompt reads "Remove this booking from the calendar?". With a stubbed backend on the local admin.html, Remove on "Alice Confirmed" logged only `deleteHold req1aaaaa`; afterwards the day panel read "Morning 8:00–11:00 · 0 of 2 taken" while the week-ahead panel still listed "Alice Confirmed · Morning (8:00–11:00) · Confirmed" and the request kept status confirmed and its date.

**Impact.** The owner believes a booking is gone, another visitor can take the window, and the original client still sees a confirmed visit for that day on account.html: a double booking or a no-show by the business.

**Suggestion.** Make Remove act on the request in the same batch as the hold (decline it, or clear its date/slot through reschedule), or relabel it "Free this window only" and keep showing the request as scheduled.

### [HIGH] Footer "Staff" link signs clients out of their account
`admin.js:579` · bug · lens: auth-flows

admin.html calls B.signOut() for any signed-in user who is not on the team. The site has one Firebase session for clients and staff, and every page's footer carries a "Staff" link to admin.html, so a curious client who taps it is logged out everywhere and shown a staff-only message.

**Evidence.** admin.js:577-579: `B.isAdmin(u).then(function (rec) { if (rec) {...} else { show("login"); $("[data-admin-notadmin]").hidden = false; B.signOut(); } })`. Footer link in all 12 pages (`grep -c 'href="admin.html"' *.html`, e.g. account.html:224). Mock run (pages.test.js A1): a verified client opening admin.html produced calls `["isAdmin(client@example.com)","signOut"]`, localStorage mkg.account was cleared, and the page showed "This account isn't on the team. Ask the owner to invite this email, then sign in here with Google." (screenshot admin-client-signed-out.png).

**Impact.** Clients lose their session on a stray tap; email-link clients must request and open a new link to get back in. The message also tells clients to ask the owner for an invite.

**Suggestion.** When isAdmin() is null, keep the session and show a client-facing notice with a link to account.html instead of signing out (only call signOut for the removed-admin path in leave()); consider dropping the "Staff" link from client-facing footers.

### [HIGH] Recurring plans book a single date; no series or next visit
`backend.js:163` · ux · lens: booking-integrity

A weekly, biweekly or monthly plan request holds exactly one date and window. There is no next-visit field, no generated series, and marking the first visit done releases the only hold, so recurring clients vanish from the calendar.

**Evidence.** backend.js:163-165 writes one hold `if (doc.date && doc.slot) { batch.set(holdRef(ref.id), {...}) }`; quote.js:396 stores `p.date`/`p.slot` once and quote.js:408 stores `frequency` as a label only; plans.js:115-128 and 263-279 define the frequencies. A grep of admin.js, backend.js and account.js finds no recurrence, series or next-visit logic; the edit panel can only change the frequency label (admin.js:90-101), and "done" calls releaseHolds (admin.js:316, backend.js:396-400).

**Impact.** After the first visit, a weekly client's windows show as open to the public and are absent from the week-ahead panel; the owner must add a phone booking by hand every week to keep the calendar truthful, or stop trusting it and over-book their own regulars.

**Suggestion.** Add a `nextVisit` date (or generate holds for the next N occurrences) on confirmed plan requests, created when the owner confirms or marks a visit done, and list it in the week-ahead panel.

### [HIGH] Password sign-up with a stranger's email can pre-hijack their account
`backend.js:195` · security · lens: web-security · votes 3/3 real (impact: medium, reproduce: high, refute: medium) · **confirmed**

Anyone can create an email+password account for an address they do not own; Firebase then emails that address a genuine 'Verify your email for MKG Services' link. If the real person clicks it, the attacker's account becomes verified and the rules grant it every request under that email, including notes that the commercial form invites to contain alarm or key details, plus the ability to move or cancel bookings.

**Evidence.** backend.js:195-203 `signUpPassword(name, email, password)` calls `auth.createUserWithEmailAndPassword(email, password)` then `u.sendEmailVerification({ url: actionUrl() })` with no ownership check; firestore.rules:35 `verified()` (`email_verified == true`) and :93 `isCustomerOf(r) { return verified() && r.emailLower == myEmail(); }` are the only gates; commercial-quote.html:136 placeholder: 'Alarm or key details, areas to skip...'. Once verified, a later Google sign-in by the real owner links to the same account (Firebase 'one account per email'), so the attacker's password stays.

**Impact.** A targeted attacker who knows a client's email (public for most businesses) is one unsolicited-but-genuine verification click away from that client's name, phone, ZIP, booking times and notes, and can cancel their visits. Reasoned from backend.js and the rules; could only be fully confirmed against the live Auth project.

**Suggestion.** For clients keep only Google and the one-time email link (both prove mailbox possession at sign-in time) and remove the password sign-up tab; keep passwords for staff accounts the owner creates. If passwords must stay, a beforeSignIn blocking function can refuse password sign-ins whose account was verified long after creation, or email the owner on each new account.

### [HIGH] Admin sees only the 300 newest requests, including spam
`backend.js:378` · bug · lens: booking-integrity

The request stream is `orderBy createdAt desc, limit(300)` with no status or date filter. Anything older than the 300 newest records disappears from the request list, the week-ahead panel and the day-panel names, while its hold still blocks the calendar.

**Evidence.** backend.js:378 `db.collection("requests").orderBy("createdAt", "desc").limit(300).onSnapshot(...)`; admin.js:339-342 builds the week ahead from that `items` array and admin.js:365-369 `nameFor` falls back to "#ABCDEF" when a hold's request is not loaded. Requests can be created without signing in (firestore.rules:117) and App Check is monitor-only.

**Impact.** A burst of 300 bot submissions (or a busy year) hides older but still-upcoming confirmed bookings from the only admin view; 50 form submissions also exhaust the monthly Formspree quota so real requests stop emailing the owner.

**Suggestion.** Stream open requests separately (`where status in ['new','confirmed']`, with a high or no limit) plus a paginated history, show a "N more not shown" count, and enforce App Check.

### [HIGH] Admin page silently truncates to the newest 300 requests
`backend.js:379` · bug · lens: data-model-cost

watchRequests() loads only the 300 most recently created requests with no pagination; once the business passes 300 requests, older open bookings disappear from the request list, the week-ahead panel, the calendar day panel, the time clock, the repeat-customer check and the tab counts.

**Evidence.** backend.js:379 `db.collection("requests").orderBy("createdAt", "desc").limit(300).onSnapshot(...)`. Everything in admin.js derives from that array: admin.js:68 `all: items.length` (counts cap at 300), :339-342 week-ahead filters `items`, :367-368 nameFor() shows `"#" + id.slice(0,6)` when the hold's request is not loaded, :127 isRepeat() scans `items` only, timeclock.js:45 reqById() uses the same list. admin.html has no 'load more' control (grep). A confirmed recurring customer booked months ago is older than 300 newer quote requests well within a year at a few requests a day.

**Impact.** Lost bookings: a confirmed job vanishes from the schedule and the crew's 'my day' list without any notice; the calendar shows an anonymous '#A1B2C3' hold the owner cannot identify; repeat customers who referred themselves get a second welcome credit because the earlier request is no longer in the window; the search box only searches what is loaded.

**Suggestion.** Load open work without a cap (`where("status", "in", ["new","confirmed"])`, which needs a composite index on status+createdAt committed in firestore.indexes.json) and page the Finished list with startAfter plus a 'load older' button; show 'newest N of M' when truncated.

### [HIGH] App Check monitor-only leaves anonymous Firestore writes unthrottled: calendar and inbox flooding
`docs/SETUP-firebase.md:201` · security · lens: web-security · votes 3/3 real (impact: medium, reproduce: high, refute: medium) · **confirmed**

App Check is registered but not enforced, so firestore.rules is the only gate on the public create path, and the rules (correctly) cannot check capacity, horizon or rate. A script using the public config at /__/firebase/init.json and the Firestore REST API can hold every open window for 60 days and push every real request off the 300-newest admin list.

**Evidence.** docs/SETUP-firebase.md:201-203 `It starts in **monitor-only** mode. To block everything else ... then **Enforce**.` (not yet done per the FACTS). firestore.rules:117 `allow create: if isAdmin() || quoteFields(request.resource.data);` accepts unauthenticated creates, and lines 150-156 let the same batch create the hold; quoteFields only checks shapes (`dateOrNone(d.date) && str(d.slot, 10)`), not that the day is bookable, the slot is a real id, or the window has capacity. backend.js:378 `db.collection("requests").orderBy("createdAt", "desc").limit(300)` is what admin.html shows. plans.js:74-77 capacity 2, 4 slots, 60-day horizon: 480 writes mark every window 'Full' in calendar.js (`left === 0`).

**Impact.** Visitors see 'Full' on every day and cannot book (lost bookings); 301 junk requests hide every real request from the admin page; every write is billed on Blaze. No attacker is needed for the 'Enforce' step to be the documented next action, but today nothing stops a trivial script.

**Suggestion.** Follow the doc's own next step and enforce App Check for Cloud Firestore once the Verified percentage looks right; longer term move `submitRequest` behind a callable Cloud Function that enforces App Check and a per-IP/per-email rate limit and validates date, slot and capacity server-side, and make the admin list page or filter by status rather than newest-300.

### [HIGH] quoteFields() sits within 3 fields of Firestore's 1,000-expression rule ceiling
`firestore.rules:60` · bug · lens: data-model-cost

The single `quoteFields()` expression is so long that a commercial request with a date and an invite code is evaluated within ~26 trivial expressions of Firestore's per-request limit of 1,000 evaluated expressions; adding three more optional form fields makes those real submissions fail with PERMISSION_DENIED, and quote.js then hides the failure behind the email-only fallback.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:60-83: a 40-key `hasOnly` list, 43 `&&` clauses, 16 optStr, 10 optNum, 6 str and refOk's exists+2 gets, all in one function. Measured in the emulator with exact replicas of quote.js payload() + backend.js submitRequest() (rules-test/realistic.js, realistic.log; rules-test/resid.js, resid.raw.log): every real path is ALLOWED today, but padding the function shows the headroom: commercial plan+date+code with hold refused after +27 `&& true`, or after +3 present optional string fields, or +9 absent ones; commercial follow-up+date+code +43; residential plan+date+code +48. A payload carrying all 40 allowed keys is already refused with 'Unable to evaluate the expression as the maximum of 1000 expressions to evaluate has been reached. for create @ L117' (more.raw.log), and +64 trivial clauses make the compiler reject the ruleset ('Expression is too complex to evaluate safely'). Production counts may differ slightly; only a live Rules-Playground test settles the exact margin.

**Impact.** The rules comment invites adding fields ('exactly what the quote form sends'); the next field (gate code, parking, preferred crew) silently breaks the highest-value path: commercial requests with a chosen window. quote.js:507-511 catches the refusal, emails Formspree and shows 'Request sent', so no request or hold is saved, the window stays open for double booking, and nobody sees an error.

**Suggestion.** Split the validation into small per-segment functions selected by `d.segment`, stop storing fields the database never reads (`_subject`, `submittedAt`, `requestedTime`), keep size caps only on free-text fields, and run the emulator rules test in the PR workflow so headroom is measured on every rules change. Verify the live margin now with the console's Rules Playground on a commercial payload.

### [HIGH] Any team member can set a client's payment link and final price
`firestore.rules:118` · security · lens: rules-adversarial

`allow update: if isAdmin() || ...` lets every admin, including one who exists only as an adminInvites entry, change any field of any request with no shape check. That includes payLink, quotedPrice, creditApplied, youPay and even emailLower, which are money fields the owner may assume are theirs.

**Evidence.** Emulator check S01: signed in as admins/staff-uid with role 'admin', update requests/req-bob {payLink:'https://evil.example/pay', quotedPrice:1} was ALLOWED; S06 {creditApplied:999, youPay:0} ALLOWED; S02 changing a request's email/emailLower to the staff member's own address ALLOWED. account.js:105 `var pay = safeLink(r.payLink) || safeLink(C.payments && C.payments.link)` and :123 renders it as the 'Pay online' button on every confirmed request; safeLink only requires https://. admin.js:241 shows the payment-link field to every admin, not just the owner.

**Impact.** A departing or compromised staff Google account can point every confirmed client's 'Pay online' button at their own Stripe link, or mark visits as paid with credit, and nothing on the admin page tells the owner. The owner's own setup doc describes admins as trusted, but the UI and rules give cleaners the same money controls as the owner.

**Suggestion.** In the request update rule, require isTeamOwner() when changed().hasAny(['payLink','quotedPrice','creditApplied','youPay','email','emailLower']) (add a `changedByAdmin` helper), and hide those inputs for non-owners in admin.js card(). Consider the same owner gate for days writes beyond blocking single windows.

### [HIGH] Credits ledger: any admin or invitee can mint, edit or delete credit
`firestore.rules:258` · security · lens: rules-adversarial

`allow write: if isAdmin()` on credits has no shape or amount validation and applies to every admin and to anyone whose email merely appears in adminInvites. The admin page hides the Credits tab from non-owners (admin.js:526), so the owner believes adjustments and deletions are theirs alone; the rules say otherwise.

**Evidence.** Emulator checks: S03 staff-uid (role admin) set credits/staff-gift {emailLower:'friend@example.com', amount:100000, type:'adjust'} ALLOWED; S04 a credit with no emailLower and amount 'lots' ALLOWED; S05 deleting a client's welcome credit ALLOWED; I02 an account with only an adminInvites entry and no admins document wrote a credit ALLOWED; S07 the same staff account deleted users/alice-uid (her referral record) ALLOWED. admin.js:305-309 'Apply credit' subtracts a balance from a visit's price and writes youPay.

**Impact.** Credits are money off future visits (plans.js referral.maxPercentOfVisit is 100, so a visit can be free). Any team member can grant themselves or a friend unlimited credit from the browser console, or erase a client's earned credit, and the ledger shows it only if the owner happens to look.

**Suggestion.** Split the credits rule: create allowed for isAdmin() only when type in ['welcome','referral','used'], emailLower is a string, amount is a number bounded by the plans.js rewards (welcome/referral) or negative (used), requestId a string and the id follows the 'welcome-'/'referral-'/'use-' pattern; create of type 'adjust', every update, and every delete only for isTeamOwner(). Gate users/referrers deletes to the owner too.

### [HIGH] Public Formspree endpoint lets anyone forge owner notifications and burn the quota
`plans.js:40` · security · lens: web-security · votes 3/3 real (impact: high, reproduce: high, refute: high) · **confirmed**

The notification endpoint is a bare Formspree form URL that any browser or curl can POST to with arbitrary field names, so the owner's inbox can be filled with messages that look exactly like real site notifications (including a 'Manage it' link of the attacker's choosing), the Reply-To can be anyone, and the free tier's 50 emails a month can be exhausted in seconds so real booking emails are held in Formspree's 'Over limit' folder.

**Evidence.** plans.js:40 `formEndpoint: "https://formspree.io/f/xjyglbgo"`; app.js:346-353 `notify(fields)` does `fetch(C.formEndpoint, { method: "POST", ... body: JSON.stringify(fields) })` from the visitor's browser with whatever keys it is given; quote.js:454 adds `put("Manage it", location.origin + "/admin.html")` to every email, and quote.js:509-510 (`// Firebase refused ... the email copy alone still reaches us.`) means an email with no matching database record is already a normal, legitimate state, so a forged one cannot be told apart. Formspree disables its reCAPTCHA for JSON/AJAX submissions, so nothing in front of the endpoint checks origin or humanity. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:75-77 confirms the 50/month limit and the 'Over limit' behaviour.

**Impact.** A spammer or competitor can silently stop the owner's booking notifications for the rest of any month (the admin page still shows them, but the owner relies on email per the docs), and a targeted attacker can send a pixel-perfect 'Quote request from ...' email whose 'Manage it' link points at a look-alike admin login to capture the owner's password, which then opens the whole customer database.

**Suggestion.** Send notifications server-side instead of from the browser: a Firestore onCreate trigger (the project is already on Blaze) via the Trigger Email extension the setup doc already mentions, then set `formEndpoint` to ""; until then, drop the 'Manage it' link from emails (teach the owner to type the admin URL) and turn on Formspree's spam filtering and honeypot.

### [HIGH] Privacy page promises deletion and retention that nothing implements
`privacy.html:83` · legal · lens: data-model-cost

privacy.html tells clients they can have their data deleted and that records are deleted once no longer needed, but the only delete path removes one request at a time (owner only) and leaves the credits ledger, the referral code records, the Auth account and the Formspree copy in place; no retention timer exists anywhere.

**Evidence.** privacy.html:83 'Email {email} to see what we hold about you or to have it deleted. We keep request records for as long as we may need them ... then delete them.' (same in Spanish, i18n.js:1520). backend.js:423-430 deleteRequest() deletes requests/{id}, holds and internal/{id} only. credits lines keyed by emailLower (backend.js:482), users/{uid} and referrers/{code} have `allow delete: if isAdmin()` (firestore.rules:239, 252) but no page calls them; account.html has no delete-account control (grep). Emulator D4/D5 (run.log): after the owner deleted Jane's request her credits line still existed. No TTL field, scheduled job or doc step mentions retention (grep for backup/ttl/retention in README.md, docs/*.md returns nothing).

**Impact.** A deletion request from a client cannot be honoured as written without console work in Firestore, Firebase Auth and Formspree, so the public promise is currently untrue; as the customer list grows this becomes a standing compliance and trust gap, and Formspree keeps its own copy of every name, phone and note.

**Suggestion.** Add an owner-only 'forget this client' action that batches deletion of their requests, holds, internal docs, credits, users/referrers records and deletes the Auth user via a small Cloud Function; set a `deleteAfter` timestamp on closed requests and enable a Firestore TTL policy on it; document the Formspree inbox step; or soften the privacy wording to match.

### [HIGH] Quote form accepts closed days, full windows and foreign slot ids
`quote.js:34` · bug · lens: booking-integrity

`bookable()` checks only lead time and horizon. A day that is a non-workday, closed or blocked by the owner, already full, or a slot that does not exist for the segment passes from the URL and at submit, and the rules accept it too.

**Evidence.** quote.js:32-36 `return ymd >= first && ymd <= last;` is the only check at init (572-574) and at submit (468). Playwright on the local copy: `quote.html?date=2026-10-25&slot=0800` (a Sunday) reached review with "Timing Sunday, October 25, Morning (8:00–11:00)" while the calendar cell was `is-closed is-selected` and disabled; with formEndpoint blanked the demo confirmation showed the Sunday. `?slot=1830` on the residential page gave "After hours (6:30–9:30 pm)" (a commercial-only window) and `?slot=bogus` gave "Requested: Monday, October 26, bogus". firestore.rules:70 `dateOrNone(d.date) && str(d.slot, 10)` accepts all of these, plus past dates and strings like 2026-13-45.

**Impact.** No crafted URL is needed: a visitor picks a day on availability.html, the owner closes it or it fills before they finish, and the request still lands with a hold on a closed day; the client sees a confirmation for a day the business is off and the admin card shows no flag.

**Suggestion.** Have `bookable()` use `cal.dayInfo(date)` (closed, blocked, full, slot in this segment's list) and re-fetch availability on submit; mirror it in the rules (slot in the fixed list, date not before request.time, weekday in the list, `days/{date}` not closed or blocked for that slot).

### [HIGH] Refused invite code is dropped silently; visitor still told $25 off
`quote.js:501` · ux · lens: referral-credits

When Firestore refuses a request because of its code (the visitor's own code, or a referrer whose record was deleted), quote.js resubmits without the code and stores refDropped, but the visitor is never told. Before submit the field says "Invite applied: $25 off your first visit", the confirmation page still lists the invite row, the code stays in localStorage, and admin.html never displays refDropped.

**Evidence.** quote.js:498-502 `catch(function (err) { if (!data.ref) throw err; ... delete plain.ref; delete plain.refAmount; plain.refDropped = data.ref; ... return B.submitRequest(plain); })`; quote.js:333 and 477-481 render the done summary from draft.ref via refApplied(), not from what was saved; quote.js:505 only calls INV.forget() when data.ref survived. grep shows refDropped is read nowhere in admin.js. Simulated with the mock (dropped.js, rejectRef mode): submits were [{ref:K7M2QX, refAmount:25}, {refDropped:K7M2QX}], yet the confirmation page (quote-done-dropped.png) showed "Invite: Friend's code K7M2QX: $25 off your first visit" and localStorage mkg.ref still held the code. Signed in as the code's owner, the quote page also showed "Invite applied: $25 off your first visit" (q_signedin_status) because checkReferral only tests that the code exists.

**Impact.** A client who tested their own link (plausible: the QR card invites it) carries their own code for 60 days, is promised $25 on their next booking, gets nothing, and the owner cannot see why because the dropped code is stored but never surfaced; genuine friends of a deleted referrer hit the same wall.

**Suggestion.** Detect the own-code case client-side (compare the checked referrer uid with the signed-in user, or let checkReferral return the owner's emailLower hash) and show a clear message; when the fallback path runs, re-render the done summary without the invite row, forget the stored code, and show refDropped on the admin card.

### [HIGH] Time clock marks visits done without granting referral credits
`timeclock.js:131` · bug · lens: referral-credits

Clocking out of a job offers to mark the request done and updates the status directly; grants() in admin.js only runs from the request card's status buttons, so the referrer's reward (and a skipped welcome credit) is never written. Once the status is done, the card hides the Mark done button, so there is no button left that would grant it.

**Evidence.** timeclock.js:131 `return B.updateRequest(r.id, { status: "done" }).then(function () { return B.releaseHolds(r.id); });` with no call into admin.js grants(); admin.js:313-320 is the only caller of grants(r, what); admin.js:230 renders Mark done only when `s !== "done"`. The card afterwards shows "Referrer credit: pending" (admin.js:147) with no way to resolve it except Reopen then Mark done.

**Impact.** Whenever a crew finishes a referred friend's first visit from the time clock (the natural flow for staff), the referrer never receives the $25/$50 the site promised ("Yours is added when that visit is done"), and the owner has no prompt that anything is outstanding.

**Suggestion.** Move grants() into backend.js (or expose it on MKG) and call it from the time clock's done path too; alternatively show a "Grant pending referral credit" button on done cards where refRewardGranted is false.

### [MEDIUM] Rules are pasted by hand; CI deploys hosting only, so drift is invisible
`.github/workflows/firebase-hosting-merge.yml:29` · devops · lens: rules-adversarial

firebase.json:2-4 already declares `"firestore": {"rules": "firestore.rules"}`, but the merge workflow uses FirebaseExtended/action-hosting-deploy, which deploys hosting only; firestore.rules lines 3-5 say the file is published by pasting it into the console. Nobody can tell from the repo whether the live ruleset matches HEAD, and every finding in this audit is about the repo file.

**Evidence.** firebase-hosting-merge.yml:29-34 has a single deploy step (`uses: FirebaseExtended/action-hosting-deploy@v0 ... channelId: live`) and no firebase-tools call; firestore.rules header: 'Published in the Firebase console (Firestore > Rules); GitHub does not deploy this file'; docs/SETUP-firebase.md:26-27 and :94-96 repeat the manual-paste instruction. There is no rules test in the repo.

**Impact.** A fix to the rules can sit unpublished for weeks, or an older paste can remain live after a repo change; the setup doc's 'every rule above is enforced by firestore.rules' claim is only as true as the last manual paste.

**Suggestion.** Add a step after hosting deploy: `npx firebase-tools deploy --only firestore:rules --project gen-lang-client-0946383119` using the same service-account secret (grant it the Firebase Rules Admin role), preceded by `firebase emulators:exec --only firestore 'node rules-attack.test.js'`-style tests so a broken ruleset never ships; then remove the manual-paste instructions from the docs.

### [MEDIUM] Deploy ships hosting only; rules and indexes are pasted by hand
`.github/workflows/firebase-hosting-merge.yml:29` · devops · lens: data-model-cost

firebase.json declares firestore.rules but the workflow uses action-hosting-deploy, which deploys hosting only, so the rules in the repo and the rules in production drift; there is no firestore.indexes.json, and a rules refusal is masked by the quote form's email fallback.

**Evidence.** .github/workflows/firebase-hosting-merge.yml:29-34 `uses: FirebaseExtended/action-hosting-deploy@v0 ... channelId: live` is the only deploy step; firebase.json:2-4 `"firestore": { "rules": "firestore.rules" }`; docs/SETUP-firebase.md:26-27 'Repeat this step whenever firestore.rules changes in the repo (GitHub does not deploy it automatically)'. `ls firestore.indexes.json` -> no such file. quote.js:507-511: on any Firestore error the form posts to Formspree and calls done(false), and no page element shows a reference id (grep data-request-id across *.html finds none), so a saved and an unsaved request look identical to the visitor.

**Impact.** A PR that touches firestore.rules (new field, new collection) deploys the JavaScript immediately while the database still enforces the old rules: every affected submission is refused, emailed instead, and never appears on admin.html or holds its window. The first query a developer adds with a filter plus orderBy on another field (e.g. open requests by date) will fail in production until an index is created by hand.

**Suggestion.** Add a step `npx firebase-tools deploy --only firestore:rules,firestore:indexes --project gen-lang-client-0946383119` using the same service-account secret (grant it the Firebase Rules Admin and Cloud Datastore Index Admin roles), commit an empty firestore.indexes.json, and run the emulator rules test in the pull-request workflow.

### [MEDIUM] 24-hour change cutoff computed in the client's time zone
`account.js:33` · bug · lens: booking-integrity

`startsAt` builds the visit time in the browser's zone and compares it with the device clock; the rules put no time condition on customer moves or cancels. A client west of Charlotte, or one who changes the clock, keeps the Change and Cancel buttons inside the notice window the terms rely on.

**Evidence.** account.js:29-36 `return new Date(Number(p[0]), Number(p[1]) - 1, Number(p[2]), hh, mm).getTime();` and `Date.now() > s - NOTICE_HOURS * 36e5`; firestore.rules:102-113 `customerMoves`/`customerCancels` check fields only. Reproduced in node: for a visit 2026-11-05 08:00, TZ=Pacific/Honolulu computes the cutoff as 2026-11-04T18:00Z versus 13:00Z for Charlotte. A stubbed account.html at 2026-10-21T13:30Z with a confirmed visit on 2026-10-22 08:00 showed "This visit is less than 24 hours away. To change or cancel it, call or text (980) 319-0015." in America/New_York but still offered "Change the day or time" and "Cancel this request" in Pacific/Honolulu.

**Impact.** A late cancel or move slips through online, the confirmed visit silently drops to New with no late-fee path (terms.2.p1), and the crew may already be scheduled; clients east of Charlotte are cut off hours early.

**Suggestion.** Compute the cutoff in America/New_York (Intl.DateTimeFormat with timeZone, or store a `startsAt` timestamp when confirming) and enforce it in the rules, e.g. `request.time < resource.data.startsAt - duration.value(24, 'h')` for customer moves and cancels of confirmed requests.

### [MEDIUM] Wrong email on cross-device link shows "link expired" message
`account.js:353` · ux · lens: auth-flows

When a sign-in link is opened on a device without the stored email, the confirm form asks for the address; if the person types a different one, Firebase rejects the email/code pair, but the page discards the error code and always says the link has expired or was used. The person then requests a new link and loops.

**Evidence.** account.js:353: `B.completeLink(email).catch(function () { err("email2", t("acct.linkBad")); });` ignores `e.code`. Mock run C2: typing other@example.com with the backend rejecting `auth/invalid-email` displayed "That sign-in link has expired or was already used. Request a new one." (screenshot account-link-wrong-email.png). The oobCode also stays in the URL after failure (C3), so a reload re-attempts the dead link.

**Impact.** Mobile users (link requested in Chrome, opened in Safari or the Gmail app) are the common case for this form; a typo or a different alias sends them back to request another link rather than fixing the address.

**Suggestion.** Map `auth/invalid-email` (and any code other than invalid/expired-action-code) to "That doesn't match the address the link was sent to" and keep the form; call clearActionParams() when the link is really dead.

### [MEDIUM] Errors after a redirect sign-in are never surfaced
`account.js:421` · bug · lens: auth-flows

When the popup fallback redirects to Google and the round trip fails (unauthorized domain, disabled user, network, storage blocked), the Firebase SDK swallows the error at startup and only exposes it through getRedirectResult(), which nothing on the site calls. The visitor just lands back on the sign-in form with no message.

**Evidence.** `grep -rn getRedirectResult *.js` returns nothing; account.js:421 only registers `B.onAuth(onUser)`, admin.js:575 likewise. Firebase SDK 10.14.1 (node_modules/@firebase/auth esm2017 index, tryRedirectSignIn): `catch (e) { // Swallow any errors here; the code can retrieve them in getRedirectResult(). await this._setRedirectUser(null); ...`. The redirect path is the one taken whenever the popup is blocked (backend.js:183).

**Impact.** Mobile and in-app users who hit any redirect-time failure get no feedback and retry blindly; the owner gets no error report to act on.

**Suggestion.** On account.html and admin.html call `auth.getRedirectResult().catch(e => show authMessage(e))` inside B.ready before onAuth, and log the code to console.warn.

### [MEDIUM] Copy says credit is applied at confirm; code only adds a balance
`admin.js:172` · content · lens: referral-credits

Confirm writes the welcome credit into the ledger, but taking it off the visit is a separate manual click ("Apply to this visit") that only appears after the credits snapshot re-renders. The banner, the done page, the quote-page credit note and refer.html all tell the client the credit comes off the price when the visit is confirmed.

**Evidence.** admin.js:172 addCredit({ id: "welcome-" + r.id, ... }) on confirm; applying is admin.js:304-309 behind the data-act="applyCredit" button rendered by creditBlock() only when `bal(r.emailLower) > 0` (admin.js:159-164). Copy: i18n.js:731 "Request a quote and it's applied when we confirm.", i18n.js:741 "Your {amount} welcome credit is added when we confirm your visit, and it comes off the price.", i18n.js:742 "We'll take it off this visit's price when we confirm.", i18n.js:793 "It comes off the price of a visit when we confirm it". Mock run: after Confirm the card required a second click before creditApplied/youPay were written.

**Impact.** If the owner confirms and moves on, the client pays full price while $25 sits in a balance they may not know exists (only account holders see it); the written promise is not kept and support questions follow.

**Suggestion.** After the welcome grant on Confirm, automatically apply min(balance, cap) to that request (one chained write), or change the four strings to say the credit is added to their balance and applied at the owner's confirmation step.

### [MEDIUM] Status change and hold release are two separate writes
`admin.js:316` · bug · lens: booking-integrity

Confirm, decline, done and reopen update the request first and touch the hold in a second request. A failure between the two leaves a closed request still holding its window, or a confirmed one with no hold, and nothing reconciles it later.

**Evidence.** admin.js:315-317 `p = B.updateRequest(id, { status: what }).then(function () { if (CLOSED[what]) return B.releaseHolds(id); return B.ensureHold(Object.assign({}, r, { status: what })); })`; backend.js:396-408 run `releaseHolds`/`ensureHold` as their own batches even though `reschedule` (343-359) and `cancelRequest` (361-367) already do request+hold in one batch. A stubbed declined request whose hold survived showed "Afternoon 3:00–6:00 · 1 of 2 taken … Eve Declined (Declined)" on the day panel, and calendar.js:52 counts that hold for the public.

**Impact.** A dropped connection or closed tab at the wrong moment makes visitors see Full for a window that is actually free, or shows a confirmed visit's window as open so it gets double-requested.

**Suggestion.** Put the status patch and the hold set/delete in one `db.batch()`, and on admin load compare open requests with holds and offer a one-click fix for any mismatch.

### [MEDIUM] Owner's admin login is a public email plus password with no MFA
`admin.js:557` · security · lens: web-security · votes 1/1 real (reproduce: low) · **confirmed**

The admin page is linked from every footer, the owner's email is printed in every page header, and the setup doc has the owner sign in with that email and a password. Password reuse or stuffing is the realistic route to the whole customer database, and nothing adds a second factor.

**Evidence.** admin.js:557 `B.signInPassword(email, pass)`; index.html:298 `<a href="admin.html" data-i18n="nav.staff">Staff</a>` (same in every page footer); plans.js:29 `email: "mkgservices704@gmail.com"` rendered in the top bar; docs/SETUP-firebase.md:43-44 'Add user: the business email and a strong password'. firestore.rules:37 `hasAdminDoc()` grants the owner document full read of `requests`, `internal`, `credits` and `shifts` with no verification or provider check.

**Impact.** One leaked or reused password exposes every client's name, phone, ZIP, booking schedule and notes (which may hold alarm/key details), and lets the attacker confirm, delete or re-price bookings. Firebase's built-in brute-force throttling slows guessing but does not stop a reused password.

**Suggestion.** Have the owner sign in with Google (with 2-Step Verification on that Google account) and remove the password provider from the owner account in the console, or upgrade Auth to Identity Platform (free tier, available on Blaze) and require TOTP MFA for `admins`; optionally require `request.auth.token.firebase.sign_in_provider == 'google.com'` in `isAdmin()` once everyone is on Google.

### [MEDIUM] Admin login reports every failure as wrong password
`admin.js:557` · ux · lens: auth-flows

The staff form maps any sign-in error to "Wrong email or password.", including Firebase throttling (auth/too-many-requests), network failures and a disabled account. An owner who is being rate-limited keeps retrying, extending the lockout, or resets a password that was correct.

**Evidence.** admin.js:557: `B.signInPassword(email, pass).catch(function (e) { $('[data-error="login"]').textContent = t("adm.badLogin"); ...})`. Mock run E: `auth/too-many-requests`, `auth/network-request-failed` and `auth/user-disabled` all rendered "Wrong email or password." account.js:50-59 already has an authMessage() mapper that distinguishes these.

**Impact.** The owner, the person who most needs to get in quickly during a lockout or an outage, gets misleading advice.

**Suggestion.** Reuse account.js's authMessage() (move it into backend.js or i18n) so throttling and connection errors show their own copy on admin.html.

### [MEDIUM] PR preview channels read and write the production database
`backend.js:37` · devops · lens: data-model-cost

Hosting serves the project's init.json on every preview channel, and backend.js only treats localhost as the emulator, so a preview URL is 'live': test submissions create real requests and holds on the public calendar and send real Formspree emails, without App Check.

**Evidence.** backend.js:37 `var SITE = /^(www\.)?mkgservices\.com$/.test(location.hostname);` decides App Check only; :53 `useEmu` is true only for localhost/127.0.0.1 or ?emulator=1; :64 `fetch(base + "init.json")` succeeds on <project>--pr-N.web.app, so `ready` resolves true and submitRequest() writes to the same Firestore. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:179-180 confirms 'the same files work on the live domain and preview channels'. .github/workflows/firebase-hosting-pull-request.yml:18 builds a preview for every PR.

**Impact.** Anyone testing a pull request pollutes the owner's inbox and calendar, consumes the 50-email Formspree quota, and skews the repeat-customer and credit logic; once App Check is enforced, previews instead start failing silently through the same email fallback.

**Suggestion.** In backend.js treat any host that is neither the live domain nor the emulator as 'not connected' (demo mode), or point previews at a separate Firebase project via init.json override; document that previews are UI-only.

### [MEDIUM] Staff sign-in is a single password with no MFA and no session expiry
`backend.js:174` · security · lens: auth-flows

The owner account created in the console (email+password, unverified) guards every client's name, phone, address and notes. There is no second factor, and Firebase's default local persistence (IndexedDB) keeps staff signed in indefinitely on any device; the only revocation is removing the member or changing the password in the console.

**Evidence.** backend.js:174 `signInPassword` and admin.js:553-558 password form; `grep -rn setPersistence *.js` finds nothing, and @firebase/auth-compat (index.esm2017.js:755-763) resolves the default to indexedDBLocalPersistence. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:43-51 creates the owner with a password in the console. The 2-minute stillAdmin() recheck (admin.js:542) only covers removed members, not the owner.

**Impact.** A phished or reused owner password, or a staff phone left unlocked, exposes the full client list; sessions on shared tablets never time out.

**Suggestion.** Prefer Google sign-in for staff (the business email is a Gmail account with 2-Step Verification) and disable or stop using the password provider for admins, or upgrade to Identity Platform for SMS/TOTP MFA; set `auth.setPersistence(SESSION)` on admin.html so closing the browser signs staff out.

### [MEDIUM] Google sign-in fails silently inside Instagram/Facebook in-app browsers
`backend.js:183` · ux · lens: auth-flows

In embedded in-app browsers (Instagram, Facebook, TikTok, Gmail on iOS) window.open is blocked, so signInGoogle() falls back to signInWithRedirect, and Google's OAuth policy refuses embedded webviews with a 403 disallowed_useragent page. The site never detects these browsers or steers the visitor to the email link.

**Evidence.** backend.js:181-184: `signInWithPopup(provider).catch(... if (code === "auth/popup-blocked" || ... ) return auth.signInWithRedirect(provider); ...)`. SDK (auth index-68602d24.js:10004) throws `popup-blocked` when `window.open` returns null, which is what WKWebView-based in-app browsers do. No UA check anywhere (`grep -rn "FBAN|Instagram" *.js` empty). Google's "Use secure browsers" policy blocks OAuth in embedded webviews; this cannot be exercised locally and is stated from the policy, not observed.

**Impact.** Visitors arriving from social ads who tap "Continue with Google" land on a Google error page and never return to the site; for a cleaning company advertising on Facebook/Instagram this is a realistic share of sign-ins.

**Suggestion.** Detect in-app UAs (FBAN|FBAV|Instagram|Line|TikTok|Snapchat|GSA) and hide the Google button or show "Open in Safari/Chrome, or use the email link"; keep the email-link option first on those browsers.

### [MEDIUM] Account emails and reset pages come from gen-lang-client-…firebaseapp.com
`backend.js:190` · ux · lens: auth-flows

Verification, password-reset and sign-in-link emails are generated by Firebase with its default hosted action page on gen-lang-client-0946383119.firebaseapp.com (and, unless changed in the console, a noreply@ sender on that domain). account.js has its own reset and verify screens, but they only run when the console's "Customize action URL" points at account.html, which the setup notes say the project does not keep.

**Evidence.** backend.js:190 `function actionUrl() { return location.origin + "/account.html"; }` is only the continueUrl. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:152-156: "the link opens Firebase's page on firebaseapp.com, which sends the client on to account.html. (Authentication > Templates > Customize action URL would skip that hop, but the console doesn't keep the setting in this project…)". .firebaserc project id `gen-lang-client-0946383119`. Mock run H1/H2 shows the account.html reset form works when the parameters do arrive. The console's actual sender name/domain could only be settled against the live project.

**Impact.** Every password sign-up needs this email to see requests; a message titled for an auto-generated project id from a firebaseapp.com address reads as phishing and gets ignored or spam-filtered, so clients stay unverified and see an empty account.

**Suggestion.** In Authentication > Settings set the public-facing name to MKG Services, customise the sender (Templates > Customize domain, DNS at Cloudflare) and retry "Customize action URL" = https://mkgservices.com/account.html so the page's own Spanish/English reset and verify screens take over.

### [MEDIUM] Repeat detection sees only 300 recent requests and no phone bookings
`backend.js:378` · security · lens: referral-credits

isRepeat() runs over the admin page's in-memory list, which watchRequests caps at the 300 most recent requests, and bookings taken by phone are stored with empty email and phone. Customers from before the site, older than the window, or booked by phone therefore always look new.

**Evidence.** backend.js:378 `db.collection("requests").orderBy("createdAt", "desc").limit(300)`; backend.js:414 addPhoneBooking() writes `email: "", emailLower: "", phone: ""`; admin.js:125-131 isRepeat() iterates `items` only. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:102 promises "Repeat customers (same email or phone as an earlier confirmed/done request) get no automatic credit".

**Impact.** MKG has been cleaning since 2014; its existing customer base is exactly who will be referred by friends, and none of them can be recognised, so the "new customers only" rule relies entirely on the owner's memory at Confirm time.

**Suggestion.** Record a phone number on phone bookings, and before granting do a server-side lookup (query requests by emailLower and by normalised phone, or keep a small `customers/{phoneDigits}` index) instead of scanning the capped list.

### [MEDIUM] Unbounded admin listeners: whole internal collection, every shift ever
`backend.js:384` · perf · lens: data-model-cost

watchInternal() streams the entire internal collection and watchMyShifts() every shift a member ever recorded, so each admin page open (and each reconnect after 30 minutes in the background) re-reads an ever-growing set; with the 300 requests and 1,000 credits listeners the page already costs 1,300+ reads per open, and the $1 budget alert will fire on normal growth.

**Evidence.** backend.js:384 `db.collection("internal").onSnapshot(...)` with no limit; backend.js:312 `db.collection("shifts").where("uid", "==", uid).onSnapshot(...)` with no date bound, while timeclock.js only needs the open shift, today, the last 5 and this week (timeclock.js:63, 78, 107, 112-113). Emulator E1/E2 (run.log) read all 40 seeded internal docs and all 40 seeded shifts in one query each. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:214-216: budget alert at 10%/50%/100% of $1 a month, 'It warns; it does not stop charges'. Firestore's free daily quota is 50k reads; a listener disconnected over 30 minutes is billed as a fresh query.

**Impact.** With three staff opening the admin page on phones several times a day, the free 50k reads/day is reachable within a year of history; the money beyond that is small (cents per 100k reads in nam5) but a $0.10 alert on a $1 budget trains the owner to ignore billing emails, and page load slows with every month of shifts.

**Suggestion.** Bound watchMyShifts to inAt >= 14 days ago, load internal docs only for the request ids on screen (documentId() 'in' chunks) or add `updatedAt` and query the last few months, and set the budget to a figure that means something (for example $10) with the alert at 50% and 100%.

### [MEDIUM] Credit balances summed client-side from the newest 1,000 lines
`backend.js:478` · bug · lens: data-model-cost

watchCredits() streams up to 1,000 ledger lines to every admin and balances are recomputed in the browser; once the ledger passes 1,000 entries the oldest lines drop out of the sum and balances silently change, and every staff login already pays for the whole ledger.

**Evidence.** backend.js:478 `db.collection("credits").orderBy("createdAt", "desc").limit(1000).onSnapshot(...)`; admin.js:119-122 recompute() sums `ledger` into `balances`; admin.js:533 starts the listener for every admin although the Credits tab is owner-only (admin.js:526), and creditBlock() (admin.js:152-165) uses those balances on request cards. Each referred visit produces three lines (welcome-, referral-, use-), so ~330 referred visits reach the cap.

**Impact.** Money: an early -25 'used' line falling out of the window re-credits a client; an early +25 welcome falling out removes credit the owner promised. Cost: every admin page open reads N ledger documents against the 50k/day free reads.

**Suggestion.** Keep a balances/{emailLower} document updated in the same batch as each ledger line (rules can enforce new balance == old + amount via getAfter), show the ledger per selected email on demand, and start the ledger listener only for the owner.

### [MEDIUM] Unanswered requests hold windows indefinitely with no expiry
`calendar.js:52` · ux · lens: booking-integrity

Every hold counts against capacity from the moment a request is sent, whatever its status, and nothing expires it or flags how long a `new` request has waited. Dead requests block windows until the owner declines each one by hand.

**Evidence.** calendar.js:52-55 `var taken = holds.filter(function (h) { return h.date === ymd && h.slot === s.id; }).length; ... left = Math.max(0, (AV.capacity || 1) - taken)`; backend.js:163-165 writes the hold with the request; plans.js:68-72 documents it ("Visitors see a window as full once that many requests hold it"). The admin card (admin.js:186-250) shows the sent time but no age warning, and there is no scheduled cleanup anywhere in the repo.

**Impact.** With capacity 2, two tyre-kickers or spam requests the owner has not declined make a window Full for everyone; the calendar's accuracy depends on the owner triaging every request promptly.

**Suggestion.** Flag `new` requests older than N days on admin.html with a one-click "decline and free the window", or count only confirmed holds against capacity and show pending ones separately, or auto-release via a scheduled function.

### [MEDIUM] Reopened day or unblocked window stays closed on admin grid
`calendar.js:73` · bug · lens: booking-integrity

The calendar merges `days` by adding keys and never removes one. Because backend.js deletes the `days/{date}` document when a day is all clear, the grid keeps showing the old closed or blocked state after a refresh until the page is reloaded.

**Evidence.** calendar.js:73 `Object.keys(r.days).forEach(function (d) { days[d] = r.days[d]; });` and line 170 `refresh: function () { cache = {}; holds = []; return load(); }` (days is not reset); backend.js:430-433 `setDay` deletes the document when `!data.closed && !blockedSlots.length`. Stubbed admin.html: after "Close this day" the cell read "Friday, October 23, Closed"; after "Reopen this day" the panel button flipped back to "Close this day" but the grid still read "Closed"; blocking then unblocking 8am left the cell at "3 open" instead of "4 open".

**Impact.** The owner sees a day they just reopened still marked Closed and may toggle it again or assume the save failed; the day panel and the grid contradict each other.

**Suggestion.** Reset `days = {}` in `refresh()`, or delete this month's keys from `days` in `apply()` before merging.

### [MEDIUM] No backup, export or point-in-time recovery for the customer database
`docs/SETUP-firebase.md:160` · devops · lens: data-model-cost

Nothing in the repo or setup docs enables Firestore scheduled backups or PITR, there is no export of requests (the only export is the time-clock CSV), and the owner's delete is permanent; the customer list exists only inside Firestore with no way out.

**Evidence.** docs/SETUP-firebase.md:160-181 documents the data layout but no backup step; grep -rni 'backup|export|pitr|point-in-time' README.md docs/*.md .github/workflows returns only the brand PNG line in README.md:42. backend.js:423-430 deleteRequest() is a hard delete ('gone for good'); timeclock.js:279-290 exportCsv() is the sole download, for shifts only; admin.html has no export control.

**Impact.** A mistaken bulk change, a bad migration (such as the legacy-notes cleanup above), a compromised admin account, or simply wanting to move to an invoicing/CRM tool leaves the owner with no copy of their bookings and customers.

**Suggestion.** Enable daily scheduled backups with 7-14 day retention (`gcloud firestore backups schedules create --database='(default)' --recurrence=daily --retention=7d`, free apart from backup storage), note it in SETUP-firebase.md, and add a 'Download requests as CSV' button on the admin page.

### [MEDIUM] Hosting sends no CSP, frame-ancestors, nosniff, Referrer-Policy or Permissions-Policy
`firebase.json:17` · security · lens: web-security · votes 1/3 real (impact: low, reproduce: medium, refute: low) · rejected

The only custom headers are Cache-Control, so the site runs with no Content-Security-Policy as a second line of defence behind an entirely innerHTML-templated UI, admin.html and account.html can be framed, and content-type sniffing and referrer leakage are left to browser defaults.

**Evidence.** firebase.json:17-36 `"headers": [ { "source": "**/*.html", "headers": [ { "key": "Cache-Control", ... } ] }, { "source": "**/*.@(js|css)", ... } ]` and nothing else; `grep -rn -i "content-security|x-frame|nosniff|referrer-policy|permissions-policy"` over the repo returns nothing. Firebase Hosting adds HSTS on its own but none of these (live headers could only be confirmed against mkgservices.com, which this container cannot reach). The only inline scripts are `window.MKG_SEGMENT = "commercial"` on three pages (commercial.html:286 etc.) and JSON-LD, so a strict CSP is cheap to adopt.

**Impact.** Any future injection bug (every render path concatenates HTML) would execute with no mitigation; clickjacking of admin actions is possible in browsers that still share Firebase's IndexedDB session into third-party frames; policy scanners and insurers flag the missing headers.

**Suggestion.** Add a headers block for `**` with `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `Permissions-Policy: camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=()`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, and a CSP (start as Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only) allowing self, fonts.googleapis.com/gstatic.com, *.googleapis.com, formspree.io, www.google.com/recaptcha and the /__/auth/ frame, with `frame-ancestors 'none'`; move the `MKG_SEGMENT` inline script to a `data-segment` attribute on `<html>` so no 'unsafe-inline' is needed.

### [MEDIUM] quoteFields is about 30 terms from Firestore's 1000-expression ceiling
`firestore.rules:60` · maintainability · lens: rules-adversarial

The request-create rule (quoteFields + refOk + the hold rule evaluated in the same batch) runs close to Firestore's limit of 1,000 evaluated expressions per request. The next developer who adds the validations recommended elsewhere in this audit will silently start refusing real quote requests, and the quote page will still tell the visitor it was sent.

**Evidence.** Padded a scratch copy of the rules with N extra `&& d.status == 'new'` comparisons at the end of quoteFields and replayed the real quote batch in the emulator (expr-limit.test.js): N=20 both quotes accepted; N=30 the quote with a valid invite code refused with 'Unable to evaluate the expression as the maximum of 1000 expressions to evaluate has been reached'; N=40 every quote refused; N=60 the ruleset no longer compiles ('L61:14 Expression is too complex to evaluate safely'). Moving the padding into 20-term helper functions did not help (N=40 split still refused). The emulator already prints the 1000-expression message when it explains ordinary denials at line 117.

**Impact.** When rules refuse, quote.js:507-510 falls back to the Formspree email and shows the normal confirmation, so requests would vanish from the admin page while visitors believe they booked; the owner would notice only through email. Right now it works; it is a trap for the next change.

**Suggestion.** Shrink quoteFields: drop fields nothing reads (_subject, refAmount, submittedAt, requestedTime, estimateFullPrice, estimatePerMonth), fold the per-field optStr chain into fewer checks, and consider moving creation into a callable Cloud Function where validation is unbounded. Keep the emulator rule tests from this audit and run them before every rules change.

### [MEDIUM] Anyone can plant a request under another client's email and account
`firestore.rules:68` · security · lens: rules-adversarial

quoteFields only requires email to be a string longer than 3 characters with emailLower equal to its lowercase; there is no format check and, when the writer is signed in, no tie to their own verified email. account.js:238 lists every request whose emailLower matches the signed-in user, so a stranger can insert content into another person's account page and trigger a business email with Reply-To set to the victim.

**Evidence.** Emulator checks: V08 unauthenticated request with email 'alice@example.com' and company 'YOUR ACCOUNT IS SUSPENDED call 555-0199' ALLOWED; V08b Alice's own myRequests query then returned it; V09 email of four spaces ALLOWED; C16 a signed-in client creating a request under bob@example.com ALLOWED. account.js:107-108 renders company, zip and the raw service string in the card; quote.js:424 sends the same email address to Formspree as the reply-to field.

**Impact.** A victim sees a request they never sent, with attacker-written text, in their account and can be tricked into calling a number; the business receives the matching email and may phone the wrong person. It is the usual contact-form spoofing risk, but the account page makes it look authentic.

**Suggestion.** Add an email pattern check (same regex as adminInvites) and, when signedIn(), require d.emailLower == myEmail() for non-admins; enforce App Check; in account.js show a 'sent without signing in' badge on requests that lack a signed-in marker (for example a `uid` field set only by the signed-in path and validated in rules).

### [MEDIUM] Unvalidated `days` elements let a stranger write ~1 MB request documents
`firestore.rules:78` · security · lens: rules-adversarial

The days field is only required to be a list of at most 7 elements; the elements themselves can be any type and size. Every other string field is capped (notes 2000, etc.), so this is the one field that lets a visitor approach Firestore's 1 MiB document limit, and admin.js loads the 300 newest requests in full.

**Evidence.** Emulator checks: V13 a request with days = seven 120 KB strings (about 840 KB) ALLOWED; V12 days = [{a:1}, 12345, true, null] ALLOWED. backend.js:378 watchRequests() streams `orderBy('createdAt','desc').limit(300)` complete documents to every admin session; backend.js:115 only filters non-strings on read.

**Impact.** A few hundred such requests make the admin page download hundreds of megabytes on every open (Blaze bills Firestore egress and the page would crawl or fail), and the stored junk costs storage until the owner deletes each request by hand.

**Suggestion.** Validate the elements: `d.days.hasOnly(['Mon','Tue','Wed','Thu','Fri','Sat','Sun'])` (quote.js only sends those labels) or at least each a string of at most 10 characters; the same hasOnly idea caps contactMethod/timeOfDay to their known values at no extra expression cost.

### [MEDIUM] Price estimates are client-supplied numbers that admin and reports trust
`firestore.rules:81` · pricing · lens: rules-adversarial

estimatePerVisit, estimateOneVisit, estimateFullPrice, estimatePerMonth, discount and refAmount are accepted from the visitor as any number from 0 to 9,999,999 with no relation to plans.js or the size fields. The admin card, the account page, the credit cap and the time-clock revenue figures all read them as if the site computed them.

**Evidence.** Emulator checks: V10 estimatePerVisit:1, discount:5000, refAmount:999999 ALLOWED; V11 estimatePerVisit:0 ALLOWED. admin.js:189-191 prints the stored value as the estimate ('estimate was ...'), admin.js:123 visitPrice() caps credit by it and admin.js:309 writes youPay = price - credit from it, timeclock.js:47 price() feeds revenue-per-hour from it, quote.js:442 labels it 'Site estimate' in the email. refAmount is stored and never read (admin.js uses INV.rewards()).

**Impact.** A client can submit a $300 clean that shows as a $1 or $0 estimate; if the owner confirms without typing a final price the client sees that number as the site's own estimate, and payroll/revenue stats are skewed. No automated money loss, but a dispute lever and misleading reports.

**Suggestion.** Recompute the estimate in admin.js and account.js from the stored size fields with A.estimate() (plans.js is the source of truth) and show the stored value only as 'what the client's browser showed'; drop refAmount, estimateFullPrice and estimatePerMonth from quoteFields since nothing reads them. If rules must keep them, bound them (for example estimatePerVisit <= estimateFullPrice and discount <= 1).

### [MEDIUM] Self-referral with a second email has no admin cross-check
`firestore.rules:89` · security · lens: referral-credits

The only self-referral defence is that the request email differs from the code owner's account email; a client who creates a code and then requests a quote under any other address passes refOk, and admin.html shows nothing that compares the friend's phone, name or address with the referrer's own requests.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:85-91 refOk() checks `...users/$(referrers.uid).data.emailLower != d.emailLower` only; users/{uid} stores just code, emailLower, createdAt (rules:232) so there is no phone to compare; admin.js:141-151 refRows() prints "Referred by code X · invited by <email>" and the repeat warning, which only looks at the friend's own earlier requests. refer.html:92 promises "You can't refer yourself, your household, or your own business".

**Impact.** A brand-new client (no prior visit to match on) can earn $50 off their own first visit ($100 commercial) by using a second mailbox; the owner would only catch it by reading the referrer's email next to the friend's name.

**Suggestion.** On the admin card, look up the referrer's own requests (items filtered by owners[r.ref]) and warn when their phone digits, last name or ZIP+street match the friend's; optionally require the referrer to have had a done visit before their code pays out.

### [MEDIUM] Plus-alias or dotted-Gmail requests never appear in the account
