`firestore.rules:93` · ux · lens: auth-flows

A request is visible only when its emailLower equals the signed-in token email exactly, so a quote sent as name+mkg@gmail.com or first.last@gmail.com is invisible to the Google account name@gmail.com / firstlast@gmail.com. The on-page hint tells them to "sign out and sign in with that one", which Google cannot do for an alias; only the email link can.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:93: `function isCustomerOf(r) { return verified() && r.emailLower == myEmail(); }`. Emulator: E03 `john.doe@example.com reads request sent as john.doe+mkg@example.com` DENIED (permission-denied); E05 (dots) DENIED; E04 shows an email-link account created under the alias does see it. i18n.js:674 acct.wrongEmail: "Used a different email on a request? Sign out and sign in with that one." Case differences are handled (E01/E02 ALLOWED via lower()).

**Impact.** Clients who habitually use +tags see "No requests yet" and cannot move or cancel online; some will phone or re-submit, creating duplicate holds.

**Suggestion.** Either normalise gmail.com/googlemail.com addresses (strip dots and +tag) into emailLower in submitRequest and in rules myEmail() via replace(), or change the hint to "Sent it with another address or a +alias? Use 'Email me a sign-in link' with that exact address."

### [MEDIUM] Client move/cancel rules ignore the 24-hour policy and calendar constraints
`firestore.rules:102` · security · lens: rules-adversarial

customerMoves()/customerCancels() (lines 102-113) check shape and the hold mirror but never the clock or the calendar: a verified client can cancel or move a confirmed visit minutes before it starts, or move it to a past date, a closed day, a blocked or full window, or a slot id that does not exist. account.js:36 tooLate() is the only thing enforcing the policy in terms.html, and it runs in the client's browser.

**Evidence.** Emulator checks as alice@example.com (verified) on her confirmed request dated today: C01 cancel ALLOWED; C02 move to 2000-01-01 ALLOWED; C03 move to days/2026-12-25 {closed:true} ALLOWED; C04 move into a blockedSlots window ALLOWED; C05 move into a window already holding 5 requests ALLOWED; C06 slot 'ZZZ' ALLOWED. i18n.js:719 (terms.2.p1) promises free changes only with 24 hours' notice. admin.js:339-342 lists any non-closed dated request in the week-ahead panel, so a request moved onto a closed day appears there as if valid.

**Impact.** The late-cancel charge the terms rely on cannot be enforced from the data, and a client can park a visit on a day the owner closed or in a full window, which the public calendar then shows as over capacity. It takes a browser console rather than the normal page, so the main exposure is a determined client rather than everyone.

**Suggestion.** For confirmed requests require request.time + duration.value(24,'h') < the visit start (store a startsAt timestamp when the admin confirms, or compute it with timestamp.date(int(...)) from date and slot), and reuse the same slot-id, date-range and closed-day checks recommended for holds. Keep the UI gate as the friendly message.

### [MEDIUM] Holds are listable forever: full booking history, request ids, legacy notes
`firestore.rules:149` · security · lens: rules-adversarial

`allow read: if true` on holds permits unbounded list queries, not just the date-ranged query backend.js:133 runs. Every hold ever written is public with date, slot, requestId (the request document id whose first six characters are the reference number clients quote by phone), segment and createdAt; older holds with requestId 'manual' may carry a `note` typed by the owner, which admin.js:366 still displays.

**Evidence.** Emulator check V23: an unauthenticated collection('holds').get() with no filter returned all 16 seeded/created holds with fields date, slot, requestId, segment, createdAt; V23b returned the seeded legacy hold's note 'Mrs Smith, 123 Elm St'. Whether any such 'manual' holds with notes exist live can only be checked in the Firebase console; admin.js:366 (`hold.note`) shows the code expects them. Nothing in backend.js deletes holds once a date has passed unless the request is marked done/declined/cancelled.

**Impact.** Competitors or scrapers get the company's complete booking volume by month and segment; the public reference number lets a caller impersonate a client by phone if staff treat it as proof; any legacy notes containing names or addresses are public. Low effort for the attacker, but the data is business-sensitive rather than regulated PII.

**Suggestion.** Run a one-off cleanup (console query holds where requestId == 'manual', delete or strip note; delete holds with date before today), then limit bulk dumps with `allow list: if request.query.limit <= 100` and `allow get: if true`; add a nightly sweep (scheduled Function, or an owner button) for holds older than today, and tell staff the reference number is not identity.

### [MEDIUM] Entire holds and days collections are listable by anyone, forever
`firestore.rules:149` · security · lens: data-model-cost

holds are world-readable with no query bound, and each hold carries createdAt and segment, so one unfiltered query returns the business's complete booking history and lead times; nothing deletes holds for past dates, and the admin calendar cannot even navigate back to remove them.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:149 `allow read: if true;` (holds) and :177 (days). backend.js:154 writes `{ date, slot, requestId, createdAt: now, segment }`; calendar.js:52 only uses `h.date` and `h.slot`, so createdAt and segment are published for nothing. Holds are removed only via releaseHolds/cancel/reschedule on status changes; calendar.js:33-34 sets the admin grid's first day to today, so past holds are invisible and unremovable from the page. Emulator B1/B2 (run.log): unauthenticated `getDocs(collection("holds"))` returned every hold with its createdAt.

**Impact.** A competitor can scrape how many jobs MKG books per week, residential vs commercial mix, how far ahead customers book and which days the owner closes (days docs), and the collection grows without bound; request ids leak too, though requests themselves stay unreadable.

**Suggestion.** Drop createdAt and segment from the public hold (keep them on the request), add `allow list: if request.query.limit <= 100` on holds, and clean up past holds with a Firestore TTL policy on an `expiresAt` field (date + 1 day) or a nightly scheduled function.

### [MEDIUM] Admin credit note labelled private but readable by the client
`firestore.rules:257` · security · lens: referral-credits

The credits read rule lets a verified client read every field of any ledger document under their email, including the `note` the owner types in the Credits tab, while the form label promises "Note (only you see this)". The account page does not render the note, but any client can fetch it with the Firebase SDK already loaded on the page.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:256-257 `allow read: if isAdmin() || (verified() && resource.data.emailLower == myEmail());` with no field restriction; backend.js:468-470 credits() returns full documents; admin.html:123 `<label for="cf-note" data-i18n="adm.credit.note">Note (only you see this)</label>` (i18n.js:807, Spanish 'Nota (solo tú la ves)'). Mock account run: myCredits('cust@x.com') returned adjust:"goodwill after complaint".

**Impact.** Candid staff remarks about a client (complaints, goodwill reasons, suspected abuse) are exposed to that client, contradicting the UI promise; this is the same class of leak the project avoided for request notes by moving them to internal/{id}.

**Suggestion.** Keep notes out of the client-readable document (store them in a staff-only collection keyed by credit id, as internal/ does for requests) or change the label to say the client can see it.

### [MEDIUM] Any invited staff member can write the credit ledger and client prices
`firestore.rules:258` · security · lens: web-security · votes 1/1 real (reproduce: low) · **confirmed**

The rules let every admin, not just the owner, create and delete credit entries and change any field of any request, while the admin UI deliberately hides the Credits tab and the delete button from non-owners. The owner/staff split the site advertises is enforced only in JavaScript for money-related fields.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:255-259 `match /credits/{id} { allow read: if isAdmin() || ...; allow write: if isAdmin(); }` and :118 `allow update: if isAdmin() || (...)` with no field restriction for admins (quotedPrice, creditApplied, youPay, payLink, replyNote all writable); admin.js:526 `ct.hidden = !isOwner();` hides the Credits tab and :248 shows Delete only `isOwner()`; by contrast the rules do reserve delete (:122 `isTeamOwner()`), team changes (:200) and shift edits (:221) for the owner.

**Impact.** A staff member (or anyone who takes over a staff Google account) can grant themselves or a friend unlimited credit, set a client's final price to $0, or point a client's 'Pay online' button at any https URL, all without the owner's page showing who did it.

**Suggestion.** Change `credits` write to `isTeamOwner()`, and for non-owner admins restrict request updates with `changed().hasOnly(['status','date','slot','updatedAt','replyNote','refWelcomeGranted','refRewardGranted'])` so price, credit and payment-link edits stay with the owner; consider logging `updatedBy: request.auth.uid` on admin writes.

### [MEDIUM] Any team member, not only the owner, can write or delete credits
`firestore.rules:258` · security · lens: referral-credits

Credits are writable by every admin record with no shape check, so an invited staff member can add arbitrary amounts to any email (including their own or a friend's) or delete ledger lines with the SDK, even though the Credits tab is hidden from non-owners in the UI.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:258 `allow write: if isAdmin();` with isAdmin() = hasAdminDoc() || invited() (rules:40); no validation of amount, type or emailLower. admin.js:526 hides the tab for non-owners (`ct.hidden = !isOwner()`), which is UI only; request-card apply/undo (admin.js:304-312) is available to all admins by design.

**Impact.** Credit is money off a visit; a staff member can grant themselves or relatives free cleans without a trace beyond the ledger line, and the owner's "only you" assumptions about the Credits tab do not hold server-side.

**Suggestion.** Split the rule: let any admin create only the deterministic welcome/referral/used entries with amounts equal to plans.js rewards or a non-negative apply, and reserve `adjust` entries and deletes for isTeamOwner(); validate keys and types as the other collections do.

### [MEDIUM] Credits are owner-only in the UI but any admin may write them
`firestore.rules:258` · security · lens: auth-flows

The Credits tab is hidden for non-owners (admin.js:526) but firestore.rules lets every admin, including an invited person who has no admins document yet, create and delete credit ledger entries. The owner-only boundary the UI implies is not enforced server-side.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:258: `allow write: if isAdmin();` where isAdmin() = hasAdminDoc() || invited() (line 40). Emulator (identity.test.js): T05 `non-owner staff writes a credit` ALLOWED, T06 `non-owner staff deletes a credit entry` ALLOWED, I05 `invited, no admins doc: write a credit entry` ALLOWED. By contrast request delete (T07), invites (T03) and admin removal (T04) are correctly DENIED for non-owners. admin.js:526: `ct.hidden = !isOwner();` is the only gate.

**Impact.** A staff member (or anyone whose invited mailbox is compromised) can grant themselves or friends unlimited dollar credit, which Apply credit then subtracts from a visit price; the owner cannot tell from the UI who wrote the entry.

**Suggestion.** Change credits write to `isTeamOwner()` (grants from the request card are owner-only too, or keep `type in ['welcome','referral','used']` writes for admins and `adjust` for the owner only) and record `createdBy: request.auth.uid` in the rule.

### [MEDIUM] Formspree's 50 emails a month covers requests, moves, cancels and fallbacks
`plans.js:40` · devops · lens: data-model-cost

Every new request, every client move or cancel, and every database-refused submission posts to the same free Formspree form, capped at 50 a month; past the cap Formspree holds messages silently and the site cannot tell, yet the docs say email is how the owner notices client changes.

**Evidence.** plans.js:40 `formEndpoint: "https://formspree.io/f/xjyglbgo"`; quote.js:459 mailCopy() per request; account.js:182-205 tellBusiness() per move/cancel ('so nobody has to notice it on the admin page first'); quote.js:510 the fallback copy when Firestore refuses. app.js:346-353 notify() only returns r.ok. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:75-77: 'The free plan covers 50 emails a month; past that, Formspree holds them in an Over limit folder'.

**Impact.** At two requests a day the cap is hit mid-month; from then on new requests and client cancellations arrive only on admin.html, where a cancellation freeing a window can go unnoticed, and the fallback copies of refused submissions (the only trace of them) are held too.

**Suggestion.** Since the project is already on Blaze, send notifications from the database instead: the Trigger Email extension or a small Cloud Function on requests onCreate/onUpdate, which removes the quota and the dependence on the browser; or at least upgrade Formspree and show the monthly count on the admin page.

### [MEDIUM] Request stores client-computed prices and display names, not verified data
`quote.js:408` · pricing · lens: data-model-cost

The estimate, discount and refAmount saved on the request are whatever the browser sent, and plan/frequency/service are saved as English display names; the admin page, credit cap and revenue-per-hour trust those numbers, and renaming a plan in plans.js orphans every existing request.

**Evidence.** quote.js:404-410 `p.estimatePerVisit = e.perVisit; p.estimateFullPrice = e.oneTime; p.discount = e.discount;` and :408 `p.plan = A.plan(s.plan).name.en; p.frequency = A.frequency(s.frequency).label.en;`. firestore.rules:81-82 only require non-negative numbers below 10,000,000. Emulator A2 (run.log): an anonymous create with `estimatePerVisit: 1, refAmount: 9999` was allowed. Consumers: admin.js:189-191 shows it as the estimate, admin.js:123 visitPrice() feeds the credit cap and youPay (admin.js:309), timeclock.js:47 price() feeds revenue/hour and the payroll CSV. Matching by name: admin.js:77-78 and account.js:80-83 `x.name.en === r.plan`.

**Impact.** A tampered request shows a $1 'site estimate' the owner may quote from; honest requests carry a frozen number with no record of which price table produced it (the README says today's prices are placeholders to be replaced). After the owner renames 'Family' or changes 'Every 2 weeks', old requests show raw English text to Spanish clients, the edit panel loses the selection and 'book this again' links break.

**Suggestion.** Store plan/frequency/service ids plus a pricing version alongside the names, and have the admin page recompute the estimate from the stored inputs (bedrooms, bathrooms, sqft, restrooms, plan, frequency) with A.estimate() and flag any difference from the stored figure.

### [MEDIUM] Email-only fallback shows 'sent' while nothing was saved or held
`quote.js:510` · bug · lens: web-security · votes 1/1 real (reproduce: medium) · **confirmed**

When Firestore refuses a write for any reason other than a dropped referral code (a rules mismatch, App Check enforcement blocking a real visitor, an outage), the visitor still sees the normal confirmation with a blank reference number, no calendar hold is made, the request never appears in their account or on admin.html, and the owner receives an email that looks like every other one.

**Evidence.** quote.js:507-512 `.catch(function (err) { ... // Firebase refused (offline, or a rule): the email copy alone still reaches us.\n if (C.formEndpoint) return mailCopy(data).then(function (ok) { ok ? done(false) : fail(); });` calls `done(false)`, which hides `[data-demo-note]` and never fills `[data-request-id]`. A concrete trigger today: quote.js:572 `draft.slot = q.get("slot") || ""` takes any string from a shared link while firestore.rules:70 `str(d.slot, 10)` and :128 `str(h.slot, 10)` reject longer ones, so `quote.html?date=2026-11-05&slot=AAAAAAAAAAAA` ends in this branch. mailFields (quote.js:426-456) adds no 'not saved' marker.

**Impact.** The window the visitor chose is not held, so a second visitor can book it (double booking); the client cannot see, move or cancel the request from their account; the owner has to re-key it by hand and has no signal that it is missing from the admin page; combined with finding 1, a forged email and a real-but-unsaved request are indistinguishable.

**Suggestion.** In that branch show a distinct confirmation ('we got your details by email; we will confirm the time by reply') and add a field such as `Saved: NO - enter on the admin page` to the email; validate `?slot=` against `C.availability.slots` ids the way `?date=` is validated.

### [MEDIUM] Quote form locks email to the signed-in account, blocking staff
`quote.js:539` · ux · lens: auth-flows

Whoever is signed in on the browser, including the owner on admin.html, gets the quote form's email field forced to their own address and made read-only. Staff taking a phone or walk-in request on their own device cannot enter the client's email without "Not you? Sign out", which also ends their admin session.

**Evidence.** quote.js:538-539: `acctUser = u; email.value = u.email; email.readOnly = true;` with no check of who the user is. Mock run F1/F2: field value `Jane@Example.com`, readOnly=true, typing ignored; F3: "Not you? Sign out" calls `signOut` (global). The admin page's "add booking" (admin.js:399-404) captures only a name, so there is no staff path to a full request for a client.

**Impact.** The owner's most likely workflow (quote a caller while signed in to admin on the same phone) either files the request under the owner's email or logs the owner out of the admin page.

**Suggestion.** Skip the lock when the signed-in user is an admin (B.isAdmin cached, or a `?for=client` mode), or make the lock a prefill with an "Use a different email" link that keeps the session.

### [MEDIUM] Disabling the program leaves refer.html and codes active
`referral.js:182` · content · lens: referral-credits

With `referral.enabled: false` the banner and quote field disappear, but ?ref= codes are still captured and stored for 60 days, refer.html still shows the full table of amounts and a CTA to the (now hidden) account panel, the footer link and terms section 5 still promise the program, and a stored code is auto-applied again if the program is re-enabled.

**Evidence.** referral.js:182 `function init() { current(); fillAmounts(); banner(); }` calls current() (which writes localStorage, line 38) and fillAmounts() (no enabled check, lines 46-51) regardless of R.enabled; refer.html has no enabled gate. Verified by serving plans.js with enabled:false (public.js section 4): index.html?ref=K7M2QX stored {"code":"K7M2QX"} with no banner; refer.html rendered "Homes $25 $25 / Businesses $50 $50" and "Get my invite link -> account.html" (refer-disabled.png); terms.html still showed "Referral credit is for first-time customers..."; re-enabling within 60 days prefilled the quote field with the stored code. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:107 says enabled:false will "hide the program".

**Impact.** If the owner pauses or ends the program, visitors keep being promised $25/$50 on a public page and in the terms, and old links keep collecting codes that come back the moment it is switched on again.

**Suggestion.** Gate current() and fillAmounts() on R.enabled (clear the stored code when disabled), make refer.html show a "paused" notice and drop the amounts/CTA when disabled, and hide the footer link and terms section the same way.

### [LOW] Deploy action unpinned and workflow lacks a permissions block
`.github/workflows/firebase-hosting-merge.yml:29` · devops · lens: web-security

The production deploy runs a third-party action by mutable tag with the long-lived Firebase service-account key and the repo's default GITHUB_TOKEN permissions, so a compromised action release could publish a modified site or exfiltrate the key.

**Evidence.** .github/workflows/firebase-hosting-merge.yml:29 `- uses: FirebaseExtended/action-hosting-deploy@v0` with :33 `firebaseServiceAccount: ${{ secrets.FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT }}` and no top-level `permissions:`; the PR workflow (firebase-hosting-pull-request.yml:7) does declare `permissions`, and its :12 `if: github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name == github.repository` correctly keeps forks away from the secret.

**Impact.** A modified site served from mkgservices.com runs with the owner's own Firebase session, which defeats every rule; the blast radius of a supply-chain compromise is the whole database.

**Suggestion.** Pin the action to a full commit SHA (with the version in a comment), add `permissions: contents: read` to the merge workflow, and rotate the service-account key on a schedule.

### [LOW] Disabled accounts and other codes get the "check your connection" message
`account.js:59` · ux · lens: auth-flows

authMessage() maps auth/user-disabled (an account the owner disabled in the console), auth/web-storage-unsupported and auth/unauthorized-domain to the generic "Something went wrong. Check your connection and try again." Google failures all collapse to one sentence too.

**Evidence.** account.js:50-59 lists only invalid-credential, email-already-in-use, weak-password, too-many-requests, invalid-email, action-code and popup codes before `return t("acct.err.generic")`. Mock run D2-auth/user-disabled: email error "Something went wrong. Check your connection and try again."; D-auth/web-storage-unsupported: "Google sign-in didn't finish. Try again, or use your email below."

**Impact.** A deliberately disabled client keeps retrying and calls about a "connection problem"; private-mode Safari users with blocked storage are told to try again rather than to leave private mode.

**Suggestion.** Add mappings for user-disabled ("This account has been turned off; call us") and web-storage-unsupported ("Sign-in needs cookies/storage; leave private browsing"), in EN and ES.

### [LOW] Client's own hold makes their current window read Full
`account.js:150` · ux · lens: booking-integrity

The move picker counts the client's own hold against capacity, so when their window is at capacity it shows Full and disabled, as if their booking were at risk, and they cannot re-pick it after exploring other days.

**Evidence.** account.js:150-157 `Cal.mount(el, { segment: r.segment || "residential", pickable: true, value: editing[id], ...})` and calendar.js:52 counts every hold including `holds/{thisRequestId}`; a stubbed account with the client's own 11:30 booking plus one other hold in a capacity-2 window rendered "Midday 11:30–2:30 Full [disabled]".

**Impact.** Confusing for clients, especially if capacity is ever set to 1 (every current window would show Full); the only way back is the Keep date button.

**Suggestion.** Pass the request id to the calendar and exclude that hold from `taken`, or render it as "your booking".

### [LOW] Database refusal on the account page reads as "No requests yet"
`account.js:239` · ux · lens: auth-flows

If myRequests() is rejected (rules not re-published after a change, App Check enforcement misfiring, a stale ID token after verification elsewhere), the catch sets an empty list and the page shows the normal empty state with a "Request a cleaning" link, not an error.

**Evidence.** account.js:238-239: `B.myRequests(user.email).then(...).catch(function (e) { items = []; renderList(); if (window.console) console.warn(e); });`. Mock run B4 (verified user, backend rejecting permission-denied): page text "No requests yet. Request a cleaning.", no flash or error. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:203-207 plans App Check enforcement, the most likely source of such refusals for a real client.

**Impact.** A client with a confirmed booking would think it was lost and may re-book or call; the owner gets no signal that the database is refusing reads.

**Suggestion.** On a rejected read show acct.failed ("That didn't save…" style copy adapted to loading) with a retry button, and keep the empty state for an actual empty result.

### [LOW] Reward amount keyed to client-chosen segment; admin cannot change it
`admin.js:169` · pricing · lens: referral-credits

The welcome and referrer amounts come from the segment the visitor picked by choosing commercial-quote.html, and the admin edit panel cannot move a request to another segment, so a household using the commercial form earns $50/$50 instead of $25/$25.

**Evidence.** admin.js:169 `var rw = INV.rewards(r.segment || "residential")`; firestore.rules:71 only requires segment in ['residential','commercial']; admin.js:238 serviceOptions() lists services from segOf(r) only. Mock run: request R6 (segment commercial, code K7M2QX) wrote welcome-R6gggggg amount 50 on Confirm.

**Impact.** Doubles the payout for anyone who fills in the business form for a home; the owner can only fix it afterwards with a manual negative adjustment.

**Suggestion.** Let the admin edit panel change the segment, or grant the residential amount unless the request carries commercial-only fields (company, sqft) that the owner has confirmed.

### [LOW] Request cards never flag over-capacity or closed-day windows
`admin.js:202` · ux · lens: booking-integrity

The request card prints the requested day and window without checking the loaded holds or days, so the owner's main view gives no hint that a request is the third in a two-crew window or sits on a closed day.

**Evidence.** admin.js:202 `details.push([t("review.timing"), esc(whenText(r))]);` with no lookup; in the stubbed admin.html "Dan New (3rd in a cap-2 window)" showed "Timing Friday, October 23, Midday (11:30–2:30)" with Confirm enabled, while only the day panel read "3 of 2 taken".

**Impact.** The owner confirms a clash from the default New filter and finds out when a crew is short; until capacity is enforced server-side this is the only place to catch it.

**Suggestion.** Add a warning line on the card computed from the current month's holds and days ("3 of 2 in this window", "day is closed") and ask for confirmation before confirming into a full window.

### [LOW] Fields written inconsistently: updatedAt doubles as cancel time, dead submittedAt
`admin.js:211` · maintainability · lens: data-model-cost

updatedAt is rewritten by every admin save, credit grant and hold fix but is displayed as the cancellation time; submittedAt (client clock) duplicates the server createdAt and is never read; the Formspree subject is persisted into Firestore; only phone bookings carry a source field.

**Evidence.** admin.js:211 shows `r.updatedAt` after 'Cancelled by client/you', while backend.js:381 updateRequest() stamps updatedAt on every patch including admin.js:173/180 `refWelcomeGranted`/`refRewardGranted` and :309 credit application. quote.js:385 `submittedAt: new Date().toISOString()` is allowed by firestore.rules:62 but grep finds no reader. quote.js:412 writes `p._subject` into the saved document (rules:65 allow it) while mailFields() builds a different subject at :423. backend.js:415 sets `source: "phone"` and admin.js:187 tests it, but form requests have no source (rules:61 would refuse one; emulator A4).

**Impact.** The 'cancelled at' shown to the owner drifts to the time of any later edit; every request stores two 'when' values that can disagree; the schema carries Formspree-specific data; reports that want to split web from phone bookings must infer it from the absence of a field.

**Suggestion.** Add explicit cancelledAt/confirmedAt timestamps, drop submittedAt and _subject from the stored document (keep them only in the email payload), write `source: "web"` from the form, and list the status vocabulary (new, confirmed, declined, done, cancelled) once in backend.js for all pages and rules to share.

### [LOW] Phone bookings skip capacity, closed-day and segment checks
`admin.js:403` · ux · lens: booking-integrity

"Add booking" takes only a name from a prompt, guesses the segment from the window (shared windows are always residential), stores no phone number, and never warns when the window is full or the day closed; the request cannot be edited to add those later.

**Evidence.** admin.js:400-404 `var name = prompt(t("adm.addPrompt")); ... var seg = sl && sl.segments && sl.segments.indexOf("residential") === -1 ? "commercial" : "residential"; p = B.addPhoneBooking(openDay, sid, name.trim(), seg);`; backend.js:412-419 writes `phone: ""` and `email: ""`; the edit panel (admin.js:236-243) has no name or phone fields. The stubbed day panel showed "Add booking" active on a window already reading "3 of 2 taken".

**Impact.** A third job lands in a two-crew window or on a closed day unnoticed; a commercial client booked by phone is filed as residential; the most useful detail of a phone booking, the phone number, is lost.

**Suggestion.** Replace the prompt with a small form (name, phone, segment) that warns when the window is full or the day closed, and let the edit panel change name and phone.

### [LOW] Decimal credit amounts render as $12.5 and $37.5
`app.js:48` · ux · lens: referral-credits

Admin adjustments accept cents (rounded to 2 decimals) but dollars() formats with toLocaleString and no fraction digits option, so balances and ledger lines show one decimal.

**Evidence.** app.js:48 `function dollars(n) { return "$" + n.toLocaleString("en-US"); }`; admin.js:505 `rounded(Number(...))` allows 12.5. Mock run: Credits tab showed "Balance: $12.5" and "+$12.5" (admin-credits.png); account.html showed "$37.5" as the client's balance (account-390.png).

**Impact.** Money shown to clients in a non-currency format looks like a bug and is hard to read.

**Suggestion.** Use minimumFractionDigits/maximumFractionDigits 2 when the value is not an integer (or restrict credit amounts to whole dollars in the admin form).

### [LOW] Nav "My account" flag can drift from the real session
`app.js:334` · ux · lens: auth-flows

Seven pages (index, plans, commercial*, privacy, terms, refer) never load backend.js, so their nav label comes only from the localStorage flag; it is also left untouched when Firebase fails to load on a page that does include it, and staff signing in on admin.html flips the public nav to "My account" for them.

**Evidence.** app.js:334: `signed = localStorage.getItem("mkg.account") === "1"` with no other check; backend.js:239-240 only updates the flag inside `auth.onAuthStateChanged`, which is never registered when `ready` resolves false (backend.js:237-238). Pages without backend.js: `for f in *.html; do grep -q backend.js $f || echo $f` lists index.html, plans.html, commercial.html, commercial-plans.html, privacy.html, refer.html, terms.html. Mock run G: with the flag set, index.html/privacy.html/refer.html show "My account" while `window.MKG.backend` is undefined.

**Impact.** Mostly cosmetic: a revoked or expired session still reads "My account" until a Firebase page loads, and staff see a client-account link they don't need.

**Suggestion.** Treat the flag as a hint only (keep), but clear it in the `ready` false branch and on `auth/user-token-expired`, and skip setting it for users who have an admins record.

### [LOW] Admin sign-in persists on the device indefinitely
`backend.js:174` · security · lens: web-security

No persistence is set, so Firebase keeps admin sessions in IndexedDB (LOCAL) until an explicit sign-out; a shared or lost phone stays signed in to the full customer database.

**Evidence.** backend.js:174 `function signInPassword(email, password) { return auth.signInWithEmailAndPassword(email, password); }` and the Google path at :177-185, with no `auth.setPersistence(...)` anywhere (`grep -n setPersistence backend.js` is empty); admin.js:542-545 only rechecks team membership every two minutes, not session age.

**Impact.** Staff using a shared tablet or a phone that is lost or resold leave the customer list, notes and the ability to confirm or delete bookings open to whoever picks it up.

**Suggestion.** On admin.html call `auth.setPersistence(firebase.auth.Auth.Persistence.SESSION)` before sign-in so closing the tab signs out, or add an idle timeout; keep LOCAL for the client account page where convenience matters more.

### [LOW] Lead time depends on the visitor's device date, not Charlotte's
`calendar.js:32` · bug · lens: booking-integrity

"Today" and the earliest bookable day are computed from the browser clock and zone, and the rules never compare the date with request.time, so the two-day lead is advisory.

**Evidence.** calendar.js:32-34 `var today = new Date(); ... var first = opts.admin ? today : D.addDays(today, AV.leadDays || 0);` and quote.js:34 likewise. Playwright at the same instant 2026-10-21T09:30Z (05:30 in Charlotte): America/New_York first bookable 2026-10-23, Pacific/Honolulu 2026-10-22 (its local date is still Oct 20). firestore.rules:70 only checks the date format.

**Impact.** Visitors west of Charlotte or with a wrong clock can request a day inside the lead time; the owner gets requests they cannot staff and has to decline them.

**Suggestion.** Derive today in America/New_York (Intl.DateTimeFormat with timeZone in backend.dates) and add a lead/horizon check on `d.date` against request.time in the rules.

### [LOW] Admin calendar cannot navigate to past months
`calendar.js:81` · ux · lens: booking-integrity

In admin mode `first` is today, so the previous-month button is disabled at the current month and the owner cannot review last month's schedule or leftover holds from the calendar.

**Evidence.** calendar.js:33 `var first = opts.admin ? today : ...` and line 81 `var canPrev = new Date(vy, vm, 1) > new Date(first.getFullYear(), first.getMonth(), 1);`; admin.js:416 mounts with `admin: true`, and the week-ahead panel (admin.js:338-341) also looks forward only.

**Impact.** Checking what happened on a past day (a dispute, a payroll question) means searching the request list or the time-clock CSV instead of the calendar.

**Suggestion.** Allow `canPrev` back to a configurable number of months in admin mode (`first` = today minus 6 months).

### [LOW] Invites never expire and grant full admin before any acceptance step
`firestore.rules:39` · security · lens: rules-adversarial

invited() makes any verified sign-in whose email matches an adminInvites document a complete admin (read all customer data, write credits, close days) with no admins record, no expiry and no acceptance. The design is sound (Google or email-link proves the address), but a mistyped invite is a standing grant to whoever owns that address.

**Evidence.** Emulator checks: I01 an account with only adminInvites/invited@mkg.test (no admins doc) listed every request ALLOWED; I02 wrote a credit ALLOWED; I05 the same email signed in with an unverified password account DENIED; I06 a verified stranger registering admins/{own uid} DENIED. adminInvites rule (lines 200-203) validates the email format but stores no expiry, and backend.js:305-308 inviteAdmin() writes only email, invitedBy, createdAt.

**Impact.** If the owner invites jon@gmial.com by mistake, the typo stays listed as '(invited)' until noticed, and the holder of that address becomes an admin the first time they sign in with Google on the site. Low likelihood, high consequence (all client PII).

**Suggestion.** Add an expiry: require request.time < get(adminInvites/$(myEmail())).data.createdAt + duration.value(7,'d') inside invited(), and have the admins create rule delete-or-ignore stale invites; show the invite's age in the team list so typos stand out.

### [LOW] refAmount is accepted from the client but never used
`firestore.rules:80` · maintainability · lens: referral-credits

The quote form sends refAmount (the amount it displayed), the rules accept any number below ten million, backend.js keeps it as a numeric field, and nothing on the admin or account side reads it. It is a trap for a future change that trusts the stored amount.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:80 `optNum(d, 'refAmount')`; quote.js:397 `p.refAmount = friendAmount();`; backend.js:108 lists refAmount in NUM; grep across the repo finds no other reader. Admin grants use INV.rewards() from plans.js (admin.js:169), which is the correct source.

**Impact.** No visitor impact today; a developer adding "show the promised amount" later could display or grant a client-chosen number.

**Suggestion.** Stop sending refAmount from quote.js and remove it from quoteFields()/NUM, or validate in rules that it equals the configured reward for the segment.

### [LOW] 24-hour change and cancel notice is enforced only in the browser
`firestore.rules:102` · security · lens: web-security

terms.html promises free changes with 24 hours' notice and the account page hides the buttons inside that window, but the rules let a verified customer move or cancel a confirmed visit at any time, so the late-cancel policy can be bypassed from the console or REST and the owner gets no 'late' marker.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:102-113 `customerMoves(id)` and `customerCancels(id)` check `changed().hasOnly([...])`, status and the hold, but never compare `resource.data.date`/`slot` with `request.time`; account.js:36 `function tooLate(r) { ... Date.now() > s - NOTICE_HOURS * 36e5; }` is the only check, and plans.js:94 `policy: { changeNoticeHours: 24 }` is read only by that page.

**Impact.** A client who knows the trick can cancel an hour before a confirmed visit without the call the terms require; the crew may still drive out, and the owner cannot tell from the request that the cancel was late.

**Suggestion.** Mirror the policy in the rule: build the visit start from `date` and the 4-digit `slot` (`timestamp.date(int(date[0:4]), int(date[5:7]), int(date[8:10]))` plus the slot hours) and require `request.time < start - duration.value(24, 'h')` for customer moves/cancels of confirmed requests; at minimum let the client write `lateChange: true` and show it on the admin card.

### [LOW] Request IDs are attacker-chosen and reach an unescaped selector
`firestore.rules:115` · bug · lens: web-security · votes 1/1 real (reproduce: medium) · **confirmed**

The rules put no constraint on the document id of an anonymously created request, and admin.js builds CSS selectors from that id with string concatenation, so an id containing a quote throws in the week-ahead click handler and after Save.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:115 `match /requests/{id}` with :117 allowing unauthenticated create; the SDK generates 20-char alphanumeric ids but a REST caller supplies any string. admin.js:359 `var art = $('[data-id="' + id + '"]');` and :299 `$('[data-id="' + id + '"] [data-saved]')`. Observed with the hostile-data harness (stub backend feeding an id containing `"><img ...>`): `pageerror: Failed to execute 'querySelector' on 'Document': '[data-id=""><img src=x onerror=...id"]' is not a valid selector.` Every other use of the id is escaped (`esc(r.id)`), so this is not XSS.

**Impact.** Cosmetic today (scroll-to-request and the 'Saved' flash stop working for that card), but it is the one path where untrusted data reaches a non-escaped sink, and a hostile id can also impersonate the legacy `manual` hold name (admin.js:366).

**Suggestion.** In the anonymous create branch require `id.matches('^[A-Za-z0-9]{20}$')` (and `holds` likewise), and wrap the id with `CSS.escape(id)` in the two selectors.

### [LOW] Client can re-create a hold the owner removed, without touching the request
`firestore.rules:137` · security · lens: rules-adversarial

customerHolds() lets a verified client create holds/{id} whenever the request is still new or confirmed and the hold mirrors the request's date and slot; it does not require the request to be written in the same batch. The setup doc's data-model row says holds are only 'created together with its request; moved or dropped together with it; admins may remove one'.

**Evidence.** Emulator check C07: with requests/req-alice2 confirmed and its hold deleted (as admin.js:405-407 'Remove' does), Alice's bare `holds/req-alice2.set({date, slot, requestId, createdAt: serverTimestamp(), segment})` was ALLOWED. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:165 describes the intended lifecycle.

**Impact.** The owner's 'Remove' on a day panel frees a window only until the client (with a console write) puts the hold back; no normal page does this, so the exposure is a determined client and a confusing calendar for the owner.

**Suggestion.** In customerHolds() also require getAfter(reqPath(id)).data.updatedAt == request.time, so a client can only write a hold as part of a move the rules already validate; the owner frees a window properly by changing the request's date or declining it.

### [LOW] Verification banner copy claims a link was just sent
`i18n.js:648` · content · lens: auth-flows

acct.verifyText ("We sent a link to {addr}") is shown on every visit by an unverified account, including weeks later and for the console-created owner who never received one; and a throttled resend reuses the password error "Too many tries. Wait a few minutes, or reset your password."

**Evidence.** i18n.js:648: "Confirm your email to see your requests here. We sent a link to {addr}; …"; account.js:256-262 renders it unconditionally for `!user.emailVerified`. Mock run K1 (owner@mkg.test, unverified, no sign-up this session) shows the same banner; B2: resend rejecting auth/too-many-requests shows i18n.js:645 acct.err.tooMany. Spanish strings (i18n.js:1455, 1458) have the same wording.

**Impact.** Minor confusion for unverified users and the owner; the reset-password suggestion is wrong advice in a verification context.

**Suggestion.** Reword to "Confirm your email to see your requests. Check {addr} for our link, or send it again." and give the resend button its own throttle message in both languages.

### [LOW] CSV formula guard misses tab and carriage-return prefixes
`timeclock.js:285` · security · lens: web-security

The payroll CSV neutralises cells starting with = + - @, which covers the common formula injection, but OWASP's list also includes a leading tab or CR, and customer-supplied names reach this file.

**Evidence.** timeclock.js:285 `if (/^[=+\-@]/.test(v)) v = "'" + v;` run in node against the same regex: `"\t=1+1" -> "\t=1+1"` and `"\r=1+1" -> "\r=1+1"` pass through unprefixed while `=HYPERLINK(...)`, `+1`, `-5`, `@SUM(1)` are correctly prefixed. `r.name` (any visitor, up to 120 chars per firestore.rules:67, no character restriction) is written at :283.

**Impact.** Marginal: only some spreadsheet importers evaluate such cells, and the owner would have to open a payroll export containing a maliciously named request.

**Suggestion.** Extend the test to `/^[=+\-@\t\r]/` (one character change) and keep the existing quoting.

### What this area found well done

- Escaping is complete and provable: every untrusted value that reaches innerHTML in admin.js, account.js, timeclock.js, quote.js, referral.js, calendar.js and availability.js goes through esc()/A.esc(), and the i18n engine escapes translated strings before substituting its fixed placeholders (i18n.js:1718-1721). A Playwright harness that replaced backend.js with a stub returning hostile Firestore-shaped data (names, notes, emails, phones, slots, days, contact methods, referral codes, credit notes, shift notes, team emails, holds and hostile document ids) through every render path of admin.html and account.html, including the ES language re-render, executed zero payloads and created zero injected elements. _(web-security)_
- URL and link inputs are allowlisted rather than trusted: ?lang is checked against ["en","es"] (i18n.js:1647-1649), ?ref against ^[A-Z2-9]{6}$ (referral.js:20-27), ?plan/?freq/?service against the ids in plans.js (app.js:73-74, quote.js:570), ?date against a strict date regex (quote.js:572); the client-facing payment link renders only when it is https:// with no whitespace (account.js:37 safeLink, admin.js:286 on input), and the passwordless sign-in return URL is pinned to location.origin + location.pathname (account.js:342) rather than taken from the page's query string. _(web-security)_
- firestore.rules is a genuine server-side allowlist, not a mirror of the UI: anonymous creates are limited with keys().hasOnly() and length caps that exactly match the forms' maxlength attributes (name 120, email 200, phone 40, notes 2000, company 160), createdAt must equal request.time, a hold must mirror its request via getAfter() and is removed when the request closes, customers can only move (back to 'new') or cancel their own request, staff self-registration requires an owner invite plus a provider-verified email, deletes/team/shift edits are owner-only, referral codes can be checked one at a time but never listed, and staff-only notes live in a separate collection the customer can never read. _(web-security)_
- Third-party code is kept at arm's length: the Firebase SDK is pinned at 10.14.1 and served same-origin from Hosting's reserved /__/firebase/ URLs with the config fetched from /__/firebase/init.json (no CDN to tamper with and nothing to paste), App Check is wired in with reCAPTCHA Enterprise and auto-refresh, authDomain is the site's own domain so the Google popup says mkgservices.com, and the QR library is a local MIT copy (vendor/qrcode.js) rather than a remote include. _(web-security)_
- Many small privacy and hygiene details are right: the CSV export quotes every cell and neutralises =, +, - and @; 'forgot password' returns the same message whether or not an account exists (account.js:331-336, admin.js:563-564); admin.html, account.html and both quote pages are noindex and absent from sitemap.xml; oobCode/apiKey are scrubbed from the URL after an email-link action (backend.js:224-226); the public holds collection carries no personal data; and privacy.html discloses Firebase, Formspree, Google Fonts and reCAPTCHA in both English and Spanish. _(web-security)_
- Request and hold are genuinely bound together with getAfter()/existsAfter(): in the emulator a visitor could not create a hold for an existing request (V20), overwrite or delete someone else's hold (V21, V22), and a client could not move a request without moving its hold (C08) or reopen a done request (C10). _(rules-adversarial)_
- Timestamps and diffs are used the right way: createdAt/movedByClientAt/updatedAt must equal request.time (a client-supplied Timestamp.now() was refused, V17), and changed().hasOnly() is applied to the merged post-write document so field removal and extra fields are caught (C09, S10, S11, S13 all denied). _(rules-adversarial)_
- Email verification is enforced where it matters: an unverified password account using a client's address could not list her requests or credits (C25, C26), and an invited email signed in without verification got nothing (I05), matching what docs/SETUP-firebase.md promises. _(rules-adversarial)_
- The referral pair is airtight: users/{uid} and referrers/{code} must be created together with mutual getAfter checks, update is false on both, a second code (C19) and a code pointing at someone else's uid (C20) were refused, referrers cannot be listed (V28), and self-referral by the same email is blocked at write time (V14). _(rules-adversarial)_
- Default deny holds: there is no catch-all match, so an unauthenticated visitor could read only holds, days and single referrers (V24-V31 denied), non-owner admins cannot delete requests, invite, promote or remove the owner (S08, S14, S15, O03), and team members see and edit only their own open shift (S09-S13). _(rules-adversarial)_
- A request and its hold are written in one batch (backend.js:148-166) and the rules force the hold to mirror its request (`mirrors`, `holdMatches`, `staleHold`), so a visitor or client cannot create a hold whose day or window disagrees with the request it belongs to. _(booking-integrity)_
- Customer-side rules are tight: `customerMoves` whitelists the changed fields, forces the status back to new and stamps `movedByClientAt`; `customerCancels` requires the hold to be gone; a client may delete only holds of their own requests that are no longer needed, and `manual` holds are protected. _(booking-integrity)_
- Holds and days carry no personal data, so the public calendar works without sign-in and without leaking who booked what; staff-only notes live in a separate `internal` collection. _(booking-integrity)_
- quote.js re-validates the picked day at submit and clears it with a clear bilingual message (q.dateStale), and `reschedule`, `cancelRequest` and `deleteRequest` are each a single atomic batch. _(booking-integrity)_
- Date handling is consistently local-midnight YYYY-MM-DD (`parse`, `ymd`, `addDays`) with no UTC date-only parsing; verified in node that the grid math has no off-by-one across the 2026 spring-forward and fall-back changes in America/New_York. _(booking-integrity)_
- Code creation is genuinely atomic and collision-safe: users/{uid} and referrers/{code} are written in one batch (backend.js:457-466), the rules cross-check each half with getAfter() (firestore.rules:237, 250), neither document can be updated afterwards, and createCode() re-reads an existing code before retrying up to five times. _(referral-credits)_
- Grants are idempotent by construction: deterministic ledger ids (welcome-<id>, referral-<id>, use-<id>) in addCredit() plus refWelcomeGranted/refRewardGranted flags; a confirm, done, reopen, confirm, done cycle in the mock run produced exactly two ledger lines and no duplicates. _(referral-credits)_
- Server-side validation of the invite: refOk() in firestore.rules requires the code to exist and not belong to the requester's email, the client-sent refAmount is never used for amounts (plans.js is the single source), credits are admin-write-only, and clients can only read their own ledger. _(referral-credits)_
- Codes are hard to enumerate and easy to read aloud: six characters from a 32-letter alphabet without 0/O/1/I generated with crypto.getRandomValues (about a billion combinations), referrers/{code} allows single gets but no listing, and the public document carries no personal data. _(referral-credits)_
- The copy and the amounts come from one place and render correctly in both languages: i18n's LIVE placeholders turn {maxPct}, {homeFriend} and friends into live elements that referral.js fills, so refer.html, the account panel, the banner and the share messages all showed $25/$50 and 100% consistently in EN and ES. _(referral-credits)_
- authDomain is overridden to the site's own hostname on the live domain (backend.js:68) with /__/auth/handler served by Hosting: this is the documented fix for Safari/Firefox third-party-storage blocking, keeps the Google consent screen branded as mkgservices.com, and makes popup-then-redirect work where cross-origin authDomains fail. _(auth-flows)_
- Customer access is keyed on emailLower == request.auth.token.email.lower() and requires email_verified (firestore.rules:35, 93): the emulator confirmed an unverified password sign-up with a victim's address can read nothing (identity.test.js E06-E10), case differences are tolerated (E01/E02), and invited-but-unverified accounts get no admin rights (I07/I08). _(auth-flows)_
- The unverified-account path on account.html is complete and bilingual: a clear banner, an "I've confirmed it" button that reloads the user and force-refreshes the ID token so rules see the claim (backend.js:212-216), a resend button, and the referral/request loaders are skipped until verified (account.js:251-254). _(auth-flows)_
- Cross-device email-link sign-in is handled rather than assumed: the stored-email miss shows a confirm form (account.js:417), the oobCode and apiKey are scrubbed from the URL after sign-in (backend.js:259), continueUrl is fixed to account.html (no open redirect), and Google uses prompt=select_account for shared devices. _(auth-flows)_
- Team removal is enforced server-side and the admin page re-checks membership from the server every two minutes (backend.js:285, admin.js:542-545); password-reset and link requests answer the same way whether or not an account exists (account.js:331-336, admin.js:563-564), and the sign-in form switches autocomplete between new-password and current-password per tab (account.js:294). _(auth-flows)_
- No composite indexes are needed today: all ten queries in backend.js (holds date range at 122, days documentId range at 123, holdsOf/credits/users/shifts/requests equality filters, requests and credits orderBy createdAt, shifts inAt range with orderBy on the same field) are served by automatic single-field indexes, so nothing fails on first use in production. _(data-model-cost)_
- Customer-side writes are genuinely constrained by the rules, not just the pages: in the emulator a verified customer could not cancel while leaving a hold behind (C7), could not create a hold with a random id or for an existing request (A7/A8), and an unverified password account with the same email saw no requests (C3). holdMatches()/mirrors()/staleHold() in firestore.rules:96-146 are careful work. _(data-model-cost)_
- Timestamps are server-authoritative (createdAt == request.time, inAt == request.time) and the client-initiated multi-document changes that matter most (submitRequest, reschedule, cancelRequest, deleteRequest, createCode) are single batches, so client clocks and half-finished writes cannot corrupt those paths. _(data-model-cost)_
- Privacy-conscious data layout: staff-only fields were split into internal/{id}, referrers/{code} is get-only so codes cannot be enumerated (emulator B5), public holds carry no personal data, and credit lines use idempotent ids (welcome-<id>, referral-<id>, use-<id>) so a retried grant cannot double-pay. _(data-model-cost)_
- docs/SETUP-firebase.md:160-176 documents every collection with who may read and write it, and that table matched the rules in all 35 emulator checks; cleanRequest()/cleanShift() in backend.js make the pages tolerant of odd records, which is the right defensive posture for a hand-pasted ruleset. _(data-model-cost)_

## Correctness (85 findings: 11 high, 35 medium, 39 low)

_Scope: JavaScript behaviour and bugs in every page script._

### [HIGH] Changing the service keeps the old plan's estimate
`admin.js:94` · pricing · lens: admin-timeclock · votes 3/3 real (impact: medium, reproduce: medium, refute: medium) · **confirmed**

servicePatch only rewrites service/plan/frequency; estimatePerVisit, estimateOneVisit, estimateFullPrice, estimatePerMonth and discount stay from the original quote. The card, the client's account page and the time-clock revenue all keep showing the previous plan's price under the new service name.

**Evidence.** admin.js:94 `return s ? { service: s.name.en, plan: del, frequency: del } : {};` and :98 for plans; nothing touches the estimate fields. Mock run: Family / Every 2 weeks ($177) changed to Deep clean and saved -> write `{service: "Deep clean", plan: <DELETE>, frequency: <DELETE>, ...}`; card now reads "Service: Deep clean · Home ... Estimate: $177 per visit". account.js:73 would show the client "$177 per visit · estimate" for a deep clean, and a plan-to-plan change keeps "About $X per month" from the old frequency.

**Impact.** Clients are shown an estimate that belongs to a different service, and the owner's $/hour report values the visit at the wrong price, until someone remembers to also type a final price.

**Suggestion.** When the service choice changes, delete the estimate* and discount fields (or recompute them from C.segments[seg] with the request's bedrooms/bathrooms/sqft/restrooms) and prompt for a final price.

### [HIGH] Referral credit cap and "you pay" use the placeholder estimate, not the quoted price
`admin.js:123` · pricing · lens: estimator-engine · votes 3/3 real (impact: high, reproduce: high, refute: high) · **confirmed**

When the owner applies referral credit, the maximum and the client-facing "you pay" figure are derived from the site's placeholder estimate (estimatePerVisit / estimateOneVisit) even after the owner has typed a final quotedPrice on the same card. timeclock.js already prefers quotedPrice, so the two admin screens disagree on what a visit costs.

**Evidence.** admin.js:123 `function visitPrice(r) { return typeof r.estimatePerVisit === "number" ? r.estimatePerVisit : typeof r.estimateOneVisit === "number" ? r.estimateOneVisit : null; }` — no quotedPrice branch. admin.js:161 `var max = Math.min(b, INV.cap(visitPrice(r)));` prefills the credit input; admin.js:305-309 re-checks `n > INV.cap(price)` and writes `youPay: Math.max(0, rounded(price - n))` with the same price. account.js:114 then shows that youPay to the client. Contrast timeclock.js:47 `function price(r) { return typeof r.quotedPrice === "number" ? r.quotedPrice : ... }`. Backend is absent locally, so this is reasoned from the code; it could only be observed against the live database.

**Impact.** Money: a Family 3bd/2ba estimate is $195 today (placeholder) while the real quote may be, say, $150; a client with $195 credit gets $195 applied (over the real price by $45) and account.html tells them "You pay $0" computed from the wrong base. If the real price is higher than the estimate, the client sees an understated "you pay".

**Suggestion.** Make visitPrice() prefer quotedPrice exactly as timeclock.js does (`typeof r.quotedPrice === "number" ? r.quotedPrice : ...`), and recompute youPay whenever quotedPrice is edited after credit was applied.

### [HIGH] Credit math uses the estimate, not the final price
`admin.js:309` · pricing · lens: admin-timeclock · votes 3/3 real (impact: high, reproduce: high, refute: high) · **confirmed**

Apply credit computes the cap and the client's "You pay" from estimatePerVisit/estimateOneVisit (visitPrice, line 123) and never looks at quotedPrice. Once the owner sets a final price, the request carries two contradictory numbers that the client sees side by side on account.html.

**Evidence.** admin.js:123 `function visitPrice(r) { return typeof r.estimatePerVisit === "number" ? r.estimatePerVisit : ... }` and :309 `youPay: price === null ? null : Math.max(0, rounded(price - n))`. Driving admin.html in Playwright with backend.js swapped for an in-memory mock (scratch mock-backend.js / drive.js): request with quotedPrice 80, estimatePerVisit 150, balance $25 -> click Apply wrote `updateRequest(r2, {creditApplied: 25, youPay: 125})`; the card then reads "$80 per visit · confirmed price (estimate was $150 per visit) ... Credit applied: $25. Client pays $125." account.js:114 prints the same youPay under "You pay". The cap `INV.cap(visitPrice(r))` likewise allows more credit than the final price. timeclock.js:47 `price()` does prefer quotedPrice, so the two files disagree.

**Impact.** Clients with a confirmed price and a referral credit are told a wrong amount to pay (too high here; too low or over-credited when the final price is above the estimate), which means disputes or lost money for the owner.

**Suggestion.** Make visitPrice prefer quotedPrice (as timeclock.js does), recompute youPay whenever quotedPrice is saved, or stop storing youPay and derive it on display from price minus creditApplied.

### [HIGH] "Any window" requests never occupy calendar capacity
`backend.js:163` · bug · lens: i18n-engine-calendar

A request with a date but no window (the "Any window that day" button the calendar offers on every pickable day) creates no hold, and confirming it later still creates none, so the public calendar keeps showing every window of that day as fully open. Only requests with a specific window are ever counted.

**Evidence.** backend.js:163 `if (doc.date && doc.slot) { batch.set(holdRef(ref.id), ...` and backend.js:403 `if (!req.date || !req.slot || !OPEN[...]) return Promise.resolve();`. calendar.js:52 counts holds per window only: `holds.filter(function (h) { return h.date === ymd && h.slot === s.id; }).length`. calendar.js:160 offers `cal.anyWindow` on every day, and admin.js:214 defaults the window select to "Any window", so the owner can confirm without ever assigning one. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:165 describes a hold as "the day + window a request is holding" with no mention of day-level holds. Reasoned from code: the live database was not reachable.

**Impact.** Two or more clients can pick the same day while the team already has unassigned jobs on it; the calendar shows green until the owner manually assigns windows, so double-booking and last-minute reschedules are possible.

**Suggestion.** Either require a window before a request can be confirmed (make the admin select mandatory on confirm), or write a day-level hold (slot "") and count slotless holds against every window of that day in dayInfo().

### [HIGH] Keyboard focus is lost after every calendar interaction
`calendar.js:117` · a11y · lens: i18n-engine-calendar

render() replaces the whole widget with `root.innerHTML = html`, so the button the user just activated is destroyed and focus falls to <body>. This happens on day selection, window selection and month navigation, and the aria-live month heading is recreated each time instead of updated.

**Evidence.** calendar.js:117 `root.innerHTML = html;` runs from the day click handler (:125), the slot click handler (:133) and both nav handlers (:119-120). Observed on availability.html in Chromium (audit.js S3): focus a day and press Enter -> `document.activeElement` is BODY; click a window -> BODY; focus "Next month" and press Enter -> BODY. audit2.js: `document.querySelector('.cal-month') !== h3a` is true after navigation, i.e. the aria-live element is a new node.

**Impact.** Keyboard and screen-reader users must Tab from the top of the page after each of the three steps of picking a visit (day, window, next month), and the month change is unlikely to be announced. The same picker is used in the quote form, the account page and admin.

**Suggestion.** After re-rendering, restore focus to the element with the same data-cal-day/data-cal-slot/data-cal-next attribute (or update cells in place), and keep the month heading as a persistent aria-live element whose textContent you change.

### [HIGH] Anyone can create holds; calendar can be blocked without sign-in
`firestore.rules:152` · security · lens: quote-flow

The create rule lets any unauthenticated client write a request plus its hold, and nothing limits how many. With App Check still in monitor-only mode (docs/SETUP-firebase.md) and capacity 2 per window, a short script, or a few prank submissions, marks every window 'Full' on the public calendar until the owner declines each one by hand.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:117 `allow create: if isAdmin() || quoteFields(request.resource.data);` and 150-156 `allow create: if holdShape(request.resource.data) && (isAdmin() || (hid == request.resource.data.requestId && ((getAfter(reqPath(hid)).data.createdAt == request.time && mirrors(hid)) || customerHolds(hid))));` with no count, rate or identity requirement. backend.js:163-165 writes the hold in the same batch; calendar.js:54-55 computes left = capacity − holds, so two writes per window hide it from visitors.

**Impact.** Lost bookings: real visitors see 'Full or closed' days and can only send 'flexible' requests; the owner must find and decline hundreds of fake requests to reopen the calendar. Benign duplicates (double submits after a hang, see the write-timeout finding) also eat capacity.

**Suggestion.** Finish the documented App Check step (Enforce for Cloud Firestore once requests show 'Verified'), and consider a small server-side guard (a Cloud Function or an honest cap such as one hold per email per day enforced in rules via a per-email document).

### [HIGH] Draft is re-saved after submit, so the next request is pre-filled
`quote.js:365` · bug · lens: quote-flow

done() calls clearDraft() and then go('done'), but go() unconditionally calls saveDraft(), so the full draft (name, email, phone, ZIP, notes, date and slot) is written straight back to sessionStorage with step 'done'. Opening quote.html again in the same tab (including the 'book this again' link from account.html) restores everything, including the previously requested day and window.

**Evidence.** quote.js:480-481 `clearDraft(); go("done");` and go() ends with `saveDraft();` at line 365. Observed after a successful submit: sessionStorage['mkg.quote'] = {"zip":"28202","name":"Test Person","email":"test@example.com","phone":"(980) 555-1234",…,"step":"done","date":"2026-10-22","slot":"0800"}; a fresh load of quote.html showed ZIP, name, email and phone filled and the review step read 'Timing = Thursday, October 22, Morning (8:00–11:00)' again.

**Impact.** A second request from the same tab (a second property, or the account page's book-again button) silently carries the old date/slot and notes, producing duplicate or wrong-day requests and holds; personal details also linger in storage after the flow the privacy policy describes as ended.

**Suggestion.** In go(), skip saveDraft() when n === 'done' (or call clearDraft() after go('done')), and have the book-again link append a parameter that forces a clean draft.

### [HIGH] Refused invite code is dropped silently; nobody is told
`quote.js:501` · bug · lens: account-availability-referral

When Firestore refuses a request because the code is the customer's own or no longer exists, the form resubmits without the code, but the done page says nothing, the invite banner keeps promising the credit, and the owner never sees refDropped.

**Evidence.** Lines 498-502: `.catch(function (err) { if (!data.ref) throw err; var plain = Object.assign({}, data); delete plain.ref; delete plain.refAmount; plain.refDropped = data.ref; data = plain; return B.submitRequest(plain); })`. Line 505 only shows the welcome note and calls `INV.forget()` `if (data.ref)`, which is now unset, so the 60-day localStorage code and the "A friend invited you: $25 off" banner survive. firestore.rules line 80 accepts `refDropped` but `grep refDropped admin.js` returns nothing, so the admin card never shows it. The review step had shown "Friend's code K7M2QX: $25 off your first visit" because q.ref.ok only checks that referrers/{code} exists (rules line 243), not refOk().

**Impact.** A customer who entered their own code, a household member's, or a removed code leaves expecting $25-$50 off and the owner has no flag to explain why it was not applied; the banner keeps advertising the discount on every page.

**Suggestion.** When refDropped is set, show a done-page note ("we couldn't apply code X") and call INV.forget(); render refDropped on the admin request card next to the referral row.

### [HIGH] Firestore refusal falls back to email but shows normal success
`quote.js:510` · bug · lens: quote-flow

When the database write is refused (a rules mismatch, App Check enforcement, a bad slot value, an SDK load failure) the form sends only the Formspree email and then shows the ordinary 'Request sent' screen, including the promise that the request is in the visitor's account or can be followed by creating one. Nothing was saved, so neither the admin page nor the account page will ever show it; the only trace is a console.warn.

**Evidence.** quote.js:507-511: `.catch(function (err) { if (window.console) console.warn("request not saved", err); if (C.formEndpoint) return mailCopy(data).then(function (ok) { ok ? done(false) : fail(); }); fail(); })` and done() calls renderTrack() (556-558) which renders 'It's in your account…' when signed in, or 'Want to follow it…? Create a free account with the same email' otherwise. Observed locally (no Firebase, Formspree stubbed to 200): step 'done', demo note hidden, track text 'Want to follow it, change the day, or cancel online? Create a free account with the same email.' (screenshot en-done-ok.png). The same code path runs on the live site for any refused write.

**Impact.** A visitor is told their request is tracked and can be moved or cancelled online when it does not exist in the database; the owner only has an email, the calendar hold was never made, and a future payload-vs-rules drift would go unnoticed because visitors see success.

**Suggestion.** Treat an email-only send as a degraded outcome: show a distinct message ('We got your details by email; we'll confirm by reply') and hide the account-tracking paragraph, and report the Firestore error somewhere the owner sees it (e.g. a 'Saved: no' line in the Formspree email).

### [HIGH] Spanish QR card prints the code over the caption
`referral.js:109` · bug · lens: account-availability-referral

downloadCard() places the caption at a fixed y and the code at a fixed H-140, but the Spanish caption wraps to two lines, so the 64px code is drawn on top of the second line. The English card is one line but its descenders touch the code.

**Evidence.** Lines 105-109: `var ty = oy + qs + 90; ... wrap(g, t("ref.qrCaption", ...), W / 2, ty, W - 260, 50); g.font = '800 64px ui-monospace...'; g.fillText(code.split("").join(" "), W / 2, H - 140);`. Clicked "Descargar tarjeta con QR" on account.html?lang=es in Chromium with Bricolage Grotesque loaded (document.fonts reported 700/800 loaded) and captured the canvas: fillText calls were "Escanea y recibe $25 de descuento en" (722px) at y=1065, "tu primera limpieza" at y=1115 and "K 7 M 2 Q X" at y=1120; the saved PNG shows the code overprinting "tu primera limpieza". In English the single line sits at y=1075 with its y/$ descenders touching the code's tops.

**Impact.** Every Spanish-speaking client who downloads their invite card gets an unreadable code line (the QR still scans); English cards look cramped. This is the artefact clients are meant to post and print.

**Suggestion.** Have wrap() return the baseline of its last line and place the code and domain relative to it (or enlarge the canvas), and keep the code below the caption by at least one line height in both languages.

### [HIGH] Marking a job done from the time clock skips referral credits
`timeclock.js:131` · bug · lens: admin-timeclock

The End button's "Mark {name}'s booking as done?" path writes status done and releases holds but never runs admin.js grants(), so the friend's welcome credit and the referrer's reward are never created. The card then shows both as "pending" with only a Reopen button, so the owner has no way to trigger them short of Reopen + Mark done.

**Evidence.** timeclock.js:131 `return B.updateRequest(r.id, { status: "done" }).then(function () { return B.releaseHolds(r.id); });`. Mock run as a crew member on a confirmed request with ref ABCDEF: writes were exactly `clockOut`, `updateRequest {status: done}`, `releaseHolds`; the Finished card reads "Welcome credit: pending · Referrer credit: pending | Reopen". The owner's Mark done button (admin.js:315-320) on the same request wrote addCredit welcome-r2, refWelcomeGranted, ownerOfCode, addCredit referral-r2, refRewardGranted.

**Impact.** Every booking finished by the crew from the time clock (the normal path once staff use it) silently breaks the refer-a-friend promise on refer.html; referrers never get their $25/$50 and nobody is warned.

**Suggestion.** Expose one finish(request) in admin.js (status + releaseHolds + grants) and call it from timeclock.js, or run grants from the watchRequests snapshot whenever a request with ref becomes confirmed/done and its flags are unset.

### [MEDIUM] Pay online button vanishes once a visit is marked Done
`account.js:123` · ux · lens: account-availability-referral

The pay link, even one pasted on that specific request, is shown only while the status is 'confirmed'.

**Evidence.** Line 123: `if (pay && s === "confirmed") actions.push('<a class="btn btn-sm" href="' + esc(pay) + '" target="_blank" rel="noopener">' + esc(t("acct.pay")) + "</a>");`. Rendered with a mock backend: the Done request #FFFFFF carrying `payLink: "https://buy.stripe.com/test_done"` showed only "Book this again", while the Confirmed one showed "Pay online". docs/SETUP-firebase.md section 6 says a link on a request wins "for that client" without mentioning the status limit.

**Impact.** If the owner collects after the visit (or marks Done before the client pays), the client has no way to pay from their account and payments are chased by text.

**Suggestion.** Show the button whenever a per-request payLink is set regardless of status, and the global link for confirmed and done visits.

### [MEDIUM] Rule rejections are reported as 'check your connection'
`account.js:177` · ux · lens: backend-wrapper

backend.js throws bare Errors and passes Firestore errors through untouched; account.js and admin.js map every failure to acct.failed / adm.failed ('That didn't save. Check your connection and try again.'). A permission-denied from the rules (the realistic case: the admin marked the request done or declined after the client's page loaded, so `resource.data.status in ['new','confirmed']` fails) is shown as a network problem.

**Evidence.** account.js:177 `.catch(function (e) { ... $("[data-acct-msg]", art).textContent = t("acct.failed"); })`; i18n.js:509 "That didn't save. Check your connection and try again."; admin.js:41 `function fail(e) { alert(t("adm.failed")); ... }`. firestore.rules:119 `isCustomerOf(resource.data) && resource.data.status in ['new', 'confirmed']` rejects a move/cancel once the status changed server-side, and account.js never re-reads the list after a failure (items stay stale). backend.js has no mapping of e.code ('permission-denied', 'unavailable', 'failed-precondition') to i18n keys at all.

**Impact.** Clients retry a 'connection' problem that will never succeed; the owner cannot tell a stale-rules deployment from an outage. Spanish copy has the same wording (i18n.js:1319, 1355).

**Suggestion.** Add a small mapper in backend.js (permission-denied -> 'this request has changed, reload'; unavailable -> connection; failed-precondition -> 'database needs an index/rules update') and reload the client's list on permission-denied.

### [MEDIUM] Previous user's requests and credit shown to the next sign-in
`account.js:244` · bug · lens: account-availability-referral

showAccount() reveals the list section before the new user's data arrives and never clears the old cards, balance or ledger; signOut resets the arrays without re-rendering. The list is also a one-time query, so owner confirmations never appear without a reload.

**Evidence.** Lines 243-251 call `show("list")`, set the email and then `loadRequests()`, with no `box.innerHTML = ""`; line 280 signOut sets `items = []; ... ledger = []` but does not call renderList/renderRef. Observed with a mock backend that answers myRequests after 800 ms: after signing out and signing in as other@example.com the header read other@example.com while nine cards for jane@example.com and "$35.5" credit stayed on screen until the new query resolved. backend.js line 336 uses `.get()`, not onSnapshot.

**Impact.** On a shared tablet or family computer the next person briefly sees someone else's bookings, phone-free but with addresses and prices; clients also act on stale statuses.

**Suggestion.** Clear the list, ledger and balance (or render a loading row) at the top of showAccount and in signOut; consider onSnapshot or a Refresh button for the requests.

### [MEDIUM] Sign-in tabs carry aria-pressed on role="tab" buttons
`account.js:287` · a11y · lens: account-availability-referral

setTab writes both aria-selected and aria-pressed on the two role="tab" buttons, and both tabs point aria-controls at the same form that has no tabpanel role.

**Evidence.** Line 287: `b.setAttribute("aria-selected", String(...)); b.setAttribute("aria-pressed", String(...));` on account.html lines 73-74 `<button ... role="tab" id="tab-signin" aria-controls="acct-pw" ...>`. axe-core 4.10 on account.html reports aria-allowed-attr (impact: critical) for #tab-signin and #tab-signup on both the sign-in and sign-up states.

**Impact.** Screen readers announce a toggle button and a tab at once for the first control on the page; the tablist has no panel to move to.

**Suggestion.** Remove aria-pressed (keep aria-selected) and add role="tabpanel" with aria-labelledby to #acct-pw, or drop the tab roles and use plain aria-pressed buttons.

### [MEDIUM] Switching language on the sign-in view erases status notices
`account.js:386` · i18n · lens: account-availability-referral

The language-change handler re-runs setTab without keepNotice, which clears the notice and field errors instead of translating them.

**Evidence.** Line 386: `if (view === "signin") setTab(tab);` and line 295 inside setTab: `if (!keepNotice) { clearErrors(); notice(""); }`. Observed: after "Forgot your password?" the page showed "If there's an account for jane@example.com, a link to choose a new password is on its way..."; clicking ES left [data-acct-notice] empty and hidden. The same happens to "Your email is confirmed. Sign in to continue." and "That link has expired" (lines 408, 413, 418), which arrive from Firebase emails in whatever language the link carried.

**Impact.** A Spanish speaker who lands from an English email and switches language loses the one sentence telling them what just happened.

**Suggestion.** Call setTab(tab, true) on mkg:langchange and keep the notice as a key plus vars so it can be re-rendered in the new language.

### [MEDIUM] Welcome credit granted again after a cancelled referred booking
`admin.js:128` · pricing · lens: admin-timeclock · votes 3/3 real (impact: low, refute: medium, reproduce: medium) · **confirmed**

isRepeat() only counts earlier requests that are confirmed or done, and nothing reverses a welcome credit when its booking is cancelled or declined. A referred client who books, gets confirmed (credit granted), cancels, and books again with the same link receives a second welcome credit and no "returning customer" warning.

**Evidence.** admin.js:128 `if (o.id === r.id || (o.status !== "done" && o.status !== "confirmed")) return false;` and :170-174 (grant on confirm). Mock run: Dan's cancelled request had ref ABCDEF, refWelcomeGranted true and a welcome-r4 ledger line; a new request r7 from the same email/phone with the same code showed "Welcome credit: pending" with no repeat warning, and Confirm wrote `addCredit welcome-r7 (+25)` -> balance $50. Also styles.css has no rule for the `.admin-warn` class used at admin.js:149, so the warning renders as plain text when it does fire.

**Impact.** Money leaks $25/$50 per confirm-then-cancel cycle, and the owner is not warned; the referral terms on refer.html speak of a first visit only.

**Suggestion.** Treat any earlier request from the same email/phone with refWelcomeGranted (any status) as repeat; grant the welcome credit only at Done, or write a reversing ledger line when a request with refWelcomeGranted is cancelled/declined; add a `.admin-warn` style.

### [MEDIUM] Live updates wipe the caret while staff type in a card
`admin.js:254` · ux · lens: admin-timeclock · votes 3/3 real (impact: low, refute: low, reproduce: medium) · **confirmed**
