Every snapshot from requests, internal, credits or team replaces the whole list with innerHTML. Drafts survive, but focus and the caret do not: keystrokes typed after a redraw go to the body and are lost. A colleague's Save causes two redraws (updateRequest + saveInternal).

**Evidence.** admin.js:254 `box.innerHTML = list.map(card).join("");` called from watchRequests (:531), watchCredits (:535), watchInternal (:539) and watchTeam (:447). Mock run: typed "We will arr" in the note textarea, emitted a credits snapshot -> document.activeElement became BODY; typing "ive at 8" left the textarea value at "We will arr". timeclock.js:254 does the same to the owner's shift-fix inputs on every clock-in/out by anyone.

**Impact.** Anyone writing a note to a client or private notes while a client moves a booking or a colleague works loses text mid-sentence; on a busy day this makes the edit panel unreliable.

**Suggestion.** Skip re-rendering a card whose edit panel contains document.activeElement (re-render it when it loses focus), or diff per card and restore focus/selectionStart after innerHTML.

### [MEDIUM] Apply-credit writes the ledger line and the request flag separately
`admin.js:308` · bug · lens: backend-wrapper · votes 3/3 real (impact: low, reproduce: medium, refute: low) · **confirmed**

'Apply credit' first writes a negative credits entry ('use-<id>') and then, in a second write, sets creditApplied/youPay on the request. If the second write fails, the client's balance is already reduced but the card shows no 'applied' marker and no Undo, and a retry with a smaller amount leaves the ledger and the request disagreeing.

**Evidence.** admin.js:308-309 `p = B.addCredit({ id: "use-" + id, email: r.emailLower, amount: -n, type: "used", requestId: id }).then(function () { return B.updateRequest(id, { creditApplied: n, youPay: ... }); });`. backend.js:485-491 addCredit is a get-then-set, not part of any batch; admin.js:155-164 creditBlock decides what to show from `r.creditApplied > 0`, and on retry addCredit returns false (entry exists) while updateRequest then records the new amount.

**Impact.** Money: a client's balance can be consumed without the discount being shown on their request, and the owner has to notice it in the Credits tab ledger to fix it.

**Suggestion.** Make addCredit accept an optional batch, or add backend.js applyCredit(requestId, email, amount, youPay) that writes the ledger line and the request patch in one db.batch() (rules allow both for admins).

### [MEDIUM] Confirm never checks the window is still open
`admin.js:317` · ux · lens: admin-timeclock · votes 3/3 real (impact: low, reproduce: medium, refute: low) · **confirmed**

Confirming (or reopening) a request writes its hold without looking at days/{date} (closed day, blocked window) or capacity, and the card shows no warning. The owner who closed a day for vacation and then works the New list confirms bookings onto that day.

**Evidence.** admin.js:317 `return B.ensureHold(Object.assign({}, r, { status: what }));` and backend.js:402-408 ensureHold only checks date/slot/status before writing holds/{id}; calendar.js dayInfo (closed/blocked/full) is available via `cal.dayInfo` but never consulted by card() (admin.js:186-250). Reasoned from code; the live calendar could only confirm the owner's current blocked days.

**Impact.** Overbooked windows (3 of 2 taken) or visits confirmed on days the team is off; the client gets "You're on the calendar" for a day nobody will come.

**Suggestion.** In card(), compute cal.dayInfo(r.date) for new requests and show a warning line when the day is closed, the window blocked or full; ask for confirmation in act() before writing.

### [MEDIUM] Calendar "Remove" frees the window but leaves the booking confirmed
`admin.js:407` · bug · lens: admin-timeclock · votes 3/3 real (impact: medium, refute: medium, reproduce: medium) · **confirmed**

Remove in the day panel only deletes the holds/{id} document. The request stays confirmed with its date and window, still appears in The week ahead and in the crew's "Unassigned today" with a Start button, while the public calendar now offers that window to new clients.

**Evidence.** admin.js:407 `p = B.deleteHold(b.getAttribute("data-hold"));` (backend.js:429 deletes one hold doc). Mock run: Remove on "Phone Pat (Confirmed)" wrote only `deleteHold r5`; afterwards request status = confirmed, week panel still lists "Phone Pat · Afternoon (3:00–6:00) · CONFIRMED", time clock still lists it under Unassigned today, and the window shows "0 of 2 taken". The confirm text (i18n adm.removeConfirm) says "Remove this booking from the calendar?" and nothing more.

**Impact.** A second client can book the same window while the crew still goes to the first job; or the owner believes a phone booking was cancelled when it was not.

**Suggestion.** For holds backed by a request, make Remove set the request to cancelled (cancelledBy: "admin") via the existing status path so holds, lists and the client's account agree; keep the bare hold delete only for legacy requestId == "manual".

### [MEDIUM] Deleting a "used" ledger line leaves the credit applied on the request
`admin.js:501` · pricing · lens: admin-timeclock · votes 3/3 real (impact: low, refute: medium, reproduce: medium) · **confirmed**

The Credits tab Delete button removes any ledger document, including the use-{id} line written by Apply credit, but does not reset creditApplied/youPay on the request. The balance comes back while the request (and the client's account page) still says the credit was applied, so it can be applied twice. Deleting a request likewise leaves its use-{id} line, so the client silently loses that balance.

**Evidence.** admin.js:501 `B.removeCredit(b.getAttribute("data-credit-del"))` with no request update. Mock run: Apply $25 on r2, then Delete the "Applied to a visit −$25" entry -> write `removeCredit use-r2EfGh` only; request still `creditApplied: 25, youPay: 125`, card still "Credit applied: $25. Client pays $125. Undo", bob's balance back to $25 and Apply offered again elsewhere. Separately, Delete request on r2 after applying credit wrote `deleteRequest` only and bob's ledger kept `use-r2EfGh: -25`.

**Impact.** Double-applied credit (owner loses money) or lost credit after a delete (client loses money), both without any warning.

**Suggestion.** For type "used" entries, route Delete to the same undo as the card (removeCredit + updateRequest creditApplied 0, youPay null) or disable it with a hint; in deleteRequest, also delete credits/use-{id} or confirm with the owner first.

### [MEDIUM] "7+" and "20,000+" steppers price and submit the cap, not the real size
`app.js:160` · pricing · lens: estimator-engine · votes 2/2 real (reproduce: medium, impact: low) · **confirmed**

At the top of the range the stepper displays "7+ bed" / "20,000+ sq ft", inviting larger homes and facilities, but the estimate, the fine print, the review and the request payload all carry exactly 7 / 20,000. The owner receives "20,000 sq ft" for a 45,000 sq ft warehouse with no flag that the cap was hit.

**Evidence.** app.js:160 `out.textContent = num(state[key]) + (state[key] === d.max ? "+" : "") + ...`; quote.js:395 `C.dims.forEach(function (d) { p[d.id] = s[d.id]; });` sends the clamped number. Observed in Chromium: dial shows `7+ bed` while dial.fine reads "Estimate for a 7-bedroom, 3-bathroom home."; commercial dial shows `20,000+ sq ft`, "$710 per visit, About $15,386 per month", fine print "Estimate for about 20,000 sq ft"; the quote link is `commercial-quote.html?plan=professional&freq=daily` with no size qualifier.

**Impact.** Large commercial prospects (the most valuable leads) get a knowingly low estimate and the owner cannot tell from the request or email whether the space is 20,000 or 60,000 sq ft; the same applies to 7+ bedroom homes.

**Suggestion.** Either drop the "+" and raise max, or when state equals max send `sqftAtMax: true` (and "20,000+ sq ft" in the email) and show "20,000 sq ft or more: we'll confirm on the walk-through" in the estimate box and review.

### [MEDIUM] Commercial plan cards show "About $1,717" with no "per month"
`app.js:223` · ux · lens: estimator-engine · votes 2/2 real (reproduce: medium, impact: medium) · **confirmed**

renderPlanCards prints the monthly figure as t("plans.monthly") + amount, so the cards read "About $1,266" / "About $1,717" / "About $2,384" with no unit, right under a "$198 per visit" line. The "plans.monthlyUnit" key ("per month" / "al mes") exists in i18n.js but is never used.

**Evidence.** app.js:223 `'<p class="plan-monthly" data-monthly hidden>' + esc(t("plans.monthly")) + ' <span data-price="' + p.id + '" data-price-part="perMonth"></span></p>'`. i18n.js:43-44 define `"plans.monthly": "About"` and `"plans.monthlyUnit": "per month"`; grep finds no other use of plans.monthlyUnit. Observed in Chromium on commercial.html and commercial-plans.html: card text `"monthly": "About $1,717"` (screenshot commercial-cards.png), Spanish `"Aprox. $1,717"`; the dial on the same page correctly says "About $1,717 per month".

**Impact.** Every commercial visitor sees an unlabelled second dollar figure on all three plan cards on both commercial pages; it reads as a different per-visit price or a mistake and undermines the "per visit" framing.

**Suggestion.** Append `' ' + esc(t("plans.monthlyUnit"))` after the perMonth span in renderPlanCards (or reuse the dial.perMonth string), in both languages.

### [MEDIUM] Nothing stops a window being overbooked: no capacity check at write time
`backend.js:163` · bug · lens: backend-wrapper

submitRequest writes the hold without reading how many holds the window already has, firestore.rules cannot count documents, and the quote form never re-reads availability before sending. Two visitors who opened the calendar minutes apart can both take the last spot, and the owner only sees it as '3 of 2' on the day panel.

**Evidence.** backend.js:163-165 `if (doc.date && doc.slot) { batch.set(holdRef(ref.id), { date: doc.date, slot: doc.slot, requestId: ref.id, createdAt: now, segment: ... }); }` with no prior read of holds. firestore.rules:150-156 checks only holdShape and mirrors(hid). calendar.js:65 `if (cache[k]) { apply(cache[k]); return Promise.resolve(); }` caches each month after one get; quote.js:468 re-checks only `bookable(draft.date)` (lead time and horizon) at submit, not capacity. admin.js:384 renders `cal.adminTaken` as '{taken} of {cap}', so an overbooked window reads '3 of 2' with no warning.

**Impact.** Two clients can hold the same crew window on the same day; the owner finds out on the day panel or on the day itself and has to move someone (a lost or annoyed booking). Rare at today's volume, but it grows with traffic and with the 60-day horizon.

**Suggestion.** In quote.js, call cal.refresh() (or B.availability(date, date)) right before submitRequest and refuse with a 'that window just filled' message when taken >= capacity; in admin.js highlight any window where taken > AV.capacity. Server-side enforcement would need a Cloud Function or a per-day counter document updated in the same batch and checked by the rules.

### [MEDIUM] isAdmin() turns any read error into 'not on the team' and signs the owner out
`backend.js:280` · bug · lens: backend-wrapper

isAdmin's catch returns null for every failure, including a Firestore 'client is offline' or a slow first connection. admin.js treats null as 'this account isn't on the admin list', shows that notice and calls signOut, so a flaky connection at page load logs the owner out with a misleading message.

**Evidence.** backend.js:272-280: `return ref.get().then(...).catch(function () { return null; });`. admin.js:577-579: `B.isAdmin(u).then(function (rec) { if (rec) {...} else { show("login"); $("[data-admin-notadmin]").hidden = false; B.signOut(); } });`. The author already avoided this pattern for the 2-minute recheck (backend.js:282-285, stillAdmin 'rejects (rather than answering no) when it can't be reached, so a dropped connection signs nobody out'), but the sign-in path does not get the same care. The web SDK rejects get() with 'Failed to get document because the client is offline' when it cannot reach the server and the doc is not cached.

**Impact.** The owner or a cleaner opening admin.html on a weak mobile connection is signed out and told they are not an admin; they may think they were removed from the team. Could only be fully confirmed against the live database, but the code path is unconditional.

**Suggestion.** Make isAdmin distinguish permission-denied/not-found (return null) from unavailable/network errors (reject), and have admin.js show 'couldn't reach the database, retry' without signing out on a rejection.

### [MEDIUM] watchRequests limit(300) silently drops older requests
`backend.js:378` · maintainability · lens: backend-wrapper

The admin stream is `orderBy('createdAt','desc').limit(300)`. Once the business passes 300 requests, older ones vanish from 'All', search, the day panel (names become '#ABC123'), the time-clock job picker, and from isRepeat(), which then grants 'new customer' referral credit to returning clients.

**Evidence.** backend.js:378 `db.collection("requests").orderBy("createdAt", "desc").limit(300).onSnapshot(...)`. admin.js:125-131 isRepeat() searches `items` (the same 300) for an earlier confirmed/done request with the same email or phone; admin.js:167-168 grants the welcome/referrer credits unless isRepeat(r). admin.js:367-368 nameFor() falls back to '#' + id when byId() misses; timeclock.js:46 jobName() likewise. Documents without createdAt (e.g. one typed into the console) are excluded by the orderBy entirely.

**Impact.** Money (unearned referral credits, $25-$50 each) and confusion once volume grows; nothing in the UI says the list is truncated. A recurring-clean business can reach 300 requests within a year or two.

**Suggestion.** Either paginate ('show older'), or keep the live stream for open statuses and fetch closed ones on demand; for isRepeat, query `requests.where('emailLower','==',...)` directly instead of relying on the loaded window. Show a 'showing newest 300' note when the snapshot hits the limit.

### [MEDIUM] Admin page only loads the 300 newest requests
`backend.js:378` · maintainability · lens: admin-timeclock

watchRequests is capped at 300 documents ordered by createdAt. Phone bookings are requests too, so a year of normal activity reaches the cap; older requests then vanish from All/search, holds show as "#ID", and shifts tied to them lose their job name and count $0 revenue in the time report.

**Evidence.** backend.js:378 `.orderBy("createdAt", "desc").limit(300).onSnapshot(...)`; admin.js:68 `all: items.length` labels the All tab from that list; admin.js:368 falls back to `"#" + hold.requestId.slice(0,6)` when byId() fails; timeclock.js:45-47 reqById/price return undefined/0 for unknown ids, and :173 `visitPrice` becomes 0.

**Impact.** Silent data loss in the owner's own tools after the business grows: search says "Nothing here" for a real client, and payroll/revenue figures drop.

**Suggestion.** Query open requests (status in new/confirmed) plus the last N months, and fetch by id any request referenced by a hold or shift that is not loaded; raise the limit meanwhile.

### [MEDIUM] Admin status change and hold release are two separate writes
`backend.js:396` · bug · lens: backend-wrapper

Declining, finishing, confirming or reopening a request updates the request first and then touches the hold in a second round-trip (releaseHolds / ensureHold). The rules only enforce request-hold consistency on the customer branch, so if the second write fails the request is declined but its window stays 'taken' on the public calendar (or confirmed with no hold).

**Evidence.** admin.js:315-317 `p = B.updateRequest(id, { status: what }).then(function () { if (CLOSED[what]) return B.releaseHolds(id); return B.ensureHold(Object.assign({}, r, { status: what })); })`. backend.js:396-400 releaseHolds and 402-408 ensureHold each start with their own holdsOf(...) query and commit their own batch. firestore.rules:118-121: holdMatches(id, ...) is only required inside the `isCustomerOf` branch; `isAdmin()` alone allows the request update. Same shape in timeclock.js:131. ensureHold also returns early when any legacy random-id hold exists (backend.js:405 `if (r.docs.length) return null;`) even if that hold has a stale date.

**Impact.** A declined or cancelled job can keep its window marked full for up to 60 days (lost bookings), or a confirmed job can be invisible on the calendar; the admin page does not show the mismatch.

**Suggestion.** Add a backend.js setStatus(req, status) that reads holdsOf once and commits the status update plus hold delete/set in one batch (the admin branch of the rules already allows it); have admin.js and timeclock.js call that instead of two steps.

### [MEDIUM] Admin calendar cannot show any earlier month
`calendar.js:33` · ux · lens: i18n-engine-calendar

In admin mode `first` is today, and canPrev only allows months after the month of `first`, so on the 1st of a month the owner loses the grid view of the previous month entirely even though past days inside the current month are shown and clickable.

**Evidence.** calendar.js:33 `var first = opts.admin ? today : D.addDays(today, AV.leadDays || 0);` and :81 `var canPrev = new Date(vy, vm, 1) > new Date(first.getFullYear(), first.getMonth(), 1);`. Observed (audit2.js): a calendar mounted with `{ admin: true }` on 2026-10-20 has `[data-cal-prev].disabled === true` while `[data-cal-day="2026-10-01"]` is enabled and shows "4 open".

**Impact.** The owner cannot review last month's bookings, closed days or phone bookings from the calendar during the first days of a month; the requests list is the only way back.

**Suggestion.** In admin mode let `first` be some months back (for example today minus 12 months) while keeping lead-day logic for visitors, or compute canPrev from a separate `minView` date.

### [MEDIUM] Availability calendar grid has no rows or gridcells
`calendar.js:92` · a11y · lens: account-availability-referral

The month grid is a role="grid" containing columnheaders and day buttons directly, with no row structure, so assistive tech cannot treat it as a grid.

**Evidence.** Lines 92-93: `'<div class="cal-grid" role="grid">' + wd.map(function (w) { return '<div class="cal-wd" role="columnheader">' ...` followed by `<button ... data-cal-day=...>` cells. axe-core on availability.html (1280 px, after picking a day): aria-required-children (critical) on .cal-grid and aria-required-parent (critical) on all seven .cal-wd; heading-order on .cal-month (h3 under an h1 with no h2 before it).

**Impact.** Screen-reader users on the public calendar, the quote step 5 and the account mover hear an invalid grid; the same markup is reused on four pages.

**Suggestion.** Wrap each seven-day run in role="row" and give cells role="gridcell" (or drop role="grid" and use a list of buttons), and make the month heading an h2.

### [MEDIUM] Calendar grid misuses ARIA grid roles, no arrow-key navigation
`calendar.js:92` · a11y · lens: i18n-engine-calendar

The grid is `role="grid"` containing `role="columnheader"` divs and plain <button>s directly, with no row or gridcell roles and no roving tabindex, so assistive tech gets an invalid grid and keyboard users must Tab through every bookable day (9 in the current month, about 25 in a full month).

**Evidence.** calendar.js:92-93 `'<div class="cal-grid" role="grid">' + wd.map(... role="columnheader" ...)` and :109 day buttons with no role. axe-core 4.10 on [data-calendar] of availability.html (audit.js S3): `aria-required-children` (critical) on `.cal-grid` and `aria-required-parent` (critical) on all 7 column headers. Keyboard: focus first enabled day, press ArrowRight -> activeElement unchanged (f0 === f1 === "2026-10-22"); `tabStopsInGrid` = 9 enabled days in October.

**Impact.** Screen-reader users hear a broken table structure; keyboard users cannot move by week or day as in a standard date picker.

**Suggestion.** Either drop role="grid"/columnheader and expose the days as a list of buttons with visually-hidden headers, or implement the WAI-ARIA grid pattern (rows, gridcells, one tab stop, arrow keys, Home/End, PageUp/PageDown).

### [MEDIUM] Reopened day stays "Closed" in the grid until page reload
`calendar.js:170` · bug · lens: i18n-engine-calendar

refresh() clears the month cache and holds but not the `days` map, and apply() only overwrites keys present in the new response. backend.setDay() deletes the days document when a day is reopened or its last blocked window is unblocked, so the deleted entry is never removed and the cell keeps rendering as closed/blocked.

**Evidence.** calendar.js:170 `refresh: function () { cache = {}; holds = []; return load(); }` and :73 `Object.keys(r.days).forEach(function (d) { days[d] = r.days[d]; });`; backend.js:431 `if (!data || (!data.closed && !(data.blockedSlots || []).length)) return db.collection("days").doc(date).delete();`. Reproduced in Chromium with a mocked backend (audit.js, scenario S4): a day loaded as `{closed:true}` had class `cal-cell is-closed`; after deleting it from the mock and awaiting `cal.refresh()`, the cell was still `cal-cell is-closed` and `cal.dayInfo(date).closed === true`. Same result in admin mode and for a blocked window (0800 stayed `blocked` after refresh).

**Impact.** The owner clicks "Reopen day" or unblocks a window in admin.html (admin.js:395-409 call cal.refresh() afterwards) and the grid still says Closed; the day panel disagrees, so they may toggle again and close the day for real. Clients loading the page fresh are unaffected.

**Suggestion.** Reset `days = {}` in refresh(), or in apply() delete every `days` key for the loaded month before merging `r.days`.

### [MEDIUM] Anonymous request+hold creation is unlimited while App Check is monitor-only
`firestore.rules:117` · security · lens: backend-wrapper

Any unauthenticated caller may create a request and its hold (`allow create: if isAdmin() || quoteFields(...)`), and App Check is still in monitor mode. A short script using the public config at /__/firebase/init.json can write `capacity` holds for every window for 60 days and the public calendar will show every day as full.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:117 `allow create: if isAdmin() || quoteFields(request.resource.data);` and 152-154 allow the hold when the request is created in the same write. plans.js:74 `capacity: 2`, so two scripted requests per window per day (about 4 windows x 2 x 52 days, ~400 batched writes) mark the whole horizon full. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:201-203: 'It starts in monitor-only mode ... check that nearly all recent requests are Verified, then Enforce.' admin.js act() (line 267) declines one card at a time; there is no bulk decline or hold purge.

**Impact.** A deliberate (or merely curious) script can make the calendar unbookable and bury the admin list in junk requests, each of which also triggers a Formspree email against the 50/month cap. Clean-up is one card at a time.

**Suggestion.** Finish the documented step: in the Firebase console confirm the App Check 'Verified' share and switch Cloud Firestore to Enforce (then re-test on the live site, since preview channels stop working). Separately, give admin.html a bulk 'decline all new before/after' or 'delete holds for day' action so a flood can be undone in one click.

### [MEDIUM] 24-hour change/cancel notice is enforced only in the browser
`firestore.rules:118` · security · lens: account-availability-referral

The customer update rule has no time condition, so the notice that terms.html relies on is just a hidden button; a stale page or a console call can move or cancel a confirmed visit inside the window.

**Evidence.** Lines 118-121: `allow update: if isAdmin() || (isCustomerOf(resource.data) && resource.data.status in ['new', 'confirmed'] && request.resource.data.updatedAt == request.time && (customerMoves(id) || customerCancels(id)));`. account.js line 36 `tooLate()` only decides whether to render the buttons, from a one-shot list (`B.myRequests`, line 238) in the visitor's local clock (line 33). A client whose page loaded while the request was still 'new' keeps the Cancel button after the owner confirms it. docs/SETUP-firebase.md says "Every rule above is enforced by firestore.rules, not just by the pages, so editing the site's JavaScript in a browser can't get around it".

**Impact.** The owner can lose a confirmed slot with no notice and no record that the policy was bypassed; the setup doc overstates what the rules guarantee.

**Suggestion.** Have admin.js stamp a `startsAt` timestamp when confirming and require in customerMoves/customerCancels that `resource.data.status == 'new' || request.time < resource.data.startsAt - duration.value(24, 'h')`; re-read the request before a move or cancel on the account page.

### [MEDIUM] Any team member can write the credit ledger
`firestore.rules:258` · security · lens: admin-timeclock

credits/{id} allows create, update and delete for every admin, while the page hides the Credits tab and its adjust/delete controls from non-owners. A crew member can still add credit to any email (including their own) or delete lines through the console or devtools, and the visible Apply credit button lets them change what a client pays.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:258 `allow write: if isAdmin();` versus admin.js:526 `ct.hidden = !isOwner()` (tab) and :164 Apply button rendered for everyone. Mock run as role admin: 0 Delete buttons, Credits tab hidden, but 1 "Apply to this visit" button present; nothing server-side distinguishes owner from crew for credits.

**Impact.** The ledger is money; a disgruntled or careless staff member can grant themselves or friends credit, and the owner only sees it if they read the ledger.

**Suggestion.** Restrict adjust entries and all deletes to isTeamOwner(); let admins create only welcome/referral/used entries whose id matches `{type}-{requestId}` and whose amount matches plans.js, or run grants only from the owner's session.

### [MEDIUM] Spanish day badge reads "1 libres" when one window is left
`i18n.js:1253` · i18n · lens: i18n-engine-calendar

`cal.open` is a single string with no plural form, so a day with exactly one open window shows "1 libres" in the cell, its aria-label and the admin grid; the string also never says what is counted (windows, not spots).

**Evidence.** i18n.js:1253 `"cal.open": "{n} libres"` used at calendar.js:101-102 `t("cal.open", { n: info.openCount })`. Reproduced in Chromium with a mocked backend (audit.js S4, ?lang=es): a residential day with two of three windows full rendered `<span class="cal-state">1 libres</span>` and `aria-label="jueves, 22 de octubre, 1 libres"`. The engine already has `plural()` (i18n.js:1680) and .one/.other keys for other strings.

**Impact.** Spanish-speaking visitors see ungrammatical copy on exactly the days that are nearly full, which is the state the yellow "is-few" styling draws attention to.

**Suggestion.** Add `cal.open.one` / `cal.open.other` ("1 ventana libre" / "{n} ventanas libres", "1 window open" / "{n} windows open") and render them through plural() with {n} substituted.

### [MEDIUM] Comparison table credits Professional with interior glass; its card does not
`plans.js:335` · content · lens: estimator-engine

The commercial comparison row "Interior glass, partitions, entry doors" has values [0, 2, 1], i.e. Professional = on rotation, while the Professional plan's includes list (and "Everything in Professional" on Complete) never mentions glass. The two renderings of the same plan on commercial-plans.html disagree.

**Evidence.** plans.js:335 `{ label: { en: "Interior glass, partitions, entry doors", ... }, values: [0, 2, 1] }` versus plans.js:302-308 Professional includes: Everything in Essentials / Desks... / Kitchen or breakroom... / Floors throughout... / Supply report... (no glass). Complete's includes at plans.js:318 list "Interior glass, partitions, and entry doors" as a Complete feature. Observed in Chromium on commercial-plans.html: comparison row renders `-RY`, the Professional card lists no glass item.

**Impact.** A Professional client who read the table expects glass and partitions cleaned on alternate visits; the crew working from the card's checklist will not do it, producing complaints or unpaid work. The other 11 commercial rows and all 12 residential rows agree with the cards.

**Suggestion.** Decide which is true and align them: change the row to `values: [0, 0, 1]`, or add "Interior glass and entry doors on rotation" to the Professional includes and its Spanish text.

### [MEDIUM] Phone is labelled Optional but required for the default contact method
`quote.html:190` · ux · lens: quote-flow

The phone label reads 'Phone Optional', yet 'Text' is the pre-checked way to reach you and validate() requires ten digits for Text or Call. Every visitor who trusts the label and leaves the phone blank is stopped with an error on their first Continue.

**Evidence.** quote.html:190 `<label for="phone"><span data-i18n="q.phone">Phone</span> <span class="muted" … data-i18n="q.optional">Optional</span></label>`; line 198 `<input type="radio" name="contactMethod" value="Text" checked>`; quote.js:296-297 `if ((method === "Text" || method === "Call") && value("phone").replace(/\D/g, "").length < 10) { setError("phone", t("err.phone." + method)); ok = false; }`. Observed on quote.html and commercial-quote.html in EN and ES: name and email filled, phone empty, Continue → 'Add a phone number so we can text you.' / 'Agrega un número de teléfono para poder enviarte un texto.'

**Impact.** A contradictory form on the step that collects the lead; a small but universal stumble that lowers completion, especially on phones.

**Suggestion.** Either default contactMethod to Email, or replace 'Optional' with 'Needed for text or call' and toggle the hint live when the method changes.

### [MEDIUM] Validation errors are invisible to screen readers
`quote.js:41` · a11y · lens: quote-flow

setError() only writes text into a <p> and toggles a CSS class. The error paragraphs have no aria-live/role, the inputs get no aria-invalid or aria-describedby, and focus stays on the Continue button, so an assistive-technology user who presses Continue hears nothing and does not know why the step did not advance.

**Evidence.** quote.js:37-42 `function setError(key, msg) { var el = $('[data-error="' + key + '"]'); if (el) el.textContent = msg || ""; var input = form.elements[key]; if (input && input.classList) input.classList.toggle("is-invalid", !!msg); }`; quote.html:91 `<p class="field-error" data-error="zip"></p>`. Observed after an empty Continue on step 1: #zip aria-invalid=null, aria-describedby=null, class 'input is-invalid', error <p> aria-live=null role=null, document.activeElement = BUTTON.btn.

**Impact.** Blind and low-vision visitors can get stuck on steps 1 and 4 with no feedback; the same applies in Spanish.

**Suggestion.** Give each field-error paragraph an id and role="alert" (or aria-live="polite"), set aria-invalid="true" and aria-describedby on the input in setError(), and move focus to the first invalid input when validate() fails.

### [MEDIUM] Invite code shown as applied without confirmation, then dropped silently
`quote.js:104` · bug · lens: quote-flow

refApplied() treats an unchecked code (refValid null, which checkReferral returns whenever the lookup fails or the backend is absent) as valid, so the visitor reads 'Invite applied: $25 off your first visit' and the review lists the credit. If Firestore then refuses the code (unknown, or the visitor's own code), the request is resent without it and the done screen says nothing about the credit being removed; admin.js never displays refDropped.

**Evidence.** quote.js:104 `return !!(INV && INV.enabled && friendAmount() > 0 && INV.valid(draft.ref) && draft.refValid !== false);` and 109-112 show q.ref.ok unless refValid is exactly false; backend.js:450 returns null on any get() error. Submit path 499-502: `var plain = Object.assign({}, data); delete plain.ref; delete plain.refAmount; plain.refDropped = data.ref; data = plain; return B.submitRequest(plain);` and 505 only shows q.done.refNote when data.ref survived. Observed locally: typing K7M2QX showed 'Invite applied: $25 off your first visit.' and the review row "Invite = Friend's code K7M2QX: $25 off your first visit" although no check could run. grep for refDropped in admin.js: no matches.

**Impact.** A new customer (or a client using their own code) expects $25/$50 off, the owner's screen shows no code at all, and the dispute surfaces at invoice time.

**Suggestion.** Only say 'applied' when refValid === true (show 'We'll check this code when we reply' otherwise), and when the retry drops the code, show a done-screen note that the invite could not be applied; render refDropped on the admin card.

### [MEDIUM] Local or preview copies email the owner; demo note never shows
`quote.js:495` · devops · lens: quote-flow

The 'not connected' demo confirmation only appears when formEndpoint is empty. With the Formspree endpoint set in plans.js, any copy of the site without Firebase (a developer's local server, a file copy) posts a real email to the business on every test submit and shows the ordinary success screen without the 'nothing was sent' note, contradicting the header comment in backend.js and the audit brief.

**Evidence.** quote.js:494-496 `if (!live) { if (!C.formEndpoint) { setTimeout(function () { done(true); }, 400); return; } return mailCopy(data).then(function (ok) { ok ? done(false) : fail(); }); }`. backend.js:7-9 says 'Opened from anywhere else … the quote form shows its demo confirmation.' Observed at http://127.0.0.1:8787/quote.html: with formspree.io blocked the visitor gets 'Couldn't send your request. Check your connection and try again or email mkgservices704@gmail.com.'; with it answering 200 the visitor gets 'Request sent' and the demo note stays hidden. The demo note only appeared after setting MKG.formEndpoint = '' in the console.

**Impact.** Test submissions from a dev machine consume the 50-email Formspree quota and land in the owner's inbox as real requests; anyone reviewing a local copy sees an error instead of the documented demo.

**Suggestion.** In the !live branch, only notify when the page is on the live host (backend.js already computes SITE) and otherwise show done(true); or add a 'demo' note whenever live is false regardless of formEndpoint.

### [MEDIUM] Firestore write has no timeout: 'Sending…' can hang forever
`quote.js:498` · ux · lens: quote-flow

The submit handler disables the button, shows 'Sending…' and waits on B.submitRequest() with no time limit. The Firestore SDK queues a write made while the connection is down and does not settle the promise until the server acknowledges it, so a visitor whose connection dropped after the page loaded (mobile, elevator, café Wi-Fi) sees 'Sending…' indefinitely; the 8-second race only guards the email copy.

**Evidence.** quote.js:475 `btn.disabled = true; btn.textContent = t("q.sending");` then 498 `return B.submitRequest(data).catch(…)` with no Promise.race, versus 459 `Promise.race([A.notify(mailFields(d, id)), new Promise(function (r) { setTimeout(function () { r(false); }, 8000); })])` for the email only. backend.js:166 `return batch.commit().then(function () { return ref.id; });` has no timeout either. Could only be reproduced against the live database; reasoned from the SDK's offline-queue behaviour.

**Impact.** Some visitors never see a result and either give up (lost request) or reload and resend (the draft is still in sessionStorage), which creates duplicate requests and holds once the connection returns.

**Suggestion.** Race the write against a timeout (e.g. 15 s) that re-enables the button with the existing err.submit copy, and show a small 'still trying' note; if the SDK later completes the write, the duplicate guard on reload should drop the date/slot or warn.

### [MEDIUM] A refused save still shows the visitor 'Request sent'
`quote.js:507` · ux · lens: backend-wrapper

When submitRequest is rejected (a rule mismatch after the hand-pasted rules drift, App Check enforcement scoring a real visitor low, or a transient Firestore error), quote.js silently falls back to the Formspree email and renders the normal confirmation. The request is not in the database, holds no window, never appears in the client's account, and the email does not say so.

**Evidence.** quote.js:507-511 `.catch(function (err) { ... if (C.formEndpoint) return mailCopy(data).then(function (ok) { ok ? done(false) : fail(); }); fail(); })` with done(false) rendering the same 'done' step and hiding the demo note. mailFields (quote.js:419-455) differs only by omitting the 'Reference' line when there is no id. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:75-77: past 50 emails a month Formspree holds them in an 'Over limit' folder, so in that state the request is lost entirely while the visitor sees success.

**Impact.** The owner may never see the request (nothing in admin.html, no reference number, possibly no email); the client waits for a reply that never comes and their account page shows nothing. Exactly the failure the README warns about (forgetting to re-paste firestore.rules) becomes invisible.

**Suggestion.** When the database write fails, add a loud line to the email ('NOT SAVED to the admin page, enter by hand: ' + err.code) and show the visitor a softer confirmation ('we got your details by email; no reference number yet'). Log err.code to console and consider a tiny 'errors' collection admins can see.

### [MEDIUM] Reload mid-flow returns to step 1 although the step is saved
`quote.js:595` · ux · lens: quote-flow

saveDraft() records the current step, but loadDraft() never reads it and init() always calls go(1), which immediately overwrites the saved step with 1. A visitor who refreshes (or whose mobile browser reloads the tab) on the When or Review step lands on the ZIP step and must click Continue up to five times to get back.

**Evidence.** quote.js:50 `… outOfArea: draft.outOfArea, step: current,` is written; loadDraft() (57-73) restores every field except step; line 595 `go(1);`. Observed: reloading on step 6 showed step 1 with ZIP/name/email filled, and sessionStorage draft.step changed from 6 to 1.

**Impact.** Friction right before sending; on phones, where a backgrounded tab is often reloaded, visitors may assume their work was lost and abandon.

**Suggestion.** After loadDraft(), call go(Number(d.step) || 1) when the saved step is within 1–6 and the earlier steps still validate, and stop saving the step inside go() when it equals the restored value.

### [MEDIUM] referral.enabled false leaves the program advertised
`referral.js:48` · maintainability · lens: account-availability-referral

fillAmounts() and amount() read the rewards regardless of enabled, and refer.html, the footer link and the terms section have no gate, so switching the program off only hides the banner, the quote field and the account panel.

**Evidence.** Lines 44-51: `function rewards(segment) { return (R.rewards || {})[segment || C.segment] ...` and `el.textContent = A.dollars(amount(...))` with no `R.enabled` check; line 182 `init()` runs `current()` unconditionally. Served plans.js with `enabled: false` into Chromium: refer.html still rendered "$25 / $25 / $50 / $50", "up to 100%" and the "Get my invite link" button, every page footer still links "Refer a friend", and index.html?ref=K7M2QX stored `{"code":"K7M2QX",...}` in localStorage. docs/SETUP-firebase.md line 107 says "set enabled: false to hide the program".

**Impact.** If the owner pauses the program as documented, the public page keeps promising credit that the quote form will not apply, and codes keep being collected for 60 days.

**Suggestion.** On refer.html replace the content with a paused notice when !MKG.invite.enabled and hide the nav/footer links, or state in the docs that the page must be unlinked; skip current() when disabled.

### [LOW] Past confirmed visits read "less than 24 hours away"
`account.js:36` · ux · lens: account-availability-referral

tooLate() has no upper bound and computes the start in the visitor's time zone, so a visit that already happened but is not yet marked Done shows the pre-visit notice.

**Evidence.** Line 36: `function tooLate(r) { var s = startsAt(r); return r.status === "confirmed" && s !== null && Date.now() > s - NOTICE_HOURS * 36e5; }` and line 33 `new Date(Number(p[0]), Number(p[1]) - 1, Number(p[2]), hh, mm)` in local time. Rendered with the mock: a confirmed commercial visit dated yesterday showed "This visit is less than 24 hours away. To change or cancel it, call or text (980) 319-0015."

**Impact.** Clients reading their account after the visit see a wrong sentence until the owner marks it Done; travellers see the cutoff shift by their UTC offset.

**Suggestion.** Use separate copy once the start time has passed (e.g. hide the actions or say the visit has taken place) and compute the start in America/New_York via Intl, or have the owner's confirmation stamp a startsAt timestamp.

### [LOW] Mover calendar counts the client's own hold as taken
`account.js:150` · ux · lens: account-availability-referral

The calendar opened by "Change the day or time" is given the request's current value but not told to ignore that request's hold, so the client's own window reads as partly or fully booked.

**Evidence.** Lines 150-151: `Cal.mount(el, { segment: r.segment || "residential", pickable: true, value: editing[id], ...` with no exclusion; calendar.js line 52 counts every hold `holds.filter(function (h) { return h.date === ymd && h.slot === s.id; }).length`. Observed with the mock: with the client's own hold alone the current window showed "Midday 11:30-2:30 1 of 2 open"; with one other hold it rendered selected, disabled and "Full" at the same time.

**Impact.** Confusing while choosing, and with capacity 1 (one crew) the client's current window always reads Full.

**Suggestion.** Add an ignoreRequestId option to calendar.mount and filter that hold out in dayInfo when the account page mounts the mover.

### [LOW] Account-created flash shows at the wrong sign-in
`account.js:318` · bug · lens: account-availability-referral

flashNext is set after signUpPassword resolves, but Firebase has already fired onAuthStateChanged and showAccount() by then, so the message is never shown at creation and surfaces on whoever signs in next on that page.

**Evidence.** Line 318: `B.signUpPassword(name, email, pass).then(function () { flashNext = t("acct.created", { addr: email }); })`; backend.js lines 197-201 still await updateProfile and sendEmailVerification after the user exists, and showAccount (line 250) consumes flashNext only when onUser runs. Observed with the mock: after sign-up the flash was hidden (only the verify banner showed); after signing out and signing in as verified@example.com the page displayed "Your account is ready. To see your requests here, confirm your email with the link we just sent to jane@example.com."

**Impact.** A verified client can be told to confirm someone else's email; the intended confirmation after sign-up is never seen (the verify banner covers it).

**Suggestion.** Set flashNext before calling signUpPassword (or rely on the verify banner alone) and reset flashNext in signOut.

### [LOW] Mistyped email on a cross-device link reads as "expired"
`account.js:353` · ux · lens: account-availability-referral

Every failure of completeLink, including Firebase's email-mismatch rejection, is reported as an expired or used link, and every Google failure as "didn't finish".

**Evidence.** Line 353: `B.completeLink(email).catch(function () { err("email2", t("acct.linkBad")); });` (line 418 does the same on the automatic path) and line 347: `err("google", authMessage(e) && t("acct.googleFailed"))`. With the mock rejecting with auth/invalid-email the page showed "That sign-in link has expired or was already used. Request a new one."; auth/user-disabled on password sign-in showed "Something went wrong. Check your connection and try again."

**Impact.** The person asks for a new link instead of correcting the address; disabled accounts are told to check their connection.

**Suggestion.** Route these through authMessage(e), reserve acct.linkBad for invalid/expired-action-code, and add a sentence for user-disabled and account-exists-with-different-credential.

### [LOW] Reset form can be replaced when the auth state arrives late
`account.js:380` · bug · lens: account-availability-referral

onUser only protects the reset view when no user is signed in; if a reset link is opened in a browser that is still signed in and the auth state resolves after checkReset, the account view replaces the password form.

**Evidence.** Lines 380-381: `if (view === "reset" && !u) return; if (u) { showAccount(); return; }`. Reproduced with the mock by delivering the auth state 200 ms after checkReset: the page landed on the list view with resetCode still set and the form hidden. The "Set or change my password" button (line 275) sends exactly this kind of link to a signed-in client.

**Impact.** Rare (auth usually restores from local storage first), but the client has to reopen the email link when it happens.

**Suggestion.** Return early whenever view === "reset" and let the reset submit handler sign the user in afterwards.

### [LOW] Tab title falls back to English on the Spanish admin page
`admin.js:29` · i18n · lens: admin-timeclock · votes 3/3 real (impact: low, reproduce: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

baseTitle is captured when admin.js runs, before i18n.apply() translates <title> at DOMContentLoaded, and counts() rewrites the title on every render, so signed-in Spanish users get an English tab title with the counter, and the English title stays after sign-out. (Related Spanish nit: adm.weekH "La semana que viene" means next calendar week, not "The week ahead" / next 7 days.)

**Evidence.** admin.js:29 `var baseTitle = document.title;` and :71 `document.title = (c.new ? "(" + c.new + ") " : "") + baseTitle;`, :550 `document.title = baseTitle`. i18n.js:1773-1775 defers apply() to DOMContentLoaded. Mock run with ?lang=es and auth resolving 800 ms after load: title "(2) Admin | MKG Services" while the H1 reads "Solicitudes y calendario"; after Sign out "Admin | MKG Services". Switching EN->ES while signed in gave the same.

**Impact.** Spanish-speaking staff see an untranslated tab; minor, but it is the one place the counter lives.

**Suggestion.** Use t("meta.title.admin") inside counts() and leave() instead of a cached string; retitle adm.weekH to "Los próximos 7 días".

### [LOW] Search ignores the # and phone formatting it advertises
`admin.js:58` · ux · lens: admin-timeclock · votes 3/3 real (impact: low, reproduce: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

matches() does a plain lowercase substring match on the raw fields, so the "#ID" shown on cards and any phone typed with different punctuation finds nothing, although the placeholder promises both.

**Evidence.** admin.js:58-59 `var hay = [r.name, r.email, r.phone, ...].join(" ").toLowerCase(); return query.split(/\s+/).every(function (w) { return !w || hay.indexOf(w) !== -1; });`. Mock run: "#R1ABCD" -> no results, "r1abcd" -> found; for a phone stored "(980) 555-0101", "980-555-0101" and "9805550101" -> no results, only "555-0101" matched. Placeholder (i18n adm.searchHint): "Search by name, email, phone, ZIP, or #ID".

**Impact.** Staff pasting the reference from a client's email or typing a number from caller ID conclude the request does not exist.

**Suggestion.** Strip a leading # from each word and, when a word is mostly digits, compare digits-only against digits(r.phone) and the id.

### [LOW] "Moved by client" note survives the owner's own date change
`admin.js:198` · ux · lens: admin-timeclock · votes 3/3 real (impact: low, reproduce: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

The badge and the "From X, changed Y. Confirm the new time" row are shown whenever a new request has movedByClientAt, and an owner-side reschedule never clears those fields, so after the owner moves the request to a third date the card still blames the client and cites the wrong origin.

**Evidence.** admin.js:198 `moved = s === "new" && r.movedByClientAt` and :111-114 movedText; backend.js:348-351 sets movedByClientAt/movedFrom only when byClient and never deletes them. Mock run: saved a new date/window on Eve's moved request -> writes `reschedule(r6, 2026-10-25, 1500, false)`; card then read "Sunday, October 25, Afternoon | MOVED BY CLIENT | From Wed, Oct 21, Midday, changed Oct 20, 2:34 PM. Confirm the new time...".

**Impact.** Confusing for whoever confirms next, and the client-facing history is wrong if it is ever surfaced.

**Suggestion.** In reschedule() with byClient false (and on confirm), write FieldValue.delete() for movedByClientAt and movedFrom.

### [LOW] dollars() prints "$124.5" and throws on undefined
`app.js:48` · bug · lens: estimator-engine · votes 2/2 real (reproduce: low, impact: low) · **confirmed**

dollars() is `"$" + n.toLocaleString("en-US")` with no fraction handling, so any non-integer amount renders with one decimal, and null/undefined throw a TypeError. Estimates are integers, but admin-entered quotedPrice is stored unrounded and credits/youPay are rounded to cents.

**Evidence.** app.js:48 `function dollars(n) { return "$" + n.toLocaleString("en-US"); }`. Harness output: `dollars(149.5): $149.5`, `dollars(1716.66): $1,716.66`, `dollars(undefined) THROWS: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'toLocaleString')`. admin.js:285 `patch.quotedPrice = Number(v)` (the input is type=number with inputmode="decimal" and is never validated for step), admin.js:117 `function rounded(n) { return Math.round(n * 100) / 100; }` feeds youPay and credit amounts.

**Impact.** A final price of 149.50 shows as "$149.5" on account.html and admin.html and in the credit ledger; a future caller passing a missing estimate crashes the render instead of showing a blank.

**Suggestion.** Use `Number(n).toLocaleString("en-US", { minimumFractionDigits: Number.isInteger(n) ? 0 : 2, maximumFractionDigits: 2 })` and return "" for non-finite input; optionally round quotedPrice on save.

### [LOW] Non-integer discounts and bad comparison values render wrong without warning
`app.js:50` · maintainability · lens: estimator-engine

The owner is told to edit the ladder discounts in plans.js, but pct() rounds to a whole percent, so a 12.5% discount is charged at 12.5% while every button and pill says "13% off"; similarly a comparison `values` entry outside 0-2 renders the literal text "undefined", and a mistyped followUp.defaultTier makes the quote page throw when the follow-up option is picked.

**Evidence.** app.js:50 `function pct(d) { return Math.round(d * 100) + "%"; }` (harness: `pct(0.125): 13%`); app.js:250 `row.values.map(function (v) { return "<td>" + marks[v] + "</td>"; })` indexes a 3-element array with no bounds check; quote.js:20 `recency: (C.followUp || {}).defaultTier` and quote.js:231 `var e = fuEstimate(draft.recency); ... A.dollars(e.perVisit)` where fuEstimate returns null for an unknown tier.

**Impact.** Any of these owner edits (README "Before launch" step 1) silently ships a wrong label, a stray "undefined" cell, or a dead follow-up option, and nothing in the console says so.

**Suggestion.** Format percentages with up to one decimal, guard marks[v] with a fallback, fall back to tiers[0] when defaultTier is unknown, and add a small startup sanity check that console.warns on these config errors.

### [LOW] load() keeps saved sizes that are off the stepper grid
`app.js:66` · maintainability · lens: estimator-engine

Saved dimension values are clamped to min/max but not snapped to `step`, so a value that is no longer on the grid (after the owner changes step or min in plans.js, which the README expects them to do) is displayed and stepped from as-is.

**Evidence.** app.js:66 `C.dims.forEach(function (d) { if (typeof saved[d.id] === "number") state[d.id] = clamp(saved[d.id], d); });` and app.js:61 clamp has no rounding to step. Harness: saved `{bathrooms: 2.5}` → state 2.5; Chromium shows the dial output "2.5 bath", fine print "3-bedroom, 2.5-bathroom home", and one "+" click gives "3.5 bath"; saved sqft 3100 steps 3350, 3600 ... instead of 3250, 3500.

**Impact.** Returning visitors after a config change see odd sizes and prices until they clear storage; the quote then sends the odd number to the owner. Not reachable from the UI today.

**Suggestion.** In clamp (or load) snap to the grid: `n = d.min + Math.round((n - d.min) / d.step) * d.step` before clamping.

### [LOW] Plan and frequency from a link are not saved, so the home page reverts
`app.js:72` · ux · lens: estimator-engine · votes 2/2 real (reproduce: low, impact: low) · **confirmed**

load() applies ?plan= and ?freq= to the in-memory state but never calls save(), so a visitor who arrives on quote.html?plan=essentials&freq=weekly and then opens the home or plans page sees the stored (or default) Family / every-2-weeks price instead of what they were just quoted. Any stepper click on the quote page does persist it, so the inconsistency is only for the no-interaction path.

**Evidence.** app.js:71-74 `var q = new URLSearchParams(location.search); if (plan(q.get("plan"))) state.plan = q.get("plan"); if (frequency(q.get("freq"))) state.frequency = q.get("freq");` with no save(). Observed in Chromium: on quote.html?plan=essentials&freq=weekly `state={plan:essentials,frequency:weekly}` while `localStorage.mkg.state` still holds family/biweekly; navigating to index.html then shows plan=family, freq=biweekly, $231.

**Impact.** Visitors coming from a plan-specific link (ads, the referral QR, a texted link) see one price on the quote page and a different plan and price when they look at the plans page before sending, which reads as the site changing its mind.

**Suggestion.** Call save() at the end of load() when a URL parameter changed the state (or persist on first render), so the choice from the link survives navigation.

### [LOW] Steppers have no accessible group name; quote page aria-labelledby is lost
`app.js:121` · a11y · lens: estimator-engine

buildSteppers turns the container into a bare div with two buttons and an output, without role="group" or a name, so the live output announces "3 bed" with no context and quote.html's aria-labelledby="beds-label" on the container has no role to attach to.

**Evidence.** app.js:121-125 `el.classList.add("stepper"); el.innerHTML = '<button ... aria-label="Fewer bedrooms">−</button><output class="stepper-value" aria-live="polite"></output><button ...>+</button>'` (no setAttribute("role")). quote.html:114 `<div data-stepper="bedrooms" aria-labelledby="beds-label"></div>`. Chromium evaluation of the step-2 steppers: `stepperRoles: ["null/beds-label", "null/baths-label"]`; rendered dial markup: `<div data-stepper="bedrooms" class="stepper"><button ... aria-label="Fewer bedrooms">` with no group role.

**Impact.** Screen-reader users on the dial and the quote form hear four unlabelled -/+ buttons and announcements like "3 bed" and "2 bath" that are not tied to a named control; the buttons' own labels mitigate but the group is unnamed.

**Suggestion.** In buildSteppers set `el.setAttribute("role", "group")` and, when the element has no aria-labelledby, `aria-label` from the existing stepper.more/fewer keys or a new stepper.name.<dim> key.

### [LOW] ?emulator=1 is honoured on the live site and skips App Check
`backend.js:53` · devops · lens: backend-wrapper

useEmu is true for any URL containing emulator=1, not only on localhost. On mkgservices.com that path loads /__/firebase/init.js?useEmulator=true from production Hosting, skips App Check activation and skips the authDomain override, so the page runs against the real database without the protections the file documents.

**Evidence.** backend.js:53-54 `var useEmu = /[?&]emulator=1/.test(location.search) || /^(localhost|127\.0\.0\.1)$/.test(location.hostname); var useAppCheck = SITE && !useEmu && !!C.appCheckKey;` and 63 `if (useEmu) return script(base + "init.js?useEmulator=true");`, bypassing line 68 `if (SITE) cfg.authDomain = location.hostname;`.

**Impact.** Harmless while App Check is monitor-only; once enforced, a visitor who lands on a link carrying ?emulator=1 (or who keeps it after a dev session) gets a calendar that reports 'not connected' and a quote form that cannot save. Also one more undocumented mode on the live domain.

**Suggestion.** Only honour ?emulator=1 when the hostname is localhost/127.0.0.1 (`useEmu = isLocal && (flag || true)`), or drop the flag entirely.

### [LOW] Formspree-only _subject is persisted on every request document
`backend.js:154` · maintainability · lens: backend-wrapper

submitRequest copies the whole payload into Firestore, including `_subject` (an email subject built for Formspree) and `submittedAt` (a client-clock ISO string that duplicates the server createdAt). The rules had to be widened to allow them, and nothing ever reads them back.

**Evidence.** quote.js:412 `p._subject = (C.segment === "commercial" ? "Commercial quote request: " : ...) + ... + " (ZIP " + p.zip + ")";` and 385 `submittedAt: new Date().toISOString()`; backend.js:154 `var doc = Object.assign({}, data, {...})` passes both through; firestore.rules:62 and 65 list 'submittedAt' and '_subject' in quoteFields. backend.js:103-108 STR/NUM clean lists do not include _subject, so no page uses it.

**Impact.** Noise in every record and a wider rule surface than needed; the next person reading the data model will wonder what _subject is.

**Suggestion.** Strip `_subject` (and `submittedAt`, or keep it as the client-clock audit field on purpose) in submitRequest before the batch and remove them from quoteFields.

### [LOW] Auth helpers throw synchronously when Firebase is absent
`backend.js:217` · bug · lens: backend-wrapper

Only ready/availability/submitRequest/onAuth/signOut/refreshUser/isLink/currentUser/checkReferral guard against db/auth being null; the other exported helpers dereference them directly and throw a TypeError instead of rejecting. One caller is reachable: the admin 'Forgot your password?' button sits outside the form that admin.js disables when offline.

**Evidence.** Observed on the local copy with Playwright (http://127.0.0.1:8787/admin.html): ready resolved false, `[data-admin-form] button` disabled, `[data-admin-forgot]` not disabled (admin.js:574 disables only the form's button and the Google button; the forgot button is outside the form at admin.html:80). Clicking it after typing an email produced `pageerror: Cannot set properties of null (setting 'languageCode')` from backend.js:217 `function sendReset(email) { auth.languageCode = lang(); ... }`, and the message area stayed blank. A sweep of the exports in the same page: signInPassword, signInGoogle, sendLink, sendReset, signUpPassword, sendVerification, isAdmin, myRequests, cancelRequest, ensureCode, watchTeam and clockIn all threw synchronously ('Cannot read properties of null').

**Impact.** Only the not-connected copy today (previews have Firebase), but it makes the wrapper's contract uneven: some failures are rejected promises, others are uncaught exceptions, so callers' .catch() handlers do not run.

**Suggestion.** Wrap each helper in `ready.then(ok => { if (!ok) throw new Error('no-backend'); ... })` (or a shared `need()` guard) so every export rejects consistently; disable the admin forgot button with the rest when !live.

### [LOW] Sign-in-link email lingers in localStorage unless the link is completed
`backend.js:250` · privacy · lens: backend-wrapper

sendLink stores the typed email under mkg.signInEmail and only completeLink removes it. If the person never opens the link, signs in another way, or signs out, the address stays in that browser's localStorage indefinitely.

**Evidence.** backend.js:249-250 `.then(function () { try { localStorage.setItem(LINK_KEY, email); } ... })`; the only removeItem is at 258 inside the success path of signInWithEmailLink. signOut (173) and the auth-state listener (239-242, which clears mkg.account) do not touch LINK_KEY.

**Impact.** On a shared or library computer the last client's email stays readable to the next user of that browser; minor, but it is personal data the privacy page does not mention.

**Suggestion.** Remove LINK_KEY in signOut() and in the onAuthStateChanged listener when a user is present, and store it with a timestamp so completeLink ignores entries older than the link's validity.

### [LOW] Accepted invites are never removed; an invite alone still grants admin
`backend.js:278` · security · lens: backend-wrapper

When an invited person first signs in, isAdmin() creates admins/{uid} but leaves adminInvites/{email} in place, and the rules treat an existing invite plus a verified email as admin. The in-app Remove deletes both, but deleting someone's admins document in the Firebase console (the way the setup doc says the owner's own record is managed) does not revoke their access.

**Evidence.** backend.js:275-278 `return db.collection("adminInvites").doc(user.email.toLowerCase()).get().then(function (inv) { ... return ref.set(rec).then(function () { return rec; }); });` with no delete of the invite. firestore.rules:39-40 `function invited() { return verified() && exists(.../adminInvites/$(myEmail())); } function isAdmin() { return hasAdminDoc() || invited(); }`. backend.js:310-315 removeAdmin deletes both docs, so only console-side removal is affected; stillAdmin (284-286) checks only admins/{uid}, so the person would be signed out of admin.html yet still pass every rule from a script.

**Impact.** A console-only removal (or a future script that prunes admins/) leaves a former staff member able to read and change every request.

**Suggestion.** Delete the invite in the same batch that creates the admins record (rules already let the owner delete invites; add `|| request.auth.uid == uid`-style allowance for the invitee), or make invited() also require that no admins doc has ever existed. Document that removal must be done from the Team list.

### [LOW] 'Any window' bookings never hold capacity, even once confirmed
`backend.js:403` · ux · lens: backend-wrapper

A request with a date but no window gets no hold at creation, and ensureHold does nothing on confirm unless a slot is set. The job is real and confirmed but the public calendar still shows that day fully open, so other visitors can fill every window and leave no room for it.

**Evidence.** backend.js:163 `if (doc.date && doc.slot)` and 403 `if (!req.date || !req.slot || !OPEN[req.status || "new"]) return Promise.resolve();`. firestore.rules:99 forbids a hold for such a request on the customer branch (`(d.date == '' || d.slot == '') && !existsAfter(holdPath(id))`). calendar.js:52 counts holds per slot only. The only place these jobs appear is the admin 'week ahead' panel (admin.js:339-342).

**Impact.** The owner confirms a flexible-time job, forgets to assign a window in the edit panel, and the day overbooks around it.

**Suggestion.** When an admin confirms a request that has a date but no slot, require (or default to) picking a window in the same action, or write a hold whose slot the admin chooses; alternatively count 'any window' holds against the day's total in calendar.js.

### [LOW] Lead time and horizon follow the visitor's clock, not Charlotte's
`calendar.js:32` · bug · lens: i18n-engine-calendar

today, the first bookable day (leadDays) and the horizon are computed from `new Date()` in the visitor's browser time zone; quote.js bookable() does the same, so an out-of-zone visitor can request a day that is already inside the lead time in Charlotte.

**Evidence.** calendar.js:32 `var today = new Date(); today = new Date(today.getFullYear(), ...)` and :33-34 addDays(today, leadDays/horizonDays). Observed with Playwright `page.clock.setFixedTime("2026-10-21T04:30:00Z")` (00:30 in Charlotte): timezoneId America/New_York -> first enabled day 2026-10-23; America/Los_Angeles -> first enabled day 2026-10-22 and today highlighted as 2026-10-20. quote.js:34 uses the same `new Date()` so the request is accepted.

**Impact.** A relative booking from the west coast, or the owner travelling, sees a different lead-time cut-off (up to one day earlier) than the business intends; low frequency for a local service.

**Suggestion.** Derive today from the business time zone (for example Intl.DateTimeFormat with timeZone "America/New_York" formatToParts) in one shared helper used by calendar.js and quote.js.

### [LOW] Hold segment is stored but never read; capacity is shared across lines
`calendar.js:52` · maintainability · lens: backend-wrapper

Every hold carries a segment (residential/commercial) and the admin 'add booking' picks one, but no reader uses it: capacity counting is per window regardless of segment. If that is intended (one crew pool), the field is dead weight; if commercial and residential are meant to have separate capacity, the calendar is wrong.

**Evidence.** backend.js:164, 355, 406, 416 all write `segment: ... || "residential"`. calendar.js:52 `var taken = holds.filter(function (h) { return h.date === ymd && h.slot === s.id; }).length;` and admin.js:382 `r.holds.filter(function (x) { return x.slot === s.id; })` ignore it; `grep -n 'h\.segment\|hold\.segment\|x\.segment' *.js` returns nothing. plans.js:69-71 documents capacity as 'how many jobs can start in the same window on one day'.

**Impact.** Confusing for the next developer and for the owner reading the data; admin.js:403 even derives a segment for phone bookings purely to store it.

**Suggestion.** Decide: either filter by segment in dayInfo()/renderDay() with a per-segment capacity in plans.js, or drop the field from the writes and from holdShape in the rules.

### [LOW] Start-window times are raw strings, untranslated and without am/pm
`calendar.js:150` · content · lens: i18n-engine-calendar

The window times from plans.js are rendered verbatim with esc(), never through L(), so they cannot be bilingual; three of the four read "8:00–11:00", "11:30–2:30", "3:00–6:00" with no am/pm while the fourth says "6:30–9:30 pm", and Spanish readers see the English "pm".

**Evidence.** calendar.js:150 `'<span class="cal-slot-time">' + esc(w.slot.time || "") + '</span>'` and :178 slotLabel appends `.time` the same way; plans.js:79-82 define `time: "8:00–11:00"`, `"11:30–2:30"`, `"3:00–6:00"`, `"6:30–9:30 pm"`. Observed on availability.html?lang=es: slot times "8:00–11:00", "11:30–2:30", "3:00–6:00" and the side list "Fuera de horario 6:30–9:30 pm" (screenshot avail-es-mock.png).

**Impact.** "11:30–2:30" is ambiguous at a glance and the inconsistency with the after-hours window looks careless; Spanish visitors get a mixed-language label. The same strings go into the owner's emails and the client's summary.

**Suggestion.** Store `time` as { en, es } ("8:00–11:00 am" / "8:00–11:00 a. m.") and render it through L(), or build the label from start/end hours with toLocaleTimeString in the current locale.

### [LOW] Every calendar mount adds a permanent langchange listener
`calendar.js:168` · perf · lens: i18n-engine-calendar

mount() registers `document.addEventListener("mkg:langchange", render)` and never removes it; account.js re-mounts a calendar inside every request card on each renderList(), which itself runs on every language switch and after each move or cancel, so detached calendars accumulate and re-render on every toggle.

**Evidence.** calendar.js:168 `document.addEventListener("mkg:langchange", render);` with no unmount API (:169-175). account.js:147-150 `$all("[data-acct-cal]", box).forEach(... Cal.mount(el, ...)` inside renderList(), called again from the langchange handler at account.js:384-385. Observed (audit2.js): counting registrations with a patched addEventListener, 3 mounts into the same replaced box raised the count from 3 to 6; none were released.

**Impact.** A signed-in client who toggles the language a few times or moves several visits leaks DOM trees and does redundant work; not visible today, but it grows with the number of requests and switches.

**Suggestion.** Return a destroy()/unmount in the mount API (remove the listener) and have account.js call it before re-rendering, or attach the listener once per page and look the live instance up through the root element.

### [LOW] Pricing PDF generator rounds .5 differently from the site
`docs/make-pricing-pdf.py:87` · maintainability · lens: estimator-engine

The PDF says prices are "rounded to the nearest dollar" and computes them with Python's round(), which rounds exact halves to the even dollar, while app.js uses Math.round, which rounds halves up. Across the full size grid 984 plan/size/frequency cells land on an exact .5 and differ by $1. Today's printed examples happen not to hit one.

**Evidence.** docs/make-pricing-pdf.py:87 `return one, round(one * (1 - discount))` versus app.js:36 `var perVisit = Math.round(oneTime * (1 - discount));`. Harness: `residential family {bedrooms:1,bathrooms:4} biweekly: x=202.5 js=203 py=202`, `complete {2,2} biweekly: x=238.5 js=239 py=238` (984 differences in total). The PDF's worked examples (3bd/2ba Family $195; 3,000 sq ft Professional $198) match the site.

**Impact.** When the owner replaces the placeholder rates and regenerates the PDF, a printed example or hand calculation can disagree with the site by $1, which is exactly the kind of discrepancy a client will point at.

**Suggestion.** Round half up in the generator (`math.floor(x + 0.5)`), and say "halves round up" in the PDF's formula line.

### [LOW] Spanish follow-up note says "recámaras" while the rest says "habitaciones"
`i18n.js:1000` · i18n · lens: estimator-engine

The follow-up estimate note is the only Spanish string that calls bedrooms "recámaras"; the dial, steppers, review and unit strings all use "habitaciones". Both are correct Spanish but a reader sees two words for the same thing on one page.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:1000 `"q.fu.note": "Según una casa de {bedrooms} recámaras y {bathrooms} baños..."` versus i18n.js:881 `"dial.fine": "Estimado para una casa de {bedrooms} habitaciones y {bathrooms} baños."` and i18n.js:895-896 `"unit.bedroom.*": "habitación/habitaciones"`. grep: 1 occurrence of "recámara", 9 of "habitaci".

**Impact.** Minor inconsistency for Spanish-speaking visitors on the quote page's follow-up option.

**Suggestion.** Change "recámaras" to "habitaciones" in q.fu.note.

### [LOW] Spanish follow-up note says 'recámaras' where the site says 'habitaciones'
`i18n.js:1000` · i18n · lens: quote-flow

The follow-up estimate sentence uses 'recámaras' for bedrooms while every other Spanish string on the quote page (steppers, step-3 intro, review, unit labels) uses 'habitaciones', so the same screen shows two words for the same thing.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:1000 `"q.fu.note": "Según una casa de {bedrooms} recámaras y {bathrooms} baños. …"` versus 1029 `"q.s3.p": "Los precios son estimados para una casa de {bedrooms} habitaciones y {bathrooms} baños…"` and 895-896 `"unit.bedroom.one": "habitación"`. Observed on quote.html?lang=es, step 3 with the follow-up option: 'Según una casa de 3 recámaras y 2 baños.' directly under 'estimados para una casa de 3 habitaciones y 2 baños'.

**Impact.** Minor inconsistency that reads as a different author on the same step; 'recámaras' is also regional (Mexico) while the rest of the copy is neutral.

**Suggestion.** Change 'recámaras' to 'habitaciones' in q.fu.note (ES).

### [LOW] ?lang= is case-sensitive and ignores navigator.languages
`i18n.js:1649` · i18n · lens: i18n-engine-calendar

Only the exact values "en" and "es" are honoured in the URL; "?lang=EN" or "?lang=es-MX" is silently ignored and the stored or browser default wins. Detection also reads navigator.language alone, not the navigator.languages list.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:1649 `if (LANGS.indexOf(q) !== -1) return q;` and :1651-1652 `var nav = (navigator.language || "").toLowerCase(); return nav.indexOf("es") === 0 ? "es" : "en";`. Observed (audit.js S1): after a Spanish visit, loading index.html?lang=EN kept `html lang="es"` and the Spanish title; ?lang=fr fell back to English as expected.

**Impact.** A hand-typed or copied link with a different case does not switch the language; a visitor whose first browser language is English but who lists Spanish second is never offered Spanish by default.

**Suggestion.** Normalise the query value with `.toLowerCase().slice(0, 2)` before the LANGS check, and scan `navigator.languages || [navigator.language]` for the first supported prefix.

### [LOW] t() substitutes variables unescaped; safety relies on each caller
`i18n.js:1671` · maintainability · lens: i18n-engine-calendar

Values passed to t(key, vars) are inserted verbatim, so any string that reaches innerHTML must be escaped by the caller. Every current call site does so (quote.js:525 and referral.js:171 wrap with esc(); the rest use textContent), but the rule is undocumented and the live-placeholder path in fill() behaves differently (escapes first).

**Evidence.** i18n.js:1671 `if (vars) s = s.replace(/\{(\w+)\}/g, function (m, k) { return vars[k] !== undefined ? vars[k] : m; });`. Verified in Chromium: `MKG.i18n.t("cal.open", { n: "<b>x</b>" })` returns "<b>x</b> libres" and `t("cal.open", { n: null })` returns "null libres". The header comment (i18n.js:1-16) documents the LIVE placeholders but not this contract.

**Impact.** The first future `el.innerHTML = t("...", { name: r.name })` with a client-supplied name becomes a stored XSS in admin.html; today nothing is exploitable.

**Suggestion.** Document the contract in the header comment, or add an escaping variant (for example `t.html(key, vars)` that escapes vars) and use it wherever the result goes into innerHTML.

### [LOW] plural() cannot substitute variables, so callers bypass it
`i18n.js:1680` · maintainability · lens: i18n-engine-calendar

plural(keyBase, n) calls t() without a vars argument, so a plural string containing {n} comes back with the raw placeholder; the one such family (ref.friends.*) is therefore rendered by hand-building the key in account.js.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:1680 `function plural(keyBase, n) { return t(keyBase + (n === 1 ? ".one" : ".other")); }`; i18n.js:763 `"ref.friends.one": "{n} friend has booked"`; account.js:216 `t("ref.friends" + (friends === 1 ? ".one" : ".other"), { n: friends })`. Verified: `t("cal.open")` without vars returns "{n} libres" (audit.js S2 `tNoVar`).

**Impact.** The next developer who uses plural() for a counted string will ship a visible "{n}"; the plural rule is also duplicated in two places.

**Suggestion.** Change the signature to plural(keyBase, n, vars) that merges `{ n: n }` into vars, and use it at account.js:216 and for cal.open.

### [LOW] Review list puts buttons inside a <dl> and names them all 'Change'
`quote.js:345` · a11y · lens: quote-flow

Each review row is a <div> inside <dl> that contains a <button> beside the dt/dd, which is invalid definition-list content (axe 'definition-list', serious), and all six buttons have the identical accessible name 'Change', so a screen-reader user cannot tell which row a button edits.

**Evidence.** quote.js:344-345 `'<div class="review-row"><dt>' + A.esc(r[0]) + "</dt><dd>" + A.esc(r[1]) + "</dd>" + (editable ? '<button type="button" data-goto="' + r[2] + '">' + A.esc(t("q.change")) + "</button>" : "")`. axe on step 6 and on the done screen: {"id":"definition-list","impact":"serious","nodes":["dl[data-review]"]}; accessible names of the six buttons: Change,Change,Change,Change,Change,Change.

**Impact.** Assistive-technology users get an unlabelled list of identical buttons on the step where they confirm what is being sent.

**Suggestion.** Render the review as a <ul>/<table> or keep the <dl> and move the button into the <dd>; add aria-label="Change " + row label to each button.

### [LOW] Formspree-only _subject field is written into every Firestore request
`quote.js:412` · maintainability · lens: estimator-engine

payload() sets p._subject (a Formspree directive) and that object is what backend.submitRequest stores, so every request document carries an unused `_subject`; the email path builds its own subject in mailFields, so the stored one is dead data that the rules had to whitelist.

**Evidence.** quote.js:412 `p._subject = (C.segment === "commercial" ? "Commercial quote request: " : "Quote request: ") + ...`; quote.js:499 `B.submitRequest(data)` passes the same object; quote.js:423 mailFields sets its own `_subject`; firestore.rules:65 lists `'_subject'` among the allowed request keys.

**Impact.** Harmless to visitors but it bloats each record, confuses the data model in docs/SETUP-firebase.md, and widens the write rule for no reason.

**Suggestion.** Drop the `_subject` line from payload() and remove it from the allowed keys in firestore.rules.

### [LOW] Owner email says "after hourss" for commercial time-of-day preferences
`quote.js:436` · bug · lens: estimator-engine

mailFields pluralises the timeOfDay value by appending "s", which works for the residential values (Morning, Afternoon) but produces "after hourss", "before openings" and "during hourss" for the commercial values.

**Evidence.** quote.js:436 `d.timeOfDay && d.timeOfDay !== "Either" ? d.timeOfDay.toLowerCase() + "s" : ""`; commercial-quote.html:246-248 values are "After hours", "Before opening", "During hours". Captured Formspree body in Chromium: `"Day and window": "None picked yet. Best for them: after hourss"`.

**Impact.** Every commercial request without a picked date sends the owner a garbled line in the notification email; the review screen uses the proper i18n key (review.time.*) so only the email is affected.

**Suggestion.** Use the existing English strings: `I.dictionaries.en["review.time." + d.timeOfDay]` (with the commercial: namespace for Either) instead of appending "s".

### [LOW] 8-second email race causes false failures on the fallback path
`quote.js:459` · bug · lens: quote-flow

When the request goes by email only (no database), a Formspree response slower than 8 seconds is treated as failure even though the email is delivered, and the visitor is told to try again, which sends a second email (and, on the live site, a second request if Firestore had failed transiently).

**Evidence.** quote.js:459 `return Promise.race([A.notify(mailFields(d, id)), new Promise(function (r) { setTimeout(function () { r(false); }, 8000); })]);` used at 496 and 510 with `ok ? done(false) : fail()`. Observed with formspree.io answering 200 after 10 s: 8,894 ms later the form showed 'Couldn't send your request. Check your connection and try again…', clicking Send again produced a second POST (2 calls counted).

**Impact.** Duplicate emails toward the 50-per-month Formspree quota and confused visitors, only when the database is unavailable.

**Suggestion.** On the fallback path let the notify request finish without a race (or use 20 s), and remember a sent flag so a retry does not post the same request twice.

### [LOW] Window capacity is not re-checked at submit; month cache goes stale
`quote.js:468` · ux · lens: quote-flow

The submit guard only checks that the picked day is still inside the lead/horizon range.
