The calendar's holds are cached per month when step 5 is first shown and never refreshed, and firestore.rules does not count holds, so two visitors who both saw '1 of 2 open' can both hold the last spot, and a tab left open can request a window that filled up meanwhile.

**Evidence.** quote.js:468 `if (draft.date && !bookable(draft.date)) {` with bookable() at 32-36 comparing dates only; calendar.js:65 `if (cache[k]) { apply(cache[k]); return Promise.resolve(); }` and no refresh() call anywhere in quote.js. Observed: after stubbing availability to report the chosen window as full (2 holds), Send still produced a payload with date 2026-10-22 and slot 0800 and no error.

**Impact.** Over-requested windows the owner must sort out by hand; visitors may be declined for a window the site told them was open.

**Suggestion.** Before sending, call cal.refresh() and check cal.dayInfo(draft.date) for the chosen slot's `left > 0`; if full, show the existing q.dateStale-style message and clear the slot.

### [LOW] Request reference is never shown: no [data-request-id] element
`quote.js:504` · ux · lens: quote-flow

After a successful save the code fills every [data-request-id] with the six-character reference that the owner's email ('Reference: …') and the account page ('#ABC123') use, but neither quote page contains such an element, so the visitor never sees the reference.

**Evidence.** quote.js:504 `$all("[data-request-id]").forEach(function (el) { el.textContent = id.slice(0, 6).toUpperCase(); });`; grep -rn 'data-request-id' *.html returns nothing; mailFields() line 453 `put("Reference", id ? id.slice(0, 6).toUpperCase() : "")` and account.js:117 show the same reference elsewhere.

**Impact.** A visitor who calls or texts about their request has nothing to quote; the owner has to search by name or email.

**Suggestion.** Add `<p>Reference <b data-request-id></b></p>` to the done section of quote.html and commercial-quote.html (hidden when empty).

### [LOW] Date and slot from the URL are not checked against the calendar
`quote.js:572` · bug · lens: quote-flow

A ?date= and ?slot= pair is accepted after only a format check on the date and a lead/horizon check; the slot is not matched to the segment's windows and the day is not checked for closed/weekend status. The residential page accepts the commercial-only 1830 window, a Sunday is carried through to the payload, and a long slot string exceeds the rules' 10-character limit so the write is refused and the email-only fallback runs.

**Evidence.** quote.js:572 `if (/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(q.get("date") || "")) { draft.date = q.get("date"); draft.slot = q.get("slot") || ""; }` and 32-36 bookable() only compares against leadDays/horizonDays. Observed: quote.html?date=2026-10-25&slot=0800 (Sunday; the calendar cell is 'is-closed … disabled') reviewed as 'Timing = Sunday, October 25, Morning (8:00–11:00)' and the captured payload had date 2026-10-25; ?slot=1830 on quote.html showed 'After hours (6:30–9:30 pm)' with no window pressed in the calendar; ?slot=whatever-this-is-long produced payload slot of 21 characters, which firestore.rules:70 `str(d.slot, 10)` rejects.

**Impact.** Hand-edited or stale links (shared availability links after plans.js changes) create requests for days the business does not work or windows it does not offer; the over-long case triggers the misleading success screen.

**Suggestion.** After reading the URL, keep the slot only if it is one of C.availability.slots for this segment, and drop the date if the calendar's dayInfo(date) reports closed or no open windows (the calendar module already exposes dayInfo).

### [LOW] Share message always quotes the home amount
`referral.js:63` · content · lens: account-availability-referral

The text, email and QR-card wording hardcode the residential friend reward even though the panel above lists a different business amount.

**Evidence.** Lines 63-64: `function message(code) { return t("ref.shareMsg", { amount: A.dollars(amount("friend", "residential")), ... }` and `subject()` likewise; line 107 draws the card caption with `amount("friend", "residential")`. On the rendered panel the sms: and mailto: hrefs say "$25 off your first visit" while the list beside them says "Businesses: your friend gets $50 off".

**Impact.** A business client inviting another business promises half the real reward; a card scanned by a business says $25.

**Suggestion.** Mention both amounts in ref.shareMsg/ref.qrCaption or pick the segment of the referrer's last request.

### [LOW] Seconds-long shifts count as full-price visits in the report
`timeclock.js:185` · bug · lens: admin-timeclock

A visit is any shift with a requestId on a day, regardless of length, so a mis-tap (Start then End), or a fixed shift whose clock-out precedes clock-in (clamped to 0 h), adds one visit and the job's full price to the totals and inflates hours-per-visit.

**Evidence.** timeclock.js:167-171 registers `visits[v]` for every span; :185 `revenue: vids.reduce(function (a, v) { return a + visitPrice(v); }, 0)`. Node run of _computeStats (scratch pure.js): a shift with outAt < inAt gave 0 h but visits=1 and added $100 to totals.revenue (400 instead of 300). Browser run: an 8-second shift on an $80 job produced "Total: 7.0 hours, 3 visits, $437 of work".

**Impact.** The owner's $ per labor hour and visit counts are overstated whenever a cleaner taps Start by mistake, which is common on phones.

**Suggestion.** Ignore visits under a few minutes in totals (or weight price by hours), and refuse Save when out < in instead of clamping (the alert at :273 already exists for the typed case; cover inAt edits too).

### [LOW] Payroll CSV: open shifts get hours, times are localized, no zone
`timeclock.js:283` · ux · lens: admin-timeclock

The export writes elapsed hours for shifts still running (blank clock-out), formats times in the UI language, dates as en-US, with no timezone or ISO column, and rounds hours to 0.1 h; a shift past midnight shows only a clock-out time.

**Evidence.** timeclock.js:283 `[s.email, new Date(ms(s.inAt)).toLocaleDateString("en-US"), fmtTime(s.inAt), s.outAt ? fmtTime(s.outAt) : "", fmtH(hours(s)), ...]`. Downloaded CSV in the mock run: `"owner@mkg.com","10/20/2026","1:34 PM","","3.0",...` for an open shift; with ?lang=es the same rows read `"2:34 p.m."`. Formula guard works (`'=HYPERLINK...`), quotes are doubled.

**Impact.** Payroll can pay for a shift that has not ended, and a spreadsheet or accountant in another zone cannot reconstruct exact times; 0.1 h rounding loses up to 3 minutes per shift.

**Suggestion.** Export ISO 8601 timestamps (or date, time and zone columns) in a fixed locale, leave hours blank or write "open" for unfinished shifts, and give minutes or two-decimal hours.

### What this area found well done

- One formula, everywhere: estimate() in app.js is the only place prices are computed and index.html, plans.html, the quote step-3 options, the estimate box, the review, the Formspree payload and docs/make-pricing-pdf.py all read plans.js; a Chromium walk-through (4bd/3ba Family, every 2 weeks) produced the same $231 / $257 / 10% on the dial, the plan cards, the quote page, the review and the captured email body, and the PDF's worked examples match the site. _(estimator-engine)_
- load() is genuinely defensive: fifteen hostile localStorage cases in the no-DOM harness (unknown plan or frequency id, ids from the other segment, bedrooms 99 / -5, string and null dims, a commercial-shaped object under the residential key, invalid JSON, an array, a bare number) all fall back to defaults or clamp without an error, and per-segment keys (mkg.state vs mkg.state.commercial) keep the two service lines from polluting each other. _(estimator-engine)_
- The controls are plain buttons with aria-pressed, aria-label and disabled-at-bounds: 30 rapid clicks in each direction stop cleanly at 7 and 1, Enter and Space on a focused +/- or frequency button work, and the price pop respects prefers-reduced-motion. _(estimator-engine)_
- The follow-up tiers reuse the frequency ladder through `uses`, so one discount edit flows to the plan cards, the follow-up estimate and the PDF, and estimate() cleanly accepts either a frequency id or a bare discount number for tiers that need their own rate. _(estimator-engine)_
- Numbers are type-checked on both sides of the wire: backend.js cleanRequest() drops non-finite estimate fields and firestore.rules optNum() bounds every estimate and size field, so one odd record cannot break the admin or account lists; the owner's email states the estimate with the full price and discount in English whatever language the client used. _(estimator-engine)_
- The payload() field set matches the firestore.rules quoteFields allow-list exactly today: every key, type and length limit was checked against the four captured payloads (plan and follow-up, residential and commercial, EN and ES), HTML maxlength values equal the rule limits (name 120, email 200, phone 40, notes 2000, company 160), days ≤ 7, numeric fields are rounded integers, and backend.js only adds allow-listed keys (status, createdAt, emailLower) in one batch with the hold, so a hold can never exist without its request. _(quote-flow)_
- The stale-date guard at submit (quote.js:468-474) works well: when the picked day is no longer bookable the date is cleared, the review re-renders as 'Any day, morning or afternoon' and a clear, translated message (q.dateStale) explains it; the out-of-area path (two-click continue, '(outside our current area)' in the review, reset when the ZIP is corrected) is equally clean and ready for when serviceZips is filled in. _(quote-flow)_
- Submission ordering is right: the database write happens first and the Formspree email is a courtesy copy that never blocks a saved request; the email itself is thoughtfully built in English for the owner (Reply-To set to the client, subject carrying name, service and visit, a 'Language: Spanish' hint, the site estimate, a reference and an admin link). _(quote-flow)_
- Both languages are complete for the whole flow: no 'i18n: missing key' warnings in any EN or ES walk, commercial pages correctly override headings via the 'commercial:' namespace ('Where's the building?', 'Cuéntanos del espacio', 'Sitio'), dates are locale-formatted ('jueves, 22 de octubre'), and switching EN↔ES on the review and done screens re-renders every dynamic part without losing state. _(quote-flow)_
- Step navigation is accessible in the basics and solid on phones: each step change moves focus to the step heading and scrolls to the top, the calendar and six-step form render at 390 px with no horizontal overflow, Enter in a hidden-step field cannot submit early (current !== REVIEW), the Send button is disabled while sending, and the ZIP/email/phone validators give specific, translated messages. _(quote-flow)_
- Every write backend.js makes lines up with firestore.rules as published: a mechanical cross-check of the 40 keys quote.js payload() plus submitRequest() can send against quoteFields().hasOnly found zero missing and zero unused, and createdAt/movedByClientAt/updatedAt all use serverTimestamp() so the `== request.time` checks pass. Request and hold go in one db.batch() so the rules' getAfter()/mirrors() checks can tie them together. _(backend-wrapper)_
- Customer move and cancel are genuinely safe: reschedule() and cancelRequest() satisfy customerMoves/customerCancels/holdMatches exactly (status back to 'new', movedFrom <= 40 chars, hold keyed by request id, stale legacy random-id holds deleted via staleHold), and the rules make the customer branch impossible to abuse from a script. _(backend-wrapper)_
- Staff-only fields live in internal/{id} rather than on the customer-readable request, cleanRequest()/cleanShift() normalise every read so one odd record cannot break a list, and the referral code is created as a two-document batch the rules verify in both directions (users.code == referrers id and referrers.uid == users id). _(backend-wrapper)_
- The bootstrap is robust: ready never rejects, config comes from Hosting's reserved /__/firebase/init.json so nothing is pasted into the repo, App Check and the custom authDomain are switched on only on the live hostname, an App Check script failure degrades gracefully, and every page has a documented 'not connected' fallback (confirmed locally: calendar shows all windows open, sign-in buttons disabled). _(backend-wrapper)_
- Team management is tight: invitees self-register with exactly the fields the admins create rule demands, stillAdmin() polls with {source:'server'} and deliberately rejects rather than answering 'no' on a dropped connection, removeAdmin() deletes record and invite together, and credit grants use deterministic ids ('welcome-<id>', 'referral-<id>') so a retry never double-pays. _(backend-wrapper)_
- Every admin write in admin.js/timeclock.js was checked against firestore.rules and none would be rejected: holds carry createdAt == request.time, internal/ docs use only the three allowed keys, shift clock-outs satisfy changed().hasOnly(['outAt','requestId','note']) with outAt == request.time, and owner-only delete/invite rules match what the UI exposes. _(admin-timeclock)_
- Credit grants are idempotent by design: deterministic ids (welcome-{id}, referral-{id}, use-{id}) plus refWelcomeGranted/refRewardGranted flags mean a retry or a double click cannot create a second ledger line, and addCredit's get-then-set makes the id a lock. _(admin-timeclock)_
- The time-clock arithmetic is sound: union of overlapping spans, crew revenue split by hours, and long/overlap flags all produced the expected numbers in node (_computeStats, _flags); week/month/7d/30d boundaries are computed in the owner's local time at midnight. _(admin-timeclock)_
- Output is hardened: every value passes A.esc, a hostile name (=HYPERLINK("http://evil")) rendered inertly everywhere, and the CSV quotes every cell, doubles quotes and prefixes =,+,-,@ cells; the mock-driven page produced zero console errors and no horizontal scroll at 390 px. _(admin-timeclock)_
- Thoughtful operator UX: drafts survive live redraws with an "unsaved changes" marker and Discard, deletion needs DELETE/BORRAR typed (both words accepted in either language), the owner cannot remove themself or the owner record, removed staff are signed out within two minutes, and an open shift that is not on today's list always gets its own End button. _(admin-timeclock)_
- Move and cancel from the account page match firestore.rules field for field (status back to 'new', movedByClientAt, movedFrom, hold mirrored or dropped in the same batch), and both actions email the business with the old and new time and a "next step" line: verified by driving the flow against a mock backend and capturing the Formspree payloads. _(account-availability-referral)_
- Every dynamic string on the account page and in the share panel goes through esc(); pay links must match ^https:// (safeLink) and open with target=_blank rel=noopener, and a link pasted on one request correctly overrides the global Stripe link. _(account-availability-referral)_
- Sign-in error handling is careful: Firebase codes become plain bilingual sentences, password problems appear under the password field, the forgot-password path never reveals whether an email has an account, and unverified password accounts see nothing until verified, matching verified() in the rules. _(account-availability-referral)_
- Referral code creation is well designed: an alphabet without 0/O/1/I, crypto.getRandomValues, users + referrers written in one batch so the rules can check both halves, and a collision retry that re-reads myCode first. _(account-availability-referral)_
- The ?ref= capture is robust (regex-validated, 60-day expiry, per-tab dismissal, commercial pages show the commercial amount, code prefilled on the quote form and forgotten after a successful send), and the availability hand-off carried date and slot, including the commercial-only after-hours window, into commercial-quote.html with the CTA hidden until a pick. _(account-availability-referral)_
- Language detection and persistence behave exactly as documented and were verified end to end in Chromium: ?lang= wins, then localStorage, then the browser language (es-MX and es-ES contexts open in Spanish), the choice follows the visitor to the next page, and every page updates <html lang>, <title> and the meta description through plain data-i18n / data-i18n-attr markup (i18n.js:1723-1742, all 12 pages). _(i18n-engine-calendar)_
- The EN and ES dictionaries are complete and consistent: 774 keys each, identical key sets, all 83 commercial: overrides present in both languages, no placeholder differences between the two languages, every {placeholder} used in page strings exists in LIVE, and no raw {braces} appear on index.html after switching. A key missing from both dictionaries keeps the page's own text rather than showing the key (i18n.js:1729-1130). _(i18n-engine-calendar)_
- The language toggle is genuinely live: plan cards, business details and referral amounts are rebuilt (app.js relocalize, referral.js), focus stays on the toggle button, and the calendar re-renders through mkg:langchange while keeping the selected day and window, with Spanish month and weekday names handled correctly (es-US locale, lower-case per Spanish convention, CSS ::first-letter capitalisation at styles.css:457). _(i18n-engine-calendar)_
- Calendar bounds and state logic are correct: lead days and the 60-day horizon produce the right first and last bookable days (Oct 22 to Dec 19 on the test date), prev/next are disabled at the edges, Sunday-first headers match the cell padding, every day button carries an aria-label with its date and state, and quote.js:574 drops a saved draft date that is no longer bookable. _(i18n-engine-calendar)_
- Hold data is handled defensively: backend.availability() discards holds without string date/slot (backend.js:137), holds are keyed by request id so moves and cancellations cannot leave duplicates, windows are filtered per segment while capacity is correctly shared across segments, and a non-connected copy shows a clear bilingual notice (cal.offline) instead of pretending to be live. _(i18n-engine-calendar)_

## Frontend (85 findings: 15 high, 38 medium, 32 low)

_Scope: accessibility, performance, SEO, responsive layout, and bilingual copy quality._

### [HIGH] Admin edit panel: eight controls with unassociated labels
`admin.js:236` · a11y · lens: accessibility-dynamic

The per-request edit panel writes a <label> as a sibling of each control with no for/id pair and no wrapping, so date, start window, service, final price, note to client, pay link, private notes and assignee are all unlabelled form fields.

**Evidence.** admin.js:236-243, e.g. `'<div class="field"><label>' + esc(t("adm.date")) + '</label><input class="input" type="date" data-f="date" ...>'`, `'<label>' + esc(t("adm.window")) + '</label><select class="select" data-f="slot">'`, and the same for serviceChoice, quotedPrice, replyNote, payLink, adminNotes, assignedTo. Compare the correctly wrapped label in creditBlock (admin.js:163) and timeclock.js:244-246. Rendering needs a signed-in admin (not possible locally), so this is verified from the template string.

**Impact.** Staff using a screen reader hear eight anonymous 'edit text'/'combo box' controls when confirming a job; typing the final price into the pay-link field or the private note into the client-visible note is a client-facing mistake.

**Suggestion.** Generate ids per request (`'<label for="f-' + r.id + '-date">' ... '<input id="f-' + r.id + '-date"'`) or wrap each control inside its <label>.

### [HIGH] Plan comparison marks are silent for screen readers
`app.js:239` · a11y · lens: accessibility-static · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: high, impact: medium, refute: high) · **confirmed**

renderComparison() marks each cell with <span aria-label="Included"> around an aria-hidden check SVG, or <span aria-label="Not included">—</span>. aria-label is prohibited on a span with no role and screen readers ignore it, so the included cells read as empty and the others as a dash; the dash itself is #d6e2de on white (1.33:1), so low-vision users cannot see it either.

**Evidence.** app.js:239-240: '<span class="no" aria-label="' + esc(t("compare.no")) + '">—</span>', '<span class="yes" aria-label="' + esc(t("compare.yes")) + '">' + CHECK + "</span>" where CHECK (line 211) is <svg ... aria-hidden="true">. axe-core 4.10 on plans.html and commercial-plans.html, EN and ES, 390 and 1280: aria-prohibited-attr [serious], 22 nodes per page, "aria-label attribute cannot be used on a span with no valid role attribute"; the .no spans are reported under the same rule as incomplete. styles.css:263 .compare .no { color: var(--line); } with --line #d6e2de on #ffffff = 1.33:1 (contrast.js). Table structure itself is good: aria-label, th scope=col in thead, th scope=row in tbody.

**Impact.** Screen-reader users on both plans pages get a 12-row table whose body cells convey nothing, so the page's main content (which plan covers what) is lost; low-vision users cannot see the not-included dash.

**Suggestion.** Render the marks as <span class="yes" role="img" aria-label="Included">…</span> (role=img makes aria-label valid) or add the word in a .visually-hidden span, and give the dash a readable colour such as var(--muted).

### [HIGH] Plan comparison 'Included' marks are silent to screen readers
`app.js:240` · a11y · lens: accessibility-dynamic

The yes/no marks in the comparison table are <span>s with an aria-label and an aria-hidden SVG. aria-label is prohibited on a role-less span (ARIA 1.2 generic), so most screen readers read the 'Included' cells as empty and the 'Not included' cells as a dash.

**Evidence.** app.js:239-240 `'<span class="no" aria-label="' + esc(t("compare.no")) + '">—</span>'` and `'<span class="yes" aria-label="' + esc(t("compare.yes")) + '">' + CHECK + "</span>"` where CHECK is `<svg ... aria-hidden="true">`. axe on plans.html and commercial-plans.html: aria-prohibited-attr (serious) x22 on `td > .yes[aria-label="Included"]`. DOM check: each .yes span has role=none and textContent ''. (Chromium's own tree happens to compute 'Included', but NVDA, JAWS and VoiceOver ignore aria-label on a generic span.)

**Impact.** The comparison table is the main content of plans.html; screen-reader users hear rows of blanks and dashes and cannot tell which plan includes what.

**Suggestion.** Render `<span class="yes" role="img" aria-label="Included">` (and role="img" for the dash), or put the text in a `<span class="visually-hidden">` next to the icon.

### [HIGH] Calendar role="grid" has no rows, gridcells or arrow keys
`calendar.js:92` · a11y · lens: accessibility-dynamic · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: medium, impact: medium, refute: medium) · **confirmed**

The month grid declares role="grid" but its direct children are role="columnheader" divs, aria-hidden pads and plain <button>s: an invalid ARIA structure with no row/gridcell, no roving tabindex and no arrow-key movement, so every bookable day is its own Tab stop. aria-pressed is set only on the selected day, so the other days announce as plain buttons.

**Evidence.** calendar.js:92 `'<div class="cal-grid" role="grid">'`, 93 `'<div class="cal-wd" role="columnheader">'`, 94 `'<div class="cal-cell is-pad" aria-hidden="true"></div>'`, 109-110 `'<button type="button" class="' + cls + '" data-cal-day=... aria-label=...' + (clickable ? "" : " disabled") + (ymd === value.date ? ' aria-pressed="true"' : "")`. axe (critical) on quote step 5 and availability.html: aria-required-children on .cal-grid ('children which are not allowed: [role=columnheader], button[aria-label]') and aria-required-parent x7 on the columnheaders. DOM: rows: 0, gridcells: 0, aria-pressed { null: 31 } before a pick; ArrowRight from a focused day leaves focus unchanged; 19 Tab stops on step 5 in October, up to 26 day buttons in a full month.

**Impact.** Screen readers announce a table/grid and switch to table navigation, but the structure is invalid (headers not associated with days, no cells); keyboard users must Tab through every open day to reach the windows, the preference chips and Continue.

**Suggestion.** Either remove role="grid"/columnheader and expose the month as a labelled group of toggle buttons (aria-pressed on every day, weekday names in a visually-hidden caption), or implement the ARIA grid pattern fully: row > gridcell wrappers, one Tab stop with roving tabindex, Left/Right/Up/Down/Home/End, aria-selected on the chosen day.

### [HIGH] Booking calendar grid breaks ARIA grid semantics
`calendar.js:92` · a11y · lens: accessibility-static · votes 1/1 real (reproduce: high) · **confirmed**

The month grid is a <div role="grid"> whose direct children are div[role=columnheader], aria-hidden padding divs and plain <button>s: no row or gridcell roles and no arrow-key handling. Screen readers that honour grid semantics (NVDA and JAWS switch to focus mode and expect arrow navigation) get a broken table, even though the individual day buttons are well labelled.

**Evidence.** calendar.js:92-94: '<div class="cal-grid" role="grid">' + wd.map(function (w) { return '<div class="cal-wd" role="columnheader">' + esc(w) + "</div>"; }) ... html += '<div class="cal-cell is-pad" aria-hidden="true"></div>'; line 109 emits '<button type="button" class="' + cls + '" data-cal-day=...' directly inside the grid. axe-core: aria-required-children [critical] on .cal-grid ("Element has children which are not allowed: [role=columnheader], button[aria-label]") and aria-required-parent [critical] on all seven .cal-wd ("Required ARIA parent role not present: row") in 24 runs: quote.html and commercial-quote.html step 5, availability.html, EN and ES, 390 and 1280. The same mount() markup is used for the account page's change-date calendar and admin.html.

**Impact.** Screen-reader users picking a day in the quote flow, on availability.html or when moving a visit from their account; the announced grid keyboard model does not exist, so the widget behaves inconsistently with what is announced.

**Suggestion.** Drop role="grid" and role="columnheader" and wrap the buttons in <div role="group" aria-label="October 2026"> (the day buttons already carry names like "Thursday, October 22, 3 open"), or build the month as a real <table> with <th scope="col"> and the buttons inside <td>.

### [HIGH] Calendar rebuilds its DOM on every press and drops focus
`calendar.js:117` · a11y · lens: accessibility-dynamic · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: high, impact: medium, refute: high) · **confirmed**

render() replaces root.innerHTML on every interaction (day, start window, previous/next month, Clear the date, language switch), so the button the keyboard user just activated is destroyed and focus falls to <body>. The month heading's aria-live region is also a brand-new node each time, so month changes are not reliably announced.

**Evidence.** calendar.js:117 `root.innerHTML = html;` is called from the day handler (122-128: `render()`), the slot handler (130-135), prev/next (119-120 via load()->apply()->render()), set() (172) and the mkg:langchange listener (168). Playwright keyboard run on quote.html step 5: focus a bookable day, Enter -> document.activeElement = BODY (selection and the polite summary did update); Enter on 'Morning 8:00-11:00' -> BODY; Enter on 'Next month' -> BODY; Enter on 'Previous month' -> BODY; Enter on 'Clear the date' -> BODY. Measured: after choosing a November day it takes 28 Tab presses to reach the first window button and, after choosing the window, 34 more to reach Continue. The `<h3 class="cal-month" aria-live="polite">` (line 89) is re-created each render: evaluate(isSameNode after next-month) -> { same: false, connected: false }.

**Impact.** Keyboard and screen-reader users lose their place three times while booking (day, window, Continue) and must Tab from the top of the page each time; many will give up before sending the request. Same code path on availability.html, account.html (move a visit) and admin.html.

**Suggestion.** Keep the calendar shell and update cells in place (toggle classes/aria-pressed, replace only the windows panel), or after re-rendering restore focus to the element with the same data-cal-day / data-cal-slot / data-cal-next attribute; keep one persistent element for the month name and update its textContent so aria-live works.

### [HIGH] Availability calendar stays blank for seconds, then shoves the page
`calendar.js:167` · perf · lens: performance

mount() only calls load(); render() runs from apply() after B.availability() resolves, which on the live site waits for four sequential Firebase SDK scripts, init.json, App Check and a Firestore query. Until then the `<div data-calendar>` (availability.html line 75) is an empty element with no height, so the page's only content is missing and the "Your pick" panel sits where the calendar will appear.

**Evidence.** Playwright, 390x844 phone, Lighthouse 4G profile (1.6 Mbps, 150 ms RTT, 4x CPU), repo served by a local gzip server with Firebase-style headers and the real firebase@10.14.1 compat files on /__/firebase/ (scripts and JSON results under /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/5cd54e72-5e10-5f26-9ec5-c19b545a368a/scratchpad/work/frontend/performance/): availability.html first paint 684 ms; `[data-calendar]` still empty at 2,000 ms (film/availability-2000.png shows the Home/Business toggle followed directly by "Your pick"); first `.cal-cell` at 2,713 ms; a layout-shift entry at 2,736 ms of 0.218 whose source is `aside.avail-side`; page CLS 0.218-0.255 across runs (Google's "poor" band starts at 0.25). The SDK chain that gates it: firebase-app-compat 762-1063 ms, auth 1074-1614, firestore 1638-2279, app-check 2314-2508, init.json 2520-2680. The live site adds the real init.json, the reCAPTCHA/App Check token and the Firestore query, so 3-4 s is realistic. calendar.js line 167 is the bare `load();`; render() is reached only via apply() (line 69-75).

**Impact.** Every phone visitor to the public availability page sees a heading, a toggle and an empty gap for 2-4 s with no loading state, then the panel they started reading jumps roughly a screen down. It reads as broken, and CLS lands in the band Search Console flags.

**Suggestion.** In mount(), call render() synchronously before load() (cells disabled with a "checking availability" note, or a visible loading state) so the grid exists at first paint, and/or give `[data-calendar]` a min-height of about 420 px on phones; the data fill can stay exactly as it is.

### [HIGH] Home title and H1 omit Charlotte and house cleaning; ES titles overlong
`i18n.js:23` · seo · lens: seo

The home page, the page most likely to rank for local queries, has a 73-character title with no city and no service keyword ("MKG Services | Home cleaning plans that cost less the more often you book") and an H1 of "A clean home, on repeat." The commercial title does include "Charlotte NC" but only at characters 60-71, past the roughly 60-character display cutoff, and several Spanish descriptions exceed 160 characters.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:23 `"meta.title.home": "MKG Services | Home cleaning plans that cost less the more often you book"` (73 chars, no "Charlotte", no "house cleaning"); index.html:97 H1 `A clean home, on repeat.`; i18n.js:295 commercial title is 71 chars ending in ", Charlotte NC"; i18n.js:833 ES home title 79 chars; i18n.js:1106 ES commercial description 187 chars; i18n.js:836 ES plans description 171 chars (lengths measured with a node script over the dictionary). The word "Charlotte" appears in the static home HTML only in the "Charlotte's own, since 2014" section and the logo alt text.

**Impact.** Title and H1 are the strongest on-page relevance signals; without the city and the service in them the home page is a weak candidate for "house cleaning Charlotte NC", "maid service Charlotte" and similar, which are the queries that bring new clients. Overlong titles and descriptions are truncated or rewritten by Google in the results page.

**Suggestion.** Lead with service and place, then the hook: e.g. "House Cleaning in Charlotte, NC | Plans From Weekly to Monthly | MKG Services" trimmed to about 60 characters, H1 "Recurring house cleaning in Charlotte that costs less the more often you book"; move "Charlotte" to the front of the commercial title; cut the Spanish descriptions to about 155 characters.

### [HIGH] Spanish site is invisible to search: client-side only, canonical to English
`index.html:19` · seo · lens: seo

All Spanish copy (titles, descriptions, body) is swapped in by i18n.js after load, every ?lang=es variant carries the English canonical, the ES toggle is a <button> (no crawlable link), and the source HTML is <html lang="en">. There is no URL Google can index as a Spanish page, so the full Spanish translation the site invested in earns zero search visibility for queries like "limpieza de casas Charlotte".

**Evidence.** Static source: index.html:2 `<html lang="en">`, index.html:19 `<link rel="canonical" href="https://mkgservices.com/">`, index.html:54 `<button type="button" class="lang-btn" data-lang-switch="es"`. Runtime (Playwright, scratch crawl.json): index.html?lang=es after JS has title "MKG Services | Planes de limpieza que cuestan menos cuanto más seguido reservas", lang=es, but canonical still https://mkgservices.com/ and og:title still the English string; with JS disabled the same URL is entirely English. i18n.js:1724 `document.documentElement.lang = lang;` is the only place lang is set. sitemap.xml lists no ?lang=es URLs.

**Impact.** Spanish-speaking households and businesses in Charlotte (a large share of the market the site explicitly targets) cannot find the site through Spanish queries; the ES toggle only helps people who already arrived. Shared ?lang=es links also preview in English (og:title/og:description are static).

**Suggestion.** Generate real Spanish pages at stable URLs (e.g. /es/index.html, /es/plans.html) from the i18n.js dictionary with a small build step (the make-pages.py pattern already exists), each with lang="es", its own self-canonical, Spanish og tags, and absolute reciprocal hreflang; list them in sitemap.xml. Until then, drop the hreflang tags so Google is not given contradictory signals.

### [HIGH] og:image is a 1.28 MB square badge; previews crop it or drop it
`index.html:25` · seo · lens: seo

Every share preview uses brand/logo-full-1024.png: 1024x1024, RGBA, 1,282,010 bytes. Facebook, LinkedIn and most link unfurlers want 1.91:1 (1200x630) and show a square image as a small thumbnail or centre-crop it, which here cuts off the "Charlotte, North Carolina" and "Established 2014" rings; WhatsApp's preview fetcher is widely reported to skip images over roughly 300 KB, so the referral links the site generates for texting would show no image at all. Six pages, including refer.html, have no og tags whatsoever.

**Evidence.** index.html:25-27 `og:image` = `https://mkgservices.com/brand/logo-full-1024.png`, `og:image:width` 1024, `og:image:height` 1024; index.html:28 `twitter:card` = `summary`. File check: `brand/logo-full-1024.png 1024 x 1024 1282010 bytes`, PNG colour type 6 (alpha). A 1200x630 object-fit:cover render of the file (scratch og-crop.png) shows only "MKG SERVICES" with the city and year text cut off. referral.js:59-61 `linkFor` returns `siteUrl + "/?ref=" + code`, so every referral share previews the home page's tags. Playwright crawl: refer.html, availability.html, privacy.html, terms.html, account.html, admin.html have og:title null; docs/make-pages.py:17 deletes the og block when generating them.

**Impact.** Referral texts, WhatsApp messages, iMessage and Facebook posts, the channel the refer-a-friend program depends on, show a cropped badge, a tiny thumbnail, or no image; the city branding on the badge is the part that gets cropped.

**Suggestion.** Export a 1200x630 JPEG or flattened PNG under 300 KB (logo on brand green with "House and office cleaning, Charlotte NC"), set og:image, og:image:type, width/height to it, switch twitter:card to summary_large_image, and add the og block to refer.html and the other generated pages (refer.html most of all).

### [HIGH] Form errors are never announced; focus stays on the button
`quote.js:37` · a11y · lens: accessibility-dynamic · votes 1/1 real (reproduce: high) · **confirmed**

setError() only writes text into a plain <p class="field-error"> and toggles a CSS class; nothing in the repo uses aria-invalid, aria-describedby, role="alert" or aria-live for errors, and focus is never moved to the failing field. The out-of-area notice, the stale-date message and the send-failure message follow the same silent pattern, and the Send button is disabled while sending, which throws focus away.

**Evidence.** quote.js:37-42 `if (el) el.textContent = msg || ""; ... input.classList.toggle("is-invalid", !!msg)`; quote.html:91/180/186/192/254 error <p>s have no live role; grep of *.html *.js finds no aria-invalid/aria-describedby/role="alert"/role="status" except the referral banner. Observed with Playwright: Enter on Continue with an empty ZIP -> error text 'Enter a 5-digit ZIP code.' with aria-live=null, #zip aria-invalid=null, focus still on Continue. Step 4 (name/email/phone) identical. Out-of-area (serviceZips set at runtime): first Continue shows the non-live notice and returns false (quote.js:284) -> nothing announced, step does not advance. Send failure: quote.js:475 `btn.disabled = true` -> activeElement = BODY, then fail() (484-488) writes into non-live [data-error=submit]; the stale-date message (472) likewise. account.js:44 err() and admin.js:557 use the same pattern (observed on account.html: 'Enter an email address we can reply to.' live=null, focus unchanged).

**Impact.** Screen-reader users press Continue/Send/Sign in and hear nothing; they cannot tell the form is stuck or why. This directly loses quote requests and client sign-ins. (WCAG 3.3.1, 4.1.3, 2.4.3.)

**Suggestion.** Give each error <p> an id and role="alert" (or aria-live="assertive"), set aria-invalid="true" and aria-describedby on the failing input and focus the first invalid field in validate(); make the out-of-area and submit notices live regions; while sending use aria-busy/aria-disabled instead of disabled so focus stays on the button.

### [HIGH] Quote form validation errors are invisible to screen readers
`quote.js:37` · a11y · lens: accessibility-static

setError() only writes the message into a <p class="field-error"> and toggles an is-invalid class. The input never gets aria-invalid or aria-describedby, the message container has no role or aria-live, and focus stays on the Continue button, so a screen-reader user who presses Continue with a missing or bad ZIP, name, email or phone hears nothing and cannot learn why the form will not advance. account.js err() (line 44) has the same pattern for sign-in errors.

**Evidence.** quote.js:37-42: function setError(key, msg) { var el = $('[data-error="' + key + '"]'); if (el) el.textContent = msg || ""; var input = form.elements[key]; if (input && input.classList) input.classList.toggle("is-invalid", !!msg); }  quote.html:91: <p class="field-error" data-error="zip"></p>. Playwright probe (measure2.js) after clicking Continue on an empty step 1: document.activeElement = the Continue button; #zip attributes = class=input is-invalid id=zip name=zip inputmode=numeric autocomplete=postal-code maxlength=5 placeholder=e.g. 28202 (no aria-invalid, no aria-describedby); the error <p> has only class and data-error, role null, aria-live null. Step 4 shows three errors (name, email, phone) with focus still on Continue (screenshot shot-quote.html-en-390-step4err.png).

**Impact.** Blind and low-vision screen-reader users (NVDA, JAWS, VoiceOver) cannot complete the quote request, the site's conversion path, in either language; the same gap affects sign-in errors on account.html.

**Suggestion.** In setError, give each [data-error] an id, set aria-invalid="true" and aria-describedby on the input, add role="alert" to the error <p>, and move focus to the first invalid field when validate() fails; apply the same to account.js err().

### [HIGH] Segmented controls overflow 320-360px phones, worst in Spanish
`styles.css:169` · bug · lens: responsive-visual

`.seg` forces every option into equal 1fr columns in one row, so the 5-option commercial schedule ladder and the 4-tier follow-up control cannot fit at phone widths. In Spanish at 320px the control is wider than its container and widens the page itself; in both languages labels wrap to 2-3 lines at 13px/11px.

**Evidence.** styles.css:168-171 `.seg { display: grid; grid-auto-flow: column; grid-auto-columns: 1fr; ... }` with :415-417 shrinking labels to 0.82rem/0.7rem at <=720px; the 5 commercial frequencies come from plans.js:263-279 ('2× por semana', '3× por semana', 'Lun–Vie' + 'ahorra 18%') and the follow-up tiers from plans.js:235-240 ('En las últimas 2 semanas', 'Hace más de un mes'), rendered by quote.js:221-227. Measured: commercial.html ES 320px: `.dial` is 330px wide inside a 288px column, `section.hero` scrollWidth 346px, mobile Chrome reports innerWidth 347 (page zoomed out); commercial-plans.html ES 320px: `.seg[data-freq-control]` 282px in a 246px toolbar, last option 'Lun–Vie ahorra 35%' is cut off by the toolbar border; quote.html step 3 ES 320px form 298px/288px; index.html ES 320px dial 298px/288px. EN 320-390px: all five commercial buttons 42-55px wide with the label and the 'off' line each on 2 lines (71px tall); follow-up tiers 'Over a month ago' on 3 lines (73px tall), ES tiers 85px tall. Screenshots: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/5cd54e72-5e10-5f26-9ec5-c19b545a368a/scratchpad/work/frontend/responsive-visual/shots/commercial__default__es__320.png (dial runs off the right edge), commercial-plans__default__es__320.png, commercial__dial__en__320.png, quote__step3-followup__es__320.png (same folder).

**Impact.** Spanish-speaking visitors on 320-360px phones (iPhone SE/8, Galaxy A-series) get a zoomed-out commercial page or a truncated last schedule option; every phone user has to read the pricing ladder in 11px two-line fragments, which undermines the site's core 'more often costs less' pitch.

**Suggestion.** At <=720px let `.seg` wrap (`display:flex; flex-wrap:wrap` with `flex: 1 1 90px` on .seg-btn, like .service-tabs .seg already does at :430-431) so 5 options become 3+2 and 4 tiers become 2+2; add `min-width: 0` to .hero-grid children and .plans-toolbar so a control can never widen the page.

### [HIGH] Review and admin cards clip long emails with overflow hidden
`styles.css:378` · bug · lens: responsive-visual

`.review { overflow: hidden }` combined with `dd` cells that cannot break an email address means the client's own email is cut off on the quote review and 'Request sent' screens at 320-360px, and request cards in the admin clip the client's email at phone widths and at 1024px.

**Evidence.** styles.css:378 `.review { display: grid; gap: 0; border: ...; overflow: hidden; }`, :382 `.review-row dd { margin: 0; }` (no min-width:0 / overflow-wrap), :379 `.review-row { grid-template-columns: 150px 1fr auto }`. Quote step 6 and the real done screen (Formspree stubbed) at 320px: `dl.review` scrollWidth 352 vs clientWidth 286, dd 334/250; the Contact row reads 'alexandra.montgomery.whitaker@e' then 'mail.com' on the next line; at 360px still ~24px clipped. Admin with fixture data: at 390px `dl.review` 352-432 vs 314px and at 1024px 553-637 vs 516px on 3 of 8 cards, e.g. 'mariajose.fernandez.delatorre@example.co' cut at the card edge. Screenshots: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/5cd54e72-5e10-5f26-9ec5-c19b545a368a/scratchpad/work/frontend/responsive-visual/shots/quote__step6__en__320.png, shots-done/quote__done__en__320.png, shots-auth/admin__dash-new__es__1024.png and shots-auth/admin__dash-new__es__320.png (same parent folder).

**Impact.** A visitor on a small phone cannot verify the email they typed before sending (and the confirmation shows it cut off too), so typos go out unnoticed; the owner on a phone or 1024px laptop cannot read a client's full address, with no visual cue that text is hidden.

**Suggestion.** Add `.review-row dd { min-width: 0; overflow-wrap: anywhere; }` (and the same for links inside admin/account cards), and keep the rounded corners without `overflow: hidden` on the dl (put border-radius on the first/last rows instead).

### [HIGH] Tablet widths 721-948px: nav overflows, page scrolls sideways
`styles.css:398` · bug · lens: responsive-visual

The hamburger menu only appears at max-width 720px, but the seven-item desktop nav plus the brand needs about 1000px (EN) and 1120px (ES). Between 721px and roughly 950px the nav sticks out past the viewport, the whole page gets a horizontal scrollbar, the brand and several links wrap to two lines and the pill CTA becomes a two-line blob.

**Evidence.** styles.css:398 `@media (max-width: 720px) { ... .nav-toggle { display: inline-grid } ... .site-nav { display: none; ...` and :111 `.site-nav { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 28px; }` with :107 `.site-header-inner { ... height: 68px }`. Measured with Playwright at every 1px from 721 to 1120 (probe-nav.js): index.html EN document.scrollWidth > innerWidth at 721-787px (nav right edge 788px in a 768px viewport, 'Single cleans', 'Sign in' and 'Get a quote' on 2 lines up to 1000px); ES overflows at 721-935px (nav right edge 936px; 'Pedir cotización' sits at x=820-936 in a 768px viewport) and commercial.html ES at 721-947px, with 'Limpiezas únicas' and 'Pedir cotización' still wrapped at 1120px. Every page captured at 768 reports scrollWidth 780-788 (EN) / 936-948 (ES). Screenshots: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/5cd54e72-5e10-5f26-9ec5-c19b545a368a/scratchpad/work/frontend/responsive-visual/shots/index__default__es__768.png and index__default__en__768.png (same folder). An iPhone in landscape (844x390) also gets this layout.

**Impact.** Every iPad/Android tablet in portrait (768-834px) and every phone in landscape above 720px sees a broken header with the main 'Get a quote' CTA cut off; Spanish visitors get it up to ~950px wide, which includes small laptops with a sidebar or split screen.

**Suggestion.** Raise the hamburger breakpoint to about 1000px (change the 720px media query that shows .nav-toggle/hides .site-nav, or add a second one), and/or let .site-nav wrap (flex-wrap, gap 16px) and drop the fixed 68px header height so nothing can overflow.

### [MEDIUM] Account sign-in tabs mix tab and toggle roles, no arrow keys
`account.js:287` · a11y · lens: accessibility-dynamic

The Sign in / Create account buttons have role="tab" but setTab() also sets aria-pressed (not allowed on tabs), both point aria-controls at a form that is not a tabpanel, and only click is bound, so the arrow keys the tab role promises do nothing.

**Evidence.** account.js:287 `b.setAttribute("aria-selected", ...); b.setAttribute("aria-pressed", ...)` on account.html:73-74 `<button role="tab" id="tab-signin" aria-controls="acct-pw" ...>`; account.html:82 `<form id="acct-pw">` has no role="tabpanel"; account.js:298 binds click only. axe (critical): aria-allowed-attr x2 'ARIA attribute is not allowed: aria-pressed'. Playwright: focus 'Sign in' tab, ArrowRight -> focus and aria-selected unchanged; both tabs remain Tab stops (no tabindex=-1).

**Impact.** Screen-reader users hear 'tab, selected, pressed' and are told to use arrow keys that do nothing; the announced panel is the same form for both tabs.

**Suggestion.** Simplest: drop role="tablist"/"tab"/aria-selected and keep aria-pressed toggle buttons in a role="group" with the existing aria-label (as every other segmented control on the site does). Otherwise complete the pattern: role="tabpanel" with aria-labelledby, roving tabindex, Left/Right/Home/End.

### [MEDIUM] Admin request editor fields have no associated labels
`admin.js:236` · a11y · lens: accessibility-static · votes 3/3 real (impact: low, reproduce: medium, refute: medium) · **confirmed**

The per-request edit panel builds <label>Date</label><input ...> pairs where the label neither wraps the control nor uses for/id, so none of the seven controls (date, start window, service, final price, note to client, payment link, private notes, assigned to) has an accessible name and clicking a label does nothing. Not observable locally (needs a signed-in admin); reasoned from the generated markup.

**Evidence.** admin.js:236-243, e.g. '<div class="two-up"><div class="field"><label>' + esc(t("adm.date")) + '</label><input class="input" type="date" data-f="date" value="..."></div>' and '<div class="field"><label>' + esc(t("adm.note")) + '</label><textarea class="textarea" rows="2" maxlength="2000" data-f="replyNote">'. By contrast timeclock.js:244-246 and admin.js:163 wrap the input inside the label, and admin.html:142 and :166 use for/id correctly.

**Impact.** Staff using a screen reader or voice control cannot tell the final-price field from the payment-link field on a dense form; everyone loses label-click focusing.

**Suggestion.** Wrap each control in its <label> as timeclock.js does, or generate ids ("f-" + r.id + "-date") and use for=; axe's label rule will then pass.

### [MEDIUM] Admin list redraws on every snapshot, dropping focus mid-edit
`admin.js:254` · a11y · lens: accessibility-dynamic

renderList() replaces the whole request list with innerHTML and is called from every Firestore listener (requests, internal fields, credits, team) and from loadOwners(); typed values survive via drafts but focus and caret do not, so a new request arriving while the owner types a note throws focus to <body>.

**Evidence.** admin.js:254 `box.innerHTML = list.map(card).join("");`; callers: 531 (watchRequests), 535 (watchCredits), 539 (watchInternal), 447 (watchTeam), 138 (ownerOfCode resolution); the file header admits 'every update redraws the list' and only 'anything typed' is kept (262 keep()). timeclock.js:116 `box.innerHTML = h` likewise redraws after every shift snapshot, so pressing Start/End loses focus. Timing can only be confirmed against the live database, but every live update takes this path.

**Impact.** Staff lose the caret and focus while editing a request (private notes, final price); screen-reader users hear the whole list re-announced; on a busy day with several staff this happens repeatedly.

**Suggestion.** Update existing <article data-id> nodes in place and skip re-rendering the article that contains document.activeElement (or defer the redraw while an edit panel is open), then restore focus by data-id/data-f.

### [MEDIUM] Stepper +/- buttons disable under focus and drop it
`app.js:162` · a11y · lens: accessibility-dynamic

When a stepper reaches its min or max the button that was just activated is set disabled, which removes focus. With bathrooms defaulting to 2 and min 1, a single press on '−' is enough.

**Evidence.** app.js:162-163 `if (minus) minus.disabled = state[key] <= d.min; if (plus) plus.disabled = state[key] >= d.max;`. Playwright on index.html: focus 'Fewer bathrooms' (2 bath), Enter -> '1 bath', button disabled, document.activeElement = BODY; 'More bedrooms' Enter x6 -> '7+ bed', activeElement = BODY. Same markup on quote step 2 (quote.html:114/118) and commercial pages (sqft/restrooms).

**Impact.** Keyboard users lose the focus indicator; screen readers (NVDA/JAWS reset to the top of the document when focus goes to body) lose their place in the estimator or quote step. (WCAG 2.4.3.)

**Suggestion.** Use aria-disabled="true" and ignore clicks at the bound while keeping the button focusable, or move focus to the <output>/the opposite button before disabling.

### [MEDIUM] availability.html pick summary and CTA appear silently
`availability.html:79` · a11y · lens: accessibility-dynamic

On the public calendar the chosen day/window is written into a plain <p> with no aria-live (the quote page's copy has one) and the 'Request this window' link is unhidden in the sidebar, so screen-reader users never hear that a pick registered or that the CTA exists.

**Evidence.** availability.html:79 `<p class="when-summary" data-when-summary></p>` versus quote.html:219 `<p class="when-summary" data-when-summary aria-live="polite">`; availability.js:19-21 sets sum.textContent and `cta.hidden = false`. Observed: Enter on a day -> focus BODY, summary 'Thursday, October 22, any window that day', CTA unhidden, no live region. The calendar's own offline notice (calendar.js:115 `.cal-offline`) is static too.

**Impact.** Screen-reader users on the public calendar cannot confirm their pick or find the button that carries it into the quote form (it sits in the right-hand aside, after the calendar in DOM order).

**Suggestion.** Add aria-live="polite" (or role="status") to data-when-summary, and after a pick move focus to the summary/CTA; mirror the quote page's markup.

### [MEDIUM] Firebase SDK scripts load one after another on quote/availability
`backend.js:55` · perf · lens: performance

`ready` chains script() calls so firebase-app, auth, firestore and app-check compat bundles are requested strictly sequentially (each `s.async = false` tag is appended only after the previous one loads, lines 26-34), then init.json is fetched, then the first query runs. The four bundles are 538 KB raw / 156 KB gzipped, more than everything else on the page combined, and auth-compat (38.7 KB gz) is loaded on pages that never sign in.

**Evidence.** Sizes from the firebase@10.14.1 npm package (the same files Hosting serves): firebase-app-compat.js 31.8 KB (9.8 KB gz), firebase-auth-compat.js 139.3 KB (38.7 KB gz), firebase-firestore-compat.js 343.8 KB (99.4 KB gz), firebase-app-check-compat.js 23.4 KB (7.6 KB gz). Measured 4G waterfall on quote.html: app 816-1116 ms, auth 1125-1653, firestore 1704-2348, app-check 2384-2575, init.json 2584-2740: 1.9 s with the connection idle between scripts; quote.html first view is about 375 KB on the wire, of which the SDK is 156 KB. `load` moves from ~0.9 s (DOMContentLoaded 928 ms) to 2,584 ms. On availability.html the identical chain runs 762-2680 ms. auth is used only by onAuth/signIn*/reset functions (backend.js lines 172-231); availability.js and the quote form's submit path never call them (quote.js uses onAuth only for the referral credit note, line 150).

**Impact.** On a 4G phone the calendar, referral credit and request submission wait about 2 s longer than the bytes require; roughly 0.8 s of that is pure round-trip waiting. The live site adds reCAPTCHA Enterprise for App Check, which could not be measured here.

**Suggestion.** Append all four `async=false` script tags at once (execution order is preserved, downloads overlap) and start the init.json fetch in parallel; add `<link rel="preload" as="script">` for the four URLs in the head of the pages that include backend.js so they start with the HTML; load firebase-auth-compat lazily on first use so availability.html and quote.html skip 38.7 KB.

### [MEDIUM] Commercial pages' static markup is residential copy until JavaScript runs
`commercial.html:78` · content · lens: i18n-copy

commercial.html, commercial-plans.html and commercial-quote.html reuse the residential HTML verbatim and rely on the runtime commercial: overrides to swap the copy. Anything that reads the raw HTML (social scrapers, non-JS crawlers, the first paint before i18n.js runs, reader modes) gets home-cleaning copy on the commercial pages.

**Evidence.** stale.js compared the static text under every data-i18n element with the EN dictionary (segment-aware): 33 mismatches on commercial.html, 11 on commercial-plans.html, 5 on commercial-quote.html. Examples: commercial.html:78 static "Pick a plan, pick how often, and watch the price come down. Skip a visit or change your plan whenever life changes." vs commercial:hero.lead; :137 "Tell us about your home"; :226 FAQ "Do I need to be home?" vs "When do you clean?"; :95 aria-label="Plan price estimator" vs "Commercial plan price estimator"; commercial-quote.html:75 step label "Home" vs "Site"; :147 "Prices are estimates for a {bedrooms}-bedroom, {bathrooms}-bathroom home" on a square-footage form. The <title> and meta description on these pages were hand-corrected, the body was not.

**Impact.** Any business owner who gets a commercial link in a chat preview or reads it without JS sees a page about bedrooms, pets and lockboxes; search snippets built from body text can also quote residential sentences on commercial URLs.

**Suggestion.** Add a one-off build step (or extend docs/make-pages.py) that writes the EN commercial: strings into the static markup of the three commercial pages, so the no-JS text matches what visitors see.

### [MEDIUM] Commercial pages' static HTML is the residential copy until JS runs
`commercial.html:137` · seo · lens: seo

commercial.html and commercial-plans.html ship the residential text in the HTML and rely on i18n.js `commercial:` overrides to replace it after load. Googlebot renders JavaScript (with a delay) so it usually sees the right copy, but the first-pass HTML, Bing's initial crawl, AI crawlers, link unfurlers and any client with scripts blocked read a commercial page that talks about bedrooms, "your home" and "cleaning homes".

**Evidence.** commercial.html:137 `<h3 class="heading" data-i18n="how.1.h">Tell us about your home</h3>`, :145 `Come home to clean`, :191 `MKG Services, LLC has been cleaning homes in Charlotte...`, :226 `Do I need to be home?`; commercial-plans.html:77 `Set your home size`, :147 `Get an exact price for your home`. Playwright (scratch commercial-check.js): with JavaScript off, six residential phrases are present and the h2/h3 list reads "How a plan works | Tell us about your home | ... | Come home to clean"; with JavaScript on, zero residential phrases remain and the headings read "How a commercial plan works | Tell us about the space | ...".

**Impact.** The commercial landing page's topical signal is diluted and it reads as a near-duplicate of the home page before rendering, which weakens it for "office cleaning Charlotte" and similar queries and makes non-Google surfaces describe it wrongly.

**Suggestion.** Write the commercial text into the static HTML of the commercial pages (a small generator reading the `commercial:` keys from i18n.js can do it mechanically), keeping the data-i18n attributes so the Spanish switch still works.

### [MEDIUM] firebase.json disables caching of already-versioned JS and CSS
`firebase.json:32` · devops · lens: performance

`**/*.@(js|css)` is served with `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, yet every reference already carries a version (`styles.css?v=22`, index.html line 29; `plans.js?v=22` etc. lines 304-307). Every page view therefore re-validates the stylesheet (render-blocking) and 4-7 scripts, and the CDN edge is told the same, so revalidations can travel to the origin. Brand images get Hosting's default max-age=3600.

**Evidence.** Measured a second navigation (index.html then plans.html) on the 4G profile against a local server emitting the firebase.json headers: 5 conditional requests (styles.css, plans.js, i18n.js, app.js, referral.js) all answered 304, FCP 388 ms, DOMContentLoaded 441 ms. Same navigation with `max-age=31536000, immutable` for js/css: 0 revalidations, FCP 256 ms, DOMContentLoaded 341 ms. index.html then quote.html: FCP 380 ms vs 232 ms. styles.css revalidation is flagged `renderBlockingStatus: blocking`, so the 150 ms round trip sits before first paint on every page.

**Impact.** Every click between pages and every return visit paints about 130-150 ms later on 4G than it needs to, on a site whose pages otherwise paint in ~0.7 s; the extra requests also count against Hosting bandwidth.

**Suggestion.** Change the js/css rule to `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (safe because every URL carries ?v=N and the HTML stays at max-age=0) and add a `brand/**` rule with a long max-age, renaming files or bumping ?v= when artwork changes.

### [MEDIUM] i18n.js sends every page's copy in both languages
`i18n.js:832` · perf · lens: performance

One 112.7 KB file (31.6 KB gzipped) holds the English dictionary (lines 23-831) and the Spanish one (line 832 onwards) for all twelve pages, and every page loads all of it before DOMContentLoaded. The home page uses roughly 17% of the keys and only one language.

**Evidence.** Byte split measured on the file: D.en 50.8 KB, D.es 54.1 KB, engine 6.4 KB; 774 English keys. index.html references 75 data-i18n + 10 data-i18n-attr keys and app.js/referral.js call t() with 48 distinct keys (~130 of 774). By prefix, commercial:* is 18% of the English block, q.* 12%, acct.* 12%, adm.* 11%, privacy/terms/tc.* 13%: none used on the home page. On the 4G profile i18n.js is the last script to arrive (699 ms vs 528 ms for plans.js) and sets DOMContentLoaded at 926 ms; the estimator, prices and phone number appear at 943 ms. It is 42% of the home page's 75 KB of HTML+CSS+JS on the wire (148 KB with the font) and 110 KB of JS to parse at 4x CPU slowdown.

**Impact.** The estimator and translated copy appear ~150-200 ms later on 4G phones than they need to, and the file grows with every page added; minifying it recovers only 1.6 KB gz because it is mostly strings.

**Suggestion.** Split by language first (i18n.en.js / i18n.es.js chosen by a small loader using the existing detect(), or a `<script>` per language emitted by make-pages.py): halves the payload with no page changes; split per page later if desired.

### [MEDIUM] "Entrar"/"Salir" mean both sign-in/out and clock-in/out on admin page
`i18n.js:1391` · ux · lens: i18n-copy

The Spanish time clock uses "Entrar" for Clock in and "Salir" for Clock out, the same two words the same screen uses for Sign in and Sign out. A Spanish-speaking cleaner who wants to end a shift sees two "Salir" buttons with different consequences.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:1390-1391: "tc.clockIn": "Entrar", "tc.clockOut": "Salir"; i18n.js:1292 "acct.signOut": "Salir"; :1330 "adm.signIn": "Entrar"; :1284 "nav.signin": "Entrar"; :1427 "tc.clockInNoJob": "Entrar sin reserva…". admin.html:98 renders the Sign out button (acct.signOut) in the dashboard header while the time clock section at admin.html:132-137 renders the clock buttons on the same signed-in view. English keeps them distinct ("Clock in/out" vs "Sign in/out"). "tc.h": "Reloj de trabajo" (i18n.js:1388) is also an unnatural calque of "time clock".

**Impact.** Staff who speak Spanish can sign out of the admin page when they meant to clock out (leaving an open shift that inflates hours and the $/hour report) or vice versa; the time-clock and payroll data the owner relies on becomes noisy.

**Suggestion.** Use unambiguous verbs: "Marcar entrada" / "Marcar salida" (or "Iniciar turno" / "Terminar turno") for the clock, keep "Entrar" / "Cerrar sesión" for authentication, and rename the section "Registro de horas".

### [MEDIUM] hreflang tags are relative, point at non-canonical URLs, and are missing on six pages
`index.html:8` · seo · lens: seo

hreflang hrefs must be absolute URLs and the self-referencing entry must match the page's canonical; here they are relative (`index.html?lang=en`), resolve to a different URL from the canonical (`https://mkgservices.com/`), and the x-default points at `index.html` rather than `/`. Google ignores the whole set when any entry is invalid, and the generated pages (availability, refer, privacy, terms) have no hreflang at all.

**Evidence.** index.html:8-10 `<link rel="alternate" hreflang="en" href="index.html?lang=en">`, `hreflang="es" href="index.html?lang=es"`, `hreflang="x-default" href="index.html">` versus index.html:19 canonical `https://mkgservices.com/`. Same pattern in plans.html:8, commercial.html:8, commercial-plans.html:8. Lighthouse 12.8 (mobile) on index, plans and commercial scores SEO 92 with exactly one failing audit, `hreflang`, reason "Relative href value" for all three links; refer.html (no hreflang) scores SEO 100. docs/make-pages.py:16-17 strip everything before the preconnect link, so the generated pages never get hreflang.

**Impact.** The tags do nothing today and, because the EN entry names a different URL from the canonical, they add a conflicting signal about which URL is the primary one.

**Suggestion.** Either remove the hreflang tags until real Spanish URLs exist, or make them absolute and self-consistent: en and x-default both `https://mkgservices.com/` (matching the canonical), es pointing at an indexable Spanish URL with a reciprocal tag on that page.

### [MEDIUM] Spanish pages are not indexable: relative hreflang, canonical collapses ?lang=es
`index.html:8` · seo · lens: i18n-copy

The Spanish version of every page is only a client-side rewrite of the same URL with ?lang=es. The hreflang links that are supposed to tell Google about it use relative URLs (Google ignores non-absolute hreflang), exist on only 4 of 12 pages, and every page's canonical points at the English URL, so the ?lang=es variant is declared a duplicate. sitemap.xml lists no Spanish URLs either.

**Evidence.** index.html:8-10: <link rel="alternate" hreflang="en" href="index.html?lang=en"> / hreflang="es" href="index.html?lang=es"> / x-default href="index.html"> (relative); index.html:19: <link rel="canonical" href="https://mkgservices.com/">. The same block appears only in plans.html, commercial.html and commercial-plans.html; quote.html, commercial-quote.html, availability.html, refer.html, privacy.html and terms.html have canonical but no hreflang. sitemap.xml (10 <url> entries) has no ?lang=es locs and no xhtml:link alternates. <html lang="en"> is static in all 13 files and only flips at runtime (i18n.js:1724).

**Impact.** Spanish-speaking Charlotte residents searching in Spanish ("limpieza de casas Charlotte") are served, at best, the English page; the Spanish copy the author wrote for 774 keys earns no organic traffic. For a business targeting the Hispanic community this is a lost-leads channel.

**Suggestion.** Make hreflang hrefs absolute (https://mkgservices.com/index.html?lang=es), add the block to all public pages, make each language variant self-canonical (or serve Spanish at a real path such as /es/ with its own canonical), and add the Spanish URLs with xhtml:link alternates to sitemap.xml.

### [MEDIUM] Render-blocking Google Fonts CSS and stylesheet: FCP about 3 s on mobile
`index.html:13` · perf · lens: seo

The Google Fonts stylesheet and styles.css are both render-blocking in the head; Lighthouse estimates 0.9 to 2.1 s of savings per page from them and measures first contentful paint at 2.8 to 3.1 s on throttled mobile, with the LCP element being hero text that waits on the font.

**Evidence.** index.html:13 `<link href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Bricolage+Grotesque:...&display=swap" rel="stylesheet">` and :29 `<link rel="stylesheet" href="styles.css?v=22">`. Lighthouse 12.8 mobile against the local copy: index.html performance 85, FCP 3.1 s, LCP 3.2 s (element `<p class="lead" data-i18n="hero.lead">`), render-blocking-resources "Est savings of 930 ms" listing the fonts CSS (944 ms) and styles.css (903 ms); plans.html 940 ms; commercial.html 1,110 ms; refer.html 2,130 ms. Unminified JS/CSS savings 29 KiB + 7 KiB; i18n.js is 112.7 KB raw (32.3 KB gzipped) on every page, including the admin and account dictionaries. (Text-compression and cache-TTL audits reflect the local server, not Firebase Hosting, and are excluded here.)

**Impact.** Core Web Vitals are a minor ranking factor but a direct conversion factor on mobile, where most "near me" searches happen; 3 s to first paint on a text-first page is avoidable.

**Suggestion.** Self-host a subset of Bricolage Grotesque as woff2 with `font-display: swap` and a `<link rel="preload" as="font">`, or load the Google CSS with media="print" onload swap; inline the critical above-the-fold CSS; minify the JS and CSS on deploy; and split i18n.js so public pages do not download the admin, account and time-clock dictionaries.

### [MEDIUM] Google Fonts CSS blocks paint; brand font arrives 0.7 s late
`index.html:13` · perf · lens: performance

The fonts.googleapis.com stylesheet is a render-blocking third-party request placed before styles.css, and the woff2 can only be discovered after that CSS arrives, so Bricolage Grotesque lands well after first paint and text swaps from the fallback font. Self-hosting one file with a preload would remove the third-party round trips and start the font download with the HTML.

**Evidence.** 4G mobile run: the Google CSS has `renderBlockingStatus: blocking` and took 278 ms here with an already-warm connection; a real phone also pays DNS+TCP+TLS to fonts.googleapis.com and fonts.gstatic.com (about two more round trips, 300 ms+ on 4G) before any CSS can apply. The 75.1 KB latin woff2 was requested only after the CSS: font loaded at 1,422 ms vs first paint at 724 ms (plans 1,342 vs 696; quote 1,660 vs 632), so headings render in Segoe UI/system-ui for ~0.7-1 s and then swap. The five declared weights resolve to the same three subset files (fetched the CSS via the proxy: 15 @font-face blocks, 3 unique URLs), so the weight list costs nothing extra; the swap shift measured only 0.0035 CLS. Google's CSS is `private, max-age=86400`, re-fetched daily.

**Impact.** On every first visit the brand typography appears late and visibly changes; first paint is tied to a third-party host's latency. Not a correctness issue, but it is the most visible loading artefact after the empty estimator.

**Suggestion.** Download the single variable woff2 (latin subset is enough for EN/ES), serve it from the site with `@font-face { font-display: swap }` in styles.css and `<link rel="preload" as="font" type="font/woff2" crossorigin>` in the head; drop the two preconnects and the Google stylesheet.

### [MEDIUM] og:title and og:description are English-only; shared Spanish links preview in English
`index.html:22` · i18n · lens: i18n-copy

Open Graph title and description are hard-coded English with no data-i18n and no og:locale, so any link shared with ?lang=es (which the referral feature appends automatically for Spanish users) unfurls in English on WhatsApp, Facebook, iMessage and SMS previews.

**Evidence.** index.html:22-23: <meta property="og:title" content="MKG Services | Home cleaning plans that cost less the more often you book"> and og:description, with no data-i18n-attr; no og:locale on any page. Playwright load of every page with ?lang=es (es-runtime.js) shows document.title and meta description switch to Spanish while og:title stays English. referral.js:59-62 linkFor() returns base + "/?ref=" + code + "&lang=es" for Spanish sharers, so the Spanish share path is the primary one for this audience. Social scrapers do not run JavaScript, so a runtime switch would not help either.

**Impact.** Spanish-speaking clients who use the refer-a-friend share buttons send a link whose preview card is in English; the mismatch lowers click-through and undercuts the bilingual positioning.

**Suggestion.** Either serve a Spanish path with its own static og tags, or at minimum add og:locale="en_US" plus og:locale:alternate="es_US" and generate a language-specific preview for ?lang=es links (for example a tiny share endpoint or static /es/ copies produced by a build step).

### [MEDIUM] og:image is a 1.28 MB PNG on every page
`index.html:25` · seo · lens: performance

`og:image` (and the JSON-LD `image`, line 40; same tags in plans.html, quote.html and the commercial pages) points at brand/logo-full-1024.png, a 1024x1024 RGBA PNG of 1,282,010 bytes. Link-preview scrapers fetch it on every share, and several messaging apps are reported to drop preview images above a few hundred KB.

**Evidence.** `ls -l brand/logo-full-1024.png` = 1,282,010 B; PNG header 1024x1024, colour type 6 (RGBA). Re-encoding the same artwork from brand/source/logo-full-3000.png with sharp: 1200x630 JPEG q85 on the brand background = 77 KB; 1024x1024 JPEG q85 = 180 KB; palette PNG 1024 = 365 KB. `twitter:card` is `summary` (small square), so a 1200x630 image is only needed for Open Graph consumers. WhatsApp's ~300 KB og:image limit is widely reported but could not be verified from this container.

**Impact.** Each share of a page (iMessage, WhatsApp, Facebook, Slack) pulls 1.28 MB, and the refer-a-friend links the site pushes through navigator.share (referral.js) may show no picture in the apps that cap image size; the file is also served from Hosting bandwidth.

**Suggestion.** Export a 1200x630 JPEG (or PNG under ~300 KB) with the badge on the brand background, point og:image, og:image:width/height and the JSON-LD image at it on every page, and keep the 1024 PNG out of the deploy (firebase.json ignore or brand/source).

### [MEDIUM] LocalBusiness JSON-LD is thin, generic, and only on the home page
`index.html:33` · seo · lens: seo

The structured data uses the generic LocalBusiness type and omits the fields Google's local and rich-result features read: priceRange, openingHoursSpecification, geo, sameAs, postalCode, and any Service or hasOfferCatalog entries for the plans and single cleans. areaServed is a City whose name is the string "Charlotte, NC". commercial.html, the second landing page, has no structured data at all.

**Evidence.** index.html:30-47 JSON-LD block: `"@type": "LocalBusiness"` (line 33), address at line 44 `{ "@type": "PostalAddress", "addressLocality": "Charlotte", "addressRegion": "NC", "addressCountry": "US" }` (no postalCode), line 45 `"areaServed": { "@type": "City", "name": "Charlotte, NC" }`; no priceRange, openingHoursSpecification, geo, sameAs, hasOfferCatalog, makesOffer keys anywhere (grep over the repo). Playwright crawl: jsonld count is 1 on index.html and 0 on every other page. The hours exist in code: plans.js:75 `weekdays: [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6]`, plans.js:78-83 slots 8:00 to 6:00 pm (commercial to 9:30 pm); the services exist at plans.js:130-224 and 281-375.

**Impact.** Google has less to confirm the business's category, hours and offerings against the Business Profile, and the commercial page carries no entity signal; the markup earns no knowledge-panel or hours enrichment it easily could.

**Suggestion.** Switch to `"@type": ["LocalBusiness", "HomeAndConstructionBusiness"]` (or add `additionalType`), add priceRange, openingHoursSpecification built from plans.js, geo for Charlotte, postalCode, sameAs (Business Profile, Facebook, Nextdoor, Yelp), and a hasOfferCatalog listing the three plans and the deep, follow-up and move cleans as Service items; reference the same @id from commercial.html. FAQPage markup is optional: since 2023 Google shows FAQ rich results only for government and health sites, so it will not add a visible result here.

### [MEDIUM] No Google Business Profile link, sameAs, reviews, or address signals on the site
`index.html:44` · seo · lens: seo

Local rankings (the map pack) are driven by the Business Profile, reviews, and NAP consistency between the site and directories, and the site provides none of the connective tissue: no link to a Business Profile or maps listing, no sameAs profiles, no review content, no postal code or ZIP list, and the phone number is injected by JavaScript rather than present in the HTML.

**Evidence.** Repo-wide grep for `google.com/maps`, `g.page`, `facebook`, `instagram`, `yelp`, `nextdoor`, `sameAs` returns nothing outside vendor code. index.html:44 address has no streetAddress or postalCode; plans.js:51 `serviceZips: []`; plans.js:30 serviceArea is only "the Charlotte area". The phone is rendered by app.js:284-295 (`$all("[data-business]")... el.textContent = v`) into empty elements such as index.html:59 `<b data-business="phone"></b>`; the Playwright crawl with JavaScript disabled finds the phone on none of the 12 pages, with it on all 12. README.md:88 defers reviews until real ones exist (correct), but there is no place for them yet.

**Impact.** For "cleaning service near me" style queries the business competes on Business Profile strength, and the website currently neither points at a profile nor gives citation checkers and non-rendering crawlers a consistent NAP to match.

**Suggestion.** Claim or confirm the Google Business Profile as a service-area business (hide the street address, list Charlotte-area ZIPs and neighbourhoods), link it from the footer and sameAs, put the phone number and "Charlotte, NC" as literal text in the footer HTML, populate serviceZips so the quote form and an on-page service-area list agree, and add a reviews section once real reviews exist.

### [MEDIUM] No skip link; 13 tab stops before main content
`index.html:50` · a11y · lens: accessibility-static

No page has a skip link or an id on <main>; keyboard users must Tab through the top bar (EN, ES, Call, Text, email), the brand link and seven nav links on every page before reaching content. The top bar, which holds the language switch, also sits outside every landmark.

**Evidence.** measure.js: tab stops before <main> = 13 at 1280 px (15 when the referral banner is showing), 6 to 8 at 390 px; skipLink false and main.id empty on all 12 pages. index.html:50-89 (<div class="topbar"> ... <nav class="site-nav" id="site-nav">), the same chrome is pasted into every page. axe region [moderate, best-practice] on .topbar in all 136 runs: "Some page content is not contained by landmarks".

**Impact.** Sighted keyboard-only and switch users on every page, especially the quote flow where the trip repeats on each visit; screen-reader users can jump by landmark but cannot find the language switch that way.

**Suggestion.** Add <a class="skip-link" href="#main">Skip to content</a> (visually hidden until focused, with a data-i18n key) as the first child of body and id="main" tabindex="-1" on <main>; wrap the top bar in <nav aria-label="Contact and language">.

### [MEDIUM] Every page links to index.html instead of the canonical /
`index.html:72` · seo · lens: seo

The logo link on all twelve pages and the footer FAQ and single-cleans links point at `index.html`, while the canonical, sitemap and og:url use `https://mkgservices.com/`. Firebase Hosting's default is to 301 `/index.html` to `/`, so the most-clicked internal link on the site passes through a redirect on every click, and the hreflang x-default names the redirecting URL.

**Evidence.** index.html:72 `<a class="brand" href="index.html"`, index.html:289 `href="index.html#single-cleans"`, index.html:290 `href="index.html#faq"`; the same brand link is in plans.html:55, commercial.html:55 and the other pages (grep). index.html:19 canonical is `https://mkgservices.com/` and sitemap.xml:3 lists `https://mkgservices.com/`. index.html:10 `hreflang="x-default" href="index.html"`. firebase.json has no cleanUrls or redirects entries, so the hosting default applies; this cannot be verified from the container (live site blocked), so confirm with `curl -I https://mkgservices.com/index.html`.

**Impact.** An extra redirect hop on the home link for every visitor and crawler, link equity routed through a redirect, and one more mismatch between the URL the site links to and the URL it declares canonical.

**Suggestion.** Change every `href="index.html"` to `href="/"` (and `/#faq`, `/#single-cleans`), including in docs/make-pages.py so regenerated pages stay correct; keep `.html` for the other pages since the canonicals and sitemap already use it consistently.

### [MEDIUM] Estimator and plan cards render late and shift the page
`index.html:115` · perf · lens: performance
