The hero dial (`data-render-plan-picks`, steppers, frequency switch) and the plans grid (`data-render-plans`, line 186; same on plans.html) are empty containers with no reserved height until app.js runs at DOMContentLoaded. When they fill, surrounding content moves: `.hero-grid` uses `align-items: center` (styles.css line 124) so the headline re-centres as the dial grows, and on plans.html the toolbar and three cards push the pricing note and everything below it down.

**Evidence.** Measured layout-shift entries (Playwright PerformanceObserver): desktop 1280x800 on 4G, index.html: shift 0.146 at 827 ms, sources `div.hero-copy`, `div.dial.on-dark`, `div.topbar-contact`. plans.html desktop first view CLS 0.122 (sources `.plans-toolbar-home`, `.seg`, `.plans-note`); plans.html on a phone as a repeat view (font cached): CLS 0.297 at 518 ms, the shift happening when `.plan-card` elements appear; shots/nojs-plans.png shows the pre-JS layout with "Your home:" over an empty bar and the note directly beneath. Mobile first views register only 0.000-0.004 because those blocks start below the first screen.

**Impact.** A visible jump on every desktop visit to the home page and on most visits to plans.html, the page where people compare prices; CLS 0.12-0.30 is the "needs improvement" to "poor" band used in Core Web Vitals.

**Suggestion.** Reserve the space: put the stepper and frequency-button markup in the HTML with JS only updating values, give `.dial`, `.plans-toolbar` and `[data-render-plans]` a min-height matching their filled size (or `align-items: start` on `.hero-grid`), or pre-render the three plan cards statically (plans.js is the single source, docs/make-pages.py already generates pages).

### [MEDIUM] Raw {placeholder} tokens and blank phone paint before JS runs
`index.html:129` · ux · lens: performance

Strings with live tokens are written literally in the HTML (`{oneTimeStruck} at full price. You keep {saving} every visit.` line 129, `Save {discount}` 125, `Get a quote for the {planName} plan` 142, `Estimate for a {bedrooms}-bedroom...` 143, `Or call or text {phone}` 271, `Serving {serviceArea}` 282; quote.html line 275 `Call or text {phone}, or email {email}`), and the phone number elements are empty. i18n.fill() (i18n.js lines 1718-1721) replaces them only at DOMContentLoaded, after all scripts have downloaded and run.

**Evidence.** With scripts blocked (shots/nojs-index.png) the home page shows the literal tokens in the dial, the CTA button, the closing band and the footer, with no number after "Call". On the 4G profile this is the first paint: index.html FCP 724 ms, values filled at 943 ms; quote.html FCP 632 ms, filled 933 ms. On quote.html the LCP element is `p.quote-talk` and the LCP entry's text at paint time read "Rather talk it through? Call or text {phone}, or email {email}". Scaled to 3G the window is about 1 s; it is permanent if a script fails to load (the only `<noscript>` note is inside the plans grid, line 187).

**Impact.** Every 4G visitor briefly sees broken-looking copy and a missing phone number on the first screen (the dial is the home page's main object on desktop), and the largest painted text on the quote page contains curly-brace tokens.

**Suggestion.** Ship the markup fill() produces directly in the HTML (the `<span data-text=...>`/`<a data-business=...>` elements, and the phone number as text) so English pages are final before JS, or mark these elements and keep them `visibility: hidden` until apply() has run.

### [MEDIUM] Plans, prices, single cleans and phone exist only after JavaScript
`index.html:186` · seo · lens: seo

The plan cards, the comparison table on plans.html, the single-clean services, the price estimator and the contact details are all rendered by app.js from plans.js, so the static HTML carries roughly a third less text on the home page and less than half on plans.html, with none of the plan names, inclusions or prices.

**Evidence.** index.html:186 `<div class="plans-grid" data-render-plans>` (only a noscript inside), index.html:199 `<div class="singles" data-render-singles></div>`, plans.html:106 `<table class="compare" data-render-comparison></table>`. Playwright crawl word counts (JS on vs off): index.html 785 vs 515, plans.html 589 vs 262, commercial.html 887 vs 516; plan names Essentials/Deep/Move present only with JS; "Charlotte" absent from plans.html without JS.

**Impact.** plans.html, the page that should rank for pricing queries, has almost no indexable substance until Google's render queue processes it, and non-rendering crawlers and previews see an empty table; Google's two-phase indexing also means changes to plans.js propagate to the index more slowly.

**Suggestion.** Pre-render the plan cards, comparison table and single-clean cards into the HTML (a build step over plans.js, or a one-time paste that app.js hydrates), so the static page already contains the plan names, what each includes and the from-price text; keep the interactive estimator as an enhancement.

### [MEDIUM] No service or vertical landing pages for high-intent local queries
`plans.js:201` · content · lens: seo

The site has eight indexable URLs and none is about a specific service or customer type. Deep cleaning, move-in/move-out cleaning, post-construction, floor care, and office, clinic, retail and restaurant cleaning are each defined in plans.js and rendered as cards, but have no page, no URL, no title and no H1 of their own, while those are the queries with clear buying intent.

**Evidence.** plans.js:201 `id: "deep"`, :209 `id: "followup"`, :217 `id: "move"` (residential singles) and :343, :351, :367 plus floor care for commercial, all rendered into index.html:199 `data-render-singles` as cards linking to `quote.html?service=deep` etc. sitemap.xml:3-12 lists only home, plans, quote, availability, commercial, commercial-plans, commercial-quote, refer, privacy, terms. Commercial verticals appear only in the meta description (i18n.js:296 "Office, clinic, retail, and restaurant cleaning"). Internal anchor text is generic throughout: "Plans", "Get a quote", "Commercial", "Availability" (Playwright link dump).

**Impact.** Queries such as "deep cleaning Charlotte", "move out cleaning Charlotte NC" and "office cleaning Charlotte" have no matching page to rank, so that demand goes to competitors with dedicated pages.

**Suggestion.** Add one static page per single-clean service and per commercial vertical (what is included, who it suits, from-price, FAQ, quote link with the service pre-selected), use descriptive anchor text to them from the nav, footer and cards, and add them to the sitemap and JSON-LD offer catalog.

### [MEDIUM] Phone is labelled Optional but required by the default choice
`quote.html:190` · ux · lens: accessibility-dynamic · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: low, impact: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

The phone label reads 'Phone — Optional', but the default 'Best way to reach you' is Text, and validation then requires a 10-digit phone, so a visitor who trusts the label hits an error (which is also not announced, see the form-errors finding).

**Evidence.** quote.html:190 `<label for="phone"><span data-i18n="q.phone">Phone</span> <span class="muted" ... data-i18n="q.optional">Optional</span></label>`; quote.html:198 `<input type="radio" name="contactMethod" value="Text" checked>`; quote.js:296-297 `if ((method === "Text" || method === "Call") && value("phone").replace(/\D/g, "").length < 10) { setError("phone", t("err.phone." + method)); ok = false; }`. Observed: name and email filled, phone empty, Continue -> 'Add a phone number so we can text you.' and the step does not advance.

**Impact.** Every visitor who skips the 'optional' field is bounced back; for screen-reader users the bounce is silent. (WCAG 3.3.2.)

**Suggestion.** Toggle the 'Optional' hint and aria-required on the phone field from the contactMethod change handler (required for Text/Call, optional for Email), or default the contact method to Email when the phone is empty.

### [MEDIUM] Quote service tabs and follow-up tiers drop focus on activation
`quote.js:246` · a11y · lens: accessibility-dynamic

renderTabs() and renderFollowUp() rebuild their segmented controls with innerHTML on every click, so the button a keyboard user just pressed is destroyed and focus goes to <body>.

**Evidence.** quote.js:246-256 `box.innerHTML = '<div class="seg" role="group" ...` rebuilt via syncPlanUI() on each tab click; 221-230 `seg.innerHTML = C.followUp.tiers.map(...)` rebuilt on each tier click. Playwright (quote.html?service=deep): focus 'Recent deep clean follow-up', Enter -> aria-pressed updates (deep=false, followup=true) but document.activeElement = BODY; step 3, focus 'Within 2 weeks', Enter -> BODY. The frequency control, by contrast, keeps focus because render() only flips aria-pressed (app.js:153-155).

**Impact.** Keyboard and screen-reader users lose their place on steps 1 and 3 of the single-clean path after every choice.

**Suggestion.** Toggle aria-pressed on the existing buttons instead of rebuilding them (as app.js render() does), or restore focus to the button with the same data-tab / data-fu after rebuilding.

### [MEDIUM] Out-of-area notice loses the ZIP after a language switch
`quote.js:283` · i18n · lens: accessibility-dynamic

The notice's {zip} placeholder becomes a fresh empty <strong data-out-zip> every time i18n apply() runs, but quote.js fills it only inside validate(1); switching EN/ES while the notice is showing leaves a sentence with a hole.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:1704 `zip: '<strong data-out-zip></strong>'`, 1720 `el.innerHTML = esc(s).replace(/\{(\w+)\}/g, ...)` runs for every [data-i18n] on apply(); quote.js:283 `$all("[data-out-zip]").forEach(function (el) { el.textContent = zip; })` only in validate(). Observed (serviceZips set to ["28202"] at runtime, ZIP 28000, Continue): 'We don't cover 28000 yet...'; press ES -> 'Todavía no cubrimos . Igual puedes enviar tus datos y te avisamos cuando lleguemos.'; back to EN -> 'We don't cover  yet.' Today plans.js:51 serviceZips is [] so the note never appears; it will the moment the owner fills the list.

**Impact.** Out-of-area visitors who switch language (the ES toggle is in the top bar on every page) see a broken sentence instead of the ZIP they typed.

**Suggestion.** In the mkg:langchange handler (quote.js:581) re-fill [data-out-zip] from value("zip") when the note is visible, or render the note from code with t("q.outOfArea", { zip }).

### [MEDIUM] English display names are stored as the record's plan/service key
`quote.js:408` · maintainability · lens: i18n-copy · votes 3/3 real (impact: low, reproduce: medium, refute: medium) · **confirmed**

Requests are saved with the English plan name, frequency label and single-clean name as data (not the ids), and both the account page and the admin editor later identify the plan by matching those English strings. The README invites the owner to edit plan names in plans.js; doing so silently breaks the Spanish rendering and the "Book this again" prefill for every existing request.

**Evidence.** quote.js:408: p.plan = A.plan(s.plan).name.en; p.frequency = A.frequency(s.frequency).label.en; :400 p.service = singleClean(draft.service).name.en; :403 p.deepCleanWithin = fuTier(draft.recency).label.en. account.js:78-83 serviceText(): seg.plans.filter(function (x) { return x.name.en === r.plan; }) and x.label.en === r.frequency, falling back to the raw English string; account.js:86-91 againLink() does the same to build ?plan=&freq=; admin.js:84 and :98 round-trip the same strings. plans.js header comment: "plan names… the owner is likely to change".

**Impact.** After a rename (e.g. "Family" to "Family Plus"), Spanish clients see "Family, Every 2 weeks" in English on their account page, the rebook link loses the plan, and the admin service dropdown shows a blank option for old requests. deepCleanWithin is never localized anywhere.

**Suggestion.** Store the ids (planId, frequencyId, serviceId, recencyTier) alongside or instead of the English labels and resolve labels through I.L() at render time; keep the English label only for the Formspree email.

### [MEDIUM] Send failure is unannounced and focus drops to body
`quote.js:475` · a11y · lens: accessibility-static

On submit the Send button is disabled while it has focus, so the browser moves focus to <body>; if saving fails, fail() rewrites a plain <p data-error="submit"> with no live region and nothing is focused, and the stale-date message at line 472 follows the same path. A screen-reader user hears neither "Sending…" nor the failure and does not know the request was not sent. Success is handled well: go("done") focuses the "Request sent" heading.

**Evidence.** quote.js:475: btn.disabled = true; btn.textContent = t("q.sending");  quote.js:484-488: function fail() { btn.disabled = false; btn.textContent = t("q.send"); ... setError("submit", t("err.submit", { alt: alt })); }  quote.html:254: <p class="field-error" data-error="submit"></p>. Playwright probe (probe-submit.js): focus the Send button, press Enter; 3 s later document.activeElement = BODY, error text "Couldn't send your request. Check your connection and try again or email mkgservices704@gmail.com." is on the page and the error element's attributes are class and data-error only. Locally the POST to formspree.io fails (ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED), which is exactly this code path; on the live site it runs when both Firestore and Formspree refuse.

**Impact.** Screen-reader and keyboard users whose request fails (offline, App Check or rules refusal) may believe it was sent or lose their place in the form: a lost booking.

**Suggestion.** Give [data-error="submit"] role="alert" and tabindex="-1", focus it in fail() and in the stale-date branch, and keep focus on the button by using aria-busy or re-focusing it after re-enabling.

### [MEDIUM] Sitemap submits two noindex quote pages; lastmod is hand-maintained
`sitemap.xml:5` · seo · lens: seo

quote.html and commercial-quote.html carry `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">` yet are listed in sitemap.xml, which Search Console reports as "Submitted URL marked noindex". The owner should also decide deliberately whether the quote pages should be out of the index at all: they are the conversion pages and the only ones matching "cleaning quote Charlotte" intent. lastmod values are typed by hand and nothing in the deploy workflow updates them.

**Evidence.** sitemap.xml:5 `<loc>https://mkgservices.com/quote.html</loc>` and :9 `<loc>https://mkgservices.com/commercial-quote.html</loc>`; quote.html:8 and commercial-quote.html:8 `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">` (confirmed at runtime: robots=noindex in the Playwright crawl). sitemap.xml:3-12 lastmod is 2026-10-16 for nine URLs and 2026-10-17 for refer.html; the repository has a single squashed commit (git log shows only 4d1bbbf, 2026-10-17), so the dates cannot be verified and .github/workflows does not touch the file.

**Impact.** Search Console errors on every crawl of the sitemap, and the business's quote pages have no chance to rank for high-intent quote queries while the noindex stays.

**Suggestion.** Remove the two quote URLs from the sitemap, or (better) drop the noindex, add a short indexable intro above the form ("Get a house cleaning quote in Charlotte, usually answered within one business day") and keep them listed; update lastmod only when a page actually changes, or drop lastmod entirely rather than publish stale dates.

### [MEDIUM] Comparison table on phones: 6-line rows, third plan column hidden
`styles.css:260` · ux · lens: responsive-visual

Plan columns are fixed at 15% but their headings ('Essentials', 'Professional') need 88-104px, so at 320-390px the table is wider than its wrapper, the first column shrinks to ~100px with one word per line, rows are 5-6 lines tall and the 'Complete' column is only reachable by scrolling an unsignposted overflow div.

**Evidence.** styles.css:260 `.compare th:not(:first-child), .compare td:not(:first-child) { text-align: center; width: 15%; }`, :424-425 (<=720px font 0.88rem, padding 10px 8px); plans.html:105 `<div style="overflow-x: auto;">` (same in commercial-plans.html:105). Measured: at 320px the table is 332px (residential) / 381px (commercial) in a 288px wrapper, first column 98/107px, up to 6 text lines per row, table height 1310px; at 390 commercial still 381px in 358. The visible part ends with 'Com' / 'Rota' and no scroll affordance. Screenshots: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/5cd54e72-5e10-5f26-9ec5-c19b545a368a/scratchpad/work/frontend/responsive-visual/shots/plans__table__en__320.png and shots/commercial-plans__table__en__390.png.

**Impact.** The 'what each plan covers' table is the page's main decision aid; on the most common phone widths it is a 1300px-tall column of broken words and visitors can miss that a third plan exists.

**Suggestion.** On <=720px drop the 15% widths, give the table a `min-width` (about 480px) with a visible 'swipe to compare' hint and a gradient edge, or render the comparison as stacked per-plan checklists; alternatively use fixed 48px check columns so the label column keeps the remaining width.

### [MEDIUM] Text input boundaries have 1.33:1 contrast on white
`styles.css:337` · a11y · lens: accessibility-static

Inputs, textareas and selects are white boxes on a white page whose only edge is a 1.5 px #d6e2de (--line) border, 1.33:1 against the paper background, where WCAG 1.4.11 expects 3:1 for a control boundary that is the only indicator. The same token outlines the choice chips, plan options and calendar day buttons.

**Evidence.** styles.css:336-339: .input, .select, .textarea { font: inherit; width: 100%; padding: 13px 14px; border: 1.5px solid var(--line); border-radius: var(--r-sm); background: var(--paper); color: var(--ink); } with --line: #d6e2de (line 15) and --paper: #ffffff. contrast.js: line on paper 1.33:1, line on mist 1.19:1. Also styles.css:349 .choice span { border: 1.5px solid var(--line) }, :360 .plan-option-body, :467 .cal-cell. In shot-quote.html-en-390-step4err.png the invite-code field edge is barely discernible next to the red error borders.

**Impact.** Low-vision visitors and anyone on a dim or bright phone screen cannot see where to tap on the quote and sign-in forms; name, email, phone and ZIP fields have no placeholder to help.

**Suggestion.** Add a darker control-border token (e.g. --line-strong: #8aa39d, at least 3:1 on white) for .input/.select/.textarea, .choice span, .plan-option-body and .cal-cell, keeping --line for decorative dividers.

### [MEDIUM] Mobile menu is cut off and unscrollable in landscape phones
`styles.css:404` · ux · lens: responsive-visual

The open mobile menu is an absolutely positioned panel inside the sticky header with no max-height or overflow, so on a 375px-tall landscape phone its last items ('Sign in', 'Get a quote', and with the referral banner also 'Availability' and 'Commercial') sit below the viewport and scrolling the page never brings them into view.

**Evidence.** styles.css:404-408 `.site-nav { display: none; position: absolute; left: 0; right: 0; top: 62px; background: ...; flex-direction: column; ... padding: 8px 16px 16px; }` (computed overflow-y: visible, max-height: none). Measured at 667x375 with the menu open: nav bottom 495px (555px EN / 574px ES with the ?ref banner) vs innerHeight 375; after scrolling the document 400px the nav's top is still 62px because it rides on the sticky header. Screenshot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/5cd54e72-5e10-5f26-9ec5-c19b545a368a/scratchpad/work/frontend/responsive-visual/shots-extra/navopen__iphone-se-land__en__ref.png.

**Impact.** Anyone browsing in landscape on a phone (or with a large keyboard/short viewport) cannot reach the sign-in and quote links from the menu; with a referral link open, half the menu is unreachable.

**Suggestion.** Add `max-height: calc(100vh - 62px); overflow-y: auto; -webkit-overflow-scrolling: touch` to `.site-nav` when open, or render the open menu as a fixed full-height panel.

### [MEDIUM] Calendar grid cannot shrink: overflows at 320px and in the admin
`styles.css:464` · bug · lens: responsive-visual

The 7-column grid uses `repeat(7, 1fr)`, whose tracks have an `auto` minimum, so each cell is at least as wide as its '3 open' / '3 libres' badge. In Spanish the quote and availability calendars are 306px wide inside a 288px column at 320px, the admin calendar is 396px at every phone width, and the account 'change date' calendar overflows its card.

**Evidence.** styles.css:464 `.cal-grid { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(7, 1fr); gap: 5px; }`, :473 `.cal-state { font-size: 0.66rem; ... padding: 2px 6px }`, :584-586 (<=720px: `.cal-cell { min-height: 50px; padding: 5px 2px } .cal-state { font-size: 0.58rem; padding: 2px 4px }`), :563 `.admin-cal .cal-cell { min-height: 48px }`; the badge markup is calendar.js:111. Measured: quote step 5 / availability ES at 320px grid 306px (cells 40px) with innerWidth reported 322; admin ES grid 396px at 320/360/390 (the 'sáb' column and the › button are cut off); account move calendar inside the padded `.acct-item` 334px at 320px EN. At 320-360px the badge wraps ('3' / 'open'), so cells in one row are 50-65px tall, and the badge text is 0.58rem = 9.3px. Screenshots: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/5cd54e72-5e10-5f26-9ec5-c19b545a368a/scratchpad/work/frontend/responsive-visual/shots-auth/admin__cal-day__es__390.png, shots/quote__step5__es__320.png, shots-auth/account__move__en__320.png.

**Impact.** Spanish visitors picking a date on a 320px phone get a page that is 22px too wide (zoomed out), the owner cannot see Saturdays in the admin calendar on any phone, and the '3 open' availability hint is unreadably small.

**Suggestion.** Use `grid-template-columns: repeat(7, minmax(0, 1fr))` plus `.cal-cell { min-width: 0; padding-inline: 0 }`, shorten the badge on phones (a coloured dot or just the number) and raise its size to at least 0.7rem.

### [MEDIUM] Calendar availability labels render at 9.3px on phones
`styles.css:585` · a11y · lens: accessibility-static · votes 1/1 real (reproduce: medium) · **confirmed**

The "3 open" / "Closed" / "Full" label inside each day button is 0.66rem (10.56 px) on desktop and 0.58rem (9.28 px) under 720 px, and the weekday headers are 11.8 px. That label is the information a visitor uses to pick a day on the booking step, and it is below any readable floor on a phone.

**Evidence.** styles.css:473 .cal-state { font-size: 0.66rem; ... } and :585 .cal-state { font-size: 0.58rem; padding: 2px 4px; } inside @media (max-width: 720px); :465 .cal-wd { font-size: 0.74rem; ... }. Computed in Chromium (measure2.js): .cal-state 9.28 px at 390 px, 10.56 px at 1280 px; .cal-wd 11.84 px; day cells are 49x54 px on a phone, so there is room. Screenshot shot-mobile-calendar.png. (rem resolves against the 16 px root, not the 17 px body size set in :root --text.)

**Impact.** Sighted visitors on phones on quote step 5, availability.html and the account change-date calendar; low-vision users cannot read whether a day is open without zooming.

**Suggestion.** Set .cal-state to at least 0.75rem (12 px) at every width, or on phones show only the number in a larger size and rely on the legend colour, keeping the full state in the button's aria-label as now.

### [MEDIUM] Admin time-clock report widens the phone page to ~400px
`styles.css:605` · ux · lens: responsive-visual

The owner's 'Team time' report puts a 4-button period selector and a 5-column table into the admin column; at 320-390px they are wider than the viewport, so the whole dashboard zooms out, and in Spanish the selector is wider than its 380px desktop sidebar too.

**Evidence.** styles.css:605-608 `.tc-table { width: 100%; ... font-size: 0.9rem }` with 5 uppercase headers from timeclock.js:223 (Who / Hours / Visits / Hrs/visit / $/hour) and the period `.seg` built at timeclock.js:220-222 with the labels i18n.js:589-592 / :1399-1402 ('Últimos 7 días', 'Últimos 30 días'). Measured with mock data: `[data-tc-report]` scrollWidth 380 vs 292 (EN) and 396 vs 317 (ES) at 320px; once the details is open mobile Chrome reports innerWidth 396-413 at 320/360/390 (page zoomed out); at 1024/1440 ES the `.tc-periods` seg is 396px in the 380px `.admin-clock` column and 'Últimos 30 días' wraps to 2 lines even in EN ('Last 30 days'). Screenshots: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/5cd54e72-5e10-5f26-9ec5-c19b545a368a/scratchpad/work/frontend/responsive-visual/shots-auth/admin__timeclock-fix__en__390.png ($ / HOUR column cut off) and shots-auth/admin__timeclock__es__390.png.

**Impact.** The owner checking payroll hours from a phone gets a shrunken, side-scrolling dashboard; the right-most 'hours per visit' and '$ / hour' columns are the ones hidden.

**Suggestion.** Wrap the report in a `div style="overflow-x:auto"` or stack it as cards on <=720px, drop the two derived columns on phones, and let `.tc-periods` wrap to two rows (flex-wrap) instead of 4 fixed columns.

### [MEDIUM] Admin credits and team lists cannot wrap long emails
`styles.css:703` · bug · lens: responsive-visual

Balance rows use `white-space: nowrap` on a flex child without `min-width: 0`, and team rows are a no-wrap flex line, so a long email pushes the credits view to ~475px and the team list to ~371px on phones; in Spanish the team list even widens the whole 380px desktop sidebar and the page at 1024px.

**Evidence.** styles.css:702-703 `.admin-bal { display: flex; justify-content: space-between; gap: 12px; ... } .admin-bal span { overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis; white-space: nowrap; }` (the ellipsis never applies because the span is a flex item with min-width:auto); :590 `.admin-team-list li { display: flex; justify-content: space-between; gap: 10px; align-items: baseline; }`; :562 `.admin-cal { ... display: grid }` lets children stretch to the widest min-content. Measured with mock data: credits tab at 390px `div.admin-credits` scrollWidth 475 vs 358, document 492px wide; at 320px `ul.admin-team-list` 371 vs 292px; at 1024 ES the aside is 413px wide in its 380px column and document.scrollWidth is 1033px ('someone.we.invited.recently@example.com · invitado' + Quitar). Screenshot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/5cd54e72-5e10-5f26-9ec5-c19b545a368a/scratchpad/work/frontend/responsive-visual/shots-auth/admin__credits__es__390.png (the alexandra.montgomery.whitaker@example-mail.com row runs past the right edge).

**Impact.** Owner-only screens, but on a phone the credit ledger and team list become side-scrolling or zoomed out as soon as one client or invitee has a long address, which is common with Gmail-style first.last names.

**Suggestion.** Give the email spans `min-width: 0; flex: 1` (so the ellipsis works) or `overflow-wrap: anywhere`, add `flex-wrap: wrap` to `.admin-team-list li`, and `min-width: 0` on `.admin-cal`/`.admin-clock`.

### [LOW] Minification and CSS splitting would save only 8-15 KB
`.github/workflows/firebase-hosting-merge.yml:29` · perf · lens: performance

The site has no minification and one 43.9 KB stylesheet for twelve pages, but because Hosting gzips automatically the recoverable bytes are small; this quantifies it so effort goes to the findings above rather than to a build step.

**Evidence.** terser + csso on the home page's JS+CSS: 57.9 KB gzipped today vs 49.9 KB minified+gzipped (-8 KB, 14%); quote.html -15 KB (backend.js 8.7 to 4.9 KB, quote.js 9.0 to 6.4 KB). Chrome CSS coverage: styles.css (8.8 KB gz) is 33% used on index.html, 25% on plans.html, 14% on quote.html, 20% on availability.html, so a per-page split saves ~6-7 KB gz. Duplicated inline SVGs are 8 icons / 1.7 KB raw per page (the check path three times in index.html and once more in app.js line 211) and compress to a few hundred bytes. At 1.6 Mbps all of it is 40-75 ms.

**Impact.** None worth acting on today; adding a bundler to a no-build site for this gain would add maintenance without changing what visitors feel.

**Suggestion.** Keep the no-build setup; if a build step is ever added for another reason, run `esbuild --minify` over js/css in the deploy job at this step, but do the cache headers, i18n split and font change first.

### [LOW] Account sign-in tabs mix role=tab with aria-pressed
`account.html:73` · a11y · lens: accessibility-static

The Sign in / Create account switch uses role=tablist and role=tab with aria-selected, but account.js also sets aria-pressed on the tabs (not allowed on that role; axe critical), both tabs point aria-controls at the same form, there is no role=tabpanel and no arrow-key handling.

**Evidence.** account.html:72-74: <div class="seg acct-tabs" role="tablist" ...><button type="button" class="seg-btn" role="tab" id="tab-signin" aria-controls="acct-pw" data-acct-tab="signin" aria-selected="true">; account.js:287: b.setAttribute("aria-selected", ...); b.setAttribute("aria-pressed", ...). axe aria-allowed-attr [critical] on #tab-signin and #tab-signup: "ARIA attribute is not allowed: aria-pressed" in all 8 account.html runs (EN and ES, 390 and 1280, before and after switching tabs). measure2.js: #acct-pw has no role.

**Impact.** Screen-reader users hear contradictory state ("tab, selected, pressed"); a confusion rather than a blocker since Tab and Enter still work.

**Suggestion.** Use the same pattern as the site's other segmented controls (role="group" with aria-pressed buttons; the CSS already keys on aria-pressed) and drop tablist/tab/aria-selected/aria-controls, or implement the full tabs pattern with tabpanels and arrow keys.

### [LOW] Admin sign-in intro collides with the heading
`admin.html:65` · ux · lens: responsive-visual

`.step-intro` carries a -10px top margin that assumes the 22px grid gap of a `.step` container, but on admin.html the heading and intro are direct children of `section.quote-card`, so the intro is pulled up into the heading's line box and the Email label follows with no spacing.

**Evidence.** admin.html:63-65 `<section class="quote-card" data-admin="login"> <h1 class="title" ...>Admin sign-in</h1> <p class="step-intro" ...>` versus styles.css:332 `.step-intro { color: var(--muted); margin-top: -10px; }` and :330 `.step { display: grid; gap: 22px; }`; account.html:69 wraps the same pair in `<section class="step">` and renders correctly. Screenshot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/5cd54e72-5e10-5f26-9ec5-c19b545a368a/scratchpad/work/frontend/responsive-visual/shots/admin__login__en__320.png (heading and intro touching).

**Impact.** Staff sign-in page looks cramped at every width; cosmetic only.

**Suggestion.** Add `class="step"` to the login section (or wrap the h1 and p in a `.step` div) as account.html does.

### [LOW] Hand-edited pages carry stale no-JS fallback copy
`admin.html:89` · content · lens: i18n-copy

Several static strings in the hand-maintained pages no longer match the EN dictionary that replaces them at runtime, including an admin message that tells a user to open a docs file, an out-of-date account meta description, and a footer link that still reads "Client sign-in" on all 12 pages. They are only seen before JavaScript runs or by non-JS readers, but they also show that the static fallback is not being kept in step.

**Evidence.** stale.js: admin.html:89 static "This account isn't on the admin list. See docs/SETUP-firebase.md for how to add it." vs adm.notAdmin "…Ask the owner to invite this email, then sign in here with Google."; admin.html:163 adm.teamHint pre-password wording; account.html:7 meta description "Sign in with your email to see the status…" vs i18n.js:466 "Sign in or create an account to request cleanings and manage your visits…"; footer <a data-i18n="nav.account">Client sign-in</a> on every page vs "Your account"; privacy.html:65 privacy.lead "What MKG Services collects…" vs "What we collect…". docs/make-pages.py line 1 note: pages were hand-edited after generation, "Do NOT re-run this script".

**Impact.** Search snippets and link previews for account.html use the old description; the admin fallback exposes an internal file path. Low today, but each future i18n.js edit widens the gap.

**Suggestion.** Add a small script (the stale.js check in this audit's scratch dir does it) that rewrites each data-i18n element's static text from the EN dictionary, and run it before deploy.

### [LOW] Admin and time clock show stored English service names in Spanish UI
`admin.js:188` · i18n · lens: i18n-copy

The admin request card and the time clock print the raw stored plan/frequency/service strings, which are always English, even when the owner or staff use the Spanish interface. The account page already maps these back to bilingual labels; the admin code does not reuse that.

**Evidence.** admin.js:188: var what = r.service || ((r.plan || "") + (r.frequency ? ", " + r.frequency : "")) …; timeclock.js:48: function serviceText(r) { return r.service || ((r.plan || "") + (r.frequency ? ", " + r.frequency : "")); } (used in job lists and the CSV). Compare account.js:78-83 which resolves x.name.en === r.plan to I.L(p.name). admin.js:39 tv() does translate homeType/facilityType/pets/floors, so the card mixes "Family, Every 2 weeks" with "Casa, un perro". timeclock.js:280 CSV header row is English-only ("email","date","clock in"…).

**Impact.** A Spanish-speaking owner or cleaner reads a half-translated admin page; for a two-language team the plan column in the exported CSV also stays English.

**Suggestion.** Move serviceText() from account.js into app.js (or backend.js) and call it from admin.js and timeclock.js; localize the CSV header through t().

### [LOW] availability.html is indexable but is a thin, daily-changing calendar
`availability.html:16` · seo · lens: seo

The public calendar page has a self-canonical and a sitemap entry but almost no indexable text: a heading, a sentence and a month grid rendered by JavaScript whose content changes every day, so what Google indexes is a snapshot of dates that is stale within hours.

**Evidence.** availability.html:16 `<link rel="canonical" href="https://mkgservices.com/availability.html">`, no robots meta; sitemap.xml:6 lists it. Playwright crawl: 104 words with JavaScript off, 214 with it on (locally every window shows open, the documented not-connected fallback). The nav links it from every page (index.html:80).

**Impact.** A low-quality page in the index that can surface a snippet of out-of-date open slots; it also competes with the home page for the same brand queries.

**Suggestion.** Either add `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">` and drop it from the sitemap, or give it durable copy worth indexing (service days Monday to Saturday, the three start windows, two-day lead time, how booking works) above the live grid.

### [LOW] No custom 404 page; Firebase serves its generic one
`firebase.json:5` · seo · lens: seo

There is no 404.html in the repository, so any mistyped or outdated URL (for example a shared link to an old page, or /plans without .html) shows Firebase's unbranded "Page Not Found" with no navigation, no phone number and no Spanish.

**Evidence.** `ls 404.html` fails in the repo root; firebase.json:5-36 hosting block has no `cleanUrls` and no redirects, so `/plans`, `/quote` or `/index` return the hosting default 404 page. Firebase Hosting serves a root `404.html` automatically when present.

**Impact.** Visitors arriving from a stale link or a typo hit a dead end instead of a quote button; search engines see a soft-branded error with no way back into the site.

**Suggestion.** Add a 404.html built from the site chrome (header, footer, quote and phone CTAs, EN/ES toggle) and consider `"cleanUrls": true` with redirects from the .html URLs, or at least redirects for the obvious extensionless variants.

### [LOW] Cache-Control max-age=0 on versioned JS and CSS forces revalidation each visit
`firebase.json:32` · perf · lens: seo

Every script and stylesheet already carries a cache-busting `?v=22`, yet firebase.json tells browsers to revalidate them on every navigation, so each page view makes six to eight conditional requests before the page is interactive.

**Evidence.** firebase.json:28-33 `"source": "**/*.@(js|css)"` with `"Cache-Control": "public, max-age=0, must-revalidate"`; index.html:304-307 loads `plans.js?v=22`, `i18n.js?v=22`, `app.js?v=22`, `referral.js?v=22`, plus `styles.css?v=22` at :29. Lighthouse's uses-long-cache-ttl audit lists the same six resources on every page.

**Impact.** Returning visitors and the second page of every visit pay a round trip per asset on mobile networks; the HTML max-age=0 rule alone is enough to pick up new `?v=` numbers.

**Suggestion.** Keep max-age=0 for HTML, and set `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` for `**/*.@(js|css)` and the brand images, bumping `?v=` (or using hashed filenames) on each release as already done.

### [LOW] Policy numbers hard-coded in legal and account copy, not read from plans.js
`i18n.js:719` · maintainability · lens: i18n-copy

terms.2.p1 promises "24 hours' notice" in both languages and acct.note.new promises a reply "within one business day" / "en un día hábil", while plans.js exposes policy.changeNoticeHours and business.responseTime for exactly these numbers. The account page's late-change message already interpolates {hours}, so the terms and the status note can drift from what the site enforces.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:719 EN terms.2.p1 "…free of charge with at least 24 hours' notice…" and :1529 ES "…con al menos 24 horas de aviso…" (literal 24, both languages); :496/:1306 acct.note.new "…reply within one business day…" / "…en un día hábil…" (literal) while :1067 q.done.p uses {responseTime}. plans.js:94-95: policy: { changeNoticeHours: 24 } with the comment "terms.html promises a confirmed visit can be changed or cancelled free with this much notice"; account.js:36 tooLate() enforces NOTICE_HOURS from that setting and :131 acct.tooLate interpolates {hours}. README "Before launch" item 5 tells the owner to confirm these policies in i18n.js by hand.

**Impact.** If the owner changes changeNoticeHours or responseTime in plans.js as the README invites, the enforced rule and the published terms disagree in two languages, which is a small legal-exposure and trust issue.

**Suggestion.** Add {hours} and {responseTime} live placeholders to terms.2.p1 and acct.note.new (LIVE already has responseTime) so the legal text follows the configuration.

### [LOW] Masculine-only Spanish address in several client-facing lines
`i18n.js:953` · content · lens: i18n-copy

A handful of Spanish strings assume a male reader ("¿Listo…?", "cuando estés listo", "Él ahorra", "a ti mismo") while the rest of the copy is carefully neutral. Residential cleaning clients skew female, so the two home-page calls to action are the most visible cases.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:953 "band.h2.home": "¿Listo para una semana más limpia?" (EN "Ready for a cleaner week?"); :1179 "commercial:band.h2.home": "¿Listo para un lugar de trabajo más limpio?"; :1310 "acct.note.cancelled": "Cancelada. Reserva de nuevo cuando estés listo."; :1584 "meta.title.refer.desc": "…Él ahorra en su primera visita…" (EN uses "They"); :1604 "refer.rules.4": "No puedes recomendarte a ti mismo…".

**Impact.** Reads as addressed to men on the main CTA banner and the account page; a small but noticeable tone miss for the core residential audience.

**Suggestion.** Rephrase neutrally: "¿Te animas a una semana más limpia?" / "¿Quieres un lugar de trabajo más limpio?", "Reserva de nuevo cuando quieras.", "Tu amigo ahorra…" / "Esa persona ahorra…", "No puedes recomendarte a ti mismo/a" or "No puedes usar tu propio código".

### [LOW] Lexical inconsistencies in Spanish: recámaras vs habitaciones, Texto, ej./p. ej.
`i18n.js:1000` · content · lens: i18n-copy

The Spanish is consistently Latin-American and natural, but a few terms break their own convention: one string says "recámaras" where every other string says "habitaciones"; the top bar's text-message link is the noun "Texto" while the footer says "Escríbenos"; two placeholders abbreviate "for example" differently; and a couple of Spain-leaning words appear in copy aimed at Charlotte's mostly Mexican and Central American community.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:1000 "q.fu.note": "Según una casa de {bedrooms} recámaras y {bathrooms} baños." vs :881 dial.fine and :1029 q.s3.p "…{bedrooms} habitaciones…" (same screen, step 3 of the quote). :861 "top.text": "Texto" vs :963 "footer.text": "Escríbenos" (both label the sms: link). :1008 "q.zipPh": "ej. 28202" vs :1544 "q.ref.ph": "p. ej. K7M2QX". :1016 "q.homeType.Townhome": "Casa adosada" (Spain usage; US Spanish speakers say "townhouse"/"casa en hilera"); plans.js:143,183 "encimeras" (Spain) where "mostradores"/"cubiertas" is the Latin-American term.

**Impact.** Minor credibility wobble for Spanish readers on the quote page and the top bar; "Casa adosada" may make a townhome owner pick "Casa" instead, which affects the estimate inputs the owner sees.

**Suggestion.** Change recámaras to habitaciones, use "Mensaje" or "Escríbenos" in the top bar, standardize on "p. ej.", and prefer "Townhouse" and "mostradores" (or "cubiertas") for the local audience.

### [LOW] Spanish calendar shows "1 libres" for one open window
`i18n.js:1253` · i18n · lens: accessibility-static

Count placeholders are substituted without a singular form in Spanish: cal.open becomes "1 libres" when one start window is left (the lemon "some windows left" state), and dial.fine, q.s3.p and q.fu.note produce "casa de 1 habitaciones y 1 baños". The English strings avoid the problem ("1 open", "1-bedroom") and the engine already has plural() for the steppers and ref.friends.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:1253: "cal.open": "{n} libres", used by calendar.js:101-102 t("cal.open", { n: info.openCount }); i18n.js:881: "dial.fine": "Estimado para una casa de {bedrooms} habitaciones y {bathrooms} baños."; :1029 q.s3.p and :1000 q.fu.note use the same pattern; i18n.js:1680: function plural(keyBase, n) { return t(keyBase + (n === 1 ? ".one" : ".other")); }.

**Impact.** Spanish-speaking visitors see ungrammatical copy at the moments that matter: the last open window on a day, and the one-bedroom estimate sentence the dial and quote step 3 read out.

**Suggestion.** Add .one/.other variants (cal.open.one: "{n} libre") and call I.plural() from calendar.js; reword the Spanish size sentences to avoid the number-noun agreement ("{bedrooms} hab., {bathrooms} baño(s)") or use plural keys there too.

### [LOW] Spanish plural error: calendar cell reads "1 libres"
`i18n.js:1253` · i18n · lens: i18n-copy

cal.open is "{n} libres" with no singular form, so a day with exactly one open window shows "1 libres" on the calendar grid in Spanish. English "{n} open" is number-neutral so the author never needed a .one/.other pair here, but Spanish does.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:1253: "cal.open": "{n} libres" vs :443 "cal.open": "{n} open". calendar.js:95-96: state = t("cal.open", { n: info.openCount }) for both the is-few and is-open states; with plans.js availability.capacity 2 and three residential windows, openCount === 1 happens whenever two of three windows fill, which is the normal busy-day state. The engine already supports plurals (i18n.js:1680 plural(keyBase, n)) and uses them for ref.friends.one/.other.

**Impact.** Visible grammatical error on the public availability calendar and in the quote flow for Spanish visitors, on exactly the days that are nearly booked.

**Suggestion.** Add cal.open.one "{n} libre" / cal.open.other "{n} libres" (EN "{n} open" for both) and call I.plural("cal.open", n) in calendar.js.

### [LOW] Spanish visitors see English first, then a re-render
`i18n.js:1774` · i18n · lens: performance

The HTML is always English and `lang="en"`; apply() runs at DOMContentLoaded (init registered on line 1774), so a visitor with ?lang=es, a saved choice or a Spanish browser sees English copy until all scripts have arrived, then every data-i18n node is rewritten.

**Evidence.** 4G mobile `index.html?lang=es`: first paint 716 ms in English, `<html lang>` and the h1 ("Tu casa limpia, en automático.") switched at 918 ms; `quote.html?lang=es`: 636 ms to 922 ms. The translation itself shifted layout only 0.0002-0.031, so the issue is the flash, not movement. The detect() order (URL, localStorage, navigator.language, lines 1647-1653) runs only inside this script.

**Impact.** Every page view by a Spanish-speaking customer flashes the wrong language for 0.2-0.3 s on 4G (about 1 s on 3G), and assistive tech and crawlers get lang=en until JS runs.

**Suggestion.** Either generate static Spanish pages with docs/make-pages.py, or add a ~10-line inline script in the head that repeats detect(), sets `html lang` immediately and adds a class that keeps `[data-i18n]` text hidden until apply() has run for non-English.

### [LOW] Oversized favicons and ~1 MB of unused brand PNGs deployed
`index.html:15` · perf · lens: performance

favicon-192.png (75 KB) and apple-touch-icon.png (67 KB) are full RGBA PNGs 3x larger than a quantized version, and about 985 KB of brand files nothing references are published because firebase.json's ignore list (lines 7-15) only excludes brand/*.py and brand/source/**.

**Evidence.** Sizes: brand/favicon-192.png 75,026 B, brand/apple-touch-icon.png 67,413 B; the same images palette-quantized with sharp come to 22.5 KB and 20.5 KB. grep across *.html/*.js finds no reference to favicon-512.png (390,382 B), favicon-180.png (67 KB), favicon-48.png (7 KB), logo-badge-1024/512/mono-1024/reverse-1024.png (493 KB together), logo-mark-512.png (24 KB) or the three logo-mark SVGs. logo-full-640.png (574 KB) and logo-full-320.png (175 KB) are referenced only as `<picture>` fallbacks for browsers without WebP.

**Impact.** Small: the 192 px icon is fetched by Chrome on Android for the tab switcher/home-screen shortcut and the touch icon by Safari when bookmarking, 67-75 KB each on a 4G phone; the unreferenced files cost visitors nothing but are public and inflate every deploy.

**Suggestion.** Quantize the icons (pngquant or sharp palette) and delete or ignore the unused PNGs in firebase.json; keep brand/favicon-512.png only if you add it to a web manifest.

### [LOW] Price-estimator aria-label sits on a role-less div and is dropped
`index.html:113` · a11y · lens: accessibility-dynamic

The dial container carries aria-label="Plan price estimator" (translated) but has no role, and aria-label on a generic div is ignored, so screen-reader users land in a run of twelve buttons with no region name.

**Evidence.** index.html:113 and commercial.html:95 `<div class="dial on-dark" data-i18n-attr="aria-label:dial.aria" aria-label="Plan price estimator">`. Playwright ARIA snapshot of .dial lists its children (group 'Plan', buttons, status, group 'How often') directly with no named group/region; evaluate -> { tag: 'DIV', role: null }. The dial has 12 Tab stops and no heading.

**Impact.** Screen-reader users cannot tell what the cluster of plan/stepper/frequency buttons at the top of the home page is for. Low.

**Suggestion.** Make it `<section class="dial" aria-labelledby="dial-h">` with a visually-hidden <h2 id="dial-h">, or add role="region" (or role="group") to the div so the existing translated label is exposed.

### [LOW] Logo alt text stays English in Spanish mode
`index.html:206` · i18n · lens: accessibility-static

The informative logo images (the "Charlotte's own" badge and every footer badge) carry a fixed English alt, "MKG Services, Charlotte, North Carolina, established 2014", with no data-i18n-attr, so Spanish screen-reader users hear English read by a Spanish voice for the one image with content; the engine already translates attributes via data-i18n-attr.

**Evidence.** index.html:206: <img src="brand/logo-full-640.png" alt="MKG Services, Charlotte, North Carolina, established 2014" ...> and :280 (footer); the same footer alt appears in all 12 pages (14 occurrences of alt="MKG), and no file contains data-i18n-attr="alt:. measure2.js with ?lang=es: document.documentElement.lang = "es", every aria-label on the page is Spanish, alts remain ["", "MKG Services, Charlotte, North Carolina, established 2014", ...].

**Impact.** Spanish screen-reader users; small.

**Suggestion.** Add data-i18n-attr="alt:brand.badgeAlt" to those images with an es string ("MKG Services, Charlotte, Carolina del Norte, desde 2014"), or mark the footer badge decorative (alt="") since the company name sits beside it.

### [LOW] One 640px logo for every screen; header mark upscaled
`index.html:206` · perf · lens: performance

The "Charlotte's own" badge uses a single logo-full-640.webp (108.8 KB) displayed at 220 CSS px on desktop and 168 px on phones with no srcset/sizes, and the header's logo-full-112.webp is drawn at 56 CSS px, which on a 3x phone is 168 device pixels, so it is upscaled.

**Evidence.** Measured desktop 1280x800 first view requests both brand/logo-full-640.webp (108.8 KB) and logo-full-320.webp (43.7 KB); `naturalWidth` 640 vs rendered width 220 on a 1x screen (2.9x oversampled; the existing 320 file would do). On the phone profile `loading="lazy"` kept it off the first view. Re-encoding from brand/source with sharp: 640 px WebP q80 = 84 KB (-23%), AVIF q50 = 46 KB (-58%); 112 px: 8.5 KB / 6.6 KB. Header `<img>` is 56x56 CSS px (styles.css line 110) with a 112 px source; DPR 3 needs 168 px.

**Impact.** About 65 KB wasted per 1x desktop visit (off the critical path), and a slightly soft brand mark in the header on the phones most customers use.

**Suggestion.** Add `srcset="brand/logo-full-320.webp 320w, brand/logo-full-640.webp 640w" sizes="(max-width: 980px) 168px, 220px"` (same for the PNG fallback), export a 168/224 px header variant, and re-encode the WebPs at q80 or add AVIF sources.

### [LOW] Logo alt text is English-only on every page
`index.html:206` · a11y · lens: i18n-copy

The only visible-to-assistive-tech string on the pages that bypasses i18n is the brand image alt text, repeated twice on the home pages and once in every footer. Spanish screen-reader users hear an English sentence.

**Evidence.** hardcoded.js (jsdom scan of all 13 pages for text and aria-label/alt/title/placeholder outside data-i18n) found only this and the brand name: index.html:206 and :280, commercial.html:188 and :262, and the footer badge in account.html:206, admin.html:180, availability.html:91, plans.html:161, commercial-plans.html:161, quote.html:282, commercial-quote.html:292, refer.html:101, privacy.html:90, terms.html:90: alt="MKG Services, Charlotte, North Carolina, established 2014" with no data-i18n-attr="alt:…".

**Impact.** Minor: one untranslated announcement per page for Spanish screen-reader users; also the one place a future editor will not find the copy in i18n.js.

**Suggestion.** Add a brand.logoAlt key in both languages and data-i18n-attr="alt:brand.logoAlt" on the <img> elements (the engine already supports attribute keys).

### [LOW] Comparison table scroll region unreachable by keyboard
`plans.html:105` · a11y · lens: accessibility-static · votes 3/3 real (impact: medium, reproduce: medium, refute: medium) · **confirmed**

The comparison table sits in <div style="overflow-x: auto;"> with nothing focusable inside, so whenever it overflows (Spanish and commercial versions at 390 px, every version at 320 px or 400% zoom) keyboard users cannot scroll it sideways to the Complete column.

**Evidence.** plans.html:105 and commercial-plans.html:105: <div style="overflow-x: auto;"> wrapping <table class="compare" data-render-comparison>. axe scrollable-region-focusable [serious] on #compare > .container > div:nth-child(2) in plans.html (ES, 390) and commercial-plans.html (EN and ES, 390): "Element should have focusable content / Element should be focusable".

**Impact.** Keyboard-only and screen-magnifier users at narrow widths or high zoom lose the right-hand plan columns of the comparison.

**Suggestion.** Add tabindex="0" role="region" and an aria-label (or aria-labelledby the h2) to the wrapper, or let the table stack under 720 px instead of scrolling.

### [LOW] Start-window times are unlocalized and ambiguous ("11:30–2:30")
`plans.js:80` · content · lens: i18n-copy

Slot time ranges are single strings shown verbatim in both languages; three of four omit am/pm, so "11:30–2:30" relies on the reader inferring noon crossing, and Spanish readers who expect 24-hour times get neither convention.

**Evidence.** plans.js:79-82: time: "8:00–11:00", "11:30–2:30", "3:00–6:00", "6:30–9:30 pm" (only the last carries a meridiem). calendar.js:179 slotLabel() appends " (" + time + ")" unchanged for both languages; the same strings reach the quote review, the account page, admin cards and the Formspree email (app.js:361-363).

**Impact.** Visitors picking a window, and clients re-reading their confirmation, can misread the midday and afternoon windows; slightly worse for Spanish speakers used to "14:30".

**Suggestion.** Make time bilingual like label ({ en: "11:30 am–2:30 pm", es: "11:30–14:30" }) or store start/end hours and format with toLocaleTimeString(locale, { hour: "numeric", minute: "2-digit" }).

### [LOW] Progress list has no aria-current; load moves focus to h1
`quote.html:74` · a11y · lens: accessibility-dynamic

The six-step progress list marks the current step only with CSS classes, and at phone widths the non-current labels are display:none, so the current step is visual-only. Separately, go(1) runs on load and focuses the step-1 heading, so the first Tab skips the header nav.

**Evidence.** quote.html:74-81 `<ol class="progress" aria-label="Progress"><li data-step-mark="1">...`; quote.js:356-360 only toggles is-done/is-current; grep shows aria-current only on nav links. Observed: all six li have aria-current=null. styles.css:420-421 hides non-current labels under 640px. quote.js:595 `go(1)` -> observed activeElement right after load = H1 'Where's the home?' (quote.js:366-367 sets tabindex=-1 and focuses it).

**Impact.** Screen-reader users hear 'list, 6 items' with no indication of position; keyboard users must Shift+Tab to reach the site navigation. Low.

**Suggestion.** In go(), set aria-current="step" on the current li and remove it from the others; skip the heading focus during init (only focus on user-initiated step changes).

### [LOW] Quote progress bar carries no current-step semantics
`quote.html:74` · a11y · lens: accessibility-static

The six-step progress list marks the current step only with CSS classes (is-current, is-done): no aria-current="step" and no "Step 2 of 6" text. Under 720 px every label except the current one is display:none, so a screen reader hears a six-item list with five empty items.

**Evidence.** quote.html:74-81: <ol class="progress" aria-label="Progress"><li data-step-mark="1"><span data-i18n="q.step.1">Home</span></li> ...; quote.js:356-360 only toggles is-done and is-current; styles.css:420-421: .progress li span { display: none; } .progress li.is-current span { display: block; }. measure2.js after Continue: li classes is-done|Home, is-current|Details, |Plan ... with aria-current empty on all six.

**Impact.** Screen-reader users do not know how far through the request they are; the heading focus on each step (a strength) means they still progress.

**Suggestion.** In go(), set aria-current="step" on the current <li> and remove it from the others; replace display:none with .visually-hidden for non-current labels on phones and add a visually-hidden "Step n of 6" to each step heading.

### [LOW] Review list markup invalid; six identical Change buttons
`quote.js:344` · a11y · lens: accessibility-static

Each review row is <dl><div><dt>..</dt><dd>..</dd><button>Change</button></div></dl>; a button is not allowed in a dl group (axe serious), and the six buttons share the accessible name "Change", so a screen reader's button list cannot tell them apart.

**Evidence.** quote.js:344-345: '<div class="review-row"><dt>' + A.esc(r[0]) + "</dt><dd>" + A.esc(r[1]) + "</dd>" + (editable ? '<button type="button" data-goto="' + r[2] + '">' + A.esc(t("q.change")) + "</button>" : "") + "</div>". axe definition-list [serious] on dl[data-review] in all 16 step-6 and done runs: "dl element has direct children that are not allowed: div > button". measure2.js review dump shows six buttons all named "Change".

**Impact.** Minor confusion for screen-reader users on the review step; sighted users unaffected.

**Suggestion.** Move the button inside the <dd> and name each one from its row label (aria-label = t("q.change") + " " + r[0], e.g. "Change contact").

### [LOW] Dismissing the referral banner leaves focus on <body>
`referral.js:178` · a11y · lens: accessibility-dynamic

The banner's Dismiss handler removes the banner element that contains the focused button without moving focus anywhere.

**Evidence.** referral.js:176-179 `el.querySelector(".ref-banner-x").addEventListener("click", function () { ... el.remove(); });`. Observed on index.html?ref=K7M2QX: banner has role="status" and the X has aria-label 'Dismiss'; focus it, Enter -> banner count 0, document.activeElement = BODY.

**Impact.** Keyboard and screen-reader users restart from the top of the page after dismissing the banner on every public page.

**Suggestion.** Before `el.remove()`, focus the next landmark element (e.g. `document.querySelector(".site-header .brand")`) or the first element after the banner.

### [LOW] Smooth scrolling and card motion ignore reduced-motion
`styles.css:33` · a11y · lens: accessibility-static

The price pop, button transitions and details animation are correctly gated behind prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference, but html { scroll-behavior: smooth }, the switch-card lift and shadow transition, the calendar cell lift and calendar.js's smooth scrollIntoView are not, so in-page anchor jumps (#faq, #single-cleans) and the day-pick scroll still animate for people who asked for no motion.

**Evidence.** styles.css:33: html { scroll-behavior: smooth; -webkit-text-size-adjust: 100%; } outside the @media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference) block at lines 202-208; styles.css:440: .switch-card { ... transition: transform 0.18s ease, box-shadow 0.18s ease, border-color 0.18s ease; }, :469 .cal-cell { transition: transform 0.12s ... }; calendar.js:127: w.scrollIntoView({ block: "nearest", behavior: "smooth" }). Chromium context with reducedMotion: "reduce" (measure2.js): computed scroll-behavior = smooth and .switch-card transition-duration = 0.18s, while .btn transition is 0s and the details animation is none.

**Impact.** Visitors with vestibular disorders who set reduce-motion; the smooth anchor scroll on the home page is the most likely trigger.

**Suggestion.** Move scroll-behavior: smooth into the no-preference block (or add @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { html { scroll-behavior: auto } .switch-card, .switch-arrow, .cal-cell { transition: none } }) and choose the scrollIntoView behavior from matchMedia("(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)").

### [LOW] In-page anchors scroll under the sticky header
`styles.css:102` · ux · lens: responsive-visual

Links to #faq, #single-cleans and the other section anchors jump so the section's top is under the 62-69px sticky header; on phones the heading's top 7px is hidden, and because scrolling is smooth the user watches the heading slide under the bar. No scroll-margin-top or scroll-padding-top is set anywhere.

**Evidence.** styles.css:102 `.site-header { position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 20; ... }`, :33 `html { scroll-behavior: smooth; }`, :418 `.section { padding: 56px 0 }` at <=720px; `grep -n scroll-margin styles.css` returns nothing. Measured after `scrollIntoView` on each anchor target: section top 0px vs header bottom 63px (phones) / 69px (desktop); h2 top 56px vs header bottom 63px at 320-390 on index, commercial, plans, commercial-plans and quote (results.json anchors).

**Impact.** Mobile visitors tapping FAQ or Single cleans in the menu land with the heading partly covered; minor but it affects the two most-used in-page links.

**Suggestion.** Add `[id] { scroll-margin-top: 80px; }` (or `html { scroll-padding-top: 80px }`).

### [LOW] Steppers, language toggle and calendar controls under 44px
`styles.css:188` · a11y · lens: responsive-visual

The bedroom/bathroom (and sq ft/restroom) stepper buttons are 34x36px, the EN/ES buttons 38x28px, the month arrows 38x38px and calendar cells 38px wide at 320px, all below the 44px tap-target guideline; the steppers are the most-used control on the site.

**Evidence.** styles.css:188 `.stepper { display: inline-grid; grid-template-columns: 34px auto 34px; ... }` and :193 `.stepper-btn { ... height: 36px }` (468 stepper buttons measured at 34x36 across the 320-390px captures); :91 `.lang-btn { ... padding: 4px 10px }` → 38x28; :459 `.cal-nav { width: 38px; height: 38px }`; `.cal-cell` 38x50 at 320px (results.json smallTargets).

**Impact.** Mis-taps on the −/+ buttons change the estimate the visitor is reading; the language toggle is hard to hit for Spanish speakers landing on the English default.

**Suggestion.** Make the stepper columns and buttons 44px, give `.lang-btn` 44px min-height (padding 10px 12px) and `.cal-nav` 44px; the pills still fit inside the dial.

### [LOW] Plan-card CTAs wrap unevenly at 981-1024px and ≤360px
`styles.css:234` · ux · lens: responsive-visual

At 981-1024px the three plan cards are ~312px wide, so 'Get a quote for Essentials' and 'Get a quote for Complete' wrap to two lines while 'Get a quote for Family' stays on one, giving three different button heights side by side; the same happens at ≤360px and for all three commercial cards.

**Evidence.** styles.css:234 `.plans-grid { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(3, 1fr); gap: 20px; }` applies above 980px; :237 `.plan-card { padding: 26px }`; button text from app.js:231 `t("plans.cta", { name: L(p.name) })`. Measured at 981, 1000 and 1024 (EN and ES): 2-3 of the 3 card CTAs on 2 lines; the commercial dial's 5-option seg also wraps labels at 981-1000 in EN. Screenshot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/5cd54e72-5e10-5f26-9ec5-c19b545a368a/scratchpad/work/frontend/responsive-visual/shots/plans__default__en__1024.png.

**Impact.** Common laptop widths (1024, 1280 with a bookmarks sidebar) show a slightly ragged pricing grid on the page meant to close the sale.

**Suggestion.** Shorten the card CTA to 'Get a quote' / 'Pedir cotización' (the plan name is the card heading) or reduce `.plan-card` padding to 20px below 1100px.

### [LOW] Estimate box: unit label wraps beside the price at 320px
`styles.css:370` · ux · lens: responsive-visual

The estimate box reserves an `auto` column for the Save pill, so at 320px the price line has only ~150px and the unit breaks into 'per / visit' or 'one / visit' next to the big number.

**Evidence.** styles.css:368-375 `.estimate-box { ... display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1fr auto; gap: 6px 18px; } .estimate-price { display: flex; align-items: baseline; gap: 8px; }`. At 320px quote step 3 shows '$195 per' / 'visit' with the pill on the right and the follow-up box '$126 one' / 'visit' (both languages). Screenshot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/5cd54e72-5e10-5f26-9ec5-c19b545a368a/scratchpad/work/frontend/responsive-visual/shots/quote__step3-followup__en__320.png (crop around the estimate box) and shots/quote__step3__en__320.png.

**Impact.** Polish only, but it is the number the visitor is about to commit to.

**Suggestion.** Let the pill drop below the price on narrow screens (`grid-template-columns: 1fr` at <=360px with the pill `justify-self: start`), or add `white-space: nowrap` to `.price-unit`.

### [LOW] Review rows on phones: Change button drops to its own centred row
`styles.css:422` · ux · lens: responsive-visual

At <=720px the review row grid is `1fr auto` with the value spanning both columns, so the auto-placed Change button lands in a third row in column 1; the second column collapses to 0px and every row becomes label / value / a centred 'Change' link.

**Evidence.** styles.css:422-423 `.review-row { grid-template-columns: 1fr auto; } .review-row dd { grid-column: 1 / -1; }` with the button appended after the dd in quote.js:344-345. Computed `grid-template-columns` on every `.review-row` at 320/360/390 is '238px 0px' / '278px 0px' / '308px 0px'; the screenshot shows 'Change' centred under each value, three lines per row. Screenshot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/5cd54e72-5e10-5f26-9ec5-c19b545a368a/scratchpad/work/frontend/responsive-visual/shots/quote__step6__en__320.png.

**Impact.** The review step is roughly a third taller than needed on phones and the edit affordance looks like a stray centred link rather than a row action.

**Suggestion.** Place the button explicitly: `.review-row button { grid-column: 2; grid-row: 1; justify-self: end; }` so it sits beside the label.

### [LOW] Admin and account inputs under 16px trigger iOS zoom
`styles.css:611` · a11y · lens: responsive-visual

Three places override the 16.5px form size: the time-clock fix row, the credit amount box and the account invite-link field inherit 13.6-14.7px, so Safari on iPhone zooms the page when they are focused.

**Evidence.** styles.css:611 `.tc-fixrow label { font-size: 0.85rem; ... }` with `.input, .select { font: inherit }` at :336-337 → the datetime-local inputs and the job select compute to 13.6px; :710 `.admin-credit label { ... font-size: 0.9rem }` → `[data-credit-amount]` 14.4px; :674 `.ref-link .input { ... font-size: 0.92rem }` → `#ref-link` 14.72px. Measured in the signed-in sweep at every width; all other inputs are 16.5px.

**Impact.** Owner correcting a shift or applying credit from a phone, and clients copying their invite link, get an unexpected zoom-in that hides the surrounding form.

**Suggestion.** Set `font-size: 1rem` (>=16px) on `.tc-fixrow .input, .tc-fixrow .select, .admin-credit .input, .ref-link .input`.

### What this area found well done

- Step changes in the quote form move focus to the new step's <h1> (quote.js:366-367 sets tabindex=-1, focus with preventScroll, then scrolls to top), including the 'Request sent' confirmation and the review-page 'Change' buttons; verified with Playwright: document.activeElement is the new heading after every Continue/Back/Change/Send. _(accessibility-dynamic)_
- The language toggle is a model: buttons carry lang attributes and aria-pressed (i18n.js:1740), <html lang> is updated (1724), and every dynamically rendered widget is rebuilt with translated ARIA (frequency group 'Cada cuánto' with 'Semanal, ahorra 15%', steppers 'Menos habitaciones', calendar 'Mes anterior' and 'jueves, 22 de octubre, 3 libres', weekday headers, slot statuses). The EN and ES dictionaries have identical key sets (774 each) with no placeholder mismatches and no untranslated strings; no missing-key console warnings on any page in either language. _(accessibility-dynamic)_
- Segmented controls and plan pickers use real <button>s with aria-pressed, CSS keyed to that state (styles.css:180), and accessible names that include the discount ('Every 2 weeks, 10% off'); steppers have labelled −/+ buttons and a polite <output> that announces '3 bed'; the estimate box and referral-code status are polite live regions, so prices and code validation are announced. _(accessibility-dynamic)_
- Calendar day buttons carry complete accessible names with state ('Thursday, October 22, 3 open', 'Sunday, October 25, Closed'), unbookable days are genuinely disabled, windows read 'Morning 8:00–11:00 2 of 2 open' with aria-pressed, and on the quote page the chosen day and window are echoed in a polite live region (quote.html:219: 'Requested: Thursday, October 22, Morning (8:00–11:00)'), so the pick itself is announced even though focus is lost. _(accessibility-dynamic)_
- Native semantics are used wherever they fit: <details>/<summary> FAQ (Enter and Space toggle, verified), native confirm()/prompt() for destructive admin actions, nav toggle with aria-expanded/aria-controls, password show/hide with aria-pressed/aria-controls, a 3px :focus-visible outline with an on-dark variant (styles.css:48-49), and animations guarded by prefers-reduced-motion (styles.css:202). axe reports no violations on index, commercial, quote steps 1-4 and the done state, admin sign-in, privacy and terms beyond the shared top-bar landmark note. _(accessibility-dynamic)_
- Step changes in the quote flow move keyboard focus to the new step's <h1> (quote.js:366-367, tabindex=-1 with preventScroll) and to the "Request sent" heading on success; verified in Chromium: after Continue, document.activeElement is the H1 "Tell us about it", so screen-reader users always hear where they are. _(accessibility-static)_
- A consistent, high-contrast focus ring everywhere: global :focus-visible 3 px pine outline (styles.css:48), switched to white on the dark dial and band (.on-dark, line 49), and the custom chip radios and plan options keep a ring through input:focus-visible + span (lines 353, 363). Every text token passes AA by a margin (muted 6.0:1 on white, pine 6.3:1, dial secondary text 6.1 to 9.8:1, ink on the lemon pill 10.5:1); axe reported zero color-contrast violations across 136 runs. _(accessibility-static)_
- Calendar day buttons are fully named ("Thursday, October 22, 3 open") with aria-pressed for the selected day, unbookable days are disabled rather than hidden, previous/next buttons are labelled and translated, and the month heading is a polite live region (calendar.js:88-111); only the grid wrapper semantics need changing. _(accessibility-static)_
- Language handling is thorough: <html lang> follows the toggle and ?lang= deep links, the EN/ES buttons carry their own lang and aria-pressed, and every aria-label, title, meta description, placeholder and JS-generated control label is translated (in ES mode all 28 aria-labels on the home page are Spanish, e.g. "Menos habitaciones", "Semanal, ahorra 15%"). _(accessibility-static)_
- Icons and images are handled with care: every decorative SVG is aria-hidden, the header logo is hidden inside an aria-labelled brand link, the informative badge has a descriptive alt, the QR code is role="img" with a translated label, steppers announce their value through <output aria-live>, segmented controls expose aria-pressed with descriptive names, and the price pop and transitions are gated behind prefers-reduced-motion. _(accessibility-static)_
- Every indexable page has an absolute self-referencing canonical, which correctly folds the ?lang=, ?ref=, ?plan= and ?service= URL variants into one indexed URL (index.html:19, plans.html:19, refer.html:16). _(seo)_
- Private surfaces are handled correctly: account.html and admin.html carry noindex, have no canonical and are not in the sitemap, the quote pages carry noindex, and robots.txt does not block them, so the noindex can actually be seen by crawlers. _(seo)_
- Each page has a unique, translated title and meta description driven by data-i18n/data-i18n-attr, and the segment-aware `commercial:` key lookup (i18n.js:1655-1663) gives the commercial pages their own titles rather than reusing the residential ones; document.documentElement.lang is updated when the language changes. _(seo)_
- Image handling is clean: the logo is served as WebP through <picture> with width and height set and lazy loading below the fold, which Lighthouse confirms with CLS 0 on every page measured; the decorative header mark has alt="" with an aria-label on the link, and the content logos carry a descriptive, location-bearing alt. _(seo)_
- NAP is consistent where it appears (telephone +1-980-319-0015 in JSON-LD matches plans.js:28 and the footer), the JSON-LD has a stable @id, logo and foundingDate, and Lighthouse best-practices scores 100 with total blocking time of 50 ms or less on every page. _(seo)_
- First paint is fast on every audited page: HTML 5 KB and CSS 8.8 KB gzipped, scripts at the end of body, so on a throttled 4G phone index/plans/quote/commercial/availability all paint at 0.63-0.76 s and LCP equals first paint (text), far inside the 2.5 s "good" band. _(performance)_
- Images are handled well: every <img> has width/height, WebP via <picture> with PNG fallback, loading="lazy" below the fold; measured image-caused layout shift is zero on all pages and the phone first view never downloads the big badge until scrolled. _(performance)_
- Main-thread work is light: at 4x CPU slowdown no long task exceeded 180 ms, total long-task time was under 0.4 s on the home page, and no page produced a console error or warning (the only 404 is the expected init.json when Firebase is absent); pages are usable before Firebase arrives and `ready` never rejects. _(performance)_
- Font setup has the right safety nets: preconnect to both Google hosts, display=swap, and a fallback stack whose metrics are close enough that the swap shifted layout by only 0.0035; the five-weight request resolves to a single 75 KB variable file, so declaring weights costs nothing extra. _(performance)_
- Assets are already versioned (?v=22) and Hosting compresses text automatically, so the largest remaining caching win is a two-line firebase.json change with no risk of stale files, and the generated-pages tooling (docs/make-pages.py) gives a ready path to pre-render the dial, plan cards and Spanish pages. _(performance)_
- Type and spacing tokens hold up: across 830 captures (12 pages, 2 languages, 320-1440px) no heading or paragraph ever overflowed its box, including the Spanish display headlines at 320px, thanks to clamp()-based sizes (styles.css:61-62) and the gutter/body retune at 720px (styles.css:399). _(responsive-visual)_
- The quote form degrades cleanly on phones: .two-up grids collapse to one column, choice chips wrap, the progress bar hides every label except the current step (styles.css:419-421), and stepper values never overflow even at '7+ bed' or '20,000+ sq ft' in either language. _(responsive-visual)_
- The mobile menu has 48px rows, closes on link tap (app.js:311-316), and the referral banner stacks neatly above it at 320px (shots/index__refbanner__en__320.png); the sticky header stays a compact 62px with a translucent backdrop. _(responsive-visual)_
- Mobile-first thinking in the signed-in areas: the admin grid puts the time clock first on phones (styles.css:579-580), the calendar auto-scrolls to the windows list on narrow screens (calendar.js:127), and the account page's QR panel reflows to a single column at 720px (styles.css:718-722). _(responsive-visual)_
- Form fields inherit the 16.5px body size (styles.css:336-337) so the main quote and sign-in inputs never trigger iOS focus zoom; the web font is preconnected with display=swap and text-size-adjust is pinned (styles.css:33), so the Bricolage Grotesque layout is what phones actually render. _(responsive-visual)_
- Dictionary parity is exact: a scripted load of i18n.js (parity.js) found 774 EN and 774 ES keys with zero missing, zero empty, zero placeholder mismatches, no duplicate keys in either map, every commercial: override backed by a base key, and every data-i18n/data-i18n-attr key in all 13 HTML files and every literal or prefix-built t()/plural() call in the JS resolvable in both languages (dynamic prefixes status., acct.note., ref.type., q.day., review.time., review.prefers., err.phone., q.homeType./q.facilityType., review.pets./review.floors. all have full value coverage against the form values and backend status words). _(i18n-copy)_
- HTML wiring is complete: a jsdom scan of every text node and aria-label/alt/title/placeholder found only the brand name and the logo alt outside i18n; titles and meta descriptions use data-i18n/data-i18n-attr, the noscript message is written in both languages inline, and every confirm()/prompt()/alert() goes through t(), with the destructive delete accepting both DELETE and BORRAR (admin.js:275). _(i18n-copy)_
- The engine is thoughtfully built: segment-namespaced lookup with fallback (i18n.js:1658-1667), t() returning the key and logging when missing, apply() preserving page text for unknown keys, html lang and aria-pressed updated on switch, ?lang= persisted and rewritten into the URL, referral links carrying &lang=es for Spanish sharers (referral.js:59-62), and Firebase Auth emails sent with auth.languageCode set to the visitor's language before every send (backend.js:180-249). Playwright loads of all 12 pages with ?lang=es produced no i18n warnings. _(i18n-copy)_
- Spanish copy quality is high and consistent: natural Latin-American register, correct accents and ñ throughout, a consistent tú voice from marketing through account, admin and the privacy/terms pages, and all dates and times formatted through Intl with es-US/en-US (calendar.js:23-25, account.js:48, admin.js:33, timeclock.js:34), including stripping the trailing period from Spanish weekday abbreviations (calendar.js:84). _(i18n-copy)_
- Language-aware data handling: form values are stored as stable English words and translated on display via tv() (admin.js:39), the request records the client's language (quote.js:386) which the admin shows as "escribió en español" and the owner email flags as "Language: Spanish (reply in Spanish if you can)" (quote.js:433), and the client's account page maps the stored English plan/frequency/service names back to bilingual labels (account.js:78-83). _(i18n-copy)_

## Product (89 findings: 1 critical, 28 high, 42 medium, 18 low)

_Scope: user experience, conversion, pricing logic and presentation, content, legal, and growth gaps versus the plan._

### [CRITICAL] Admin credit math uses the estimate, not the confirmed price
`admin.js:123` · pricing · lens: pricing-logic

When the owner applies referral credit, the cap and the 'Client pays' figure are computed from the site estimate even after a final price (quotedPrice) has been set. The client's account then shows a confirmed price next to a 'You pay' line derived from a different number.

**Evidence.** admin.js:123 `function visitPrice(r) { return typeof r.estimatePerVisit === "number" ? r.estimatePerVisit : typeof r.estimateOneVisit === "number" ? r.estimateOneVisit : null; }` (quotedPrice never consulted); admin.js:161 `Math.min(b, INV.cap(visitPrice(r)))` and admin.js:309 `youPay: ... Math.max(0, rounded(price - n))` where `price = visitPrice(r)` (line 305). account.js:72 shows `quotedPrice` as 'confirmed price' and account.js:112-114 then prints `creditApplied` and `youPay`. By contrast timeclock.js:47 correctly prefers quotedPrice. Worked case: estimate $195, owner sets final $240, applies $25 credit: client sees '$240 per visit · confirmed price', 'Credit applied −$25', 'You pay $170'. Reverse case: estimate $195, final $150, balance $195: cap allows applying $195 and youPay becomes $0 on a $150 visit. youPay is also never recomputed if quotedPrice is edited after credit was applied.

**Impact.** Owner (money: over-applied credit or disputed invoices) and signed-in clients, who are shown a contradictory, binding-looking 'You pay' amount on account.html.

**Suggestion.** Make visitPrice() prefer quotedPrice (as timeclock.js does), recompute youPay whenever quotedPrice or creditApplied changes, and have account.js derive 'You pay' from the displayed price rather than a stored number.

### [HIGH] A recurring plan is one dated request; nothing schedules the next visit
`account.js:120` · product-gap · lens: ux-account-admin

The data model and both pages treat a 'Family, Every 2 weeks' plan exactly like a one-off clean: one request, one date, one hold. Mark done closes it and frees the window, and the client is then told to book again from scratch.
