There is no series, no next-visit date, no skip/pause, and no per-customer view, although plans with recurring discounts are the site's stated differentiator.

**Evidence.** account.js:120 picks the status note by status alone: '<p class="acct-status-note">' + esc(t("acct.note." + s)); with the mock backend a Done card reads "Finished. Thank you! Book again any time." and its only action is "Book this again" (line 131), which reopens the six-step quote and creates a New request to re-confirm. admin.js:316 'if (CLOSED[what]) return B.releaseHolds(id);' frees the window on Mark done; the edit panel (admin.js:236) has a single Date field; firestore.rules:61-70 quoteFields allows one date and one slot. terms.html:75 promises 'Pause or stop with a week's notice' but no control exists for either.

**Impact.** After the first visit the owner must re-create every recurring visit as a phone booking (which has no email, so it never appears in the client's account and credits cannot apply) or the client must re-request and wait to be re-confirmed each time. The plan discount the client was quoted is not attached to anything after visit one.

**Suggestion.** Add a 'next visit' step to Mark done for requests that have a frequency: propose date + interval (weekly 7, biweekly 14, monthly 28/calendar month, commercial ladders by weekday) and create the next confirmed request plus hold with the same client fields, quotedPrice, assignee and a seriesId; show 'Next visit' with move/cancel on the client card and add Skip/Pause actions that write to the series.

### [HIGH] 'Pay online' disappears the moment the visit is marked done; no paid flag
`account.js:123` · bug · lens: ux-account-admin

The payment button is shown only while a request is confirmed, but the crew marks the visit done at clock-out, which is exactly when the terms say payment is due. Nothing records whether a visit was paid, on either page.

**Evidence.** account.js:123 'if (pay && s === "confirmed") actions.push(... t("acct.pay") ...)'; timeclock.js:130-131 sets done on End; terms.html:74 / i18n terms.4.p1 'Payment is due at or after each visit'. No paid field exists in backend.js STR/NUM (lines 103-108) and admin.js:228-233 has no Paid action; the Done card in the mock shows only 'Book this again'.

**Impact.** A client who opens their account after the clean to pay finds no button; the owner has no view of outstanding balances and cannot tell credited, paid and unpaid visits apart.

**Suggestion.** Show the Pay button for confirmed and done requests until paidAt is set; add a 'Mark paid' action and a Paid/Unpaid pill on admin and client cards, and a 'Finished' filter sub-count of unpaid; later a Stripe Payment Link with client_reference_id and a webhook can set paidAt automatically.

### [HIGH] No Google reviews link and no step that asks for a review
`account.js:131` · content · lens: growth-gap

The site has no link to a Google Business Profile (or any external profile) and nothing in the flow asks a finished client for a review. A finished request only offers 'Book this again'; the 'done' status note says 'Finished. Thank you! Book again any time.' README item 6 rightly forbids invented reviews, but there is also no way to collect or point to real ones.

**Evidence.** account.js:131 for status done/declined/cancelled pushes only `'<a ... href="' + esc(againLink(r)) + '">' + esc(t("acct.again"))`; i18n.js:498 `"acct.note.done": "Finished. Thank you! Book again any time."`. Playwright dump of index, plans, refer, account and commercial on the local copy reports `externalLinks: []` on every page; `grep -rniE "google\.com/maps|g\.page|business\.google|yelp|facebook|sameAs"` over *.html and *.js returns nothing; the LocalBusiness JSON-LD (index.html:30-47) has no `sameAs`.

**Impact.** For a Charlotte home-services search, Google Business Profile reviews are the main ranking and trust signal. Reviews accrue only by chance, the site cannot even say 'see our reviews on Google', and the 'done' moment, the best time to ask, is wasted. This is the single largest lever on new bookings that costs nothing to build.

**Suggestion.** Add `business.reviewUrl` (the GBP 'write a review' short link) to plans.js; render 'Read our Google reviews' in the footer and the home trust block, and 'Leave us a review' with that link in acct.note.done, the done branch of account.js card(), and the owner's post-visit message; add the GBP URL to JSON-LD `sameAs`. About 20 lines, no backend change.

### [HIGH] Re-classifying a request in admin keeps the old estimate
`admin.js:98` · bug · lens: pricing-logic

When the owner changes a request's service or plan on admin.html, only the names are patched; estimatePerVisit/estimateOneVisit/estimateFullPrice/discount/estimatePerMonth stay as they were, so the card, the client's account and the credit cap keep showing the previous service's number.

**Evidence.** admin.js:90-101 servicePatch returns only `{ service: s.name.en, plan: del, frequency: del }` or `{ plan: p.name.en, frequency: ..., service: del }`; no estimate field is touched or recomputed. admin.js:189-190 then renders `r.estimatePerVisit` as the estimate for whatever service is now set, and account.js:73 shows the client e.g. 'Deep clean' with '$195 per visit · estimate'. admin.js:161 uses the same stale number for the referral credit cap.

**Impact.** Clients whose request was moved from a plan to a single clean (or to a different plan/schedule) see a wrong, official-looking estimate on account.html; staff see the same on the card.

**Suggestion.** In servicePatch, recompute the estimate from the stored size, plan and frequency via A.estimate() (or clear the estimate fields when moving to a custom single clean), and show 'estimate (plan changed)' until a quotedPrice is set.

### [HIGH] 'You pay' and the credit cap are computed from the estimate, not the final price
`admin.js:123` · bug · lens: ux-account-admin

visitPrice() never reads quotedPrice, so applying credit to a request whose final price has been set stores youPay = estimate minus credit. The client card then shows a confirmed price and a 'You pay' amount that do not add up, and youPay is not recomputed if the price is changed later.

**Evidence.** admin.js:123 'function visitPrice(r) { return typeof r.estimatePerVisit === "number" ? r.estimatePerVisit : typeof r.estimateOneVisit === "number" ? r.estimateOneVisit : null; }'; line 309 'youPay: price === null ? null : Math.max(0, rounded(price - n))'; line 161 'Math.min(b, INV.cap(visitPrice(r)))'. The save path (line 285) only patches quotedPrice. account.js:112-114 then prints the three rows: with estimate $221, final $210 and $25 credit the client sees 'Estimate $210 per visit · confirmed price', 'Credit applied −$25', 'You pay $196'.

**Impact.** Clients are shown two incompatible amounts and may pay the wrong one through the Stripe link (which asks them to type the amount); the owner has to explain the arithmetic on every credited visit.

**Suggestion.** Make visitPrice() prefer quotedPrice when it is a number, and in the save handler recompute youPay (and re-check creditApplied against the cap) whenever quotedPrice changes.

### [HIGH] Every invited team member gets the owner's powers except delete and team
`admin.js:229` · security · lens: ux-account-admin

There is one staff role in practice. Anyone the owner invites for the time clock can confirm or decline requests, set final prices and payment links, close days, block windows, add phone bookings, and read every client's notes including door codes. Only delete, the Team list and the Credits tab check isOwner().

**Evidence.** admin.js:229-232 render Confirm / Mark done / Decline / Reopen for any signed-in admin; the only role checks are isOwner() at lines 248, 442, 446, 526-527. firestore.rules:116 'allow read: if isAdmin() ...', :118 'allow update: if isAdmin() || (', :150 holds create, :169 internal, :178 days all gate on isAdmin(), and isAdmin() (:40) is any admins/{uid} document or any adminInvites email. The James Carter card in the mock shows 'Notes: Code for the door is 4471' to the staff viewer.

**Impact.** A mis-tap by a cleaner changes a booking or a price the client then sees as 'confirmed price'; every current and former cleaner has had the full client list with phone numbers and access notes; the owner cannot give someone only the clock.

**Suggestion.** Introduce role 'cleaner' alongside 'admin' and 'owner': rules let a cleaner read requests assigned to them or confirmed for today and write only their own shifts; admin.js hides the status, price, calendar and credit controls unless role is admin or owner; the invite form gets a role choice.

### [HIGH] Clients are never emailed or texted about confirmation, price, notes, or decline
`admin.js:315` · product-gap · lens: ux-account-admin

Every outbound message in the system goes to the business. Confirm, Decline, Mark done, the final price and the 'note the client sees' are written to Firestore only; the client learns of them solely by signing in. No reminder is sent before the visit, so the 24-hour change cutoff lands unannounced.

**Evidence.** admin.js:315 'p = B.updateRequest(id, { status: what })...' has no notify call, and grep for 'notify(' in admin.js, timeclock.js and backend.js returns nothing; the only senders are quote.js mailCopy (to the business) and account.js:181-205 tellBusiness ('An email to the business ... when a client moves or cancels'). docs/SETUP-firebase.md:77-78: 'A confirmation email to the client ("autoresponse") is a paid Formspree feature.' The client card promises 'We have it and will reply within one business day to confirm' (i18n acct.note.new, hard-coded rather than using {responseTime}), and the 24-hour message at account.js:129 prints the phone as plain text (observed: '<span class="muted acct-late">... call or text (980) 319-0015.</span>') instead of the tel:/sms: links used elsewhere.

**Impact.** Every status change needs a manual call or text by the owner; a client who does not sign in never learns the visit was confirmed, the price was set, or the request was declined, and gets no prompt to move a visit before the cutoff. On a phone the cutoff message cannot be tapped to call.

**Suggestion.** Add a Cloud Function on requests onUpdate (status, quotedPrice, replyNote, date/slot changes) that sends a short bilingual email via the Trigger Email extension already noted as available on Blaze (SETUP-firebase.md:218-220), plus a scheduled function 48 hours before date for a reminder with a 'change or cancel' link to account.html; say 'we'll email you when it's confirmed' on the quote done step and the New card; make the phone in acct.tooLate a tel:/sms: link.

### [HIGH] Client never gets a confirmation, status change or reminder
`admin.js:315` · ux · lens: growth-gap

After a request is sent, the only email goes to the owner (the client's address is just the Reply-To). When the owner clicks Confirm, Decline or Mark done, the page updates Firestore and nothing is sent to the client; they only learn the status by signing in. There is no day-before reminder by email or SMS.

**Evidence.** quote.js:419-425 `mailFields` sets `email: d.email` and posts to `C.formEndpoint` (the owner's Formspree form, plans.js:40). admin.js:313-321 status branch: `p = B.updateRequest(id, { status: what }).then(... releaseHolds / ensureHold ...).then(... grants ...)`; backend.js:380-383 `updateRequest` is a plain `db.collection("requests").doc(id).update(patch)`. `grep -rn "reminder\|sms" *.js` finds only the referral `sms:` share link. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:77-78: 'A confirmation email to the client ("autoresponse") is a paid Formspree feature.' The reference number shown on the done screen (quote.js:504) is never sent to the client.

**Impact.** Terms (i18n.js:719) say a visit is booked only when 'we confirm it with you', so confirmation depends on the owner remembering to text or email separately after pressing Confirm. A client who hears nothing within a day books elsewhere; without reminders, lockouts and no-shows cost crew time. Spanish-speaking clients are affected equally since `language` is stored but unused for outbound messages.

**Suggestion.** Use the Blaze plan already in place: install the Firebase 'Trigger Email' extension (SETUP-firebase.md:218-220 already names it) and a small Cloud Function on `requests` create and status update that writes a bilingual email to the client (using the stored `language`), plus a scheduled function for a day-before reminder; SMS via Twilio can hang off the same function later. About a day of work and a few dollars a month.

### [HIGH] Calendar 'Remove' deletes the hold but leaves the request confirmed
`admin.js:407` · bug · lens: ux-account-admin

The day panel's Remove button only deletes the hold document. The request keeps status confirmed with its date and slot, so it still appears in The week ahead and in the crew's Unassigned today, the client still sees 'You're on the calendar', and the public calendar shows the window open again.

**Evidence.** admin.js:405-407 'if (!confirm(t("adm.removeConfirm"))) return; p = B.deleteHold(b.getAttribute("data-hold"));' with the prompt 'Remove this booking from the calendar?'. renderUpcoming (lines 339-342) and timeclock.js:69 myDay read requests by date and status, not holds; calendar.js:52 counts holds. Nothing writes status or clears date on the request.

**Impact.** The window the owner thinks they freed can be taken by a new visitor while the original booking is still scheduled: a double booking. Phone bookings removed this way stay as phantom confirmed requests forever.

**Suggestion.** Make Remove cancel the request (status 'cancelled', cancelledBy 'staff') through the same path as Decline so releaseHolds runs, and keep the bare hold delete only for legacy requestId === 'manual' holds; reword the prompt to say the booking is cancelled.

### [HIGH] Plan cards show the monthly figure with no unit: 'About $1,266'
`app.js:223` · bug · lens: ux-commercial-journey · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: medium, impact: medium, refute: medium) · **confirmed**

renderPlanCards prints t('plans.monthly') ('About') followed by the perMonth amount and nothing else, so every commercial plan card on commercial.html and commercial-plans.html shows an unlabeled dollar figure directly under the per-visit price. The key that would finish the sentence, plans.monthlyUnit ('per month' / 'al mes'), exists but is never used.

**Evidence.** app.js:223: '<p class="plan-monthly" data-monthly hidden>' + esc(t("plans.monthly")) + ' <span data-price="' + p.id + '" data-price-part="perMonth"></span></p>'. i18n.js:44 "plans.monthlyUnit": "per month" has no reference anywhere (grep over *.html *.js). Observed card text at 1280 and 390, EN and ES: 'Essentials … $146 per visit About $1,266 $178 at full price Save 18%' / 'Esencial … $146 por visita Aprox. $1,266 …'. The dial on the same page says 'About $1,717 per month' (commercial:dial.perMonth), so the two formats disagree on one screen.

**Impact.** Every commercial visitor sees three unexplained amounts on the main pricing cards; on commercial-plans.html there is no dial above to supply the missing 'per month', so a reader can take $1,266 for a one-time price or dismiss the cards as broken.

**Suggestion.** Append esc(t('plans.monthlyUnit')) after the perMonth span in renderPlanCards (or reuse the dial.perMonth template), and add a check that no key in i18n.js is unused.

### [HIGH] Availability page: after picking a window the CTA is off-screen
`availability.html:80` · ux · lens: ux-residential-journey

On a phone the "Your pick" panel with the "Request this window" button sits below the calendar, so after a visitor taps a day and a window nothing on screen tells them what to do next.

**Evidence.** availability.html lines 77-84 put the aside after the calendar; styles.css line 578 stacks `.avail-grid` to one column under 980px and line 581 makes `.avail-side` static. calendar.js line 127 scrolls only the windows list into view. Measured in Playwright at 390x844 after tapping Oct 22 then Morning: CTA bounding box top=1013px, bottom=1064px, viewport height 844, inView=false (same in Spanish, top=1013). Screenshot shows the slot list ending with "Any window that day" and no button.

**Impact.** Visitors who use the calendar first (the page is in the main nav) select a window and then see no next step; the pick is not saved anywhere, so leaving the page loses it.

**Suggestion.** On phones render the pick summary and "Request this window" button directly under the windows list (or as a sticky bottom bar once a day is chosen), and scroll to it after a slot tap.

### [HIGH] reCAPTCHA Enterprise runs with no visible notice or Google links
`backend.js:76` · legal · lens: content-legal

On mkgservices.com backend.js activates Firebase App Check with ReCaptchaEnterpriseProvider on every page that loads it (quote, commercial-quote, availability, account, admin). App Check renders the reCAPTCHA widget in a hidden container, so no badge is shown, and Google's reCAPTCHA terms then require the "This site is protected by reCAPTCHA and the Google Privacy Policy and Terms of Service apply" text with links visibly in the user flow. None of those pages carry it, and the privacy page's mention has no links.

**Evidence.** backend.js:76 fb.appCheck().activate(new fb.appCheck.ReCaptchaEnterpriseProvider(C.appCheckKey), true); gated by backend.js:54 var useAppCheck = SITE && !useEmu && !!C.appCheckKey. Grep of all .html/.js for "recaptcha" matches only backend.js:76, plans.js:42 (comment) and privacy.html:81. Playwright hasRecaptchaText was false on quote.html, availability.html and account.html; privacy.html:81 renders "Google's Privacy Policy and Terms of Service apply." as plain text with no <a> to policies.google.com. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:201 says App Check is in monitor-only mode while privacy.3.p3 says reCAPTCHA is used "To keep automated spam out of the booking calendar". Could only be confirmed on the live site, since App Check never starts locally.

**Impact.** Breach of Google's reCAPTCHA / App Check terms of use on every booking page; a key suspension would cut the site off from Firestore once enforcement is switched on. Visitors are not told on the page that their interaction data goes to Google.

**Suggestion.** Add a small field-hint under the quote send button, under the availability calendar and under the account sign-in form, EN/ES, with the standard sentence and links to https://policies.google.com/privacy and https://policies.google.com/terms; hyperlink the same in privacy.3.p3 and soften "keep spam out" to "help us tell people from bots" while enforcement is off.

### [HIGH] Recurring plans are sold, but every booking is one dated request
`backend.js:148` · ux · lens: growth-gap

The proposition is a weekly, bi-weekly or monthly plan at a discount, but a request holds a single `date` and `slot`; there is no series, no next-visit date, and no skip or pause that acts on a plan. After a visit is marked done the client's only option is 'Book this again', which reopens the six-step quote form.

**Evidence.** backend.js:148-167 `submitRequest` stores `date: data.date || "", slot: data.slot || ""` and one hold; `frequency` is a text label in STR (backend.js:103). account.js:125-127 offers only move/cancel on the single request; account.js:86-91 `againLink` rebuilds the quote URL; i18n.js:498 'Book again any time.' admin.js:267-323 `act()` has no 'schedule next visit'; `grep -rn "series\|recurr\|nextDate" *.js` returns nothing. Meanwhile index.html:138 promises 'Skip, pause, or switch plans with no fee' and index.html:219 'Skip a visit, pause for a trip'.

**Impact.** The owner must recreate every repeat visit as a phone booking (backend.js:412) or ask the client to resubmit; the calendar cannot show recurring load weeks ahead; the 'discount applies to every visit' promise is not tracked anywhere; clients on a plan get no 'your next visit is Thursday' and drift away.

**Suggestion.** Add a `series` concept: when a plan request is confirmed, admin.html creates the next N linked requests and holds on the chosen weekday cadence (`seriesId`, `seriesIndex`), account.html shows 'Next visit' with a Skip button, and firestore.rules extends `quoteFields`/`holdShape` accordingly. Medium cost across backend.js, admin.js, account.js and the rules, but it operationalises the product the site sells.

### [HIGH] Commercial pages never mention insurance, COI, bonding or background checks
`commercial.html:194` · content · lens: ux-commercial-journey · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: high, impact: medium, refute: medium) · **confirmed**

The 'what your business can count on' block (three cards) and the six FAQs cover price, month-to-month and the checklist, but nothing an office manager uses to shortlist a vendor: proof of insurance (COI on request), bonding, background-checked and uniformed staff, a W-9, how keys and alarm codes are held, or that a written proposal follows the walk-through. The only insurance statement on the whole site is one clause on terms.html.

**Evidence.** commercial.html:194-207 renders why.1-3 ('The price before the first visit', 'Month to month', 'The same checklist, every visit'); FAQ summaries at 218-239 are discount/schedule/when/supplies/price/where. Full text extracted with Playwright at 390 and 1280, EN and ES, contains no 'insur', 'bond', 'background', 'W-9' or 'certificate'. The sole mention is i18n.js:722 terms.3.p1 'We're insured; damage caused by our team is reported to you the same day.' PLAN.md section 8 item 5 lists these claims as an unanswered owner question.

**Impact.** Office managers, property managers and clinic administrators routinely require a COI and background-check statement before they will book a walk-through; with none on the page, the comparison with any established janitorial vendor is lost before the quote form is reached.

**Suggestion.** Once the owner confirms what is true, add a fourth 'For your vendor file' card (insured, COI on request, background-checked staff, W-9 available, keys/alarm codes logged) under the Charlotte's-own section and a matching FAQ, in both languages; say that a written proposal with the scope follows the walk-through.

### [HIGH] Skip/pause 'no fee' copy contradicts terms' late-cancel charge
`i18n.js:68` · legal · lens: pricing-logic · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: high, impact: medium, refute: medium) · **confirmed**

The dial and FAQ promise skipping or pausing 'with no fee' and 'let us know before your visit', while the terms charge up to half the visit price inside 24 hours and require a week's notice to pause or stop. README item 5 asks the owner to confirm policies, but the live copy already disagrees with itself.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:68 dial.li2 'Skip, pause, or switch plans with no fee'; i18n.js:133 faq.a2 'Let us know before your visit and we'll skip it'; i18n.js:719 terms.2.p1 'change or cancel a confirmed visit free of charge with at least 24 hours' notice; later than that, we may charge up to half the visit price'; i18n.js:720 terms.2.p2 'Pause or stop with a week's notice'. Same contradiction in Spanish (i18n.js:878, 943, 1529, 1530). Observed rendered on index.html and terms.html.

**Impact.** Owner: a client who skips a visit the same morning can cite the FAQ against the 50% charge; legally the more specific terms may still bind, but the marketing promise undermines collecting the fee.

**Suggestion.** Owner decision on the real policy, then align: e.g. 'Skip or pause free with 24 hours' notice' on the dial and FAQ, and make terms.2.p2's week's notice either disappear or match.

### [HIGH] Visitors are told prices are "placeholder pricing"
`i18n.js:107` · known-todo · lens: ux-residential-journey

The home and plans pages show the sentence "Estimates use placeholder pricing until MKG confirms rates" to every visitor, in both languages. On a live site this tells a homeowner the headline number they just dialled in is not real, which undercuts the whole "watch the price come down" proposition.

**Evidence.** i18n.js line 107 `"plans.note.home": "Estimates use placeholder pricing until MKG confirms rates. ..."` and line 109 `plans.note.plans`; Spanish at lines 917 and 919 ("precios provisionales hasta que MKG confirme sus tarifas"). Rendered by index.html line 189 and plans.html line 95; visible in the 390px screenshots under the three plan cards on both pages.

**Impact.** Every residential visitor; the phrase reads as developer jargon and invites the question "so what will I actually pay?" just before the plan-card CTAs. README "Before launch" item 1 is still open and the copy makes that visible.

**Suggestion.** Set the real base/per amounts in plans.js, then reword both notes to the customer-facing framing already used elsewhere ("Estimates are for planning; your quote reply has the exact per-visit price for your home") and drop the word "placeholder".

### [HIGH] FAQ promises free skips any time; terms charge inside 24 hours
`i18n.js:133` · legal · lens: content-legal · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: high, impact: medium, refute: medium) · **confirmed**

The home page tells customers they can skip a visit "with no fee" by letting MKG know "before your visit" and change plans "whenever you need", while terms.html says a confirmed visit cancelled with less than 24 hours' notice may cost up to half the visit price. A skipped visit is a cancelled confirmed visit, so the public pages and the terms promise different things.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:133 "faq.a2": "Yes. Let us know before your visit and we'll skip it..."; i18n.js:68 "dial.li2": "Skip, pause, or switch plans with no fee"; i18n.js:96 "how.3.p": "...Skip a week or change plans by text whenever you need." versus i18n.js:719 "terms.2.p1": "...You can change or cancel a confirmed visit free of charge with at least 24 hours' notice; later than that, we may charge up to half the visit price." Rendered at http://127.0.0.1:8787/index.html#faq and /terms.html; the Spanish keys (i18n.js:878, 943, 1529) carry the same conflict. plans.js:94 policy.changeNoticeHours and account.js:36 enforce the 24-hour rule in the account page only.

**Impact.** A customer who texts "skip tomorrow" two hours before the visit and is charged 50% can point to the FAQ; ambiguous consumer terms are read against the business (N.C.G.S. 75-1.1 territory), so the late-cancel charge is hard to collect and invites disputes and chargebacks.

**Suggestion.** Make faq.a2, dial.li2 and how.3.p (EN and ES) say "with at least 24 hours' notice" and mention the late charge, or drop the charge from the terms; source the number from plans.js policy.changeNoticeHours so all pages move together.

### [HIGH] Insurance, re-clean promise and photos are hidden in Terms
`index.html:212` · content · lens: growth-gap

The home page's trust section ('why') makes three claims: price before the visit, no contracts, same checklist. The statements that actually reassure a stranger-in-my-home buyer, 'We're insured' and 'tell us within 24 hours and we'll come back and fix it at no charge', exist only on terms.html. There are no team photos, no before/after, no background-check wording; the only images on the whole site are the logo.

**Evidence.** index.html:212-225 `<div class="why">` renders three blocks (desktop screenshot of #why: 'The price before the visit / Change your mind any time / The same checklist, every time'). `grep -rniE "insur|bonded|background" *.html *.js` returns only i18n.js:722 (`"terms.3.p1": "... We're insured; damage caused by our team is reported to you the same day."`), its Spanish twin at 1532, and terms.html:77. `grep -n "<img" *.html | grep -v brand/` returns nothing. PLAN.md:17 lists 'screened/insured staff, local ownership, years in business' as the competitor trust stack to match.

**Impact.** Visitors comparing MKG with franchise sites see no insurance, no guarantee and no faces, so the first impression is a price calculator from an anonymous company. Both residential (index.html) and commercial.html (same 'why' structure at lines 185-208) are affected.

**Suggestion.** Add a trust strip under the hero or a fourth 'why' block using the claims already true in the terms (insured, 24-hour re-clean, supplies included, Charlotte since 2014) as new i18n keys in EN and ES on index.html and commercial.html; add two or three real photos of the owner and crew under brand/ when available. Copy work, no logic.

### [HIGH] No analytics, conversion events, or error reporting on any page
`index.html:304` · devops · lens: growth-gap

No page loads a tag or beacon of any kind, the quote success path fires no event, and runtime errors are only `console.warn`. The owner cannot see which quote step loses people, residential vs commercial, EN vs ES, or which channel (Google, QR cards, referral links) produces bookings.

**Evidence.** index.html:304-307 loads only plans.js, i18n.js, app.js, referral.js; Playwright script dump on every public page lists no third-party script. `grep -rniE "gtag|googletagmanager|analytics|plausible|sentry|GTM-"` over html/js/json returns nothing. quote.js:503-506 resolves `done(false)` with no event; quote.js:508 `console.warn("request not saved", err)`; `grep -n "window.onerror\|unhandledrejection\|addEventListener(\"error\"" *.js` returns nothing. privacy copy (i18n.js:706-708) lists Firebase, Formspree and reCAPTCHA only, so a tag needs a privacy update.

**Impact.** Every other improvement in this audit cannot be measured; a JavaScript error on the live site (which this container cannot reach) would go unnoticed until a client phones. Spend on flyers, GBP posts or ads cannot be attributed.

**Suggestion.** Add GA4 (or a cookieless option such as Plausible or Cloudflare Web Analytics if the owner wants no consent banner) in the shared head of every page; fire `generate_lead` at quote.js:503, a `quote_step` event in `go()`, `sign_up` in account.js, and `referral_share` in referral.js; add a five-line `window.onerror` that posts to the Formspree endpoint or GA4 `exception`; update privacy.3.* in both languages. About an hour.

### [HIGH] One-time plan visits get a fixed estimate despite 'quoted individually'
`plans.js:116` · pricing · lens: pricing-logic

'One-time' is a schedule on every plan, so a visitor can request a single Complete or Family clean with a firm-looking estimate, although the site says single cleans are quoted individually and a first-time whole-home clean is in practice the deep clean the site refuses to price.

**Evidence.** plans.js:116-118 `{ id: "onetime", discount: 0, label: { en: "One-time" ...}, perVisitLabel: { en: "per clean" } }`. Observed quote review: 'Plan | Complete, one-time' / 'Estimate | $287 per clean' (3 bed 2 bath), stored by quote.js:408-409 as `frequency: "One-time"`, `estimatePerVisit: 287`. Meanwhile i18n.js:116 singles.lead.plans: 'Quoted individually, because a first deep clean ... is a different job', and docs/MKG-pricing-logic.pdf page 2: deep clean 'Quoted individually ... sends no number'. Complete one-time for 1 bed 1 bath is $217 including inside oven/fridge and ceiling fans (plans.js:173-175), i.e. a deep clean at a maintenance price.

**Impact.** Owner: one-off jobs on homes MKG has never seen arrive with an underpriced site estimate the client has already read as the price; it also muddies the deep-clean-vs-plan story for visitors.

**Suggestion.** Owner decision: either remove 'onetime' from plan frequencies (route 'One-time' to the single-clean flow) or keep it with a visible first-visit uplift and a note that a home not cleaned by MKG before is priced as a deep clean.

### [HIGH] Residential model ignores bedrooms on Essentials and compresses size range
`plans.js:136` · pricing · lens: pricing-logic

The residential formula has no square-footage input, Essentials prices every bedroom count the same, and per-unit increments are small next to the base, so very different homes get nearly the same number. README item 1 covers replacing the numbers, but these are structural choices that survive a rate change.

**Evidence.** plans.js:136 Essentials `per: { bedrooms: 0, bathrooms: 22 }`: grid.js output 'Essentials 1/2 vs 7/2 one-time: 133 133'; a 7-bedroom 1-bath house and a 1-bed 1-bath apartment both estimate $111 one-time (observed on index.html). Family 1/1 $159 to 7/6 $377 is only x2.37 (plans.js:154) while the work scales far more; Complete 7/6 weekly = $407 with inside oven/fridge, baseboards, ceiling fans (plans.js:168-176). quote.html:119 'Count a half bath as one' charges a half bath the full $22-26 bathroom rate. app.js:160 labels the top step '7+ bed' / '6+ bath' but quote.js:395 stores 7/6, so larger homes are silently floored.

**Impact.** Owner: small homes slightly overpriced and large homes materially underpriced versus the Charlotte market, with the stored estimate anchoring the client before the reply; a 7-bed Essentials client at $111 is unprofitable.

**Suggestion.** Owner decision: give Essentials a non-zero bedroom rate or a 'living area' factor, add an approximate sq-ft stepper (the dims mechanism already supports a third dim), price half baths at half rate, and consider a size band above which the site says 'quoted after we see the home'.

### [HIGH] Plan estimator implies first visit costs the plan rate
`plans.js:204` · pricing · lens: pricing-logic

The site says plans usually start with a separately quoted deep clean, yet the dial, plan cards, quote step 3 and the saved request present only the recurring per-visit price and promise it is 'confirmed before your first clean'. A visitor requesting 'Family, every 2 weeks, $195' has no first-visit number and the form cannot even carry 'plan + initial deep clean' in one request.

**Evidence.** plans.js:204 deep clean description: 'Also the usual first visit before starting a plan.'; i18n.js:109 plans.note.plans: 'Homes that need a deep clean first are quoted separately, so the plan price stays the same from the first regular visit on' (shown only on plans.html, not on index.html's dial or quote step 3); i18n.js:69 dial.li3 'Price confirmed before your first clean'; i18n.js:59 hero.note 'Your exact price is confirmed in your quote'. quote.js:185-186 makes plan and single clean mutually exclusive radios (`if (parts[0] === "plan") { draft.service = null; ...} else { draft.service = parts[1]; }`), and quote.js:409 stores only `estimatePerVisit`, `estimateFullPrice`, `discount`. Observed review row: 'Estimate | $195 per visit (10% off the full price of $217)' with no first-visit line.

**Impact.** Every residential plan prospect: the owner's reply must introduce a higher first-visit price the site never hinted at, which reads as a bait-and-switch and costs bookings; or the owner absorbs the deep clean at the plan rate.

**Suggestion.** Owner decision: either price the first visit in plans.js (e.g. a firstVisitMultiplier or 'first visit from $X' line shown in the dial, quote step 3 and the payload), or let a plan request include 'start with a deep clean' and say plainly in the estimator that visit one is quoted separately.

### [HIGH] Square footage is click-only: 250 sq ft steps, no typed entry, silent 20,000 cap
`plans.js:257` · ux · lens: ux-commercial-journey · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: medium, impact: low, refute: medium) · **confirmed**

The sqft dimension (500 to 20,000 in steps of 250) is rendered only as a minus/plus stepper with an <output>, so a visitor with a 15,000 sq ft building must click + 48 times (78 from the minimum) and cannot type a number. At the cap the label reads '20,000+ sq ft' but the estimate and the submitted request carry exactly 20000 with no flag that the real space is larger.

**Evidence.** plans.js:257 { id: "sqft", min: 500, max: 20000, step: 250, default: 3000 }. app.js:122-125 builds only two buttons and an <output class="stepper-value">; app.js:160 appends '+' when state[key] === d.max. Playwright: clicking + from the default until disabled took 68 clicks and showed '− 20,000+ sq ft +'; the dial then priced Professional Mon–Fri at $710 per visit for exactly 20,000; quote.js:395 copies the raw number (p.sqft = 20000) into the payload and the owner email reads 'Facility: Office, 20,000 sq ft, 2 restrooms'.

**Impact.** Managers of larger buildings, exactly the accounts worth the most, either give up on the estimator or send a request whose size and estimate are wrong by an unknown amount; the owner cannot tell a 20,000 sq ft site from a 60,000 one.

**Suggestion.** Render the sqft stepper as a numeric <input type=number step=250 min=500> with the +/- buttons as helpers (or offer size bands), raise or remove the cap, and when the cap is hit send sqftCapped: true and show 'over 20,000 sq ft, quoted after the walk-through' instead of a price.

### [HIGH] Commercial per-sq-ft rate never tapers; 20,000 sq ft daily shows $24k/month
`plans.js:315` · pricing · lens: pricing-logic

Commercial pricing is strictly linear in square feet with no volume curve or cap, so large spaces produce estimates several times typical Charlotte janitorial contracts while the 35% daily discount cannot compensate.

**Evidence.** plans.js:315 `base: 85, per: { sqft: 0.07, restrooms: 20 }` with plans.js:257 `max: 20000`. Computed via app.js:34-36 and observed on commercial.html: Complete, 20,000+ sq ft, 12+ restrooms, Mon–Fri = '$1,121 per visit · About $24,292 per month' ($1,725 full). Essentials 20,000 sq ft, 1 restroom, daily = $493/visit ≈ $10,683/month ($0.025/sq ft/visit). A 20,000 sq ft office cleaned nightly is roughly 5-7 labour hours per visit, i.e. a few hundred dollars per visit and low-to-mid four figures per month at market, so the top of the slider is 3-6x market. The PDF's only worked example (3,000 sq ft, $198/visit) sits in the plausible range, which hides the problem.

**Impact.** Larger commercial prospects (the most valuable ones) see an estimate that looks like a mistake and leave before requesting a walk-through; the figure is also what gets emailed and stored as the 'site estimate'.

**Suggestion.** Owner decision on real rates; structurally, add a declining per-sq-ft tier table (or a per-sq-ft rate that steps down above e.g. 5,000 and 10,000 sq ft) in plans.js and let app.js estimate() apply it, or lower dims.max and show 'over 10,000 sq ft: quoted after a walk-through'.

### [HIGH] Terms never shown or accepted where the booking contract forms
`quote.html:257` · legal · lens: content-legal

The quote form, the path nearly every customer takes ("No account, no commitment"), collects name, email, phone and notes and submits with no link to the privacy policy or terms and no acceptance line. The only agreement sentence is inside the password sign-up form; Google sign-up and email-link sign-in create accounts without it.

**Evidence.** quote.html:250-258 review step ends with <button class="btn" type="submit" data-submit data-i18n="q.send">Send my quote request</button> and no legal text (same at commercial-quote.html:267). A Playwright render of quote.html, availability.html, commercial.html and index.html found zero privacy/terms links inside <main>; only the footer links exist. account.html:101 <p class="field-hint" data-signup-only hidden data-i18n="acct.agree"> sits inside the password form, while the Google button (account.html:76) and "Email me a sign-in link instead" (account.html:108) bypass it.

**Impact.** The late-cancel charge, the re-clean window, the referral rules and the payment terms have no assent trail for customers who never create a password account, which is most of them; a privacy link at the point of collection is also the baseline US practice and what Google's OAuth consent screen already assumes.

**Suggestion.** Add one line above the send button in both quote forms, EN/ES: "By sending this request you agree to our {termsLink} and {privacyLink}" (the LIVE placeholders already exist in i18n.js:1714-1715); move acct.agree outside the password form so it covers Google and email-link sign-in; restate the cancellation terms in the confirmation reply.

### [HIGH] No neighbourhood or service-area pages; area is never stated
`sitemap.xml:3` · seo · lens: growth-gap

The site has ten generic URLs and describes its territory only as 'the Charlotte area'. There are no pages for Matthews, Huntersville, Ballantyne, Fort Mill, Mint Hill or any suburb, the structured data names only the city, and the ZIP step promises an area check that cannot happen because `serviceZips` is empty.

(README 'Before launch' item 4 names serviceZips; still unset.)

**Impact.** Searches such as 'house cleaning Matthews NC' or 'maid service Ballantyne' cannot land on MKG, and a visitor in Fort Mill SC has no way to know whether they are served before filling in six steps. Local-pack ranking uses areaServed and on-page place names.

**Suggestion.** Create four to six short area pages (e.g. area/matthews.html) from the quote.html chrome: the estimator, a paragraph about the suburb, ZIPs served, and the quote CTA with ?zip= prefilled; add them to sitemap.xml and the footer; set `serviceZips` and make `areaServed` a list of City entries with `priceRange`. Half a day of copy plus a page template (do not rerun docs/make-pages.py; its header note says it would overwrite hand edits).

### [HIGH] No street address anywhere; the crew's job card shows only a ZIP
`timeclock.js:82` · product-gap · lens: ux-account-admin

The quote form never asks for an address and the data model has no field for one, so the request the cleaner opens on their phone says 'Deep clean · 28205' and the admin card's Where row says 'House, 28277'. The address has to be collected by phone for every booking and kept in private notes.

**Evidence.** timeclock.js:82 '<div class="muted">' + esc(serviceText(r) + (r.zip ? " · " + r.zip : "")); admin.js:197 builds 'where' from company, homeType/facilityType and zip only; firestore.rules:61-65 quoteFields lists zip but no address key; quote.js:49/390 payload has zip, name, email, phone, notes; grep for address/street/calle/dirección across quote.html, quote.js and i18n.js finds only email/URL uses. Observed with the mock at 390px: job card 'Afternoon (3:00–6:00) · María López / Deep clean · 28205 · (704) 555-0142' with no map link.

**Impact.** The cleaner cannot navigate to the job from the page that is meant to be their day sheet; the owner must phone each new client just to get the address and retype it into private notes; confirming a booking is impossible without that extra call.

**Suggestion.** Add an address field (string, max 200) to the quote contact step and to quoteFields/STR, show it on the admin card and on the job card as a maps link (https://maps.google.com/?q=...), and include it in the edit panel so phone bookings can get one too.

### [HIGH] Referrer credit is never granted when a visit is finished from the time clock
`timeclock.js:131` · bug · lens: ux-account-admin

Referral credits are granted only inside admin.js act(). The time clock's End button offers 'Mark ... as done?' and writes status done directly, so the referrer's promised credit (and the welcome credit if the request was never Confirmed) is silently skipped, and the card's Mark done button is hidden once the status is done.

**Evidence.** timeclock.js:131 'return B.updateRequest(r.id, { status: "done" }).then(function () { return B.releaseHolds(r.id); });' versus admin.js:315-319 where only act() calls 'grants(r, what)'; grep for 'grants(' finds it only in admin.js. admin.js:230 hides Mark done when s === "done", and grants() (line 168) runs only for act 'confirmed' or 'done'. The card keeps showing 'Referrer credit: pending' (line 147) on a done request.

**Impact.** The $25/$50 reward promised on refer.html and the account page is not paid when the crew closes the job in the field, which is the normal path; the referrer sees nothing in their ledger and the owner has no prompt to fix it.

**Suggestion.** Move grants() into a shared finish(request) helper (backend.js or MKG.admin) that both act() and the time clock call, and show a 'Grant pending referral credit' button on any done request with ref set and refRewardGranted false.

### [MEDIUM] Staff cannot create a password account on the staff page; sources disagree
`admin.html:80` · ux · lens: ux-account-admin

admin.html offers Sign in, Forgot password and Google, but no Create account. The runtime hint tells invitees to create a password account on the client sign-in page; the page's default text and the setup doc say Google only. A cleaner without a Google account is routed through a page that says 'Request cleanings, follow their status'.

**Evidence.** admin.html:66-86 has only the password form, 'Forgot your password?' (line 80) and the Google button; i18n.js:569 adm.teamHint: '...or with a password: they choose Create account on the sign-in page with that email and confirm it'; admin.html:163's default copy says 'They sign in on this page with Google using that address'; docs/SETUP-firebase.md:55-57: 'They sign in on the admin page with Google ... a password alone does not'; i18n.js:522 adm.notAdmin: '...then sign in here with Google'. backend.js:274 'if (!user.email || !user.emailVerified) return null;' makes the password path work only after the email verification that only account.js handles.

**Impact.** Invited staff without Google either fail on first sign-in ('This account isn't on the team') or are sent to the client page to register; the owner reading the doc believes the password route does not exist.

**Suggestion.** Either add a 'Create your staff password' tab on admin.html that calls signUpPassword, sends the verification link and explains the confirm step, or make the hint, the HTML default and the doc all say Google only.

### [MEDIUM] Search misses '#ID' and dashed phones; Confirmed list is not in visit order
`admin.js:58` · ux · lens: ux-account-admin

The search box advertises '#ID' but the hash makes it fail, phone numbers only match in the exact stored format, only the latest 300 requests are loaded, and every filter including Confirmed is ordered by request time rather than by visit date.

**Evidence.** admin.js:58-59 'var hay = [r.name, r.email, r.phone, r.zip, r.company, r.id, ...].join(" ").toLowerCase(); return query.split(/\s+/).every(function (w) { return !w || hay.indexOf(w) !== -1; });' with placeholder 'Search by name, email, phone, ZIP, or #ID' (i18n.js:558) and cards printing '#K1L2M3'. Measured with the mock: '#K1L2M3' → 0 cards, 'K1L2M3' → 1; '(704) 555-0142' → 5 cards, '704-555-0142' → 0 (a digits() helper already exists at line 116). backend.js:378 '.orderBy("createdAt", "desc").limit(300)'. visible() (lines 61-66) keeps that order for all filters; the calendar day panel (line 387) lists names with no jump to the card, unlike the week ahead (lines 353-360).

**Impact.** Looking up a caller by the number on caller ID or by the reference printed on the card returns nothing; finding 'the Thursday after next' means scanning cards in submission order; old clients drop out of search and counts silently.

**Suggestion.** Strip a leading '#', compare digit-only forms of the query against digits(r.phone), sort the Confirmed filter by date + slot ascending, raise or paginate the 300 limit, and make day-panel names scroll to their card the way week-ahead names do.

### [MEDIUM] Confirm works on requests with no date; client reads 'You're on the calendar'
`admin.js:229` · ux · lens: ux-account-admin

The Confirm button is offered whatever the request's timing, and a flexible request confirmed without a date disappears from every schedule view while the client is told a visit is booked. Decline likewise sends the client a fixed sentence with no way to add a reason at the moment of declining.

**Evidence.** admin.js:229 '(s !== "confirmed" ? '<button type="button" class="btn btn-sm" data-act="confirmed">'' has no check on r.date; backend.js:403 ensureHold returns early without date and slot; account.js:120 shows acct.note.confirmed 'You're on the calendar. We'll see you then.' by status alone while account.js:67 prints 'Flexible, no date picked' for the same card; renderUpcoming (admin.js:341) and myDay (timeclock.js:69) require r.date so the job never shows. Decline (line 231) has no prompt; the client sees only acct.note.declined.

**Impact.** The owner confirms intending to call about a date, the client believes they are booked, and the job is on nobody's schedule; declined clients get no explanation.

**Suggestion.** When Confirm is pressed without date and slot, open the edit panel with the Date and Start window fields focused and require them before saving the status; on Decline, prompt for an optional note to the client that is saved to replyNote.

### [MEDIUM] Edit panel hides price, service and assignee; contact details cannot be edited
`admin.js:235` · ux · lens: ux-account-admin

The collapsed panel is labelled 'Date, note to client, payment link' but also holds Service, Final price, Private notes, Assigned to and Delete, with the daily action (assigning a cleaner) last. There is no field for name, email, phone or ZIP, so typos are permanent from the UI and phone bookings never gain an email.

**Evidence.** admin.js:235 summary uses t("adm.edit") = 'Date, note to client, payment link' (i18n.js:537); fields at lines 236-243 are date, slot, serviceChoice, quotedPrice, replyNote, payLink, adminNotes, assignedTo only. Measured at 390px with the panel open: 857px from the summary to Save, with the Assigned to select the last control. backend.js:414 creates phone bookings with 'email: "", emailLower: "", phone: ""', and admin.js:125-131 isRepeat matches on email/phone, so they can never be linked to a client account, credits, or repeat detection.

**Impact.** Assigning a cleaner takes three screens of scrolling per request on a phone; a mistyped phone number on a quote cannot be corrected; the owner's phone customers are second-class forever.

**Suggestion.** Rename the summary 'Edit request', move Assigned to and Final price to inline controls beside Confirm (or to the top of the panel), and add name/email/phone/ZIP inputs to the panel (firestore.rules:118 already lets admins update any field).

### [MEDIUM] Plan cards show monthly figure without 'per month'
`app.js:223` · ux · lens: pricing-logic

On commercial.html and commercial-plans.html each plan card prints 'About $12,850' under the per-visit price with no unit, so the second number reads as another visit price; the 'per month' key exists in i18n.js but is never used.

**Evidence.** app.js:223 `'<p class="plan-monthly" data-monthly hidden>' + esc(t("plans.monthly")) + ' <span data-price="' + p.id + '" data-price-part="perMonth"></span></p>'` with i18n.js:43 plans.monthly = 'About' (ES 'Aprox.'); i18n.js:44 'plans.monthlyUnit': 'per month' has no reference anywhere (`grep -rn monthlyUnit` finds only the two definitions). Observed card text: 'Essentials | $593 | per visit | About $12,850 | $913 at full price' and at phone width 'Aprox. $1,266'. The dial uses dial.perMonth 'About {perMonth} per month' correctly.

**Impact.** Commercial visitors comparing the three cards see two unexplained dollar amounts per plan; the larger one looks like a huge per-visit price.

### [MEDIUM] Quote collects only a ZIP, no street address for the walk-through
`commercial-quote.html:90` · ux · lens: ux-commercial-journey

The commercial flow promises 'We walk the site, confirm the price' and lets the visitor hold a calendar window, yet the form never asks for the building's address, suite, or a site contact. The ZIP is the only location field in the payload and the Firestore schema has no address key at all.

**Evidence.** commercial-quote.html:88-93 step 1 has a single input id="zip" maxlength=5. quote.js:382-414 payload() has zip, company, name, email, phone, notes and no address; firestore.rules:61-65 quoteFields() hasOnly list contains 'zip' but nothing like 'address'. Review step observed: 'Site: Office in 28202'. Owner email observed: 'ZIP: 28202' and a calendar hold for 'Thu, Oct 22, 2026, After hours (6:30–9:30 pm)'.

**Impact.** Every commercial request needs a reply just to learn where the building is before a walk-through can be scheduled, eating into the 'within one business day' promise, and a held after-hours window on the calendar has no site attached to it.

**Suggestion.** Add an address line (street, suite, city, autocomplete="street-address") to the commercial step 1 or 2, pass it through payload(), mailFields() and the firestore.rules field list, and show it in the review and in admin.html.

### [MEDIUM] commercial.html ships residential copy in its static HTML
`commercial.html:137` · content · lens: ux-commercial-journey

Only the h1 and a few lines were rewritten in the markup; the hero lead, the three 'how it works' steps, 'Your home:', the three 'why' cards and all six FAQ answers are the residential strings, replaced at runtime by i18n.js commercial: keys. Before scripts run, with JavaScript off, or for any crawler that does not execute scripts, the commercial page reads as a home-cleaning page.

**Evidence.** commercial.html:78 'Skip a visit or change your plan whenever life changes.', 137-138 'Tell us about your home / Bedrooms, bathrooms, pets…', 161 'Your home:', 201 'No contracts. Skip a visit, pause for a trip…', 226-227 'Do I need to be home? … a lockbox, a door code, or a neighbor', 125 'Estimate for a {bedrooms}-bedroom, {bathrooms}-bathroom home.' Loaded with javaScriptEnabled:false in Playwright, the page's main text was 'How a plan works / Tell us about your home / Can I skip a week or pause? / Do I need to be home?' with raw '{perMonth}' and '{oneTimeStruck}' placeholders visible in the dial.

**Impact.** A brief flash of home-cleaning copy on slow connections, a residential page for anyone without JS, and a maintenance trap: the markup and i18n.js can drift (the hero h1 was updated, the lead was not).

**Suggestion.** Put the commercial English strings into the commercial pages' markup (or generate the three commercial pages from the residential ones with docs/make-pages.py), so the static HTML matches what i18n.js renders.

### [MEDIUM] Stored site estimate is client-supplied and never recomputed
`firestore.rules:81` · security · lens: pricing-logic

The request's estimate fields come straight from the visitor's browser and the rules only check they are non-negative numbers; admin.html then shows them as 'Site estimate', uses them for the referral credit cap and the time-clock revenue, and nothing re-derives them from the stored size, plan and frequency.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:81-82 `optNum(d, 'estimatePerVisit') && optNum(d, 'estimateOneVisit') && optNum(d, 'estimateFullPrice') && optNum(d, 'estimatePerMonth') && optNum(d, 'discount')` with optNum (line 49) accepting 0..9,999,999; quote.js:409 sets them from A.estimate() in the browser; quote.js:442 emails `"Site estimate" usd(per)`; admin.js:123/161 and timeclock.js:47 consume them. A tampered POST (or a browser running a cached older plans.js) stores e.g. estimatePerVisit: 1 for 'Family, weekly' and the owner sees '$1 per visit' as the site's own number. Could only be fully settled against the live database.

**Impact.** Owner: low exploit value, but a wrong 'site estimate' can be used to argue a price, feeds the credit cap, and after a plans.js rate change staff cannot tell a stale estimate from the current formula.

**Suggestion.** Have admin.js recompute A.estimate() from the stored dims/plan/frequency for the request's segment and flag any difference from the stored value; optionally tighten the rules to bound estimatePerVisit to a plausible range.

### [MEDIUM] 'Since 2014' and 'We're insured' unverified; logo bakes in the date
`i18n.js:120` · known-todo · lens: content-legal · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: medium, impact: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

Every page asserts the LLC has cleaned Charlotte homes since 2014 (copy, image alt text on every footer, and the logo ring itself reads ESTABLISHED 2014), and the terms say "We're insured". README and PLAN still list confirming insurance and the policies as open owner questions, and nothing in the repository records the LLC's formation date or an insurance policy. The terms give no street or registered-agent address.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:120 "local.p": "MKG Services, LLC has been cleaning homes in Charlotte, North Carolina since 2014..." (index.html:208-209; commercial variant i18n.js:348); footer alt text on every page, e.g. privacy.html:90 alt="MKG Services, Charlotte, North Carolina, established 2014"; brand/make-logo.py:35 "bottom": "ESTABLISHED 2014"; i18n.js:722 "terms.3.p1": "...We're insured; ..."; README.md:87 "Confirm the policies in the copy and in terms.html: ... insurance"; docs/PLAN.md:150 "Licensing, insurance, and background-check claims that are accurate and can be stated." i18n.js:726 terms contact is only "MKG Services, LLC, Charlotte, North Carolina."

**Impact.** If the LLC was formed after 2014 or no general-liability policy is in force, these are deceptive statements under N.C.G.S. 75-1.1 and the first thing a customer repeats when a damage claim arises. Cannot be verified from the repository; must be checked against the NC Secretary of State record and the certificate of insurance.

**Suggestion.** Verify the exact entity name (including the comma in "MKG Services, LLC") and formation date on the NC SOS register and confirm the policy; if the LLC is newer, phrase it "cleaning Charlotte homes since 2014" without "LLC" and reconsider the logo ring; state the insurance type ("general liability insured") or remove the sentence.

### [MEDIUM] 'No contracts' and 'stop any time' versus a week's notice in terms
`i18n.js:125` · content · lens: content-legal · votes 2/3 real (reproduce: medium, impact: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

The home page and meta description say "No contracts" and that customers can "stop altogether" or change plans "whenever life changes"; the terms say recurring plans have no contract but require a week's notice to pause or stop. Calling the arrangement "no contract" also undercuts the terms of service, which are the contract the late-cancel charge depends on. Commercial pages make a different promise, "no long contracts".

**Evidence.** i18n.js:125 "why.2.p": "No contracts. Skip a visit, pause for a trip, move from weekly to monthly, or stop altogether."; i18n.js:55 "hero.lead": "...Skip a visit or change your plan whenever life changes."; i18n.js:24 meta.desc.home ends "No contracts."; i18n.js:720 "terms.2.p2": "Recurring plans have no contract. Pause or stop with a week's notice."; i18n.js:303 "commercial:hero.lead": "...Month to month, no long contracts."; i18n.js:352 "commercial:why.2.p": "No long contracts...". Spanish mirrors at i18n.js:935, 865, 1530, 1113.

**Impact.** Customers who stop with two days' notice have a reasonable reading that nothing was owed, so the week's notice is unenforceable in practice; commercial prospects may expect a written service agreement the site never mentions.

**Suggestion.** Replace "No contracts" with "No long-term commitment" or "Month to month" on all lines, state the week's notice beside the pause promise (faq.a2 and why.2.p), and add to the terms that they apply to residential and commercial work unless a separate signed agreement exists.

### [MEDIUM] Commercial copy makes operational promises the README confirm-list omits
`i18n.js:311` · content · lens: ux-commercial-journey · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: medium, impact: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

The commercial pages state specific service commitments, sign-off sheet left on site, after-hours and before-opening at no extra charge, the same crew every visit, a supply report, consumables at cost, schedule changes on a week's notice, that are not among the policies README 'Before launch' item 5 asks the owner to confirm, so they can go live unchecked.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:310 'Same checklist every visit, with a sign-off sheet on site'; 311 'After-hours or before-opening visits at no extra charge'; 312 'Month to month. Change the schedule with a week's notice'; 361 '…the same crew comes each time'; 363 '…supplied by you or by us at cost'; 335 'A sign-off sheet stays on site'. README.md 'Before launch' 5 lists: no contracts, skip/pause, supplies included, price confirmed, reply time, 24-hour notice, come-back-and-fix, insurance. Spanish mirrors each promise (i18n.js:1120-1122, 1171, 1173).

**Impact.** Night-rate labour, a fixed crew and an on-site sign-off log are real cost and staffing commitments; a client who was promised them on the website will hold the owner to them.

**Suggestion.** Extend the README confirm list with the commercial promises and have the owner tick each one; soften or remove any that are not operational yet (for example 'same crew where possible').

### [MEDIUM] Privacy policy omits Google sign-in, Stripe and most browser storage
`i18n.js:704` · legal · lens: content-legal · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: medium, impact: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

The policy says sign-in uses the email "only to show you your own requests" and that the browser keeps only calculator choices and a draft that dies with the tab. The code also stores a friend's invite code for 60 days, the sign-in email for link sign-in, the language, a signed-in flag and the Firebase Auth session; account creation stores a display name and a referral code and prefills the phone from earlier requests; Google sign-in shares the Google profile; and "Pay online" hands clients to a Stripe link the policy never names.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:701 "privacy.1.p2": "The price calculator keeps your size and schedule choices in your browser ... Your quote draft is kept in the browser only until you close the tab."; referral.js:19 var KEY = "mkg.ref", MAX_AGE = 60 * 24 * 3600 * 1000 with localStorage.setItem at referral.js:38; backend.js:246 var LINK_KEY = "mkg.signInEmail" saved to localStorage; backend.js:240 localStorage.setItem("mkg.account", "1"); i18n.js:1646 var KEY = "mkg.lang"; backend.js:199 u.updateProfile({ displayName: name }); backend.js:457-467 createCode/ensureCode write users/{uid} with emailLower; quote.js:546 phone.value = last.phone prefilled from past requests; account.js:105 and 123 render a "Pay online" button to r.payLink or C.payments.link (plans.js:86-89, "Create a Payment Link in Stripe"); terms i18n.js:724 mentions "online payment where offered" but privacy.html:71-83 has no Stripe or Firebase Authentication paragraph.

**Impact.** The "What we collect" and "How we use it" sections are inaccurate about what is stored and shared; a customer paying through Stripe or signing in with Google is not told a third party processes that data. No cookie banner is needed for first-party functional storage, but the list should be true.

**Suggestion.** Add three short paragraphs in EN/ES: Accounts (Firebase Authentication by Google; Google sign-in shares name, email and photo; we store your name and a referral code), Payments (payment links open Stripe, which handles card details under its own policy; we never see card numbers), and What stays in your browser (language, calculator choices, quote draft until the tab closes, a friend's invite code for 60 days, your email while a sign-in link is pending, a signed-in flag).

### [MEDIUM] Terms hard-code 24 hours while plans.js holds the policy
`i18n.js:719` · maintainability · lens: pricing-logic · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: medium, impact: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

plans.js documents policy.changeNoticeHours as the number terms.html promises, and account.html reads it live, but the terms sentence (EN and ES) has '24' typed in and the i18n LIVE map has no {hours} placeholder, so changing the policy silently desynchronises the legal page from the account page.

**Evidence.** plans.js:91-94 comment 'terms.html promises a confirmed visit can be changed or cancelled free with this much notice' and `policy: { changeNoticeHours: 24 }`; i18n.js:719 terms.2.p1 '...with at least 24 hours' notice...' and i18n.js:1529 '...al menos 24 horas de aviso...' contain no placeholder; i18n.js:1685-1716 LIVE map defines phone, responseTime, maxPct etc. but no `hours`; i18n.js:673 acct.tooLate uses `{hours}` filled from the policy.

**Impact.** Owner: one config change produces a terms page and an account page that state different cut-offs, which is exactly the kind of inconsistency that voids a late fee.

**Suggestion.** Add `hours` to LIVE (a span fed from C.policy.changeNoticeHours) and change terms.2.p1 to 'at least {hours} hours' in both languages.

### [MEDIUM] Terms are written for homes: per-visit payment, no invoicing or access terms
`i18n.js:724` · legal · lens: ux-commercial-journey · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: low, impact: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

The shared terms.html says payment is 'due at or after each visit', which contradicts the monthly figure and the 'about $X per month' framing the commercial pages lead with, and says nothing about invoicing, net terms, late fees, key and alarm-code custody, after-hours access, or supplies 'at cost'. The contract wording also differs: terms say 'no contract', commercial copy says 'no long contracts'.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:724 terms.4.p1 'Payment is due at or after each visit, by the method we agree on…'; i18n.js:720 terms.2.p2 'Recurring plans have no contract. Pause or stop with a week's notice.'; i18n.js:303 commercial:hero.lead 'Month to month, no long contracts.'; i18n.js:363 commercial:faq.a4 '…supplied by you or by us at cost'. terms.html rendered text (checked in Spanish at 1280) has sections Estimates, Requests/changes, Our work, Payment, Referral credit, Contact only. README 'Before launch' item 5 asks the owner to confirm policies but lists only the residential ones.

**Impact.** A business that expects a monthly invoice (net 15/30, PO number) has no basis on the site; 'at cost' consumables and key handling are promises with no terms behind them, which is where disputes start.

**Suggestion.** Add a short 'For businesses' section to terms (monthly invoicing and due date, consumables billing, key/alarm custody and liability, schedule-change notice, that a written proposal governs) and align 'no contract' wording across hero, FAQ and terms in both languages.

### [MEDIUM] Referral terms blur who earns what; repeat check has blind spots
`i18n.js:829` · legal · lens: content-legal · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: medium, impact: low, refute: medium) · **confirmed**

terms.5 says "Referral credit is for first-time customers", yet the referrer's half goes to existing customers, and anyone with a verified sign-in, customer or not, gets a code. "Stacks with no limit, doesn't expire" plus "credit already earned stays yours" is an open-ended liability with no expiry or annual cap. The automatic new-customer check scans only the admin page's last 300 requests and matches only email or a 10-digit phone, so some returning customers get the welcome credit automatically.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:829 "terms.5.p1": "Referral credit is for first-time customers and comes off the price of a visit when we confirm it. It stacks with no limit, doesn't expire, has no cash value..."; refer.html:89-93 rules; backend.js:467 ensureCode(user) creates a code for any signed-in user, allowed by firestore.rules:231 allow create: if verified() && request.auth.uid == uid; admin.js:125-132 isRepeat(r) iterates items, which backend.js:378 watchRequests caps with .limit(300), and matches only emailLower or digits(o.phone) of 10+ digits; admin.js:168 grants() then writes a welcome- credit automatically on confirm unless isRepeat(r). docs/SETUP-firebase.md:102 states the intended rule.

**Impact.** Ambiguous eligibility invites "the page said" disputes; unexpiring credit must be carried indefinitely even if the program ends; a returning customer whose earlier visit is older than the last 300 requests, or was booked by phone with a different email, receives $25 or $50 the rules say they should not.

**Suggestion.** Rewrite terms.5 as two sentences (welcome credit: new customers only, one per household or business; referrer credit: when the friend's first visit is done), decide and state an expiry (12 months is common) with 30 days' notice before ending the program, and base isRepeat on a server query by emailLower and phone rather than the loaded window.

### [MEDIUM] Spanish version is not indexable by Spanish searches
`index.html:8` · seo · lens: growth-gap

The bilingual build is a real advantage, but Google can only see one English copy. The hreflang alternates are relative query-string URLs, both versions share the English canonical, the HTML ships `lang="en"` with English title and description that only JavaScript swaps, and the sitemap has no Spanish URLs. Nothing on the English pages says Spanish is spoken.

**Evidence.** index.html:8-10 `<link rel="alternate" hreflang="es" href="index.html?lang=es">`; index.html:19 `<link rel="canonical" href="https://mkgservices.com/">` (Playwright on index.html?lang=es reports the same canonical); i18n.js:1724 `document.documentElement.lang = lang;` runs at runtime only; index.html:37 JSON-LD description is English only; sitemap.xml:3-12 contains no ?lang=es entries. `grep -n "español\|espanol" index.html` finds only the ES toggle's aria-label.

**Impact.** Charlotte has a large Spanish-speaking household and small-business market; a search for 'limpieza de casas Charlotte' or 'servicio de limpieza para oficinas' cannot surface MKG, so the Spanish site is reached only through shared ?lang=es links (referral.js:61).

**Suggestion.** Pre-render static Spanish copies (e.g. es/index.html, es/plans.html, es/quote.html) from the ES dictionary with self-canonicals and absolute two-way hreflang, list them in sitemap.xml, and add 'Hablamos español' next to the hero CTA; keep ?lang=es working for shared links. A small generator script in docs/ or hand-rendered copies.

### [MEDIUM] Commercial card and third CTA push the price dial below the fold
`index.html:102` · ux · lens: ux-residential-journey

On a 390px phone the first screen holds the headline, three equal-weight buttons and the Commercial service card; the dial that proves the value proposition ("watch the price come down") starts at 747px in English and 806px in Spanish, below the 844px viewport.

**Evidence.** index.html lines 97-110: `.hero-actions` with three `.btn` links, then `<a class="switch-card" href="commercial.html">` before the `.dial` at line 113; styles.css line 390 stacks `.hero-grid` to one column and lines 449-451 widen `.switch-card` to full width on phones. Measured: `.hero-actions` top 400, `.switch-card` top 538 (h 102), `.dial` top 747 (EN) / 806 (ES, where the three buttons stack vertically). The fold screenshots show the commercial card but no price.

**Impact.** Homeowners, the primary audience, see a commercial pitch before any number; the strongest conversion element needs a scroll on every phone.

**Suggestion.** On phones move the commercial card below the dial (or into the nav where "Commercial" already is), keep "Get my quote" plus "Compare plans" and demote "Book a single clean" to a text link under them.

The trust block is `#why` (index.html lines 203-227): logo, "since 2014" and three service points.

### [MEDIUM] Phone says "Optional" but the default contact method requires it
`quote.html:190` · ux · lens: ux-residential-journey

Step 4 labels Phone as Optional, but "Best way to reach you" defaults to Text, which makes the phone mandatory; the radios sit below the phone field, so the visitor only discovers the rule from an error after tapping Continue.

Observed at 390px: with name and email filled and phone empty, Continue shows the red "Add a phone number so we can text you." under a field marked Optional (screenshot en-32-q4-phone-error).

### [MEDIUM] Text pre-selected, phone 'Optional', no SMS consent wording
`quote.html:198` · legal · lens: content-legal

The contact step pre-checks "Text" as the way MKG will reach the customer, which makes the phone field mandatory even though its label says "Optional", and nothing on the site says what MKG will text, how often, how to stop, or that message rates may apply. Neither the privacy policy nor the terms mention texting customers.

**Evidence.** quote.html:198 <input type="radio" name="contactMethod" value="Text" checked>; quote.html:190 Phone <span ...>Optional</span>; quote.js:291-293 if ((method === "Text" || method === "Call") && value("phone").replace(/\D/g, "").length < 10) { setError("phone", t("err.phone." + method)) ... }. Observed with Playwright: filling only name and email and pressing Continue shows "Add a phone number so we can text you." under the field labelled Optional (screenshot /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/5cd54e72-5e10-5f26-9ec5-c19b545a368a/scratchpad/work/product/content-legal/quote-step4-en.png). Same markup at commercial-quote.html:198 and 206. Grep of i18n.js finds no STOP/opt-out, frequency or rates wording in either language.

**Impact.** Replies about the customer's own request are informational and generally covered by the number being supplied for that purpose, but any promotional text (referral nudges, "we have an opening", seasonal offers) needs prior express written consent under the TCPA, with statutory damages of $500 to $1,500 per message; a pre-ticked default is the pattern plaintiffs single out. The Optional/required mismatch also confuses visitors.

**Suggestion.** Default the radio to none or Email, drop "Optional" when Text or Call is selected, add under the phone field in EN/ES "We'll only text or call about this request. Reply STOP to opt out; message rates may apply.", and add a sentence to privacy.2 about how the contact preference is used.

### [MEDIUM] Step 5 captures one date, hides weekly days, and never says if it is the walk-through
`quote.js:99` · ux · lens: ux-commercial-journey

For a recurring plan such as 'twice a week' the When step only lets the visitor pick a single day and window; as soon as a date is picked the 'Days that work' and 'When should we come?' preferences are hidden, so the days of the week for the ongoing schedule are never collected. The copy also never says whether the picked day is the walk-through or the first clean, while the confirmation says the reply is 'to set up a quick walk-through'.

**Evidence.** quote.js:99 'if (prefs) prefs.hidden = !!draft.date;'. commercial-quote.html:224-225 'When would you like us? Pick a day and a start window that's still open.' versus i18n.js:414 commercial:q.done.p 'We'll reply {responseTime} to set up a quick walk-through and confirm the exact price.' Observed owner email for a twice-a-week request with a date: 'Day and window: Thu, Oct 22, 2026, After hours (6:30–9:30 pm) | Service: Professional, Twice a week', with no second weekday anywhere; backend.js:163-164 also writes a capacity-consuming hold for that one window.

**Impact.** The owner gets a one-off slot for a multi-night contract and has to ask which nights, and the visitor may believe they booked a cleaning when the site intends a walk-through first (or vice versa).

**Suggestion.** On commercial pages relabel step 5 as 'When can we walk the site?' (or 'first visit'), keep the 'Days that work' checkboxes visible for recurring plans and send them as scheduleDays, and show the chosen nights in the review and email.

### [MEDIUM] Invite code shown as applied, then silently dropped at submit
`quote.js:104` · bug · lens: ux-residential-journey

A code is treated as applied unless the check returned exactly `false`; an inconclusive check (`null`) still shows "Invite applied: $25 off". If Firestore then refuses the request because of the code (own code, removed code), quote.js resubmits without it and the confirmation never says the credit was lost.

**Evidence.** quote.js line 104 `refApplied()` returns true when `draft.refValid !== false`; backend.js line 449 `checkReferral` resolves `null` when it cannot ask Firebase and on a read error (line 450 `.catch(... null)`). Observed locally: typing ABCDEF gives "Invite applied: $25 off your first visit." and the review row "Invite: Friend's code ABCDEF: $25 off your first visit". On the live site firestore.rules lines 85-90 `refOk` refuse a request whose code belongs to the same email; quote.js lines 498-502 then catch, delete `ref`, set `refDropped` and resend, and line 505 shows the credit note only `if (data.ref)`, so the customer sees plain "Request sent".

**Impact.** A customer who was promised $25 off in step 4 and in the review learns it does not apply only when the invoice arrives; the owner sees `refDropped` in admin but the customer is not told.

**Suggestion.** Treat `null` as unverified (show "We'll confirm this code when we reply" and do not add the review row), and when the resend path drops the code, render a notice on the done screen that the invite could not be applied.

### [MEDIUM] The requester receives no confirmation email of what they submitted
`quote.js:424` · ux · lens: ux-commercial-journey

The Formspree post is addressed to the business only; the visitor's email is set purely as the Reply-To. Nothing sends the office manager a copy of the scope, estimate, size and requested window they entered, so the 'Request sent' screen is the only record (and it disappears on navigation; the draft is cleared).

**Evidence.** quote.js:422-425 var m = { _subject: …, email: d.email }; app.js:346-353 notify() posts those fields to C.formEndpoint; plans.js:34-40 describe it as 'The business ALSO gets an email'; quote.js:480 clearDraft() on done. No code path emails the client; Formspree autoresponders are a paid dashboard feature and would have to be confirmed on the Formspree side, which cannot be checked from the repo.

**Impact.** Business buyers file vendor quotes and forward them for approval; with nothing in their inbox the request is easy to forget, and the estimate they saw cannot be compared with the owner's later price.

**Suggestion.** Send a client copy (a second Formspree form with the client's address, a Firebase Trigger Email extension on requests/{id}, or Formspree's autoresponse once on a paid plan) containing the review table, the reference, and the walk-through next step; until then add a 'Print or save this page' link on the done step.
