Arriving from availability.html with `?date=...&slot=...`, the quote opens on "Where's the home?" with nothing confirming the chosen window; the visitor only sees it again four steps later on the When step.

**Evidence.** quote.js line 572 reads `date` and `slot` into `draft` but nothing renders them until `renderWhen()` at step 5 (line 363); step 1 markup (quote.html lines 84-98) has no slot for it. Observed: quote.html?date=2026-10-22&slot=0800 shows the plain ZIP step (screenshot en-73-handoff-q1); the summary "Requested: Thursday, October 22, Morning (8:00–11:00)" appears only at step 5. The `?service=deep` entry, by contrast, renders tabs and a description above the heading (quote.js lines 241-249).

**Impact.** Visitors who chose a window may doubt it carried over and re-pick or restart; those who want a different day cannot change it until step 5.

**Suggestion.** Reuse the service-tabs pattern: when `draft.date` is set, show a one-line notice above step 1 ("Requested: Thursday, October 22, Morning — Change") that jumps to step 5 or clears the pick.

### [MEDIUM] On phones, schedule and bookings sit several screens below the fold
`styles.css:580` · ux · lens: ux-account-admin

At phone width the admin grid stacks clock, then every request card, then the week ahead and calendar, so the owner scrolls past the whole inbox to see today's schedule. On the client page the referral panel with its QR code is placed before 'Your requests'.

**Evidence.** styles.css:580 '.admin-grid { grid-template-rows: none; grid-template-areas: "clock" "main" "side"; }' (within @media (max-width: 980px)). Measured with the mock at 390px: default New filter with three requests puts The week ahead at y=4772 and the calendar at y=5236 of a 6342px page; with the All filter (nine requests) the calendar is at y=11284. account.html:169 '<section class="ref-panel" data-ref-panel hidden>' precedes the 'Your requests' heading at line 193; measured: referral panel 1293px tall, first request card at y=1748.

**Impact.** The two things each user opens the page for (the owner: what is on today; the client: is my visit confirmed) are not visible without long scrolling on the device both actually use.

**Suggestion.** Below 980px turn Requests / Schedule / Clock into tabs using the existing .seg component and remember the choice; on account.html render the requests first and collapse the referral section to a one-line 'Your credit: $25 · Invite a friend' that expands.

### [MEDIUM] Terms silent on keys, access codes, lockouts, damage claims, governing law
`terms.html:77` · legal · lens: content-legal

The terms are six short paragraphs. The site invites customers to hand over lockbox codes, door codes, keys and alarm logins, and to type details into free-text notes that are emailed through Formspree, yet the terms say nothing about key custody, alarm liability, what happens when the crew cannot get in, how damage is claimed and capped, pre-existing damage, late payment, employee non-solicitation, governing law, or how the terms change.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:135 "faq.a3": "...Most clients give us a way in: a lockbox, a door code, or a neighbor."; i18n.js:361 "commercial:faq.a3": "...Most clients give us a key, a code, or an alarm login..."; quote.html:133 notes textarea (2000 chars) forwarded by app.js:346-353 notify() to Formspree. terms.html:77 / i18n.js:722 is the entire liability text: "We're insured; damage caused by our team is reported to you the same day."; terms.html:79 payment: "Payment is due at or after each visit ... Unpaid balances may pause service." No lockout, claims-deadline, liability-cap, North Carolina law or amendment clause exists in any terms.* key (grep of i18n.js).

**Impact.** In a dispute (lost key, alarm call-out fee, scratched floor, crew turned away at the door) MKG has no written position; for commercial clients who hand over alarm logins the exposure is larger. These are the first clauses a NC small-business lawyer will ask for.

**Suggestion.** Have counsel add, in plain language and both languages: access and keys (how codes and keys are stored and returned; ask customers to give codes by phone, not in the notes field), a lockout/no-access charge aligned with the late-cancel figure, damage claims (report within 24 to 48 hours; remedy limited to repair or replacement up to the insurance limit; pre-existing wear excluded), payment terms for late balances, North Carolina governing law, and "we may update these terms; the date above changes when we do".

### [MEDIUM] Cleaners see only today's jobs, no assignee on the calendar, English service names
`timeclock.js:69` · ux · lens: ux-account-admin

My day filters strictly to today, so a cleaner opening the page in the evening cannot see tomorrow; the calendar day panel shows names but not who is assigned; and the job card prints the stored English plan/service text even in the Spanish UI, unlike the client page which translates it.

**Evidence.** timeclock.js:69 'var todays = getRequests().filter(function (r) { return r.date === today && r.status !== "declined" && r.status !== "cancelled"; })'; the only other per-person view is the '→ rosa' suffix in the week ahead (admin.js:347), which lists everyone's jobs; admin.js:384-387 day panel renders holds with name and status only. timeclock.js:48 'function serviceText(r) { return r.service || ((r.plan || "") + ...' and admin.js:188 use raw stored English; observed in the ES render: 'Servicio: Essentials, Monthly · Casa', 'Floor care · Negocio', 'Pisos: Hardwood', while account.js:78-84 maps the same values through the plans.js dictionaries.

**Impact.** Cleaners must call the owner to learn tomorrow's jobs or scroll the full request list (which exposes every client's notes); a Spanish-speaking crew reads service names in English on their day sheet.

**Suggestion.** Add a 'Tomorrow' and 'This week' section to My day filtered by assignedTo; show the assignee (and an assign select) in the calendar day panel; reuse the account.js serviceText lookup in timeclock.js and admin.js so plan and service names follow the UI language.

### [MEDIUM] A forgotten clock-out can only be ended 'now'; the owner must fix each one
`timeclock.js:128` · ux · lens: ux-account-admin

Clock-out always writes the server time, and the rules forbid a member from setting an earlier time, so a shift left open overnight becomes a 16-hour shift when the cleaner next opens the page. Only the owner can correct it, one shift at a time, and nothing warns before payroll.

**Evidence.** timeclock.js:128 'B.clockOut(open.id).then(...)' and backend.js:329 'update({ outAt: stamp() })'; firestore.rules:222-224 restrict a member's update to their own open shift with 'request.resource.data.outAt == request.time'; the owner's fix path is the per-row datetime-local form at timeclock.js:242-247 and 270-274; the banner at lines 99-101 colours shifts over LONG_HOURS red but offers only End; the CSV (lines 279-289) exports hours as they stand.

**Impact.** Inflated hours flow into the payroll CSV until the owner notices the 'over 12h' flag; cleaners cannot correct their own mistake and will stop trusting the clock.

**Suggestion.** Let a member end an open shift at a chosen time between inAt and now (rules: outAt <= request.time && outAt >= resource.data.inAt) with a required note, show a 'Did you forget to clock out?' prompt with a time picker once a shift passes a threshold, and auto-close shifts after about 14 hours with a flag the owner reviews.

### [LOW] Facility type never changes the estimate or the suggested plan
`commercial-quote.html:106` · ux · lens: ux-commercial-journey

Office, medical/dental, restaurant and gym all produce the same per-visit estimate for the same size, and picking 'Medical or dental' does not nudge toward the Complete plan the cards say is 'best for medical, food, and fitness'. The list also has no warehouse/industrial, daycare, salon, multi-tenant common areas or coworking, and 'Other' has no free-text prompt.

**Evidence.** commercial-quote.html:106-112 radios Office, Medical or dental, Retail, Restaurant or café, Gym or studio, Church or school, Other. app.js:35 estimate() sums only C.dims (sqft, restrooms); plans.js:261 quote: { typeField: "facilityType" } is stored only. Observed: a 'Medical or dental' 3,000 sq ft request estimated $182 per visit, identical to Office; plans.js:314 Complete bestFor 'Client-facing spaces, medical, food, and fitness.'

**Impact.** Clinics and restaurants get an estimate that will almost always rise after the walk-through, undercutting 'the number on the invoice is the number you agreed to', while the recommended plan stays Professional.

**Suggestion.** Either add a per-type multiplier in plans.js (and say so in notes.3) or, when Medical/Restaurant/Gym is picked, preselect Complete and show a one-line note that these spaces are usually quoted on Complete; add the missing types or an 'Other: describe' hint.

### [LOW] PDF generator rounds half-to-even while the site rounds half-up
`docs/make-pricing-pdf.py:87` · maintainability · lens: pricing-logic

The pricing PDF is meant to never drift from plans.js, but its Python round()/'{:,.0f}' use banker's rounding whereas app.js uses Math.round, so for sizes other than the defaults the document and the site disagree by $1; the PDF also describes the commercial follow-up as borrowing its discounts from the ladder although one tier is hard-coded.

**Evidence.** docs/make-pricing-pdf.py:87 `return one, round(one * (1 - discount))` and :23 `"${:,.0f}".format(n)` versus app.js:36 `Math.round(oneTime * (1 - discount))`. grid.js enumeration: 7 residential combinations differ (e.g. Family every-2-weeks 1 bed 4 bath: 202.5 -> site $203, Python $202) and 3,857 commercial ones (e.g. Essentials 500 sq ft 1 restroom full price 76.5 -> site $77, Python $76). Today's PDF is correct only because its worked examples (3/2 and 3,000/2) avoid ties; the formula text 'rounded to the nearest dollar' does not say which way. make-pricing-pdf.py:48/:136 print the same fu_p paragraph ('borrowed from the schedule ladder') for commercial, but plans.js:383 has `{ id: "twoweeks", discount: 0.05 }` with no 5% commercial ladder step.

**Impact.** Owner or developer reading the PDF as the reference gets a different dollar than the site for some sizes, and a wrong description of how the commercial 2-week follow-up is set.

**Suggestion.** Use `int(math.floor(x + 0.5))` in the generator, state 'halves round up' in the formula line, and make the follow-up paragraph conditional on whether each tier has `uses` or its own `discount`.

### [LOW] No tax, surcharge or after-hours terms; calendar lacks the promised window
`i18n.js:311` · pricing · lens: pricing-logic · votes 1/3 real (reproduce: low, impact: low, refute: low) · rejected

Prices never say whether tax, travel, parking or after-hours labour is included; the commercial dial promises before-opening and after-hours visits 'at no extra charge' although the booking calendar offers no before-opening window, and nothing states whether any service is taxable.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:311 commercial:dial.li2 'After-hours or before-opening visits at no extra charge'; plans.js:78-83 slots start at '8:00–11:00' and the only extra commercial window is '6:30–9:30 pm' (id 1830), so a 'before opening' request can only be typed as a preference (commercial-quote.html timeOfDay 'Before opening'). `grep -n -i 'tax' i18n.js` matches only privacy.4.p1 (record retention); terms.4.p1 (i18n.js:724) covers payment timing only. Commercial 'Floor care' (plans.js:359-365, strip and wax, carpet extraction) is the kind of service whose sales-tax treatment in North Carolina should be confirmed.

**Impact.** Owner: an after-hours or early-morning crew cost cannot be recovered under the current promise, and a client invoiced tax on a service the site priced without it has a fair complaint.

**Suggestion.** Owner decision: confirm with their accountant which services (if any) carry NC sales tax and add 'plus tax where applicable' to the estimate notes and terms; either add a before-opening slot or soften dial.li2; state explicitly that there are no travel or parking fees if that is the policy.

### [LOW] Size sentences don't pluralise: '1 restrooms', '1 habitaciones y 1 baños'
`i18n.js:313` · i18n · lens: pricing-logic · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: low, impact: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

The live size placeholders are plain numbers, so several pricing sentences print wrong plurals for 1, and the Spanish quote step uses two different words for bedroom in adjacent sentences.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:313 commercial:dial.fine 'Estimate for about {sqft} sq ft with {restrooms} restrooms.' -> observed 'Estimate for about 500 sq ft with 1 restrooms.'; i18n.js:881 dial.fine ES 'una casa de {bedrooms} habitaciones y {bathrooms} baños' -> observed 'Estimado para una casa de 1 habitaciones y 1 baños.'; same pattern at i18n.js:1029 (q.s3.p ES) and i18n.js:1123 (commercial dial.fine ES, observed 'con 1 baños'). On the same Spanish quote step, q.fu.note (i18n.js:1000) says '3 recámaras' while q.s3.p (i18n.js:1029) says '3 habitaciones'. The LIVE map (i18n.js:1686-1689) emits bare `<span data-text>` numbers with no plural hook.

**Impact.** Visitors (notably Spanish readers, 1-bed/1-bath apartments, single-restroom suites) see ungrammatical pricing captions that undercut the 'exact price' message.

**Suggestion.** Add plural-aware LIVE placeholders (e.g. {bedroomsN} rendered via I.plural like the steppers at app.js:160) and standardise on 'habitaciones' (or 'recámaras') across i18n.js and the PDF.

### [LOW] Deletion and retention promises lack a process the site supports
`i18n.js:710` · legal · lens: content-legal · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: low, impact: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

The policy promises to show or delete what MKG holds and to delete records once no longer needed, with no period stated. Deleting a request from admin removes the request, hold and staff notes but not credit ledger lines, the users/referrers code documents, time-clock shifts that reference it, the Firebase Auth account, the Formspree submission, or the Gmail copy; staff-only notes about a customer are personal data an access request must include.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:710 "privacy.4.p1": "Email {email} to see what we hold about you or to have it deleted. We keep request records for as long as we may need them for scheduling, billing, and tax purposes, then delete them."; backend.js:420-427 deleteRequest(id) deletes only holds, internal/{id} and requests/{id}; firestore.rules:122 allow delete: if isTeamOwner();; firestore.rules:167-173 internal/{id} adminNotes up to 4000 chars, admins only; docs/SETUP-firebase.md:73-77 Formspree keeps submissions (plus an "Over limit" folder); no Auth-account deletion path exists in backend.js.

**Impact.** A deletion request answered from the admin page alone leaves copies in four other places, and an access response that omits adminNotes is incomplete. Low likelihood, but it is a written promise.

**Suggestion.** State a retention period (for example three years after the last visit, matching tax record keeping) and keep an internal checklist for deletion: Firestore request, hold, internal, credits, users and referrers; Firebase Auth user; Formspree submission; Gmail thread. Include adminNotes in access responses.

### [LOW] 24-hour notice and 'Last updated' date are hand-typed in copy
`i18n.js:719` · maintainability · lens: content-legal · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: low, impact: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

plans.js documents policy.changeNoticeHours as what terms.html promises and account.js reads it, but the terms sentence hard-codes "24 hours" in both languages, so changing the setting makes the account page and the terms disagree. The "Last updated October 16, 2026" line is also typed by hand; the referral clauses were appended after the main blocks and refer.html is dated a day later in the sitemap, with no history to show the date was bumped.

**Evidence.** plans.js:91-94 "// Changes and cancellations. terms.html promises a confirmed visit can be changed or cancelled free with this much notice ... policy: { changeNoticeHours: 24 }"; account.js:26 var NOTICE_HOURS = (C.policy && C.policy.changeNoticeHours) || 24;; i18n.js:719 and 1529 contain the literal "24 hours' notice" / "24 horas de aviso"; i18n.js:698 and 715 "Last updated October 16, 2026."; privacy.1.p3 and terms.5.* sit at i18n.js:827-829 after the refer.* block; sitemap.xml:14 lists refer.html lastmod 2026-10-17 versus 2026-10-16 for privacy and terms; git log shows one squashed commit (4d1bbbf, 2026-10-17).

**Impact.** A config change silently produces two contradictory policies; an unbumped date weakens any "you agreed to the version dated X" argument.

**Suggestion.** Add an hours LIVE placeholder (like {phone}) fed from C.policy.changeNoticeHours and write "{hours} hours" in terms.2.p1 EN/ES; keep a one-line changelog comment next to privacy.updated/terms.updated and bump both whenever a terms.* or privacy.* key changes.

### [LOW] 'A friend's invite code … welcome credit' on the business contact step
`i18n.js:733` · content · lens: ux-commercial-journey

The referral field on the commercial quote form reuses the residential wording in both languages: a friend's invite code and a welcome credit 'on your first visit', even though the program has separate $50 business rewards and refer.html has business-specific copy.

Gets you a welcome credit on your first visit."; Spanish 1543/1545 'Código de invitación de un amigo … crédito de bienvenida'. plans.js:63 commercial: { friend: 50, referrer: 50 }; i18n.js:746 ref.rewardsBiz exists for the refer page. Observed on step 4 at 390 and 1280 in EN and ES.
