### [LOW] Spanish terms: 'nuestro equipo' reads as 'our equipment'; 'due' lost
`i18n.js:1532` · i18n · lens: content-legal · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: low, impact: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

In the same Spanish paragraph "equipo" is first used for equipment ("productos y equipo") and then for the team ("daño causado por nuestro equipo"), so the liability sentence can be read as damage caused by our equipment. The payment clause renders "is due" as "se hace" (is made), dropping the obligation, and "cualquier daño" (any damage) is broader than the English.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:1532 "terms.3.p1": "Llevamos nuestros propios productos y equipo salvo que acordemos otra cosa. ... Estamos asegurados; cualquier daño causado por nuestro equipo se te informa el mismo día." versus i18n.js:722 "damage caused by our team"; i18n.js:1534 "terms.4.p1": "El pago se hace en la visita o después de ella..." versus i18n.js:724 "Payment is due at or after each visit". Rendered at http://127.0.0.1:8787/terms.html?lang=es.

**Impact.** Spanish-speaking customers rely on the Spanish version, which is ambiguous on liability wording and softer on payment than the English; in a dispute the customer's language version is the one quoted.

**Suggestion.** Use "daño causado por nuestro personal" and "El pago vence en la visita o después de ella, por el medio que acordemos..."; drop "cualquier".

### [LOW] No social profiles, sameAs, hours or price range in structured data
`index.html:30` · seo · lens: growth-gap

The footer has only phone, SMS and email, and the LocalBusiness JSON-LD lacks `sameAs`, `openingHoursSpecification`, `priceRange` and `hasMap`. Visitors cannot check the business on Facebook, Instagram or Nextdoor, and Google cannot tie the site entity to a Business Profile listing.

**Evidence.** index.html:30-47 JSON-LD fields are name, alternateName, description, url, logo, image, telephone, email, foundingDate, address, areaServed; `grep -n "sameAs\|openingHours\|priceRange" *.html` returns nothing. Footer contact block index.html:279-286 has data-business phone/sms/email only. Playwright: `externalLinks: []` on every page.

**Impact.** Charlotte neighbours look for cleaners on Nextdoor and Facebook groups; a site with no profiles reads as newer and smaller than 'since 2014' claims, and the knowledge-panel link-up is lost.

**Suggestion.** Add `business.social` (facebook, instagram, nextdoor, google) to plans.js and render icons in the footer through `fillBusiness()` in app.js; add `sameAs`, `openingHoursSpecification` (from `availability.weekdays` and slots) and `priceRange` to the JSON-LD on index.html and commercial.html. Thirty minutes once the profiles exist.

### [LOW] No gift cards, although the credits ledger could hold them
`plans.js:86` · growth · lens: growth-gap

There is no gift-card product or page, and no payment path to sell one. The admin credits ledger already supports arbitrary 'adjust' entries per email, so the missing pieces are a Stripe link and a page.

**Evidence.** `grep -rniE "gift|regalo"` over html/js finds only i18n.js:1564 'Descargar tarjeta con QR'; plans.js:86-89 `payments: { link: "" }`; admin.js:505-509 adds `{ email, amount, type: "adjust", note }` credits by hand; SETUP-firebase.md:103 'Credits stack and do not expire.'

**Impact.** Housewarming, new-baby and holiday gifts are a seasonal revenue line that brings new households at zero acquisition cost and prepays work; competitors in PLAN.md's research offer them.

**Suggestion.** Create a Stripe Payment Link for fixed amounts, add gift.html (EN/ES) that explains redemption and links to it, and on payment add the amount as an 'adjust' credit to the recipient's email on admin.html; the account page already shows the balance. An hour plus a Stripe link.

### [LOW] Follow-up 'uses' indirection is unvalidated and crashes on a typo
`quote.js:202` · maintainability · lens: pricing-logic · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: low, refute: low, impact: low) · **confirmed**

A follow-up tier's `uses` must name an existing frequency id; nothing checks it, and a bad value makes fuEstimate() return null so step 3 throws when the follow-up option is picked. The same indirection also carries visitsPerMonth into a one-visit estimate on commercial.

**Evidence.** quote.js:202 `return A.estimate(C.followUp.basePlan, A.state, tier.uses !== undefined ? tier.uses : tier.discount);` -> app.js:31-32 returns null for an unknown id; quote.js:233 then dereferences `e.perVisit` with no null check (and summary() at quote.js:316-321 likewise), so a renamed frequency id (e.g. 'biweekly' -> 'every2') breaks the quote form. On commercial, plans.js:382 `uses: "weekly"` makes app.js:44 compute `perMonth` (4.33 visits) for a single follow-up visit; it is not displayed today (quote.js:232-236) and not stored (quote.js:404), but any generic reuse of the estimate object would show a monthly figure for a one-time clean.

**Impact.** Developer/owner editing plans.js: a plausible rename takes the quote form down for the follow-up option with no error message; the README says plans.js is safe to edit.

**Suggestion.** Resolve `uses` to a discount number before calling estimate (tierDiscount() already exists at quote.js:204), guard null in renderFollowUp/summary, and have make-pricing-pdf.py or a small check fail loudly on an unknown `uses`.

### [LOW] Step heading shows a focus box on first load
`quote.js:367` · ux · lens: ux-residential-journey · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: low, refute: low, impact: low) · **confirmed**

On opening the quote page the H1 "Where's the home?" is programmatically focused before any user interaction, so Chrome applies `:focus-visible` and draws the 3px green outline around the heading.

**Evidence.** quote.js line 367 `heading.setAttribute("tabindex", "-1"); heading.focus({ preventScroll: true });` runs from `init()` via `go(1)` (line 595); styles.css line 48 `:focus-visible { outline: 3px solid var(--pine); ... }`. Playwright: `h1.matches(":focus-visible")` is true on load in EN and ES (screenshots en-20-q1-zip, en-C1-q1-service-deep show the boxed heading); after a real tap on Continue the next heading reports false.

**Impact.** Cosmetic, but it is the first thing every visitor sees on the quote page and reads like a rendering glitch.

**Suggestion.** Skip the focus call on the initial `go(1)` (focus only on user-driven navigation), or add `.step .title:focus { outline: none; }` since the headings are not interactive.

### [LOW] Owner email says 'after hourss' and 'during hourss' for commercial preferences
`quote.js:436` · bug · lens: ux-commercial-journey · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: low, impact: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

mailFields pluralises the time-of-day preference by appending 's', which works for the residential values Morning/Afternoon but not for the commercial values 'After hours', 'Before opening' and 'During hours'.

**Evidence.** quote.js:436 d.timeOfDay && d.timeOfDay !== "Either" ? d.timeOfDay.toLowerCase() + "s" : "". Observed Formspree bodies for requests sent without a date: 'Day and window: None picked yet. Best for them: Tue, Thu, Sun, after hourss', '…before openings', '…during hourss'.

**Impact.** Every commercial request without a calendar date reaches the owner with a garbled schedule line; harmless to the client, sloppy in the owner's inbox.

**Suggestion.** Map the value to a phrase ({ 'After hours': 'after closing', 'Before opening': 'before opening', 'During hours': 'during business hours' }) and keep the +'s' only for the residential values.

### [LOW] Draft is re-saved after sending, so next quote is prefilled
`quote.js:481` · bug · lens: ux-residential-journey · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: medium, refute: medium, impact: low) · **confirmed**

`done()` clears the draft and then calls `go("done")`, which saves it again with `step: "done"`; opening quote.html again in the same tab prefills every field, including notes, from the request just sent.

**Evidence.** quote.js line 480 `clearDraft();` followed by line 481 `go("done");`, and `go()` calls `saveDraft()` at line 365. Observed after a mocked successful send: `sessionStorage.mkg.quote` still holds `{"zip":"28277","name":"Ana Pérez","email":...,"notes":"Please skip the office...","step":"done",...}` and a fresh quote.html shows ZIP 28277 and "Ana Pérez" filled in.

**Impact.** A second request from the same tab (another property, a friend's home) starts with stale details; on a shared device the previous person's name, email and notes are visible to the next user of that tab.

**Suggestion.** Call `clearDraft()` after `go("done")`, or have `go()` skip `saveDraft()` when `n === "done"`.

### [LOW] No blog, tips or pricing-guide content for organic search
`sitemap.xml:12` · seo · lens: growth-gap

All ten indexed URLs are transactional. The plan's homepage order includes 'content (blog/tips)', but there is no informational page in either language, and the one piece of content that exists, the pricing-logic PDF, is excluded from hosting.

**Evidence.** sitemap.xml:3-12 (ten URLs, no articles); docs/PLAN.md:15 'content (blog/tips)'; `grep -rniE "blog|tips|consejos"` over html/js/xml returns nothing; firebase.json:11 ignores `docs/**`, which holds docs/MKG-pricing-logic.pdf (a 'how the price is calculated' explainer that pdftotext shows is already written for the public).

**Impact.** Only brand and 'cleaning plans' queries can rank; 'how much does house cleaning cost in Charlotte', 'move-out cleaning checklist' and their Spanish equivalents, the questions people ask before booking, are missed.

**Suggestion.** Publish three to five evergreen pages built from plans.js numbers (pricing guide, deep-clean checklist, move-out checklist, Spanish versions), add them to sitemap.xml and the footer, and link the pricing guide from plans.html's 'How the estimate is built'. Copy work, no code.

### [LOW] Spanish comparison table clips its last column
`styles.css:424` · i18n · lens: ux-residential-journey

At 390px the Spanish "Qué cubre cada plan" table is 372px wide inside a 358px container, so the "Completo" header is cut to "Complet" and the Rotación marks sit at the edge until the visitor scrolls sideways inside the table. A few Spanish strings also need polish.

**Evidence.** styles.css line 424 `.compare { font-size: 0.88rem; }` with 15%-width plan columns (line 260); measured `scrollWidth 372 vs clientWidth 358` in ES (EN is 358/358). Screenshot es-B2-plans-compare shows "Complet". Copy: i18n.js line 1000 uses "recámaras" while lines 881, 1017 and 1096 use "habitaciones"; line 868 `hero.single` "Una sola limpieza" is a noun where EN has the verb "Book a single clean"; lines 1080 and 1014 produce the review row "Casa: Casa en 28202".

**Impact.** Spanish-speaking visitors (the site targets them explicitly) get a slightly broken table on the plans page and small inconsistencies that make the translation feel less careful than it is.

**Suggestion.** Let the plan columns shrink (`width: auto; white-space: nowrap` only for the header) or reduce the table font on phones; change "recámaras" to "habitaciones", "Una sola limpieza" to "Reservar una limpieza", and the review label to "Vivienda".

### [LOW] Calendar day status text is 9px on phones
`styles.css:585` · ux · lens: ux-residential-journey · votes 3/3 real (reproduce: low, impact: low, refute: low) · **confirmed**

The "3 open" / "Closed" / "Full" label under each calendar day is set to 0.58rem on phones, about 9px, which is below readable size; the colour alone has to carry the state.

**Evidence.** styles.css line 585 `.cal-state { font-size: 0.58rem; padding: 2px 4px; }` inside the `max-width: 720px` block; computed font-size at 390px is 9.28px (`getComputedStyle` in Playwright). Cells are 49px wide (line 584 `min-height: 50px`). Visible in en-40-q5-when and es-40 screenshots where "3 libres" is a faint pill.

**Impact.** Mobile visitors on both the quote When step and availability.html cannot read how many windows a day has left without zooming.

**Suggestion.** Drop the count from the cell on phones and keep only the colour plus a legend, or raise the size to at least 0.7rem and show "3" rather than "3 open".

### [LOW] No minimum-age line, accessibility statement, or terms-change clause
`terms.html:83` · legal · lens: content-legal

The terms and privacy pages have no statement that the site and accounts are for adults and that MKG does not knowingly collect children's data, no accessibility statement or contact for access problems, and no sentence on how and when the terms may change. All three are standard boilerplate on US small-business sites and cost nothing.

**Evidence.** terms.html:71-83 sections are Estimates, Requests, Our work, Payment, Referral credit, Contact; privacy.html:71-83 sections are What we collect, How we use it, Where it's kept, Your choices; grep of i18n.js for "age", "18", "children", "accessib" finds no such copy in either language; account.html:84-99 sign-up asks only name, email and password.

**Impact.** Low legal exposure (COPPA applies only to sites directed at or knowingly collecting from under-13s), but a lawyer will ask for them, and an accessibility contact line is the cheapest defence against ADA demand letters.

**Suggestion.** Add to terms: "You must be 18 or older to request service or create an account; we don't knowingly collect information from children" and "We may update these terms; the date at the top shows the current version." Add a one-line accessibility note with the business email to the footer or privacy page, in both languages.

### What this area found well done

- The commercial segment is a real second configuration, not a re-skin: plans.js:253-389 gives it its own dimensions, a visits-per-week ladder with visitsPerMonth (4.33/8.67/13/21.67), plans, comparison rows, single cleans and follow-up tiers, and the estimate chain is numerically consistent from the hero dial to the plan cards, quote step 3, the review and the owner email ($198 per visit, about $1,717 per month everywhere). _(ux-commercial-journey)_
- The commercial:<key> namespace with fallback (i18n.js:1658-1667) is complete in both languages: 83 commercial keys in EN and 83 in ES, 774 keys each overall with no key missing on either side, and the Spanish is idiomatic for the audience (pies², Lun–Vie, Consultorio médico o dental, hoja de control, recorrido). _(ux-commercial-journey)_
- Per-segment state and drafts (mkg.state.commercial, mkg.quote.commercial) keep the commercial and residential journeys from contaminating each other, deep links such as commercial-quote.html?plan=complete&freq=daily preselect correctly, and the calendar filters the 6:30–9:30 pm window to commercial pages only. _(ux-commercial-journey)_
- The owner-facing email is thoughtfully built: always in English, subject carries company, service and visit, a 'Language: Spanish (reply in Spanish if you can)' line for Spanish requests, and the commercial fields (Facility, Floors, Company) are labelled rather than dumped as keys. _(ux-commercial-journey)_
- Layout holds at 390px across all three commercial pages and all six quote steps in both languages: no horizontal overflow (scrollWidth 390 throughout), choice chips wrap cleanly, the calendar and slot buttons stay tappable, and the 'Residential service' mirror card sits naturally between the hero CTAs and the dial. _(ux-commercial-journey)_
- Price-is-an-estimate messaging is consistent at every touchpoint of the residential journey: hero note (i18n.js line 59), dial fine print (line 71), step-3 intro (line 219), the review row labelled "Estimate" and the confirmation's "exact price for your home" (line 257), in both languages. _(ux-residential-journey)_
- Validation is specific and well placed: messages such as "Enter a 5-digit ZIP code." and "Add a phone number so we can text you." appear under the offending field, the field gets a red border, and errors clear as soon as the visitor types (quote.js lines 37-42 and 379). _(ux-residential-journey)_
- State carries cleanly across the journey: bedrooms, bathrooms, plan and schedule chosen on the dial or a plan card travel to the quote through the URL and localStorage (app.js lines 62-75), and the six-step draft survives a reload in sessionStorage (quote.js lines 45-73), so a visitor never re-enters what they already told the site. _(ux-residential-journey)_
- The Spanish version is a real localisation rather than a string swap: idiomatic copy ("Tu casa limpia, en automático", "No pasa nada; te proponemos una"), locale-formatted dates ("jueves, 22 de octubre", calendar.js line 23-25), and the owner's notification email flags "Language: Spanish (reply in Spanish if you can)" (quote.js line 435). _(ux-residential-journey)_
- The When step is honest and low-pressure: it says a pick "is a request, not a booking yet" (i18n.js line 250), lets the visitor continue with no date using day and time-of-day chips, the review step offers a Change button on every row, and the ?service=deep entry explains what a deep clean is before asking a single question (quote.js lines 241-249). _(ux-residential-journey)_
- The client move/cancel loop is complete and safe: firestore.rules customerMoves/customerCancels (lines 102-113) force the hold to mirror the request in the same batch and push a moved confirmed visit back to New; account.js tellBusiness (181-205) emails the owner with the old and new window; admin.js shows a 'Moved by client' pill with the previous time (111-114, 226). _(ux-account-admin)_
- Admin edits survive live redraws: drafts (admin.js:53, 260-264) keep typed values across every Firestore snapshot, with an 'unsaved changes' marker and Discard; the only irreversible request action (delete) is owner-only and needs the word DELETE/BORRAR typed (271-276). _(ux-account-admin)_
- The time clock is well thought through for a small crew: Start/End tied to a booking, a banner so nobody is stuck on an open shift (timeclock.js:97-102), overlapping shifts counted once (150-159), revenue split by hours on shared visits, long/overlap flags, and a payroll CSV that quotes cells and neutralises formula injection (284-285). _(ux-account-admin)_
- Sign-in is unusually considerate for a small-business site: Google, password, and email-link, requests linked by email with verification enforced server-side (rules :93), verify/reset/sign-in links handled on account.html with plain-language error mapping (account.js:50-60), and password reset that never reveals whether an account exists (327-336). _(ux-account-admin)_
- Mobile hygiene is solid: no horizontal overflow at 390px in any signed-in or signed-out state, the time clock is placed first for cleaners on phones (styles.css:580), tel: links on job cards, and the tab title shows the count of new requests (admin.js:71). _(ux-account-admin)_
- plans.js really is the single source of truth for pricing: the dial, plan cards, quote step 3, review rows, the Formspree email (quote.js:440-445), admin.html, account.html and docs/MKG-pricing-logic.pdf all derive from the same base/per/discount data, and every number in the PDF (bases, per-unit rates, both ladders, follow-up tiers, worked examples $217->$195, $242->$198->$1,717, $133/$113/$120/$126, $178/$160/$169) matches plans.js at HEAD line for line. _(pricing-logic)_
- The schedule ladders are internally sound: residential 0/5/10/15% and commercial 0/10/18/25/35% are monotone, visitsPerMonth (4.33, 8.67, 13, 21.67) are the correct 52/12, 104/12, 156/12, 260/12 values, and the monthly figure is computed the same way in the dial, cards, review and stored payload. _(pricing-logic)_
- Estimates are framed honestly everywhere they appear: 'estimate'/'placeholder pricing' labels on every surface, 'Custom — quoted for you' with no number for deep, move and floor-care cleans, and the email to the owner shows exactly what the visitor saw (per-visit, discount, full price, monthly) so the reply can be anchored to it. _(pricing-logic)_
- The follow-up tier design (`uses: "weekly"` etc.) keeps one discount ladder for members and recent-deep-clean clients, and the Spanish pricing copy is complete and numerically correct in practice (observed ES follow-up '$126 · Ahorra 5% · precio único $133 · Ahorras $7', ES plan options $120/$195/$258). _(pricing-logic)_
- Data hygiene around prices is good: backend.js:107-111 coerces estimate and price fields to finite numbers, firestore.rules:60-82 whitelists exactly the quote form's fields so visitors cannot set quotedPrice/payLink, admin validates the final price (adm.priceBad) and shows 'estimate was $X' beside it (admin.js:191), and timeclock.js:47 correctly prefers the confirmed price for revenue. _(pricing-logic)_
- The privacy policy is specific and mostly true to the real data flows: it names Firestore in the United States, Formspree, Google Fonts and reCAPTCHA, and a grep of every .html/.js file finds no analytics, ad pixels or tracking scripts, so "we don't sell or rent your details, and we don't use them for advertising" (i18n.js:703) is accurate. _(content-legal)_
- Both legal pages are fully bilingual through i18n keys with a faithful Spanish translation; document.documentElement.lang switches with the language (i18n.js:1724) and every placeholder ({email}, {phone}, {maxPct}) rendered correctly in EN and ES during the Playwright check, with no leftover braces. _(content-legal)_
- The change/cancel policy is enforced in code from one setting: plans.js:94 policy.changeNoticeHours drives account.js:26-36 tooLate(), which replaces the move/cancel buttons with "call or text" inside the window, and the quote flow repeatedly says a request is not a booking until confirmed (i18n.js:250, terms.2.p1). _(content-legal)_
- The referral rules on refer.html and terms.5 match the implementation: firestore.rules:85-91 refuses a customer's own code, admin.js isRepeat withholds the new-customer credit, INV.cap (referral.js:53-56) enforces maxPercentOfVisit, and the ledger a referrer sees (account.js:218-222) shows only type, date and amount, matching the privacy promise that "the person who invited you only learns that credit was earned". _(content-legal)_
- README.md:88 and docs/PLAN.md:7 forbid invented reviews, ratings and guarantees and the pages carry none; estimates are labelled as placeholders everywhere (plans.note.*), the Google OAuth consent screen links the privacy and terms pages (docs/SETUP-firebase.md:192-193), and the password sign-up shows an agreement line with working links in both languages (account.html:101). _(content-legal)_
- Bilingual end to end: every fixed string lives in i18n.js in EN and ES, owner-edited content in plans.js is written twice, ?lang=es share links are generated by referral.js:61, and the request stores `language` so the admin card shows 'speaks Spanish' (admin.js:194) and the owner email says 'reply in Spanish if you can' (quote.js:435). _(growth-gap)_
- Transparent pricing before any form: the hero dial, plan cards and comparison table all read one config (plans.js), the schedule discount is shown per visit and per month, and the follow-up tiers reuse the same ladder; this is exactly the differentiator PLAN.md section 3 asked for and competitors lack. _(growth-gap)_
- A complete referral program for a one-person shop: codes, invite links, QR cards, a banner, a credits ledger, repeat-customer detection (admin.js:125-131) and server-side enforcement that a code exists and is not the customer's own (firestore.rules:85-91). _(growth-gap)_
- Honest trust posture: no invented reviews, ratings or guarantees (README item 6), terms that promise a 24-hour re-clean and insurance in plain words (i18n.js:722), and 'price confirmed before anyone shows up' repeated in the hero, dial and FAQ. _(growth-gap)_
- Client self-service that respects policy: move and cancel from the account page with the 24-hour notice enforced both in the UI (account.js:36) and in firestore.rules (customerMoves/customerCancels), 'Book this again' prefills the quote, and the owner is emailed on every client move or cancel (account.js:183-206). _(growth-gap)_

## Ops (83 findings: 13 high, 42 medium, 28 low)

_Scope: hosting and CI, maintainability, rendered smoke tests, email notifications, documentation, and resilience._

### [HIGH] Deploy secret is an Admin SDK key with full database access
`.github/workflows/firebase-hosting-merge.yml:2` · security · lens: devops-hosting

The workflow and README tell the owner to use the key from Project settings > Service accounts > Generate new private key, which is the firebase-adminsdk service account (Firebase Admin SDK Administrator Service Agent, Editor on older projects). That key can read and change every Firestore document and Auth user, yet the pipeline only needs permission to publish Hosting. It sits in a public repo whose main branch is unprotected and runs a floating-version action.

**Evidence.** firebase-hosting-merge.yml:2-3 "Needs one repository secret: FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT (the JSON key from Firebase > Project settings > Service accounts > Generate new private key)"; README.md:72 repeats it. The key is also passed to the PR workflow (firebase-hosting-pull-request.yml:21). gh api repos/lasting-oasis/mkgservices.com shows visibility "public"; branches/main/protection returns "Branch not protected"; rulesets returns []. Any collaborator PR or a compromised action version can run `echo "$SA"` from a workflow and exfiltrate the key.

**Impact.** A single leak (collaborator, compromised GitHub account, or a malicious release of an unpinned action) exposes every customer's name, address, phone, email and booking, and allows deleting or rewriting the database and user accounts, far beyond what a hosting deploy should risk.

**Suggestion.** Replace the secret with a dedicated service account that holds only roles/firebasehosting.admin (plus roles/firebaserules.admin if CI deploys rules), which `firebase init hosting:github` creates for you, or go key-less with Workload Identity Federation via google-github-actions/auth; then delete the Admin SDK key under Google Cloud > IAM > Service accounts > Keys.

### [HIGH] README tells maintainers to run make-pages.py; it would wipe features
`README.md:64` · maintainability · lens: docs-onboarding

README:64 says `docs/make-pages.py` regenerates the availability, account, admin, privacy and terms pages, but the script's own first two lines say "Do NOT re-run this script: it would overwrite those pages. Kept for reference only." Running it also rewrites refer.html, which README does not mention.

**Evidence.** docs/make-pages.py:1-2 carries the warning; line 333 writes refer.html. I ran it on a scratch copy of the repo: the regenerated account.html loses "Continue with Google", "Create account", "Forgot your password", "Email me a sign-in link instead", "Choose a new password" and "Set or change my password" (108 differing lines against the committed file); admin.html loses "Time clock", "Team time", "The week ahead", data-admin-team, data-admin-search and timeclock.js (53 lines); every tag reverts to `?v=14` (make-pages.py:50-67) while the site is at `?v=22`.

**Impact.** A maintainer following the README reverts client sign-in and the staff features; a push to main deploys the loss within a minute through the merge workflow.

**Suggestion.** Delete the sentence from README (or move the script under docs/archive/ with the warning in its filename) and document the actual way to change shared header/footer markup: edit all 12 pages, which `grep -l 'site-header' *.html` lists.

### [HIGH] Three documents misdescribe the not-connected quote form
`README.md:84` · content · lens: docs-onboarding

README:84 says an unconnected copy "can't save and tells visitors to email instead"; docs/SETUP-firebase.md:8-9 says it "tells visitors it couldn't send and shows the business email"; backend.js:8-9 says it "shows its demo confirmation". With formEndpoint set (as it is today) none of these is true: the form POSTs the request to Formspree and shows the genuine "Request sent" screen.

**Evidence.** quote.js:494-496: `if (!live) { if (!C.formEndpoint) { setTimeout(function () { done(true); }, 400); return; } return mailCopy(data).then(function (ok) { ok ? done(false) : fail(); }); }`. Observed with Playwright on http://127.0.0.1:8787/quote.html with the formspree.io request intercepted inside the browser (nothing left the container): answered 200, the done step reads "Request sent … Estimate $195 per visit", `[data-demo-note]` stays hidden, and the intercepted payload carries Name, Phone, email, Home, ZIP and a "Manage it" link built from location.origin; with the POST aborted it shows "Couldn't send your request. Check your connection and try again or email mkgservices704@gmail.com." The demo note (i18n.js:258 q.demo) only appears when formEndpoint is "".

**Impact.** Anyone testing on a local copy or a mirror sends real emails to the owner's inbox (each one counts against the 50 a month) with no Firestore record, a blank Reference and a Manage link pointing at the copy; the visitor sees a real confirmation for a request the admin page will never show. A maintainer reading any of the three docs will not expect this.

**Suggestion.** Either make the docs state the real matrix (no Firebase + endpoint = email only; no Firebase + no endpoint = demo; Firebase refused + endpoint = email only) or change quote.js so a not-live page always takes the demo path and only the live site falls back to email.

### [HIGH] Nothing ever tells the client their visit was confirmed, declined, priced or moved
`admin.js:315` · ux · lens: email-notify-flow

Every owner action on a request (Confirm, Decline, Mark done, final price, 'note to client', date change) only writes Firestore; no email, SMS or push goes to the client. The only place the client can learn the outcome is account.html, which requires creating and verifying an account most quote senders never do.

**Evidence.** admin.js:315 `p = B.updateRequest(id, { status: what })` followed only by hold bookkeeping; the only callers of app.js notify() are quote.js:459 and account.js:205, both addressed to the business. The 'note to client' (i18n.js:540 'Note the client sees on their account page') is rendered solely by account.js:116 `if (r.replyNote) rows.push([t("acct.fromUs"), r.replyNote])`. Mock run (scratchpad shots/account-390-es.png): the note 'Confirmado. Llegamos a las 8.' appears only on the signed-in account card. The quote done page promises 'We'll reply within one business day' (i18n.js:257) and docs/SETUP-firebase.md:78 notes the Formspree autoresponse is paid, so even the receipt is missing.

**Impact.** Every booking depends on the owner remembering to email or text by hand after clicking Confirm; a client who sent a request and hears nothing assumes it was ignored and books elsewhere. The 'Text' contact preference collected on the form is never acted on automatically.

**Suggestion.** Install the Firebase 'Trigger Email from Firestore' extension (free; Blaze already on) with a small Cloud Function on requests onCreate/onUpdate that writes a `mail/{id}` doc in the client's stored `language` for: receipt, confirmed/declined, replyNote or quotedPrice set, date moved by the owner; SMTP via Gmail app password, Brevo or Resend free tiers, sender on mkgservices.com with SPF/DKIM at Cloudflare (about $0/month at this volume). For clients who chose Text, Twilio SMS costs roughly $0.008 per message plus about $1.15/month for a number and a one-time A2P 10DLC registration.

### [HIGH] Calendar silently shows every window open when Firestore cannot be reached
`backend.js:136` · bug · lens: error-resilience

availability() calls two default-source get() queries and trusts whatever resolves. Once the Firestore client decides it is offline (10 s without a backend response) those queries resolve from the empty in-memory cache instead of rejecting, so the function returns { live: true, holds: [], days: {} }: every day shows "3 open", no offline notice, and closed days and blocked windows disappear. The `metadata.fromCache` flag that would reveal this is ignored; stillAdmin() (line 288) already uses `{ source: "server" }` for exactly this reason, but the public calendar does not.

**Evidence.** Harness scenario `blocked` on availability.html with the real SDK and firestore.googleapis.com aborted: calendar box empty for 10 s, then at t=11 s `cells: 31, open: 9, full: 0, offlineNote: null` (every bookable day labelled "3 open"), while the console logged the Firestore "operating in offline mode" warning. Screenshot 03-blocked-availability.png. Code: `return Promise.all([q1, q2]).then(function (r) { … return { live: true, holds: holds, days: days }; });` with q1/q2 built from `.get()` and no fromCache check.

**Impact.** On a bad connection a visitor is shown a fully open calendar (including days the owner closed), picks a window that may be full, and then hits the hang in the previous finding; if the write later lands on reconnect it becomes an overbooked hold the owner has to decline.

**Suggestion.** Use `.get({ source: "server" })` and map rejection to live:false, or check `r[0].metadata.fromCache || r[1].metadata.fromCache` and return live:false; then have calendar.js render a "couldn't load live availability" state that hides per-window counts instead of claiming everything is open.

### [HIGH] README tells developers to run a generator that overwrites hand-built pages
`docs/make-pages.py:1` · maintainability · lens: maintainability-drift

docs/make-pages.py opens with "Do NOT re-run this script: it would overwrite those pages", yet README.md:64 still says it "regenerates the availability, account, admin, privacy, and terms pages". Its templates predate the admin time clock, team list, search box, week-ahead panel, Google and password sign-in, and the privacy policy's Formspree and reCAPTCHA paragraphs, and every tag it emits is ?v=14.

**Evidence.** Ran it in a scratch copy of the repo and diffed against the committed pages: account.html 108 changed lines (239 lines shrinks to 177: password/Google sign-in, tabs, verify, reset and change-password flows gone); admin.html 53 changed lines (time clock, team <details>, search, "The week ahead", Google sign-in, forgot-password gone); privacy.html loses privacy.3.p2 and privacy.3.p3 (the Formspree and reCAPTCHA disclosures); all 12 script/stylesheet tags on those pages revert from ?v=22 to ?v=14; it also silently rewrites refer.html, which neither its docstring nor README lists. The shebang sits on line 3 under the comment, so ./docs/make-pages.py does not run as an executable either.

**Impact.** A future developer following README would wipe five live pages in one command and the push-to-main workflow would deploy the result: the privacy page would lose legally relevant disclosures and staff would lose the time clock and team features until someone noticed.

**Suggestion.** Delete the script (or rename it make-pages.OBSOLETE.py and make it exit immediately) and remove the sentence at README.md:64; if a chrome generator is still wanted, rebuild it so it only replaces the topbar/header/footer blocks inside existing files and supports --check mode.

### [HIGH] firestore.rules declared but never deployed; manual paste invites drift
`firebase.json:2` · devops · lens: devops-hosting

firebase.json registers firestore.rules, but the merge workflow only runs the hosting action, so the rules that protect customer data are updated by copy-pasting into the console. Nothing checks that the console matches the repo, and the rules have already changed through several same-day feature commits (accounts, team, referral, time clock).

**Evidence.** firebase.json:2-4 `"firestore": { "rules": "firestore.rules" }`; firebase-hosting-merge.yml:29-34 runs only FirebaseExtended/action-hosting-deploy with channelId: live. firestore.rules:3-5 "Published in the Firebase console ... GitHub does not deploy this file"; docs/SETUP-firebase.md:26-27 and 94-96 both say to re-paste after every change. Workflow history shows 15 consecutive deploys between 2026-10-16 and 10-18, each of which shipped page code that depends on the rules version of that commit; whether the console holds the HEAD rules cannot be verified from here.

**Impact.** If a rules change (for example the email-verified gate at the heart of account privacy) is forgotten or mis-pasted, the live site runs older or hand-edited rules while the repository claims otherwise; either customer data is more exposed than intended or new features fail with permission errors.

**Suggestion.** Add a rules step to the merge workflow: write the secret to $RUNNER_TEMP/sa.json via an env var, export GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS, run `npx firebase-tools@14 deploy --only firestore:rules --non-interactive` (service account needs roles/firebaserules.admin), and add a rules unit-test job with @firebase/rules-unit-testing against the emulator on pull requests.

### [HIGH] Hosting ignore list publishes .git/ and .github/ to the live site
`firebase.json:9` · security · lens: devops-hosting

The ignore pattern "**/.*" excludes dot-files (.firebaserc) but not the contents of dot-directories, so the Action's checkout uploads the whole .git/ directory and both workflow files. The pack file inside .git/ contains every file the rest of the ignore list tries to withhold (firestore.rules, docs/, brand/source/, README, the pricing PDF).

**Evidence.** firebase.json:7-16 ignore = ["firebase.json","**/.*","**/node_modules/**","docs/**","brand/*.py","brand/source/**","README.md","firestore.rules"]. Ran firebase-tools 15.34.0's own listFiles() (the function deploy/hosting/deploy.js:41 calls; the Action runs `npx firebase-tools@latest` from the checkout dir, entryPoint ".") against a `git clone --depth 1` of the repo (what actions/checkout@v4 produces): 83 files would upload, 29 of them under .git/ (10.1 MB: config, HEAD, index, packed-refs, logs/, hooks/*.sample, objects/pack/pack-*.pack/.idx/.rev) plus .github/workflows/firebase-hosting-merge.yml and firebase-hosting-pull-request.yml. `git cat-file -s` on that pack shows firestore.rules (14,902 B), docs/SETUP-firebase.md, brand/source/logo-full-3000.png (5.9 MB) and docs/MKG-pricing-logic.pdf are all recoverable from it. Minimal test: ignore ["**/.*"] keeps .git/config and .git/objects/abc; adding "**/.*/**" removes them. Live confirmation (`curl -sI https://mkgservices.com/.git/HEAD`) could not be run from this container.

**Impact.** Anyone can fetch https://mkgservices.com/.git/objects/pack/*.pack and reconstruct the full source snapshot, including the files deliberately excluded; on an Actions checkout .git/config also carries the job's (short-lived) GITHUB_TOKEN extraheader. Security scanners flag an exposed .git directory immediately, and the repo could never safely be made private later.

**Suggestion.** Add "**/.*/**" to the ignore list (or move the site into a public/ folder and set "public": "public"), set `persist-credentials: false` on actions/checkout, and push once: Hosting releases are full snapshots, so the next deploy drops the files from the live version.

### [HIGH] Formspree endpoint is public and un-gated: quota burn and admin-login phishing
`plans.js:40` · security · lens: email-notify-flow

Anyone can POST JSON to the Formspree form ID with any field names, _subject, Reply-To and a 'Manage it' URL of their choosing. Fifty curl calls silence the owner's email for the month, and a forged message is indistinguishable from a real request except for the link it carries.

**Evidence.** plans.js:40 `formEndpoint: "https://formspree.io/f/xjyglbgo"`; app.js:350 sends `Accept: application/json`, the Formspree path that has no CAPTCHA page; nothing in the request ties it to the site (App Check at backend.js:103-107 covers Firestore only). The real email body captured locally (scratchpad quote-ok-date-es.json) is a flat set of labelled strings ending in `"Manage it": "<origin>/admin.html"` (quote.js:454), a bare URL that mail clients auto-link; an attacker can send the same shape with `Manage it` pointing at a look-alike sign-in page.

**Impact.** Denial of the owner's only alert channel for the rest of a month at zero cost to the attacker, inbox spam with arbitrary Reply-To addresses, and a credible route to the owner's Firebase password, which reads every client's name, phone, email, address notes (firestore.rules:116) and can delete requests.

**Suggestion.** Generate the owner email on the server from the validated Firestore document (same Trigger Email setup as above), which closes the forgery path, keeps the endpoint out of the page source, and makes the admin link trustworthy. In the meantime turn on Formspree's domain restriction for the form and tell the owner and team to open admin.html from a bookmark, never from the email.

### [HIGH] Requests store plan and schedule by English display name, not id
`quote.js:408` · maintainability · lens: maintainability-drift

payload() saves the plan and frequency as their English labels (`p.plan = A.plan(s.plan).name.en; p.frequency = A.frequency(s.frequency).label.en`, likewise `p.service = ...name.en` at line 400 and the follow-up tier label at 403). Every later lookup compares those labels back against plans.js, so the owner-editable copy is the record's identity.

**Evidence.** account.js:80-82 and :88-90 and admin.js:77-78 find the plan with `seg.plans.filter(function (x) { return x.name.en === r.plan; })`; admin.js:94 and :98 (servicePatch) write name.en again. README.md:34 and plans.js:5 explicitly invite the owner to edit "plan names". firestore.rules:76 validates plan/frequency only as strings. Renaming "Family" to "Household" in plans.js therefore makes every existing request miss: account.js serviceText() shows the raw English with no Spanish, againLink() drops ?plan=, admin.js serviceValue() returns "" so the edit dropdown shows a placeholder, and the next admin save writes the new name, leaving old and new records with different spellings of the same plan.

**Impact.** A one-word copy edit the documentation says is safe silently degrades the client account page, the admin edit panel and the "book this again" link for every request already in the database, in both languages.

**Suggestion.** Save `planId`, `frequencyId`, `serviceId` and `tierId` alongside the labels (add them to quoteFields in firestore.rules), look up by id everywhere, keep the English label only as a denormalised copy for emails, and comment in plans.js that `id` values are permanent.

### [HIGH] Quote submit hangs on "Sending…" forever when Firestore is unreachable
`quote.js:493` · bug · lens: error-resilience

When the Firebase SDK has loaded but Cloud Firestore cannot be reached (dropped mobile data, captive portal, corporate firewall or DNS filter on firestore.googleapis.com), B.submitRequest() never resolves or rejects: Firestore queues the batch in memory and keeps retrying silently. The button was disabled and relabelled at line 475, there is no timeout around the save (only the courtesy email has an 8-second cap at line 459), so the visitor sees a dead "Sending…" with no error and no hint to call or email.

**Evidence.** Reproduced with the real firebase@10.14.1 compat bundles served at /__/firebase/ and firestore.googleapis.com aborted (scratchpad harness.js, scenario `blocked`): after the visitor picked 2026-10-22 Morning and pressed Send, the submit button read "Sending…" / disabled=true at 6, 11, 16 … 46 s, `[data-error=submit]` stayed empty, the done step never showed; console: "@firebase/firestore: Could not reach Cloud Firestore backend. Backend didn't respond within 10 seconds … The client will operate in offline mode". Code: `return batch.commit().then(function () { return ref.id; });` (backend.js:166) with no race/timeout, inside `B.ready.then(function (live) {` at quote.js:493. Screenshot 03-blocked-quote-sending.png.

**Impact.** A visitor on a flaky connection waits, gives up and closes the tab; the request is lost (memory-only write queue) and they were never shown the "email us" alternative that the network-error path already has. Same class on account.html move/cancel (buttons disabled at account.js:175 until a commit that never comes).

**Suggestion.** Race submitRequest against a ~15 s timer; on timeout show err.submit with the call/text/email line and re-enable the button, and discard the queued write (compat `db.terminate()` drops memory-queued writes, or `db.disableNetwork()`) so a later reconnect cannot land a duplicate of a request the visitor then re-sent by email or phone.

### [HIGH] Owner email is a browser-side courtesy POST; failures and quota are silent
`quote.js:506` · bug · lens: email-notify-flow

On the live path the result of the Formspree POST is discarded, so a 500, a network error, a spam-flag or the free tier's 50-a-month limit leaves the owner un-notified with no flag anywhere: the request sits in admin.html, which the owner only sees by opening the page.

**Evidence.** quote.js:506 `return mailCopy(data, id).then(function () { done(false); })` ignores the boolean; app.js:352 `.then(function (r) { return r.ok; }).catch(function () { return false; })` swallows every error without even a console.warn, and nothing writes an `emailed` field to the request. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:75-77: over-limit submissions are 'held in an Over limit folder' (accepted, not emailed), so `r.ok` cannot tell 'emailed' from 'held'; moves and cancels (account.js:205) count against the same 50. Local run with Formspree fulfilled as HTTP 500 (scratchpad quote-fail-500.json) confirms notify() resolves false; on the live path that value is never looked at.

**Impact.** After the 50th message in a busy month, or whenever Formspree holds or flags a submission, every new request reaches only admin.html. The owner, whose documented inbox is the email, has no signal that the channel went quiet; requests age past the promised one-business-day reply and bookings are lost.

**Suggestion.** Send the owner email server-side from the saved document (Cloud Function onCreate on `requests` plus Trigger Email), which cannot be skipped by a browser and can retry. Until then: record `emailed: true/false` on the request after mailCopy, show a banner on admin.html for any New request with `emailed !== true`, and add a Gmail filter so Formspree mail is starred and never marked spam.

### [HIGH] "Request sent" shown although the database refused it; email carries no warning
`quote.js:510` · ux · lens: error-resilience

When submitRequest() rejects (permission-denied from a rules/payload mismatch, App Check enforcement, unavailable), the code falls back to the Formspree email and then shows the normal success screen: no demo note, no reference, and the "Create a free account with the same email" prompt even though the request will never appear in any account, on admin.html, or on the calendar as a hold. The email itself has no marker that the request was NOT saved, so the owner cannot tell it must be re-entered by hand. firestore.rules is pasted by hand (firestore.rules:3-5; the GitHub workflow deploys hosting only), so a deploy that adds one payload field before the rules are updated turns this into the path for every request. If Formspree is also over its 50/month limit it still answers 200 but sends nothing (docs/SETUP-firebase.md:76), and the request vanishes behind a green tick.

**Evidence.** Harness scenario `denied`: real SDK with `firebase.firestore.WriteBatch.prototype.commit` patched to reject with code "permission-denied" and formspree.io answering 200 → result `{done:true, demoNote:false, h1:"Request sent", track:"Want to follow it, change the day, or cancel online? Create a free account with the same email."}`; the captured Formspree POST had keys _subject, email, Name, Phone, …, Manage it and `Reference: (none)` and no "not saved" field (compare the Firestore-ok variant, which carried `Reference: L1RUFN`). Screenshot 04-denied-formspree-ok.png. Code: `if (C.formEndpoint) return mailCopy(data).then(function (ok) { ok ? done(false) : fail(); });` after `console.warn("request not saved", err)`.

**Impact.** Owner: requests arrive only as email, never on admin.html, never hold the window, cannot be assigned, clocked or credited; a referral welcome credit is silently dropped. Visitor: told to create an account to follow a request that is not there. With a systematic rules mismatch every booking for days goes this way with only a console warning.

**Suggestion.** In the refusal path add a prominent field to the email ("NOT saved to the database: <err.code>. Add it on admin.html by hand; no hold was placed") and show a softer confirmation without the account prompt ("We got your request by email and will confirm by reply"); add `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules` to firebase-hosting-merge.yml so rules and JS cannot drift.

### [MEDIUM] Email action links expose auto-generated project id gen-lang-client-…
`.firebaserc:3` · ux · lens: devops-hosting

The Firebase project is the auto-named Google AI Studio project gen-lang-client-0946383119, and the setup doc confirms that verification, reset and sign-in-link emails open on that firebaseapp.com host before bouncing to account.html. Clients therefore receive emails whose link points at https://gen-lang-client-0946383119.firebaseapp.com/..., which looks like phishing.

**Evidence.** .firebaserc:3 `"default": "gen-lang-client-0946383119"`; docs/SETUP-firebase.md:152-156 "the link opens Firebase's page on firebaseapp.com, which sends the client on to account.html. (Authentication > Templates > Customize action URL would skip that hop, but the console doesn't keep the setting in this project; nothing depends on it.)"; backend.js:190 `function actionUrl() { return location.origin + "/account.html"; }` only sets the continue URL, not the link host. The actual email content can only be confirmed by triggering a reset on the live site.

**Impact.** Customers asked to confirm an email or reset a password see an unfamiliar, machine-named domain in the link; some will not click, and the brand looks less trustworthy than the care taken with authDomain elsewhere.

**Suggestion.** Set Authentication > Templates > Customize action URL to https://mkgservices.com/__/auth/action (Hosting already serves /__/auth/* on the custom domain, which backend.js:68 relies on); if the console will not persist it, set notification.sendEmail.callbackUri through the Identity Toolkit admin v2 config API, and record the project id's origin in README so nobody later mistakes it for a stray AI Studio project.

### [MEDIUM] Every push to main deploys with no check and no rollback note
`.github/workflows/firebase-hosting-merge.yml:8` · devops · lens: docs-onboarding

Any push to main goes live within a minute with no syntax check, and neither README nor the setup guide says how to undo a bad deploy.

**Evidence.** firebase-hosting-merge.yml:8-10 `on: push: branches: [main]`; the only steps are the secret check (16-26), checkout (27-28) and deploy (29-34). README.md:68 explains the deploy but no document mentions Hosting > Release history > Rollback (restores the previous version in seconds) or `git revert` + push, and the pull-request preview workflow is described only as "a temporary preview URL" (README:68) rather than as the place to check changes first.

**Impact.** plans.js runs before everything else on every page, so one missing comma takes the whole site down (every page's JS stops) and the owner has no documented way back.

**Suggestion.** Add "Undoing a bad deploy" to README (console Rollback, `git revert`), recommend opening a PR for the preview, and add a `node --check *.js` step before the deploy step.

### [MEDIUM] Unprotected main deploys straight to live; Dependabot off
`.github/workflows/firebase-hosting-merge.yml:9` · devops · lens: devops-hosting

Every push to main goes live within about 35 seconds, but main has no branch protection or ruleset, Dependabot security updates are disabled and there is no .github/dependabot.yml, so action versions are never bumped and nothing stops a force-push or an accidental direct commit from publishing.

**Evidence.** Lines 9-10 `on: push: branches: [main]` with channelId: live (line 34). gh api repos/lasting-oasis/mkgservices.com/branches/main/protection -> "Branch not protected"; /rulesets -> []; security_and_analysis.dependabot_security_updates.status = "disabled"; /contents/.github/dependabot.yml -> 404; the repo is public. All 15 recorded runs are direct pushes to main (gh run list, event "push").

**Impact.** One mistaken push (or a compromised collaborator account) replaces the live site immediately with no review and no automatic way to catch stale or vulnerable action versions.

**Suggestion.** Add a ruleset on main requiring a pull request and the preview/check workflows to pass (free on public repos) and blocking force-pushes; add .github/dependabot.yml with `package-ecosystem: github-actions, schedule: weekly`; enable Dependabot security updates in Settings > Code security.

### [MEDIUM] Secret check turns a missing deploy key into a green run
`.github/workflows/firebase-hosting-merge.yml:21` · devops · lens: devops-hosting

If FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT is ever removed, rotated under a new name, or the repo is transferred, the workflow prints a note, skips checkout and deploy, and finishes with a success tick. The owner would keep pushing fixes that never reach the live site while every run shows green.

**Evidence.** Lines 21-26: `if [ -z "$SERVICE_ACCOUNT" ]; then echo "FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT is not set yet..."; echo "ready=false" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"`; lines 28 and 30 gate checkout and deploy on `steps.check.outputs.ready == 'true'`. Line 5 states the intent: "the run finishes early with a note instead of failing". The secret has existed since the first run (15 successful live deploys), so the early-exit branch now only serves to hide a future misconfiguration.

**Impact.** Silent outage of the deployment pipeline: a security fix to rules or pages appears deployed but is not, with no notification.

**Suggestion.** Make the check fail fast: `if [ -z "$SERVICE_ACCOUNT" ]; then echo "::error::FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT secret is missing; nothing was deployed"; exit 1; fi`, and drop the `if:` guards on the checkout and deploy steps.

### [MEDIUM] Floating action tag and firebase-tools@latest; no concurrency or timeout
`.github/workflows/firebase-hosting-merge.yml:29` · devops · lens: devops-hosting

The deploy depends on two unpinned moving targets: FirebaseExtended/action-hosting-deploy@v0 (a floating tag) and, inside it, `npx firebase-tools@latest` because firebaseToolsVersion is not set. The job also has no permissions block, no concurrency group (two quick pushes can finish out of order) and no timeout-minutes.

**Evidence.** Line 29 `uses: FirebaseExtended/action-hosting-deploy@v0`; the same at firebase-hosting-pull-request.yml:18. In the action's repo, refs/tags/v0 currently resolves to 500ac625 (v0.10.0, runs on node24) and src/deploy.ts:85-90 runs `npx firebase-tools@${firebaseToolsVersion || "latest"}`; action.yml:48-52 default `latest`. firebase-hosting-merge.yml has no `permissions:`, `concurrency:` or `timeout-minutes:` keys (the PR workflow at least sets permissions, lines 7-10).

**Impact.** A bad or malicious release of either dependency changes what gets published to the live site with no review; an out-of-order pair of deploys can put an older commit live after a newer one.

**Suggestion.** Pin by commit: `uses: FirebaseExtended/action-hosting-deploy@500ac625ca2dd40cbd15f7659af953801858032a # v0.10.0` with `firebaseToolsVersion: "14.x"` (and actions/checkout by SHA), and add `permissions: { contents: read }`, `concurrency: { group: live-deploy, cancel-in-progress: false }` and `timeout-minutes: 10`; let Dependabot bump the SHAs.

### [MEDIUM] No lint, HTML validation, link check or smoke test in CI
`.github/workflows/firebase-hosting-pull-request.yml:5` · maintainability · lens: devops-hosting

The only two workflows deploy; nothing validates the 12 hand-edited HTML pages, lints 112 KB of i18n strings and the shared JS, checks internal links, or loads each page to catch a console error before it is live. The PR preview workflow that would show problems has never run.

**Evidence.** Repo contains exactly two workflows (gh api actions/workflows: "Deploy to Firebase Hosting" and "Preview on pull request"). gh api pulls?state=all returns no pull requests, so firebase-hosting-pull-request.yml (on: pull_request, line 5) has executed zero times; all 15 runs are the merge workflow. Five pages are hand-maintained copies of a generated template (docs/make-pages.py:1-2 "Do NOT re-run this script"), which is exactly the kind of drift a validator catches. The local Playwright pass loaded index/quote/account/admin with no page errors, so a smoke test would pass today.

**Impact.** A typo in i18n.js or a broken href in one of the five near-duplicate pages reaches customers before anyone sees it; the developer has no safety net when editing files the README says they should not need to touch.

**Suggestion.** Add a checks.yml on push and pull_request that runs `npx html-validate '*.html'`, `npx eslint *.js` (config in-repo), a link check (`npx linkinator . --recurse --skip 'https?://'`), and a Playwright smoke step that serves the folder with `python3 -m http.server` and asserts zero console errors per page; make it a required check in the ruleset.

### [MEDIUM] No lint, test or CI check; test seams exist but nothing calls them
`.github/workflows/firebase-hosting-pull-request.yml:18` · devops · lens: maintainability-drift

The repository has no package.json, lint configuration, test file or CHANGELOG, and both workflows go straight from checkout to deploy. timeclock.js already exposes `_computeStats` and `_flags` for tests that were never written.

**Evidence.** `find` over the tree returns no package.json, test/spec file or CHANGELOG; timeclock.js:313 `C.timeclock = { …, _computeStats: computeStats, _flags: flagsFor }`; firebase-hosting-merge.yml:27-29 and firebase-hosting-pull-request.yml:17-18 are checkout then FirebaseExtended/action-hosting-deploy with no step between. A scratch eslint run (no-undef, no-unused-vars, eqeqeq, no-redeclare, browser globals) over all eleven site scripts reported 0 problems, and a Playwright pass over all 12 pages in both languages, walking the quote form, logged no console error except the expected /__/firebase 404 and rendered no raw i18n key; the clone is shallow (one commit) so change tracking could not be assessed.

**Impact.** A regression that is trivial to detect mechanically (a missing i18n key printing as its key, a header edit missed on one page, a renamed plan breaking lookups) reaches mkgservices.com on the next push to main with no gate.

**Suggestion.** Add a package.json with eslint plus a short Playwright smoke script (every page, both languages, fail on console errors, "i18n: missing key" warnings, raw keys, and a header/footer diff across pages) and run it in the pull-request workflow before deploy; keep a CHANGELOG or at least descriptive commit messages.

### [MEDIUM] No price-change runbook; PDF and ?v= convention undocumented
`README.md:57` · pricing · lens: docs-onboarding

README says plans.js is the single source of truth, but a real price change (its one open launch item) touches three other things that no document names: the `?v=` cache-busting tags on 12 pages, the pricing PDF and its generator, and the estimates already stored on saved requests.

**Evidence.** All 12 pages carry `?v=22` on every script and style tag (counted per file); the convention is written nowhere, and the only script that mentions it, docs/make-pages.py:50-67, still says `?v=14`; firebase.json:28-33 already sends `Cache-Control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate` for js/css, so the bump is largely redundant and nobody is told that either. README.md never mentions docs/MKG-pricing-logic.pdf or docs/make-pricing-pdf.py (grep: no match); the script needs Python Playwright and node (make-pricing-pdf.py:10, :17). Today the PDF matches plans.js line for line (generated October 16, 2026; PDF CreationDate 20261016), but its banner (make-pricing-pdf.py:33) still says "Replace them with real rates before launch" on a live site. backend.js:107 shows saved requests keep their own estimatePerVisit, so old requests will show old prices after a change.

**Impact.** The first price change will leave a stale PDF the owner may be handing to clients, and the owner does not know the PDF exists; a missed `?v=` bump is harmless but a maintainer will not know that.

**Suggestion.** Add "Changing prices" to README: edit plans.js, run `node --check plans.js`, run `python3 docs/make-pricing-pdf.py`, optionally bump `?v=` with one sed line, push; and drop "before launch" from the PDF note.

### [MEDIUM] README describes an older site and sends the owner to resolved tasks
`README.md:64` · docs · lens: maintainability-drift

Several README statements no longer match the code or the brand folder, and the "Before launch" list still contains items that are done, so the one document the owner is told to trust misdirects them.

**Evidence.** README.md:18 says account.html is "sign-in by emailed link" (it has Google, password, email verification, reset, move/cancel and a referral panel); :19 says admin.html is "Owner's page (password)" with no mention of the team list, time clock, credits tab, search or Google sign-in; :64 lists the behaviour files but omits timeclock.js and referral.js and recommends make-pages.py; :49 lists `logo-mark-192.png` (not in brand/) and says the mark is used in the header, while index.html:73 uses brand/logo-full-112; :50 "(+ .webp)" implies logo-full-1024.webp (absent); :51 says favicon-*.png are "linked from every page" but favicon-48/-180/-512 are referenced nowhere; :66 "Firebase Hosting, free plan" and docs/SETUP-firebase.md:4 "runs on Firebase's free plan" contradict docs/SETUP-firebase.md:213 "The project is on the Blaze (pay-as-you-go) plan"; :71 tells the reader to replace a placeholder that .firebaserc no longer contains; Before-launch items :84 (Firebase setup), :85 (serviceArea, set at plans.js:30) and :88 ("Add a logo … the header uses a simple mark") are done.

**Impact.** The owner or a new developer either wastes time re-doing finished steps, or acts on wrong facts (runs make-pages.py, assumes no billing exposure, looks for files that do not exist).

**Suggestion.** Rewrite the account/admin rows and the behaviour-file list, fix the logo table against `ls brand/`, say Blaze in both documents, drop the make-pages sentence, and turn "Before launch" into a status list with the three finished items ticked and only the pricing placeholder left open.

### [MEDIUM] Docs say free plan; project is on Blaze with a $1 budget alert
`README.md:66` · devops · lens: docs-onboarding

The README heading and the top of the setup guide say the site runs on Firebase's free plan; the same guide later records the project as Blaze (pay-as-you-go), where overuse bills the owner's card. No document says what to do when the budget alert fires.

**Evidence.** README.md:66 `## Deploying (Firebase Hosting, free plan)`; docs/SETUP-firebase.md:4 "All of it runs on Firebase's free plan"; docs/SETUP-firebase.md:213-216 "The project is on the Blaze (pay-as-you-go) plan. A budget alert emails … at 10%, 50% and 100% of $1 a month … It warns; it does not stop charges." Nothing in README.md or docs/ mentions what to check or switch off after that email (grep for "bill" finds only this paragraph).

**Impact.** Whoever reads from the top believes a runaway (bot reads while App Check is monitor-only, a listener loop in admin.js, a leaked endpoint) cannot cost money, and the owner has no steps to follow when the alert arrives.

**Suggestion.** Replace "free plan" with "Blaze plan; normal use stays inside the free tier" in both places and add a short "If the budget alert fires" runbook: Firebase console > Usage and billing, App Check > Enforce, and as a last resort disable the Firestore API or downgrade to Spark.

### [MEDIUM] No .gitignore: keys and CLI logs can be committed and deployed
`README.md:72` · security · lens: docs-onboarding

The README's only protection for the service-account key is the sentence "Never commit that file"; the repository has no .gitignore, and the Firebase CLI writes files into the root that would be both committed and served from the public site.

**Evidence.** `ls -la /home/user/mkgservices.com` shows no .gitignore. A local `firebase login`, `firebase deploy` or `firebase emulators:start` writes `.firebase/`, `firebase-debug.log`, `firestore-debug.log` and `ui-debug.log` into the root; firebase.json:7-16 ignores `**/.*` but not `*-debug.log`, and `"public": "."` (firebase.json:6) deploys everything else, so those logs would be served at mkgservices.com/firebase-debug.log. A downloaded key is named like `gen-lang-client-…-firebase-adminsdk-….json`, which nothing excludes; docs/make-pricing-pdf.py:199-207 writes docs/MKG-pricing-logic.html and removes it only on success.

**Impact.** A service-account key or a debug log is one `git add .` away from a public GitHub repository and the live site.

**Suggestion.** Add a .gitignore with `*-firebase-adminsdk-*.json`, `.firebase/`, `*-debug.log`, `docs/*.html`, `__pycache__/`, `.DS_Store`, and add `*-debug.log` to the hosting ignore list in firebase.json.

### [MEDIUM] Account page shows "No requests yet" when the list fails to load
`account.js:239` · ux · lens: error-resilience

loadRequests() maps any error from myRequests() (unavailable, permission-denied, offline cache miss) to an empty list, so renderList() shows the acct.empty state "No requests yet." with a link to make a new quote; the failure is logged to the console only. acct.failed exists for writes but no read-failure string is used.

**Evidence.** Code: `B.myRequests(user.email).then(function (list) { items = list; renderList(); }).catch(function (e) { items = []; renderList(); if (window.console) console.warn(e); });` and renderList() `$("[data-acct-empty]").hidden = !verified || items.length > 0;` (account.html:195 "No requests yet. {quoteLink}"). Reasoned from code; the live database was not reachable from this container.

**Impact.** A signed-in client on a flaky connection is told they have no bookings and may re-submit a duplicate request or call in a panic.

**Suggestion.** On rejection show acct.err.generic ("Something went wrong. Check your connection…") with a Retry button and keep the empty state for a genuinely empty result.

### [MEDIUM] Header and footer are hand-copied into 12 pages; drift has begun
`admin.html:196` · maintainability · lens: maintainability-drift

The topbar, header and footer are pasted into every page by hand with nothing generating or checking them now that make-pages.py is retired, and admin.html's footer has already fallen behind the other eleven pages.

Page bodies are duplicated too: quote.html vs commercial-quote.html differ in only 89 of ~320 lines, index.html vs commercial.html 83 of ~300, plans.html vs commercial-plans.html 45 of 190.
