### [MEDIUM] Status vocabulary repeated as literals in six files, rules, CSS and i18n
`admin.js:27` · maintainability · lens: maintainability-drift

The five request statuses (new, confirmed, declined, done, cancelled) have no single definition; which ones count as "open" or "closed" is re-declared or inlined in each file, and the same strings also appear in firestore.rules, styles.css, admin.html and i18n.js.

**Evidence.** admin.js:27 `var CLOSED = { done: true, declined: true, cancelled: true }`; backend.js:24 and account.js:23 each define `var OPEN = { new: true, confirmed: true }`; timeclock.js:54 and :69 inline `r.status !== "declined" && r.status !== "cancelled"`; admin.js:229-232 hardcode the data-act values; admin.html:146-149 hardcode data-filter/data-count; firestore.rules:119, :139 and :143 repeat `in ['new', 'confirmed']`; styles.css:531-535 `.status-new` … `.status-cancelled`; i18n.js carries `status.*` and `acct.note.*` for each status in both languages. That is roughly eleven places across eight files.

**Impact.** Adding a status such as "paused" or "no-show" needs eleven coordinated edits; missing one yields a request that is neither open nor closed (invisible to the admin filters), an unstyled pill, or the raw text "status.paused" on the client's account page, because t() returns the key when it is missing.

**Suggestion.** Define the list and the OPEN/CLOSED sets once (for example `MKG.status` in plans.js or at the top of backend.js), derive the CSS class and i18n key names from it, and cross-reference that constant in a comment in firestore.rules.

### [MEDIUM] No one-tap text from the admin card or email although 'Text' is the default preference
`admin.js:192` · ux · lens: email-notify-flow

The quote form pre-selects 'Text' as the best way to reach the client, but the admin card offers only mailto: and tel: links and the Formspree email carries the phone number as plain text, so on a phone the owner must copy the number to send a text.

**Evidence.** admin.js:192 builds `<a href="mailto:…">` and `<a href="tel:…">` only; grep for `sms:` in admin.js/account.js finds nothing (the only sms: links point at the business's own number, admin.html:31,184). Rendered mock card (scratchpad admin-390.json): `<a href="mailto:Ana.Perez@example.com">…</a> · <a href="tel:9805550123">(980) 555-0123</a> · Prefers text. · wrote in Spanish`. quote.html:198 `<input type="radio" name="contactMethod" value="Text" checked>`; quote.js:430 `put("Phone", d.phone)` is a bare string.

**Impact.** The most-chosen reply channel is the only one without a tap target; every reply costs extra steps on the owner's phone, and the Spanish flag on the card is not carried into any prefilled message.

**Suggestion.** Add an `sms:` link (and optionally a wa.me link) next to the phone, with `?body=` prefilled in the client's stored language ('Hola Ana, soy MKG Services…'), and give the mailto: a prefilled subject/body with plan, day and price; about 15 lines in admin.js. Keep the email's phone as digits only so mail clients detect it reliably.

### [MEDIUM] Admin sign-in form is live before Firebase loads; throws and sticks
`admin.js:566` · bug · lens: error-resilience

admin.html shows the sign-in form immediately (admin.html:63 has no hidden attribute) while backend.js is still fetching ~160 KB of SDK. backend.js's signInPassword/signInGoogle use `auth`/`fb` synchronously without waiting on `ready` (backend.js:165-168), so a click during that window throws a TypeError: the Google button was disabled at line 567 and never re-enabled, and the password form gives no message at all. account.js avoids this by keeping the "One moment…" view until B.ready resolves.

**Evidence.** Harness scenario `slow` (SDK responses delayed 8 s): clicking "Continue with Google" at 0.5 s → `pageerror: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'auth')`, button disabled=true; 9 s later with the SDK loaded (`offline:false`) the button was still disabled=true. Submitting email+password → `pageerror: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'signInWithEmailAndPassword')`, `[data-error=login]` empty. Code: `var b = this; b.disabled = true; … B.signInGoogle().catch(…).then(function () { b.disabled = false; });` — the throw happens before a promise exists, so neither handler runs.

**Impact.** Staff on a slow mobile connection (the people most likely to open admin.html from a driveway) get a dead Google button and a silent password form and reload in confusion.

**Suggestion.** Hide or disable the admin form until B.ready resolves (mirror account.html's loading view), or make signInPassword/signInGoogle return `ready.then(...)` so an early click simply waits.

### [MEDIUM] Preview channels and any non-live host write to production Firestore
`backend.js:37` · devops · lens: devops-hosting

backend.js loads the project's own config from /__/firebase/init.json on every Hosting domain, so a PR preview channel (and the App Check-free path it takes) talks to the same production database: test quote requests create real holds on the public calendar and real Formspree emails, and once App Check is enforced previews stop working entirely.

**Evidence.** backend.js:37 `var SITE = /^(www\.)?mkgservices\.com$/.test(location.hostname)` only toggles authDomain and App Check (lines 54, 68); line 64 `fetch(base + "init.json")` resolves to the production project on *.web.app preview URLs. firebase-hosting-pull-request.yml:18-21 deploys previews with the same service account and no projectId/target. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:203-205: after enforcement "preview channels and the emulator stop working with it". Observed locally: pages fall back to demo mode only because /__/firebase/*.js 404s on 127.0.0.1.

**Impact.** Anyone testing a preview books real windows and emails the owner; after App Check enforcement the preview workflow silently produces broken previews.

**Suggestion.** On non-live hosts either force demo mode (treat `!SITE` like `useEmu` unless ?live=1 is present) or point previews at a second Firebase project/emulator via `projectId:` in the PR workflow; document the choice in SETUP.

### [MEDIUM] Running locally and the emulator are undocumented
`backend.js:53` · maintainability · lens: docs-onboarding

backend.js switches to Firebase emulator mode on localhost/127.0.0.1 or with `?emulator=1`, but no document explains that flag, how to start the emulators, or even how to serve the site locally; firebase.json has no emulators block.

**Evidence.** backend.js:53 `var useEmu = /[?&]emulator=1/.test(location.search) || /^(localhost|127\.0\.0\.1)$/.test(location.hostname);` and :63 `script(base + "init.js?useEmulator=true")`. `grep -rn -i emulator README.md docs/` finds only docs/SETUP-firebase.md:205, which mentions the emulator in passing. README.md:3 says only that pages work "from a plain file server" in not-connected mode; on http://127.0.0.1:8787 that happens because `/__/firebase/10.14.1/firebase-app-compat.js` 404s (a side effect, not a documented path). firebase.json has no `emulators` section, so a maintainer must guess `firebase emulators:start --only hosting,firestore,auth`.

**Impact.** A new maintainer cannot test rules, sign-in, holds or the admin page without using production data; the first attempt at `python3 -m http.server` on localhost silently selects emulator mode.

**Suggestion.** Add "Running locally" to README: `python3 -m http.server 8787` for layout and copy; `firebase emulators:start --only hosting,firestore,auth` (add an `emulators` block with ports to firebase.json) for the connected parts; explain the `?emulator=1` flag and that on the live site it must not be used.

### [MEDIUM] Admin/account writes appear saved offline but are lost when the tab closes
`backend.js:81` · bug · lens: error-resilience

Firestore is created without enablePersistence(), so queued writes live only in memory and cached reads vanish on reload. On admin.html every update goes through onSnapshot listeners with latency compensation: a status change shows immediately from the local write while the real commit is still pending (and, offline, pending indefinitely). Nothing reads snapshot.metadata.hasPendingWrites or fromCache, so there is no "offline / unsynced" indicator; an owner who confirms a job with no signal and closes the tab loses the confirmation silently, and an admin page opened offline shows an empty "No requests" list from the empty cache.

**Evidence.** Code: `db = fb.firestore();` with no persistence call; watchRequests `onSnapshot(function (r) { cb(rows(r, cleanRequest)); }, …)` (backend.js:356) and admin.js:315 `p = B.updateRequest(id, { status: what }).then(…)` with `b.disabled = true` until a commit that, offline, never arrives (the same mechanism produced the 46 s "Sending…" hang observed in scenario `blocked`). The empty-cache behaviour is the one observed for availability() in that scenario.

**Impact.** Owner and staff on phones in the field (the admin page's main use) can lose status changes, notes, phone bookings and clock-ins without any warning.

**Suggestion.** Call `db.enablePersistence({ synchronizeTabs: true })` (catch and ignore failed-precondition/unimplemented) on admin and account pages so queued writes survive reloads and cached requests show offline, and surface `metadata.fromCache`/`hasPendingWrites` as a small "offline, changes will sync" banner.

### [MEDIUM] Auth emails come from noreply@gen-lang-client-0946383119.firebaseapp.com
`backend.js:190` · ux · lens: email-notify-flow

Verification, password-reset and sign-in-link emails are sent by Firebase's default sender on the project's auto-generated domain, with links that hop through that domain before reaching account.html. They look like phishing, tend to land in spam, and a password account cannot see its requests until the link is clicked.

**Evidence.** .firebaserc:3 project `gen-lang-client-0946383119`; backend.js:190 `actionUrl()` is only the continueUrl passed at 200, 208, 217 and 249, so the link itself is `https://gen-lang-client-0946383119.firebaseapp.com/__/auth/action?…`; docs/SETUP-firebase.md:155 records that 'Customize action URL' did not stick, and the account.js handlers for mode=verifyEmail/resetPassword (account.js:404-414) only run when the action URL points at account.html. The site's own copy already warns 'check your spam folder too' (i18n.js:648, 651). Verification gating: firestore.rules:35 and 93. Console settings cannot be checked from here.

**Impact.** New email+password clients may never verify, so account.html stays empty for them; reset links are distrusted; the brand shown in the From line is a Google project id.

**Suggestion.** In Authentication > Templates set the sender name, use 'Customize domain' so mail comes from noreply@mkgservices.com (SPF/DKIM TXT records at Cloudflare, free), set the public-facing name in Project settings, and retry the action URL as https://mkgservices.com/account.html (Hosting already serves /__/auth/action on the custom domain; the Identity Platform API can set it if the console will not).

### [MEDIUM] Calendar box is blank until ~160 KB of SDK loads, one script at a time
`calendar.js:167` · perf · lens: error-resilience

mount() calls load() and only renders inside apply(), which runs after B.availability() resolves; that waits on the full SDK chain. backend.js:55-57 appends firebase-app, then auth, then firestore, then app-check, then fetches init.json, each only after the previous onload — five serial round trips before anything draws. Until then step 5 of the quote and availability.html show an empty hole with no spinner, grid, or "checking availability" text.

**Evidence.** Harness scenario `slow`: on quote step 5 with the SDK delayed, `[data-calendar]` innerHTML length 0, 0 cells, no legend (screenshot 02-slow-quote-step5.png shows the gap between the intro and "No date picked yet"); with Firestore unreachable it stayed empty for the full 10 s backend timeout. Sizes measured from firebase@10.14.1 compat bundles: app 10 KB, auth 39 KB, firestore 102 KB, app-check 8 KB gzip (538 KB raw) on top of 67 KB gzip of site JS; on Chrome's Slow 3G profile (400 ms RTT, 400 kbps) that is roughly 5-7 s of blank calendar. Code: `return script(base + SDK + "/firebase-app-compat.js").then(function () { return script(base + SDK + "/firebase-auth-compat.js"); }).then(…)`.

**Impact.** Mobile visitors on 3G/LTE-edge see a broken-looking step and may skip the date or leave; the availability page looks empty.

**Suggestion.** Render the month grid at mount with a "checking live availability…" note (dayInfo already works with empty holds) and swap in counts when data arrives; append all four `<script async=false>` tags at once (execution order is preserved) or add `<link rel="preload" as="script">` for them in the HTML.

### [MEDIUM] Privacy promises deletion; no runbook, owner delete leaves most data
`docs/SETUP-firebase.md:111` · legal · lens: docs-onboarding

The privacy page promises to show or delete what is held about a person on request. The only documented tool is the owner's "delete a request", which removes three documents; everything else that holds the person's data, inside and outside Firestore, is undocumented.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:710 privacy.4.p1 "Email {email} to see what we hold about you or to have it deleted." backend.js:420-428 deleteRequest deletes only holds with that requestId, internal/{id} and requests/{id}. Not covered by any document: the Auth user (console only), users/{uid} and referrers/{code} (deletable by admins per firestore.rules:239 and :252, but no UI), credits keyed by emailLower, shifts that reference the requestId, the Formspree submission archive (every request is POSTed there with name, phone, email, home details: quote.js:496 and the intercepted payload keys), and the Gmail copies. SETUP:111 mentions only "(owner) delete a request". privacy.3.p1 (i18n.js:706) says data is "stored in Google Firebase" without naming Formspree as a second holder.

**Impact.** A deletion or access request cannot be fulfilled completely or repeatably, and the privacy page understates where the data goes.

**Suggestion.** Add a "Data request" runbook to the setup guide: search by email on admin.html and delete each request; in the console delete the Auth user and users/referrers/credits documents; delete the submissions in the Formspree inbox; delete the emails. Name Formspree in privacy.3.p1.

### [MEDIUM] No backup or export of the database anywhere
`docs/SETUP-firebase.md:160` · devops · lens: docs-onboarding

Firestore holds the only copy of requests, holds, the credits ledger and staff shifts, several admin actions are permanent, and a mistaken rules publish can lock everyone out, yet no document mentions a backup, export, or recovery step.

**Evidence.** `grep -n -i "backup\|export\|rollback\|revert" README.md docs/SETUP-firebase.md` returns nothing except the payroll CSV (SETUP:121). backend.js:420 deleteRequest, :429 deleteHold and :492 removeCredit are hard deletes; SETUP:21 instructs "Delete what's there, paste the whole contents of firestore.rules … Publish" with no check before or after.

**Impact.** One wrong click or a bad rules paste loses bookings and the credits ledger with no way back; the owner does not know this.

**Suggestion.** Document a monthly export (Firebase console > Firestore > Import/Export, or `gcloud firestore export gs://<bucket>`, one Cloud Storage bucket needed on Blaze) or enable Firestore point-in-time recovery, and say where the export lives.

### [MEDIUM] No 404.html: Firebase's generic English error page
`firebase.json:5` · ux · lens: devops-hosting

There is no 404.html in the public root, so a mistyped or outdated URL (for example an old link once cleanUrls or renames happen, or /plans) shows Firebase Hosting's plain "Page Not Found" page with no logo, navigation, Spanish text or way back.

**Evidence.** File listing of the repo root has no 404.html; firebase.json:5-37 has no rewrites to catch unknown paths. superstatic's not-found middleware (the Hosting reference implementation) serves templates/not_found.html with status 404 and `Cache-Control: public, max-age=3600`. No page or script references a 404 route (grep for 404 across *.html/*.js finds nothing outside vendor/i18n).

**Impact.** A visitor who follows a broken link from a flyer, ad or old share lands on an unbranded page and leaves; the bilingual promise is broken on that screen.

**Suggestion.** Add a 404.html built from quote.html's shared chrome (top bar, header, footer, i18n.js and styles.css?v=22) with links to the home, plans and quote pages in both languages; Hosting serves a root 404.html automatically with status 404, no config needed.

### [MEDIUM] No security headers: CSP, nosniff, Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy
`firebase.json:17` · security · lens: devops-hosting

The headers block only sets Cache-Control. There is no Content-Security-Policy (not even frame-ancestors), X-Content-Type-Options, Referrer-Policy or Permissions-Policy, so admin.html and account.html can be framed by any site and the pages have no defence against injected scripts. If a COOP header is later added for hardening, it must be same-origin-allow-popups or Google popup sign-in breaks.

**Evidence.** firebase.json:17-36 contains two Cache-Control rules and nothing else. The site has a well-bounded set of third-party origins: fonts.googleapis.com and fonts.gstatic.com (every page head, e.g. index.html:11-13), Hosting's /__/firebase/ reserved URLs (backend.js:22,55-64), Formspree via fetch (app.js:348), reCAPTCHA Enterprise (backend.js:76), and buy.stripe.com links. backend.js:181-183 uses auth.signInWithPopup with a redirect fallback. Only one executable inline script exists (`window.MKG_SEGMENT = "commercial"` on the three commercial pages); quote.html carries 8 style= attributes, no on*= handlers, no eval.

**Impact.** Clickjacking of the owner's admin page, MIME-sniffing of uploaded or mis-served files, referrer leakage of ?lang/?ref/action-code URLs to third parties, and no containment if any script path is ever compromised.

**Suggestion.** Add a `{"source": "**"}` rule with X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff, Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin, Permissions-Policy: camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=(), Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin-allow-popups, and start with Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only: default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' https://www.gstatic.com https://www.google.com https://apis.google.com; style-src 'self' https://fonts.googleapis.com 'unsafe-inline'; font-src https://fonts.gstatic.com; img-src 'self' data: blob:; connect-src 'self' https://firestore.googleapis.com https://identitytoolkit.googleapis.com https://securetoken.googleapis.com https://firebaseappcheck.googleapis.com https://content-firebaseappcheck.googleapis.com https://www.googleapis.com https://formspree.io; frame-src 'self' https://www.google.com https://recaptcha.google.com; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'self'; object-src 'none' (move the MKG_SEGMENT line to a data attribute or hash it), then switch to enforcing once the console shows no violations.

### [MEDIUM] max-age=0 on versioned JS/CSS; no cache rule for images
`firebase.json:28` · perf · lens: devops-hosting

Every script and stylesheet is already cache-busted with ?v=22, yet the headers force browsers to revalidate all of them on every page view, and the 3.3 MB of brand PNG/WebP/SVG and favicon.ico get only Hosting's default short TTL because no rule matches them.

**Evidence.** firebase.json:28-33 `"source": "**/*.@(js|css)"` with `"Cache-Control": "public, max-age=0, must-revalidate"`; 77 asset tags across the 12 pages use `?v=22` (e.g. index.html:29 styles.css?v=22, index.html:304-307 plans.js/i18n.js/app.js/referral.js?v=22). No header rule matches brand/** or favicon.ico; the deployed image set is ~3.3 MB (brand/logo-full-1024.png alone is 1,282,010 B).

**Impact.** Each navigation issues conditional requests for 5-8 JS/CSS files (i18n.js is 112 KB) before the page is interactive, and the logo is re-fetched hourly; on mobile this is a visible delay on a site whose first screen is a pricing dial.

**Suggestion.** Keep HTML at max-age=0 but set `**/*.@(js|css)` to `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (the ?v= bump then must be part of every change; one sed over *.html does it) and add `{"source": "**/*.@(png|webp|svg|ico)", "headers": [{"key": "Cache-Control", "value": "public, max-age=2592000"}]}`.

### [MEDIUM] Rules are pasted by hand with no deploy, check, or rollback step
`firestore.rules:3` · devops · lens: docs-onboarding

The rules header and the setup guide say the file is published only by pasting into the console and that GitHub does not deploy it. That is true of the Action, but firebase.json already wires the file for CLI deploys, and nothing tells a maintainer how to confirm the live rules match the repo or how to roll them back.

**Evidence.** firestore.rules:3-5 "Published in the Firebase console (Firestore > Rules); GitHub does not deploy this file."; docs/SETUP-firebase.md:26-27 "Repeat this step whenever firestore.rules changes in the repo (GitHub does not deploy it automatically)." .github/workflows/firebase-hosting-merge.yml:29-34 uses FirebaseExtended/action-hosting-deploy (hosting only), so the statement holds for the Action; but firebase.json:2-4 declares `"firestore": { "rules": "firestore.rules" }`, so `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules` already works and the workflow could run it (the service account would need the Firebase Rules Admin role). No document mentions comparing the console text with `git show HEAD:firestore.rules`, or that Rules > history in the console restores a previous version.

**Impact.** The live rules can silently differ from the file every audit reads; the most security-relevant file has no verification or recovery procedure.

**Suggestion.** Add to the setup guide: how to deploy rules from the CLI, a post-publish check (paste back into a diff or use the Rules playground), and the console history for rollback; or move the rules deploy into the merge workflow.

### [MEDIUM] "This copy of the site" fallback copy appears on the live site after any failure
`i18n.js:447` · i18n · lens: error-resilience

ready resolves false for every failure (backend.js:84 catch-all): a dropped SDK script, a blocked /__/firebase/init.json, a captive portal. The site then shows strings written for the local copy: cal.offline "Live availability isn't connected on this copy of the site, so every window shows as open" (with every day labelled "3 open"), acct.offline "Sign-in isn't available on this copy of the site", adm.offline "…isn't connected to Firebase, so there's nothing to sign in to", q.demo "This copy of the site isn't connected to its database". Spanish mirrors it (lines 1257, 1287, 1331, 1068: "esta copia del sitio"). On mkgservices.com the wording is false, gives no action (reload, check connection), and invites the visitor to pick any window.

**Evidence.** Harness baseline on availability.html and quote.html?lang=es: calendar with 9 "3 open" days plus the notice text quoted above in EN and ES (screenshot 01-baseline-availability.png, 01-baseline-quote-cal-es.png); the same branch runs on the live domain because `SITE` (backend.js:37) is never consulted when choosing the message. Code: calendar.js:115 `if (live === false) html += '<p class="notice cal-offline">' + esc(t("cal.offline")) + "</p>";`.

**Impact.** Live visitors whose SDK load failed are told the site is a copy and that every window is open; clients are told sign-in does not exist here rather than to retry.

**Suggestion.** Branch the copy on backend.js's SITE flag: on the live domain say "We couldn't load live availability. Check your connection and reload, or pick a day and we'll confirm it by reply" and hide per-window counts; keep the "this copy" wording for local and preview builds; add a Reload button to the account/admin notices.

### [MEDIUM] Terms copy hardcodes the 24-hour notice that plans.js configures
`i18n.js:719` · legal · lens: maintainability-drift

plans.js:94 `policy: { changeNoticeHours: 24 }` drives the account page's change/cancel cutoff, but the terms text in both languages, the terms page fallback text and the docs state "24 hours" as a literal, so the two can disagree after one edit.

**Evidence.** account.js:26 `NOTICE_HOURS = (C.policy && C.policy.changeNoticeHours) || 24` and :36 tooLate(); i18n.js:719 (EN) and :1529 (ES) terms.2.p1 "at least 24 hours' notice", repeated in terms.html:74, docs/SETUP-firebase.md:126 and README.md:87. The referral rules already use a live placeholder for the same problem (`{maxPct}` → `<b data-ref-max>` at i18n.js:1711), so the pattern exists. The "Last updated October 16, 2026" line is hardcoded seven times (i18n.js:698, :715, :1508, :1525, privacy.html:66, terms.html:66, docs/make-pages.py:216).

**Impact.** Change changeNoticeHours to 48 and the account page refuses a change the terms still promise is free; the mismatch is between the site and its own legal text.

**Suggestion.** Add a `noticeHours` LIVE placeholder in i18n.js that reads C.policy.changeNoticeHours and use `{noticeHours}` in terms.2.p1 in both languages; keep one `legal.updated` key for the date.

### [MEDIUM] Formspree endpoint is public and quota-limited; no rotation runbook
`plans.js:40` · security · lens: docs-onboarding

The Formspree form id is shipped in plans.js and used from any copy of the site, with no note that it is public by design (the App Check key has one) and no procedure for a burned quota or for rotating either key.

**Evidence.** plans.js:40 `formEndpoint: "https://formspree.io/f/xjyglbgo"` with no "public" remark, versus plans.js:43-44 "Public by design" for appCheckKey. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:75-77 notes the 50 emails a month and the "Over limit" folder, but there is no runbook for noticing or handling an exhausted month, for creating a new form and swapping the id (then bumping `?v=`), for rotating the reCAPTCHA key named at SETUP:196 (new key in Fraud Defense, register under App Check > Apps, paste into appCheckKey), or for Formspree's own domain-restriction setting. The endpoint is POSTed from any origin: quote.js:496, observed from 127.0.0.1.

**Impact.** Anyone with the id can exhaust the monthly 50 emails; real requests then land silently in "Over limit" while the admin page still shows them, so the owner may not notice the emails stopped; rotating either key is guesswork.

**Suggestion.** Add a "Keys" section to the setup guide stating both ids are public by design, turn on Formspree's "restrict to domain" option, and give the two rotation procedures including the `?v=` bump.

### [MEDIUM] Calendar slot ids are a hidden schema that plans.js never documents
`plans.js:79` · maintainability · lens: maintainability-drift

The slot ids "0800", "1130", "1500", "1830" look like arbitrary labels, but three other files depend on their exact shape (four digits, HHMM, no spaces, at most 10 characters) and the comment above them (plans.js:68-72) says only that a slot is a time window.

**Evidence.** account.js:32 `if (/^\d{4}$/.test(r.slot || "")) { hh = Number(r.slot.slice(0, 2)); mm = Number(r.slot.slice(2)); }` derives the visit start for the 24-hour change cutoff from the id's digits and otherwise assumes 8:00; backend.js:350 packs `movedFrom = req.date + " " + req.slot` and admin.js:112 splits it on the space; firestore.rules:70, :104 and :128 enforce `str(d.slot, 10)`; calendar.js:178 slotLabel() returns the raw id when no slot matches, so a renamed id leaves old holds labelled "0800".

**Impact.** Owner renames "1500" to "afternoon": every afternoon booking's change/cancel cutoff silently becomes 8 am, so clients are blocked seven hours earlier than the terms promise; an id with a space corrupts the admin "moved from" note; an id over 10 characters makes every request for that window fail at the rules (a lost booking with a generic error).

**Suggestion.** Document in plans.js that ids are permanent, must be HHMM and at most 10 characters; better, add an explicit `start: "15:00"` field per slot and have account.js read it, and store movedFrom as two fields.

### [MEDIUM] plans.js says terms follow changeNoticeHours; terms are hard-coded
`plans.js:91` · legal · lens: docs-onboarding

The comment on `policy.changeNoticeHours` says terms.html promises this notice period, implying one number drives both. The terms text hard-codes "24 hours" in both languages, and the late-cancel fee it promises ("up to half the visit price") exists nowhere in plans.js.

**Evidence.** plans.js:91-94 "terms.html promises a confirmed visit can be changed or cancelled free with this much notice … `policy: { changeNoticeHours: 24 }`"; account.js:26 `var NOTICE_HOURS = (C.policy && C.policy.changeNoticeHours) || 24;`; i18n.js:719 terms.2.p1 "…with at least 24 hours' notice; later than that, we may charge up to half the visit price." and i18n.js:1529 (Spanish) are literal strings; `grep -n -i "fee\|half" plans.js` finds nothing. docs/SETUP-firebase.md:127-128 repeats "matching the terms page".

**Impact.** Changing the number in plans.js makes the account page refuse changes at one threshold while the terms promise another; the fee cannot be changed from the "single source of truth" at all; README:57 and SETUP mislead the owner about where policy lives.

**Suggestion.** Interpolate `{hours}` into terms.2.p1 from policy.changeNoticeHours (i18n already supports placeholders, e.g. acct.tooLate at i18n.js:673) and add a `policy.lateCancelPercent`, or add a "Changing a policy" runbook naming plans.js, i18n.js terms.* (en and es) and the privacy/terms "Last updated" line.

### [MEDIUM] One failed script leaves raw {phone}/{email} text; quote pages have no noscript
`quote.html:275` · content · lens: error-resilience

Contact details and prices exist only after JavaScript runs: the markup has `<b data-business="phone"></b>` empty and literal placeholders such as "Call or text {phone}, or email {email}" and "Serving {serviceArea}" that i18n.js fills at runtime. If i18n.js fails to load (one dropped request on a mobile network) or JS is off, visitors see the braces and no phone number anywhere; if backend.js fails, quote.js throws at init and the Send button becomes a permanently disabled "Sending…". Eight pages (quote, commercial-quote, availability, account, admin, refer, privacy, terms) have no <noscript> fallback.

**Evidence.** Harness scenarios `scriptfail` and `nojs`: with /i18n.js aborted, quote.html body text contained `{phone} {email} {serviceArea}` and 0 prices (screenshot 09-scriptfail-i18n-quote.png), index.html showed 8 placeholder kinds (`{discount} {oneTimeStruck} {saving} {planName} {bedrooms} {bathrooms} {phone} {serviceArea}`); with JS disabled, quote.html showed the same `{phone}`/`{email}` line and 5 of 9 data-business elements empty. With /backend.js aborted, pressing Send left `btn:"Sending…", disabled:true, err:""` and `pageerror: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'ready')` (quote.js:150 in init and :493 in submit). Static grep: 20 literal `{…}` placeholders across the HTML.

**Impact.** A visitor whose connection dropped one of the seven script files, or who browses with JS off, cannot find a phone number or email and cannot send the form; the page looks broken rather than degraded.

**Suggestion.** Put the phone number and email into the static markup (they are constants in plans.js; docs/make-pages.py can stamp them) and keep brace placeholders out of visible HTML text; add a <noscript> call/text/email line to the quote, availability and account pages; in quote.js guard `B` so a missing backend takes the email path instead of throwing after the button is disabled.

### [MEDIUM] Send never re-checks the window is still open; rules cannot cap capacity
`quote.js:32` · bug · lens: error-resilience

bookable() only checks lead time and horizon. The month's holds are fetched once when step 5 first renders and cached for the life of the page (calendar.js:65); nothing refreshes before Send, and a `?date=&slot=` carried from availability.html is accepted at line 572 without checking the slot. firestore.rules:150-156 validates a hold's shape but cannot count holds per date+slot, so capacity (plans.js:74, 2 per window) is enforced only by the client-side calendar.

**Evidence.** Code: `function bookable(ymd) { … return ymd >= first && ymd <= last; }` and submit handler lines 468-474 only clear a stale date; calendar.js `if (cache[k]) { apply(cache[k]); return Promise.resolve(); }`; rules `allow create: if holdShape(request.resource.data) && (isAdmin() || (hid == request.resource.data.requestId && (… mirrors(hid)) …` with no occupancy check. Two tabs opened on the same morning both see "1 of 2 left" and both commit (cannot be settled against the live database from here, but nothing in the code or rules prevents it).

**Impact.** The last spot in a window can be taken twice (two visitors, or one who left the tab open an hour); admin.html then shows 3 of 2 taken and the owner must decline a request the visitor believed was fine.

**Suggestion.** Before submitRequest, call B.availability(date, date) fresh and, if the window is now full or blocked, return to step 5 with q.dateStale-style copy; longer term keep a per-slot counter document updated in the same batch (or a Cloud Function) so the rules can refuse the overflow.

### [MEDIUM] 'Manage it' link opens admin.html with no deep link to the request
`quote.js:454` · ux · lens: email-notify-flow

Both owner emails link to the bare admin page; the owner must sign in, then type the six-character reference into the search box to find the card the email is about.

**Evidence.** quote.js:454 `put("Manage it", location.origin + "/admin.html")` and account.js:204 `f["Manage it"] = location.origin + "/admin.html"`; admin.js reads neither location.search nor location.hash (grep: none), while the week-ahead panel already knows how to scroll to and flash a card (admin.js:359).

**Impact.** Three to four extra steps per request on a phone; with several New requests the owner has to retype a reference from the email into the search field.

**Suggestion.** Append `?req=<id>` to the link and, in admin.js enter(), set `query` to that id or scroll-and-flash the matching card after the first watchRequests callback.

### [MEDIUM] Quote draft with PII re-saved after send, prefills next request
`quote.js:481` · privacy · lens: rendered-smoke

done() clears the sessionStorage draft and then calls go('done'), but go() ends with saveDraft(), so the full request (name, email, phone, notes, chosen day and window) is written back and reloaded into the form on the next visit to the quote page in that tab.

**Evidence.** quote.js:480-481 `clearDraft(); go("done");` and go() at line 365 `saveDraft();`. Observed: after a residential submission, `sessionStorage.getItem('mkg.quote')` still held `{"name":"Jane Doe","email":"jane@example.com","phone":"9805550199","notes":"private note",...,"step":"done","date":"2026-10-22","slot":"0800"}`; opening quote.html again in the same tab showed those values in #name/#email/#phone/#notes and step 5 already read 'Requested: Thursday, October 22, Morning (8:00–11:00)' with slot 0800 pressed.

**Impact.** On a shared or kiosk device the next person sees the previous customer's contact details; a client sending a second request (e.g. for a relative) silently re-requests the same day and window and would hold it twice.

**Suggestion.** Clear the draft after go('done') (or skip saveDraft() when n === 'done') and reset draft.date/slot once a request is sent.

### [MEDIUM] Non-Firebase copies email real leads via Formspree, never show demo note
`quote.js:495` · bug · lens: rendered-smoke

When Firebase is absent, the form only shows the documented demo confirmation if formEndpoint is empty; since plans.js sets it, every static/dev copy POSTs the visitor's details to the live Formspree form and shows the normal 'Request sent' screen with no demo note and no database record.

**Evidence.** quote.js:495-496: `if (!C.formEndpoint) { setTimeout(function () { done(true); }, 400); return; } return mailCopy(data).then(function (ok) { ok ? done(false) : fail(); });`. Observed on http://127.0.0.1:8787/quote.html with the harness intercepting the network: on submit the page issued `POST https://formspree.io/f/xjyglbgo`; with a simulated 200 it reached step 'done' with `[data-demo-note]` hidden and the 'Create my account' link shown; with the POST blocked it showed 'Couldn't send your request. Check your connection and try again or email mkgservices704@gmail.com.' README.md:3 and backend.js:8-9 both say such copies show a demo confirmation. (On file:// the email's 'Manage it' line would read 'null/admin.html' since location.origin is 'null'.)

**Impact.** Anyone testing the site from a local server or file copy sends fake quote emails to the owner's inbox and consumes the 50-email/month Formspree quota, and a visitor on such a copy is told a request was sent and offered an account to track it although nothing was stored. The documented demo mode is unreachable without editing plans.js.

**Suggestion.** Send the email copy only when Firebase is live or the hostname is mkgservices.com (backend.js already computes SITE); otherwise call done(true) so the demo note shows, and fix the README/backend.js comment to match.

### [MEDIUM] The visitor never sees a reference number for their request
`quote.js:504` · ux · lens: email-notify-flow

quote.js fills `[data-request-id]` with the short id, but no page contains such an element, so the done page shows no reference even though the owner's email and the account page both identify the request by '#7KQ9MX'.

**Evidence.** quote.js:504 `$all("[data-request-id]").forEach(function (el) { el.textContent = id.slice(0, 6).toUpperCase(); })`; grep for data-request-id across *.html returns nothing; the rendered done page text (scratchpad quote-ok-nodate.json) has no '#'. The email uses `put("Reference", …)` (quote.js:453) and account.js:119 shows '#' + id.slice(0,6).

**Impact.** A client who calls or texts to follow up has nothing to cite, and there is no receipt to screenshot; the search-by-ID feature on admin.html is useless from the client side.

**Suggestion.** Add `<p>Reference <b data-request-id></b></p>` to the done step of quote.html and commercial-quote.html (hidden when empty), and include the same reference in the eventual client confirmation email.

### [MEDIUM] Email-only fallback shows 'Request sent' and invites an account for a request that was never saved
`quote.js:510` · bug · lens: email-notify-flow

When Firestore refuses the write, the site falls back to the Formspree email alone but then renders the normal success page, including 'Create a free account with the same email to follow it', although no record exists for the account to show. The email also carries no hint that the request is missing from admin.html.

**Evidence.** quote.js:510 `if (C.formEndpoint) return mailCopy(data).then(function (ok) { ok ? done(false) : fail(); })` and the identical not-connected branch at 494-496; done() then calls renderTrack() (553-558) which prints the q.done.makeAccount copy. Observed locally (scratchpad quote-ok-nodate.json): track = 'Want to follow it, change the day, or cancel online? Create a free account with the same email.', demo note hidden, and the captured email has no 'Reference' row (quote.js:453 skips it when id is empty) and no 'not saved' marker. Combined with an over-limit Formspree (HTTP 200, held) the visitor sees success while nobody receives anything.

**Impact.** The client signs up, verifies their email and finds 'nothing yet'; the owner reads an email, opens admin.html and cannot find the request; the two then disagree about whether a booking exists.

**Suggestion.** In the email-only path add a field such as 'Not saved to the database: reply by email and add it as a phone booking on admin.html', hide the account prompt and show a different done message; better, retry the Firestore write once and log the failure (Cloud Function or a `failedRequests` doc) so the gap is visible.

### [MEDIUM] ?date/slot and stale drafts bypass closed-day, window and capacity checks
`quote.js:572` · bug · lens: rendered-smoke

A date and slot arriving from the URL (availability.html's 'Request this window' link) or a saved draft is only checked for format and lead/horizon, not against the calendar's weekdays, owner-closed days, blocked windows, segment-specific slots or remaining capacity; submit re-checks only the same thing and never refreshes holds, and firestore.rules only validates the hold's shape.

**Evidence.** quote.js:572-574 accept any `^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$` date plus any slot string, then `if (draft.date && !bookable(draft.date))` where bookable() (line 32) tests lead/horizon only; the submit guard at line 468 is identical. Observed: `quote.html?date=2026-10-25&slot=0800` (a Sunday; plans.js:75 weekdays exclude 0) reached step 5 showing 'Requested: Sunday, October 25, Morning (8:00–11:00)' while that cell rendered as `cal-cell is-closed is-selected ... disabled`, the review listed it and Send proceeded to the network call. `quote.html?date=2026-10-22&slot=1830` on the residential form showed 'Requested: Thursday, October 22, After hours (6:30–9:30 pm)', a commercial-only window (plans.js:82). firestore.rules:126-156 (holdShape/mirrors) never consult days/, weekdays or hold counts.

**Impact.** The owner receives requests holding days the business is closed, windows that don't exist for homes, or a third hold on a capacity-2 window when two visitors pick the last slot at the same time; each needs a manual reschedule and the public calendar shows a window as 'Full' that was never really bookable.

**Suggestion.** At step 5 and on submit, call cal.refresh() and validate with cal.dayInfo(date): closed/blocked/full or a slot not in the segment's list clears the pick with the existing q.dateStale message; optionally add weekday/closed-day checks to the hold rule.

### [MEDIUM] Anchor links land with the heading under the sticky header on mobile
`styles.css:33` · ux · lens: rendered-smoke

The header is position: sticky (styles.css:101-102) but nothing sets scroll-margin/scroll-padding, so the #faq and #single-cleans targets, which every other page's nav and footer link to, scroll so that the section heading sits behind the 63px header at phone width.

**Evidence.** styles.css:33 `html { scroll-behavior: smooth; ... }`, 102 `position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 20;`, no scroll-margin-top anywhere. Measured after load at 390px: index.html#faq h2 top 3px / bottom 70px vs header bottom 63px; index.html#single-cleans h2 top 4px; commercial.html#faq h2 top 53px. At 1280px the h2 clears the header by 10px. Screenshots out/flows/anchor-faq-390.png and anchor-single-cleans-390.png show the FAQ accordion and the single-clean cards with their 'Questions people ask first' / 'Need one clean, not a plan?' titles hidden.

**Impact.** Phone visitors tapping 'FAQ' or 'Single cleans' from plans, quote, availability or the footer of any page arrive on a list with no title and no context; the same happens for the in-page links once the smooth scroll settles.

**Suggestion.** Add `html { scroll-padding-top: 80px; }` (or `section[id] { scroll-margin-top: 80px; }`) sized to the sticky header, and keep the rule in sync with the 62/68px header heights.

### [LOW] Logo section describes files and uses that do not exist
`README.md:49` · content · lens: docs-onboarding

The logo table lists a PNG that is not in brand/, says the compact mark is used in the header and favicon when no page loads it, and claims make-logo.py regenerates every brand file when it writes six SVGs.

**Evidence.** README.md:49 lists `logo-mark.svg / -512.png / -192.png` for "Header, favicon, social avatars, app icons"; `ls brand/` has no logo-mark-192.png and `grep -rn logo-mark *.html` matches nothing: every header loads brand/logo-full-112.png and favicons are "made from the illustrated logo" (README:51). README.md:53 "brand/make-logo.py regenerates every file", but brand/make-logo.py:193-199 writes only logo-badge(.svg/-mono/-reverse) and logo-mark(.svg/-mono/-reverse); the PNGs, favicons, apple-touch-icon.png, favicon.ico and logo-full-* are produced elsewhere, and the script needs uharfbuzz and fontTools (lines 17-19) which README does not mention.

**Impact.** Someone changing the ring text per README:53 regenerates SVGs nothing loads and leaves every shipped PNG unchanged, then wonders why the site did not change.

**Suggestion.** Rewrite the table around what pages actually load (logo-full-112/320/640/1024, favicon-*, apple-touch-icon.png) and state that the PNG set is exported by hand from brand/source/logo-full-3000.png; list make-logo.py's dependencies.

### [LOW] README deploy section contradicts the current setup and scripts
`README.md:66` · maintainability · lens: devops-hosting

The deploy instructions still describe the free plan, tell the owner to replace a placeholder project id that is already set, and say make-pages.py regenerates five pages that the script itself now says must never be regenerated.

**Evidence.** README.md:66 "Deploying (Firebase Hosting, free plan)" vs docs/SETUP-firebase.md:213 "The project is on the Blaze (pay-as-you-go) plan"; README.md:71 "Put the project ID in .firebaserc (replace REPLACE_WITH_YOUR_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID)" while .firebaserc:3 already holds gen-lang-client-0946383119; README.md:64 "docs/make-pages.py regenerates the availability, account, admin, privacy, and terms pages" vs docs/make-pages.py:1-2 "Do NOT re-run this script: it would overwrite those pages" (the note also sits above the shebang on line 3, so the file is no longer directly executable).

**Impact.** A future maintainer following the README can wipe the hand-edited admin, account and availability pages with one command, and the billing description is wrong.

**Suggestion.** Update the three README lines (Blaze, project id already set, make-pages.py archived) and add `raise SystemExit("archived: see note at top")` after the docstring in docs/make-pages.py or rename it make-pages.py.archived.

### [LOW] Move and cancel emails omit the 'reply in Spanish' hint
`account.js:200` · i18n · lens: email-notify-flow

New-request emails add a 'Language: Spanish' row, but the move/cancel emails built in tellBusiness() never look at the request's stored language, so the owner may reply in English to a Spanish-speaking client.

**Evidence.** quote.js:435 `if (d.language === "es") put("Language", "Spanish (reply in Spanish if you can)")`; account.js:183-205 builds `f` from r.name, r.phone, r.service, r.id only. Captured cancel body for a fixture with `language: "es"` (scratchpad account-cancel.json) has no Language row.

**Impact.** Inconsistent handling of the one piece of language information the site collects, on the messages where a quick phone reply matters most.

**Suggestion.** In tellBusiness add `if (r.language === "es") f.Language = "Spanish (reply in Spanish if you can)";` and consider mirroring it on the admin card's move/cancel badge.

### [LOW] Admin sign-in heading overlaps its lead paragraph on phones
`admin.html:65` · ux · lens: rendered-smoke

admin.html places h1.title and p.step-intro directly in the card rather than inside a .step wrapper, so the -10px top margin that step-intro relies on pulls the paragraph into the heading; in Spanish the two-line title collides visibly.

**Evidence.** styles.css:332 `.step-intro { color: var(--muted); margin-top: -10px; }` with the matching size reduction only in `.step .title` (styles.css:331); admin.html:64-65 render `<h1 class="title">` + `<p class="step-intro">` outside any .step. Measured at 390px: admin.html?lang=es h1 'Acceso de administración' bottom 205px vs next paragraph top 195px (10px overlap, two lines); EN one-line title has the same 10px overlap. Screenshot out/flows/admin-es-m390.png shows 'administración' touching 'Para el equipo de MKG Services'. account.html, quote pages, availability and refer have a 12-14px gap instead.

**Impact.** Cosmetic, but it is the first thing staff see on a phone and reads as broken typesetting in Spanish.

**Suggestion.** Wrap the admin login title and intro in the same `.step` structure the other cards use, or give `.quote-card > .step-intro` a margin-top of 8px.

### [LOW] admin.html footer lacks the 'Refer a friend' link every other page has
`admin.html:197` · maintainability · lens: rendered-smoke

The footer nav on admin.html is the only one of the 12 pages without the refer.html link; docs/make-pages.py (which generated the chrome) now says it must not be re-run, so the shared header/footer has started to drift by hand.

**Evidence.** quote.html:299 `<a href="refer.html" data-i18n="nav.refer">Refer a friend</a>` is present on index, plans, quote, commercial*, availability, account, refer, privacy and terms; admin.html:187-198 goes straight from 'Terms' to 'Staff'. Crawl of all rendered footers (out/smoke.json hrefs) shows refer.html absent only on admin.html. docs/make-pages.py:1-2 'Do NOT re-run this script'.

**Impact.** Minor for staff, but it shows the five generated pages no longer share one source, so the next chrome change (a new nav item, a phone change in markup) has to be made in 12 files by hand.
