### [LOW] Tab-title counter is the only alert, invisible on a phone, and reverts to English
`admin.js:71` · ux · lens: email-notify-flow

The '(N)' new-request count lives only in document.title while admin.html is open in a desktop tab; there is no notification, badge or sound. The base title is captured before i18n runs, so in Spanish the tab reads '(2) Admin | MKG Services'.

**Evidence.** admin.js:71 `document.title = (c.new ? "(" + c.new + ") " : "") + baseTitle;` with `baseTitle = document.title` at admin.js:29, evaluated before i18n apply() translates `<title data-i18n>` (i18n.js:1723-1730). Mock run: after clicking ES and after a re-render the title was '(2) Admin | MKG Services'.

**Impact.** The counter never reaches the owner's phone, which is where the quote-request emails are read; a Spanish-speaking owner sees an English tab title.

**Suggestion.** Use `t("meta.title.admin")` inside counts() instead of the cached string; for real alerting rely on the server-side email/SMS above, or add Web Push through Firebase Cloud Messaging later.

### [LOW] Migration shims for old data have no removal plan
`admin.js:366` · maintainability · lens: maintainability-drift

Several code paths exist only to tolerate records from earlier versions of the data model, none dated or listed anywhere, so a future reader cannot tell what is live behaviour and what is legacy.

**Evidence.** admin.js:366 and firestore.rules:161 special-case holds with requestId "manual" ("from before phone bookings were requests"); admin.js:44-52 and :291-292 migrate adminNotes/assignedTo off old request documents; backend.js:353 "older holds had random ids"; quote.js:570 accepts `?single=1` ("older links") although no page or document links it.

**Impact.** Each shim is an extra branch in the rules and UI to reason about; whether any such records still exist can only be settled against the live database.

**Suggestion.** List them with dates in a MIGRATIONS note, check the live collections once from the console, and delete the shims and the rules clause when the data is clean.

### [LOW] Admin reports network failures as "Wrong email or password."
`admin.js:557` · ux · lens: error-resilience

The admin password handler maps every rejection to adm.badLogin, so a network timeout (auth/network-request-failed), too-many-requests or a disabled account all read as a wrong password; the submit button is never disabled during the ~30 s Auth timeout, so repeated clicks fire repeated attempts. Separately, a failure of the requests listener prints adm.failed "That didn't save. Check your connection and try again." (line 530) for what is a read. account.js already has authMessage() that maps these codes properly.

**Evidence.** Harness scenario `authwait` with the real SDK and identitytoolkit.googleapis.com aborted: admin.html showed `Wrong email or password.` after the Auth SDK's timeout; `[data-admin-form] button[type=submit].disabled` was false during the attempt; account.html in the same run correctly showed `Something went wrong. Check your connection and try again.` Code: `B.signInPassword(email, pass).catch(function (e) { $('[data-error="login"]').textContent = t("adm.badLogin"); … });`.

**Impact.** Staff with no signal are told their password is wrong, try variants, and can trip Firebase's too-many-requests lockout.

**Suggestion.** Reuse account.js's authMessage() (move it into backend.js), disable the button while the promise is pending, and add an adm.loadFailed string for the listener error.

### [LOW] Admin 'Forgot your password?' throws uncaught TypeError when offline
`admin.js:564` · bug · lens: rendered-smoke

When Firebase is not available the sign-in form and Google button are disabled, but the 'Forgot your password?' button stays active and calls B.sendReset(), which dereferences the null auth object.

**Evidence.** admin.js:574 disables only `[data-admin-form] button` and `[data-google-signin]`; admin.js:564 calls `B.sendReset(email)`; backend.js:217 `function sendReset(email) { auth.languageCode = lang(); ... }` with auth === null. Observed on admin.html (EN and ES, 390 and 1280) after typing an email and clicking the link: pageerror `Cannot set properties of null (setting 'languageCode')`, `[data-admin-forgot-msg]` stayed empty, no feedback to the user.

**Impact.** Staff opening a preview or local copy, or the live page when the SDK fails to load, get a dead button with a console exception instead of the 'not connected' explanation; no data risk.

**Suggestion.** Disable `[data-admin-forgot]` in the !live branch of admin.js:574 (account.js already does this) or make backend.js sendReset() reject when auth is null.

### [LOW] esc, $all, locale, money and the email regex are copied across files
`calendar.js:22` · maintainability · lens: maintainability-drift

Small helpers are re-implemented per file instead of shared through the window.MKG namespace that already exists for this purpose.

**Evidence.** esc() is defined identically in app.js:212, calendar.js:22 and i18n.js:1682; $all() in app.js:92, quote.js:26, admin.js:18, timeclock.js:27, account.js:18 and $() in quote.js:25, admin.js:17, timeclock.js:26, account.js:17, availability.js:13; locale() in calendar.js:23, admin.js:33, timeclock.js:34, account.js:48 plus inline at account.js:208; money() in admin.js:35 and account.js:70 (identical) and timeclock.js:42 (different, no type check); the email regex `/^[^\s@]+@[^\s@]+\.[^\s@]{2,}$/` in quote.js:272, account.js:43, admin.js:454, :507, :562; the plan-by-English-name lookup block in admin.js:74-79 and account.js:77-84; unitKey in quote.js:304 and inline at admin.js:104.

**Impact.** A fix in one copy (escaping single quotes, accepting plus-addressed emails, rounding money) silently misses the others, and new code copies whichever version it sees first.

**Suggestion.** Export esc, $, $all, validEmail and money from app.js (it already exports esc and dollars) and locale() from i18n.js, then replace the copies.

### [LOW] Commercial quote offers Sunday although the calendar never opens Sundays
`commercial-quote.html:240` · ux · lens: rendered-smoke

The commercial 'Days that work' checkboxes include Sun while plans.js defines working days as Monday-Saturday, so the calendar on the same step shows every Sunday as Closed; the residential form correctly omits Sunday.

**Evidence.** commercial-quote.html:240 `<input type="checkbox" name="days" value="Sun">`; plans.js:75 `weekdays: [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6], // 0 = Sunday ... 6 = Saturday`. Observed on commercial-quote.html step 5: day labels ['Mon','Tue','Wed','Thu','Fri','Sat','Sun'] under a calendar whose Sundays render `is-closed` ('Closed'/'Cerrado'); quote.html lists only Mon-Sat.

**Impact.** A business that only allows cleaning on Sundays ticks a day the owner cannot schedule from the calendar, leading to back-and-forth after the request.

**Suggestion.** Either remove the Sun checkbox from commercial-quote.html or add 0 to plans.js weekdays (per segment) if commercial Sunday visits are really offered.

### [LOW] No redirects or URL normalisation: /index.html duplicates /
`firebase.json:5` · seo · lens: devops-hosting

firebase.json sets no cleanUrls, trailingSlash or redirects, so /index.html is served as a 200 duplicate of /, and 20 internal links deliberately point at index.html. The www-to-apex redirect lives only as a console setting that the repo neither records nor can reproduce.

**Evidence.** firebase.json:5-37 has no `redirects`, `cleanUrls` or `trailingSlash` keys. Explicit links: quote.html:52 and :271, terms.html:42, refer.html:42, privacy.html:42 `href="index.html"` plus #faq/#single-cleans anchors on the same pages; hreflang alternates index.html:8-10 also use index.html?lang=…. superstatic/lib/middleware/files.js (Hosting's reference logic): "Exact file always wins", so /index.html streams the file; the /index.html redirect only runs in the directory-index branch. backend.js:36 comment "www.mkgservices.com redirects here" is the only trace of the www redirect.

**Impact.** Minor duplicate-content signal (mitigated by the canonical tag on index.html:19) and inconsistent URLs in analytics; if the Hosting site is ever recreated the www redirect is lost without a note.

**Suggestion.** Add `"redirects": [{"source": "/index.html", "destination": "/", "type": 301}]` and `"trailingSlash": false`, change internal index.html links to "/", and note the console-side www redirect in README's Live address section; leave cleanUrls off unless the sitemap and canonicals are changed with it.

### [LOW] 77 hand-maintained ?v= tags that the cache headers make redundant
`firebase.json:32` · devops · lens: maintainability-drift

All script and stylesheet tags carry ?v=22 (77 occurrences across 12 files, currently all in step), but firebase.json serves js/css with max-age=0, must-revalidate, so browsers revalidate every file on every load whatever the query string says.

**Evidence.** firebase.json:23 (html) and :32 (js|css) both set `"Cache-Control": "public, max-age=0, must-revalidate"`; `grep -o '?v=22' *.html | wc -l` = 77; no document explains when to bump, and docs/make-pages.py would reintroduce ?v=14.

**Impact.** Today the chore buys nothing; if someone later raises max-age for performance, a single missed tag serves stale JavaScript against new HTML, and the breakage is per page.

**Suggestion.** Either drop the ?v= suffixes entirely, or keep them and set js/css to a long max-age with one bump command (`sed -i 's/?v=22/?v=23/g' *.html`) plus a CI grep that every tag carries the same number.

### [LOW] Ten orphaned i18n keys and three unused CSS classes
`i18n.js:475` · maintainability · lens: maintainability-drift

After accounting for every data-i18n, data-i18n-attr, t("…"), plural() call and every dynamically built key family, ten keys (times two languages) are referenced nowhere, and three CSS classes match nothing in any page or script.

**Evidence.** Unused keys (EN line / ES line): acct.sendLink 475/1285 (only in the retired docs/make-pages.py), adm.credit.h 798/1608, adm.credit.history 803/1613, plans.monthlyUnit 44/854, plans.noscript 110/920 and plans.noscriptLink 111/921 (the four `<noscript>` blocks hardcode both languages inline instead, e.g. index.html:187), tc.clockIn 580/1390, tc.note 585/1395, tc.noteHint 586/1396, why.h2 121/931. styles.css: .dial-plan (136), .ref-reqs-h (684, only in make-pages.py's old template), .measure (53).

**Impact.** Translators maintain sentences nobody sees, and a developer who edits plans.noscript to change the no-JavaScript message changes nothing on the site.

**Suggestion.** Delete the keys and rules, and make the noscript blocks use a key or drop the unused one; keep the key-usage script from this audit as part of the check.

### [LOW] One 1776-line i18n.js, two languages 800 lines apart, 28 KB admin-only
`i18n.js:832` · maintainability · lens: maintainability-drift

All fixed copy lives in one file with the English block (lines 22-830) and the Spanish block (832-1640) far apart, so every copy edit means finding the matching key 800 lines away; the file also ships admin-only strings to every visitor and uses a different bilingual convention from plans.js.

**Evidence.** 774 keys per language (parity currently perfect, verified); the adm.*, tc.* and acct.* prefixes are 484 keys (about 28 KB of the 112 KB file) used only by admin.html and account.html, yet i18n.js is loaded by all 12 pages. plans.js:133 writes bilingual values side by side (`name: { en: "Essentials", es: "Esencial" }`), the opposite shape from i18n.js's `D.en = {` / `D.es = {`.

**Impact.** EN/ES drift on each edit is held off only by discipline (no check exists), and every home-page visitor downloads time-clock and credit-ledger copy.

**Suggestion.** Split an i18n-admin.js loaded only by admin.html and account.html, or adopt the plans.js side-by-side `{ en, es }` shape for i18n too; add the parity script to CI.

### [LOW] Spanish follow-up note says 'recámaras' where the site says 'habitaciones'
`i18n.js:1000` · i18n · lens: rendered-smoke

The only Spanish string using the Mexican term 'recámaras' for bedrooms is q.fu.note; every other string (steppers, dial, FAQ, pricing notes, step 3 intro) uses 'habitaciones', and both appear on the same screen.

**Evidence.** i18n.js:1000 `"q.fu.note": "Según una casa de {bedrooms} recámaras y {bathrooms} baños. ..."` vs :1029 `"q.s3.p": "... casa de {bedrooms} habitaciones y {bathrooms} baños ..."`, :895-896 unit.bedroom 'habitación/habitaciones', :881 dial.fine. Observed on quote.html?lang=es step 3 with 'Seguimiento de limpieza profunda reciente' selected: intro reads '4 habitaciones y 2 baños' and the estimate box directly below reads 'Según una casa de 4 recámaras y 2 baños'.

**Impact.** Spanish-speaking visitors see two different words for the same thing within one step, which reads as a copy error on a pricing claim.

**Suggestion.** Change 'recámaras' to 'habitaciones' in i18n.js:1000.

### [LOW] Older browsers get an empty shell: URLSearchParams at i18n init without a guard
`i18n.js:1648` · maintainability · lens: error-resilience

The code is clean ES5 syntax, but detect() runs `new URLSearchParams(location.search)` at script evaluation with no try/catch. In a browser without it (IE11, iOS ≤10.2 Safari, Android 4.x WebView) i18n.js throws, window.MKG.i18n is never defined, and app.js, referral.js, backend-dependent scripts and quote.js all `return` at their first line (app.js:9 `if (!C || !C.i18n) return;`), leaving the page without prices, the dial, contact details or any "update your browser" message; the <noscript> does not help because JS is on. app.js:72 repeats the same unguarded call; referral.js:27 shows the guarded pattern.

**Evidence.** Code: `function detect() { var q = new URLSearchParams(location.search).get("lang"); …` executed via `var lang = detect();` at line 1645; grep over all site JS found no arrow functions, template literals, let/const or optional chaining (syntax floor is ES5), while URLSearchParams, Element.closest, CustomEvent constructor and Object.assign are used unguarded. Not reproduced in a legacy engine; reasoned from the code.

**Impact.** A small share of visitors on old phones (and anyone on an enterprise IE mode) see a page with blank price slots and no phone number.

**Suggestion.** Wrap detect() in try/catch with a regex fallback on location.search (as referral.js does), or add a two-line feature check at the top of plans.js that shows a static "please update your browser; call (980) 319-0015" banner.

### [LOW] Google Fonts stylesheet is render-blocking; a stalled CDN delays first paint
`index.html:13` · perf · lens: error-resilience

Every page loads https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?… as a plain <link rel=stylesheet> in <head>. A blocked host fails fast and the fallback stack works, but a slow or black-holed connection to fonts.googleapis.com (filtered networks, poor mobile) holds the whole first paint for as long as the request stalls, while the page's own CSS is local and ready.

**Evidence.** Harness scenario `fonts`: with fonts.googleapis.com aborted, first-contentful-paint 56 ms and computed body font "Bricolage Grotesque", "Segoe UI", system-ui, -apple-system, sans-serif (fallback rendered); with the CSS response delayed 6 s, first-paint and first-contentful-paint moved to 6056 ms and DOMContentLoaded to 6073 ms. Same <link> on all 13 pages.

**Impact.** Visitors behind networks that throttle Google domains stare at a white page before any content, even though everything else is local.

**Suggestion.** Load the font CSS non-blocking (`<link rel="preload" as="style" onload="this.rel='stylesheet'">` with a <noscript> fallback) or self-host the two woff2 files with font-display: swap.

### [LOW] Home page structured data duplicates phone and email from plans.js
`index.html:41` · maintainability · lens: maintainability-drift

README.md:36-38 says the phone and email are edited in plans.js and flow everywhere, but index.html's JSON-LD block repeats both as literals.

**Evidence.** index.html:41 `"telephone": "+1-980-319-0015"` and :42 `"email": "mkgservices704@gmail.com"` versus plans.js:28-29 `phone: "(980) 319-0015"`, `email: "mkgservices704@gmail.com"`; the block also hardcodes the description and areaServed text that plans.js serviceArea otherwise controls.

**Impact.** Changing the number in plans.js leaves search engines showing the old one; nothing in the repo flags the mismatch.

**Suggestion.** Build the LocalBusiness JSON-LD in app.js from C.business (inject a <script type="application/ld+json"> at load) or note in plans.js that index.html:41-42 must be edited too.

### [LOW] Unverifiable invite code is shown as 'Invite applied' and listed in the summary
`quote.js:104` · ux · lens: rendered-smoke

checkReferral() returns null when Firestore cannot be asked; refApplied() treats null like a confirmed code, so the field turns green with 'Invite applied: $25 off your first visit', the review and the confirmation screen list the credit, and if the server later rejects the code the request is silently re-sent without it.

**Evidence.** quote.js:104 `draft.refValid !== false`, :109-112 only `=== undefined` shows 'Checking…' and only `=== false` shows the error; backend.js:445-451 `.catch(function () { return null; })`; quote.js:501 drops the ref on rejection with `plain.refDropped = data.ref` while renderReview() still calls refApplied(). Observed offline on quote.html and commercial-quote.html (EN/ES): typing K7M2QX produced the green 'Invite applied: $25/$50 off your first visit' status although no code was checked.

**Impact.** Mostly a demo-copy issue since firestore.rules:243 allows anonymous reads of referrers/{code}; on the live site it needs a failed read, in which case the visitor's confirmation claims a welcome credit the saved request does not carry.

**Suggestion.** Treat null as 'could not check' with a neutral message, and after a refDropped resubmit re-render the done summary without the invite row.

### [LOW] Local variables shadow helper functions of the same name
`quote.js:278` · maintainability · lens: maintainability-drift

A stricter eslint pass (no-shadow) finds locals that reuse the name of a module-level function or state variable, which is harmless today but a trap for the next edit in that scope.

**Evidence.** quote.js:278 `var note = $("[data-out-of-area]")` and :521 `var note = $("[data-quote-account]")` shadow `function note(stepSel, attr, text, before)` at :137; admin.js:340 `var show = …` shadows `show(which)` at :32; admin.js:167 parameter `act` shadows `act(b)` at :267; account.js:321 and :334 `var code = (e && e.code)` shadow the module-level referral `code` at :20; timeclock.js:57 `r`.

**Impact.** The next person who calls note(…) inside validate() gets "note is not a function"; an edit inside account.js's catch handlers that expects the referral code gets the Firebase error code instead.

**Suggestion.** Rename the locals (noteEl, errCode, visible, rr) and add no-shadow to the lint configuration.

### [LOW] Every request stores a `_subject` and a localized `requestedTime` nobody reads
`quote.js:412` · maintainability · lens: email-notify-flow

payload() writes an email subject and a visitor-language date string into the Firestore document; the rules whitelist them, but no page reads either, and the stored subject does not even match the one the email actually uses.

**Evidence.** quote.js:412 `p._subject = … + " (ZIP " + p.zip + ")"` and quote.js:398 `p.requestedTime = whenText()` (Spanish when the visitor used ES) go to Firestore via backend.js submitRequest; whitelisted at firestore.rules:65 and 76; grep for `_subject`/`requestedTime` in admin.js, account.js, timeclock.js finds no reader; the email subject is rebuilt differently at quote.js:423.

**Impact.** A misleading field in every record and in the rules, and a non-canonical date string sitting next to date/slot.

**Suggestion.** Delete both from payload() and from the quoteFields whitelist; mailFields() already derives everything from the saved fields.

### [LOW] 8-second timeout double-sends and tells a slow visitor it failed
`quote.js:459` · bug · lens: email-notify-flow

The timeout race resolves false but never aborts the fetch, so in the email-only path a slow Formspree response shows the failure message while the first POST still lands; the natural retry sends an identical second email.

**Evidence.** quote.js:459 `Promise.race([A.notify(mailFields(d, id)), new Promise(function (r) { setTimeout(function () { r(false); }, 8000); })])` with no AbortController in app.js:348. Local run with Formspree delayed 10 s (scratchpad quote-hang-10s.json): error text shown at 9.4 s, two POSTs captured with byte-identical bodies after the retry.

**Impact.** Duplicate emails count twice against the 50-a-month quota and confuse the owner; the visitor believes the request failed.

**Suggestion.** Abort the fetch at the deadline (AbortController), or in the email-only path treat a timeout as 'sent, confirmation pending' rather than failure; include the reference so duplicates are obvious.

### [LOW] Email fallback retries can double-send; a failed courtesy email leaves no trace
`quote.js:459` · ux · lens: error-resilience

mailCopy() races the Formspree POST against an 8-second timer but the POST keeps running after the timer wins. In the not-live and refused paths that timeout calls fail(), the visitor presses Send again, and both emails can arrive (two of the 50 monthly Formspree messages). In the normal live path a Formspree failure after a successful save is swallowed with no record, so the owner relies on noticing the admin page.

**Evidence.** Code: `return Promise.race([A.notify(mailFields(d, id)), new Promise(function (r) { setTimeout(function () { r(false); }, 8000); })]);` and line 496/510 `.then(function (ok) { ok ? done(false) : fail(); })`; app.js notify() has no AbortController. In harness scenario `denied` (formspree-down) a retry produced a second POST to formspree.io as expected of the retry path.

**Impact.** Occasional duplicate emails and consumed free-tier quota; a silently failed email means a saved request waits unnoticed until someone opens admin.html.

**Suggestion.** Pass an AbortController signal to fetch and abort it when the timer wins; in the live path, when the email fails, keep a `notified: false` flag on the request (or a console-visible badge on admin.html) so the owner can see which requests never emailed.

### [LOW] Failure message shows the business email as plain text, not a mailto link
`quote.js:487` · ux · lens: email-notify-flow

When sending fails the visitor is told to 'email mkgservices704@gmail.com', but the address is inserted with textContent, so on a phone there is nothing to tap and the typed request is not carried into the email.

**Evidence.** quote.js:487 `setError("submit", t("err.submit", { alt: alt }))`; setError() at quote.js:38-42 assigns `el.textContent`. Observed locally with Formspree returning 500: 'Couldn't send your request. Check your connection and try again or email mkgservices704@gmail.com.' with no link.

**Impact.** The fallback for the exact moment the site cannot send asks the visitor to retype everything.

**Suggestion.** Render the error with innerHTML and a `mailto:` whose subject is 'Quote request' and whose body is the summary() rows, so the fallback is one tap.

### [LOW] Sitemap lists two pages that carry noindex
`sitemap.xml:5` · seo · lens: devops-hosting

quote.html and commercial-quote.html are submitted in the sitemap but declare <meta name="robots" content="noindex">, a contradictory signal that Search Console reports as an error. The lastmod values are hand-typed and will drift from reality.

**Evidence.** sitemap.xml:5 `https://mkgservices.com/quote.html` and :9 `https://mkgservices.com/commercial-quote.html`; quote.html:8 and commercial-quote.html:8 `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">`. All lastmod values are 2026-10-16 except refer.html (10-17) while the last deploy was 2026-10-18T02:12Z; robots.txt:4 points crawlers at this sitemap.

**Impact.** Search Console flags "Submitted URL marked noindex" and the sitemap loses credibility as a freshness signal; the quote pages themselves are correctly kept out of the index.

**Suggestion.** Remove the two quote URLs from the sitemap (or drop noindex if you want them indexed), and generate lastmod in the deploy workflow from `git log -1 --format=%cs -- <file>`.

### [LOW] Sitemap lists quote pages that are marked noindex
`sitemap.xml:5` · seo · lens: rendered-smoke

quote.html and commercial-quote.html are submitted in the sitemap but both carry a robots noindex meta tag, which is contradictory and gets flagged by Search Console.

**Evidence.** sitemap.xml:5 `<loc>https://mkgservices.com/quote.html</loc>` and :9 `.../commercial-quote.html`; quote.html:8 and commercial-quote.html:8 `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">`. All ten sitemap URLs return 200 locally; robots.txt points at the sitemap.

**Impact.** Mixed signals to Google: the sitemap asks it to crawl pages it is told not to index, producing 'Submitted URL marked noindex' warnings and wasting crawl budget on a 10-page site.

**Suggestion.** Remove the two quote URLs from sitemap.xml (they are reached from every page anyway) or drop the noindex if the quote pages should rank.

### [LOW] vendor/qrcode.js ships without the MIT permission notice
`vendor/qrcode.js:9` · legal · lens: docs-onboarding

The vendored QR library's header names MIT by URL only; the licence's permission notice, which MIT requires to accompany copies, appears nowhere in the repository, and the upstream version is not recorded.

**Evidence.** vendor/qrcode.js:5-10 "Copyright (c) 2009 Kazuhiko Arase … Licensed under the MIT license: http://www.opensource.org/licenses/mit-license.php". The file is byte-identical to qrcode-generator@1.4.4 from npm (`diff` reports 0 lines), whose package also carries no LICENSE file. The repo has no LICENSE or NOTICE file; README.md:25 says only "`vendor/qrcode.js` (MIT)".

**Impact.** Minor licence non-compliance on a public site, and no recorded version to compare against when upstream fixes a bug.

**Suggestion.** Add vendor/LICENSE-qrcode.txt with the full MIT text and the copyright line, and note "qrcode-generator 1.4.4, unmodified" in README.

### What this area found well done

- Firebase is loaded from Hosting's reserved URLs (/__/firebase/init.json, backend.js:22-70), so no API key or config is committed and the same files run on the live domain, preview channels and the emulator, with a documented and working 'not connected' fallback (verified locally: every page loads with zero page errors). _(devops-hosting)_
- Sign-in is engineered for modern browsers: authDomain is switched to the site's own domain (backend.js:68) so Google's window names mkgservices.com and third-party-storage blocking does not break it, and signInWithPopup falls back to signInWithRedirect when popups are blocked (backend.js:181-183). _(devops-hosting)_
- The PR workflow is correctly scoped: it skips fork PRs (firebase-hosting-pull-request.yml:14) and declares least-privilege permissions (lines 7-10), and the repository has GitHub secret scanning with push protection enabled. _(devops-hosting)_
- HTML is served with max-age=0, must-revalidate, so visitors never see a stale page after a deploy, and the ignore list already keeps docs, brand sources, README and firestore.rules out of the intended upload set; the deploy pipeline itself has 15 consecutive successful 30-40 s runs. _(devops-hosting)_
- Private and transactional pages (admin, account, both quote forms) carry noindex, every public page has a canonical URL, and robots.txt plus sitemap.xml are present and consistent in their host; docs/SETUP-firebase.md is unusually complete on App Check, OAuth origins, budget alerts and the data model, which makes the hosting side reproducible. _(devops-hosting)_
- Linting is clean: an eslint pass with no-undef, no-unused-vars, eqeqeq and no-redeclare over all eleven site scripts (~5,000 lines) reports zero problems; every file is a strict-mode IIFE sharing one window.MKG namespace with explicit dependency guards (`if (!I || !B || !A) return;`). _(maintainability-drift)_
- Translation discipline is exemplary: 774 keys in English and 774 in Spanish with no duplicates and none missing on either side; every dynamically built key family (statuses, home/facility types, pets, flooring, times, days, units, steppers) resolves; the runtime warns on a missing key and keeps the page's existing text rather than showing the key; a Playwright pass of all 12 pages in both languages rendered no raw keys and no console errors beyond the expected local Firebase 404. _(maintainability-drift)_
- plans.js genuinely is the single source of truth: both service lines, pricing, calendar windows, referral amounts and the change-notice policy are read from it by every page, and docs/make-pricing-pdf.py reads plans.js rather than copying numbers (docs/MKG-pricing-logic.pdf matches the current rates). _(maintainability-drift)_
- backend.js normalises every record it returns (cleanRequest/cleanShift coerce types so one odd document cannot break a list) and firestore.rules is documented collection by collection, with the same table mirrored in docs/SETUP-firebase.md for whoever maintains it. _(maintainability-drift)_
- The pages are consistent where it is hardest to keep them so: all 77 ?v= tags are at 22, the topbar and header markup is byte-identical across all 12 pages apart from the intended commercial links, and comments explain intent at the awkward spots (drafts preserved across live redraws in admin.js, the courtesy-copy email race in quote.js:457-460, the "manual" hold). _(maintainability-drift)_
- mailFields() (quote.js:419-456) produces a genuinely readable owner email: flat labelled strings in a useful order, a subject that says who, what and when, Reply-To set via the `email` field so a plain reply reaches the client, prices formatted as dollars, lists joined into sentences, and an explicit 'Language: Spanish (reply in Spanish if you can)' hint. Verified by capturing the exact JSON body locally. _(email-notify-flow)_
- The email is correctly treated as a courtesy copy: the Firestore write comes first, and the Formspree POST is raced against an 8-second cap (quote.js:459, 506) so the visitor's confirmation is never held hostage to a third-party service. _(email-notify-flow)_
- Client-initiated moves and cancellations (account.js:183-205) email the owner with the old window, the new window, whether the visit had been confirmed and that it went back to New, which is exactly what is needed to act; captured bodies confirm it. _(email-notify-flow)_
- The privacy page discloses Formspree and Firebase as processors in plain language (privacy.html:79-80), and Firebase Auth emails are requested in the visitor's language (`auth.languageCode = lang()` in backend.js), so the Spanish experience is consistent as far as the templates allow. _(email-notify-flow)_
- The admin card already carries tel:/mailto: links, 'Prefers text.' and 'wrote in Spanish' hints, a Moved-by-client badge and a week-ahead panel, and firestore.rules keeps staff-only notes out of the client-readable document, so the data needed for a good notification flow is all in place. _(email-notify-flow)_
- Clean rendered baseline: 52 loads (13 URLs x EN/ES x 390/1280) produced zero uncaught exceptions, zero console errors other than the expected local 404, no data-i18n element showing its key, no unreplaced {placeholder} in text or attributes, no empty data-business slot, and no horizontal overflow at 390px on any page or quote step. Expected local-only failures, not counted: GET /__/firebase/10.14.1/firebase-app-compat.js 404 (backend.js:55) with its 'Failed to load resource' console line on quote, commercial-quote, availability, account and admin; the Formspree POST was intercepted by the harness and never sent. _(rendered-smoke)_
- Every link resolves: all internal hrefs and #anchors across the 12 pages exist on their targets, the ten sitemap.xml URLs and robots.txt return 200, and every favicon, webp/png brand asset, script and the Bricolage Grotesque font load successfully. _(rendered-smoke)_
- Bilingual implementation is genuinely complete: the EN and ES dictionaries have the same 774 keys with identical placeholder sets, html[lang] and <title> switch on every page, ?lang=es persists through nav links and built CTA hrefs, the browser language picks Spanish on first visit, and switching language at the review or confirmation step re-renders prices, es-US dates, review rows and button labels in place. _(rendered-smoke)_
- Quote-flow validation is specific and translated at every step (5-digit ZIP, name, reply-able email, phone only when Text or Call is chosen, business name on commercial), errors clear as you type, review 'Change' buttons return to the right step, the draft survives a reload, and user text containing HTML is escaped in the review and confirmation. _(rendered-smoke)_
- The booking calendar and the availability page behave as documented offline: the offline notice is shown in both languages, the chosen day and window carry from availability.html into both quote forms (including the commercial-only 6:30 pm window), a past ?date is discarded, and account.html/admin.html show translated 'not connected' notices with the sign-in controls disabled. _(rendered-smoke)_
- docs/SETUP-firebase.md's data-layout table (lines 162-173) matches firestore.rules collection by collection: every read/write claim I checked (holds public read, customer move/cancel only while new or confirmed, owner-only request delete, shifts visible to the member and owner, credits readable by the client with that email) is enforced in the rules file exactly as described. _(docs-onboarding)_
- plans.js is a genuinely usable single source of truth: every setting carries a plain-language comment, appCheckKey is explicitly marked "Public by design", the Formspree form is described with its owner and limits, and the formula is stated at the top; the pricing PDF reads its numbers from this file rather than retyping them and currently matches it line for line. _(docs-onboarding)_
- The "Sign-in domain, anti-spam, and billing" section of the setup guide records console-side state (OAuth origins and redirect URIs, the App Check monitor-to-enforce path and the risk slider, the budget alert) that is otherwise unrecoverable from the repo; this is exactly the kind of note a future maintainer needs. _(docs-onboarding)_
- docs/make-pages.py carries a loud do-not-run warning on its first line, and firebase.json's ignore list keeps docs/, brand/source, the Python scripts, firestore.rules and README out of the public deploy, so internal material never reaches the live site. _(docs-onboarding)_
- Operational cautions that matter are written down precisely: the Cloudflare "DNS only (grey cloud)" warning for certificate renewal (README:79), the workflow comment explaining the one secret and failing softly without it, and the Formspree over-limit behaviour. _(docs-onboarding)_
- backend.js's `ready` promise is designed to never reject: every failure (script load, init.json, App Check) degrades to a documented "not connected" mode, partial App Check load is handled explicitly (line 60), and every page branches on it rather than crashing. _(error-resilience)_
- Browser storage is handled defensively everywhere: with localStorage and sessionStorage patched to throw a SecurityError, nine pages (including the full quote flow, a ?ref= capture and a language switch) produced zero page errors in the harness; i18n, app state, drafts, invite codes and the account flag all sit in try/catch. _(error-resilience)_
- Deploy hygiene prevents stale-JS skew: all 77 script/style references carry ?v=22 and firebase.json serves HTML, JS and CSS with `max-age=0, must-revalidate`; the SDK version is pinned (10.14.1) on Firebase Hosting's reserved URLs so no third-party CDN is in the critical path. _(error-resilience)_
- Form limits mirror firestore.rules exactly (name 120, email 200, phone 40, notes 2000, company 160, ref 6), the submit button is disabled on the first click, a stale date is re-checked at send (quote.js:468), drafts survive a reload via sessionStorage, and a refused invite code is retried without it (quote.js:498-502). _(error-resilience)_
- Data and session robustness on the admin side: cleanRequest()/cleanShift() normalise every field so one odd document cannot break the list, stillAdmin() uses `{ source: "server" }` and ignores failures so a dropped connection never signs the team out, addCredit() is idempotent by id, and createCode() retries code collisions. _(error-resilience)_
